Duality for automorphic sheaves with nilpotent singular support
D. Arinkin, D. Gaitsgory, D. Kazhdan, S. Raskin, N. Rozenblyum, Y. Varshavsky

Abstract
. We identify the category Shv Nilp ⁡ ( Bun G ) \operatorname{Shv}_{\operatorname{Nilp}}(\operatorname{Bun}_{G}) of automorphic sheaves with nilpotent singular support with its own dual, and relate this structure to the Serre functor on Shv Nilp ⁡ ( Bun G ) \operatorname{Shv}_{\operatorname{Nilp}}(\operatorname{Bun}_{G}) and miraculous duality.



Introduction

0.1.
What is this paper about?

This paper is a sequel to the paper
[
AGKRRV
]
by the same set of authors. In
loc. cit.

a conjecture was suggested that the trace of the Frobenius endofunctor on the category of
automorphic sheaves with nilpotent singular support is isomorphic to the space of (non-ramified)
automorphic functions. This paper carries out the first step towards the proof of this conjecture.

0.1.1.

Let
X
be a smooth projective curve and
G
a reductive group (over a ground field
k
).
Let
\operatorname{Bun}_{G}
be the moduli stack of
G
-bundles on
X
. We consider the category of automorphic
sheaves
\operatorname{Shv}(\operatorname{Bun}_{G})
(see Sect.
A.1.1
for our conventions regarding sheaf theories)
and its full subcategory

\operatorname{Shv}_{\operatorname{Nilp}}(\operatorname{Bun}_{G})\subset\operatorname{Shv}(\operatorname{Bun}_{G}).

The category
\operatorname{Shv}_{\operatorname{Nilp}}(\operatorname{Bun}_{G})
is compactly generated, and hence dualizable. So, given an endofunctor

\Phi
of
\operatorname{Shv}_{\operatorname{Nilp}}(\operatorname{Bun}_{G})
, it makes sense to consider its trace

\operatorname{Tr}(\Phi,\operatorname{Shv}_{\operatorname{Nilp}}(\operatorname{Bun}_{G}))\in\operatorname{Vect},

where
\operatorname{Vect}
is the DG category of chain complexes of vector space over the field
{\mathsf{e}}
of coefficients of
our sheaf theory.

0.1.2.

By definition, the trace of an endofunctor
\Phi
of a dualizable category
{\mathbf{C}}
is the endofunctor of
\operatorname{Vect}
equal to the composition

\operatorname{Vect}\overset{\operatorname{unit}}{\longrightarrow}{\mathbf{C}}\otimes{\mathbf{C}}^{\vee}\overset{\Phi\otimes\operatorname{Id}}{\longrightarrow}{\mathbf{C}}\otimes{\mathbf{C}}^{\vee}\overset{\operatorname{counit}}{\to}\operatorname{Vect},

where
{\mathbf{C}}^{\vee}
is the dual category, and
\operatorname{unit}
and
\operatorname{counit}
are the unit and the counit of the duality, respectively.

Hence, in order to say something explicit about the trace
\operatorname{Tr}(\Phi,{\mathbf{C}})
, one must first describe
{\mathbf{C}}^{\vee}
and the functors

\operatorname{unit}
and
\operatorname{counit}
.

This is what we do for the category
\operatorname{Shv}_{\operatorname{Nilp}}(\operatorname{Bun}_{G})
.

0.1.3.

From the above perspective, the main result of this note (Theorem
3.2.2
) is easy to explain. It says that
there exists a canonical equivalence

(0.1)

\operatorname{Shv}_{\operatorname{Nilp}}(\operatorname{Bun}_{G})^{\vee}\simeq\operatorname{Shv}_{\operatorname{Nilp}}(\operatorname{Bun}_{G}),

for which the counit of the duality is the functor

\operatorname{Shv}_{\operatorname{Nilp}}(\operatorname{Bun}_{G})\otimes\operatorname{Shv}_{\operatorname{Nilp}}(\operatorname{Bun}_{G})\to\operatorname{Vect},

denoted
\operatorname{ev}^{l}_{\operatorname{Bun}_{G}}
, given by

{\mathcal{F}}_{1},{\mathcal{F}}_{2}\mapsto\operatorname{C}^{\cdot}_{c}(\operatorname{Bun}_{G},{\mathcal{F}}_{1}\overset{*}{\otimes}{\mathcal{F}}_{2}).

0.1.4.

We should emphasize that the above pairing is really different from the usual Verdier duality pairing,
which makes sense for a quasi-compact algebraic stack
{\mathcal{Y}}
:

\operatorname{ev}_{\mathcal{Y}}({\mathcal{F}}_{1},{\mathcal{F}}_{2}):=\operatorname{C}^{\cdot}_{\blacktriangle}(\operatorname{Bun}_{G},{\mathcal{F}}_{1}\overset{!}{\otimes}{\mathcal{F}}_{2})

(here
\operatorname{C}^{\cdot}_{\blacktriangle}(-)
is the renormalized version of the functor
of sheaf cochains, see Sect.
A.2.4
).

However,
\operatorname{Bun}_{G}
is
not
quasi-compact, so the pairing
\operatorname{ev}_{\operatorname{Bun}_{G}}
would
not make sense “as-is”. Rather,
\operatorname{ev}_{\operatorname{Bun}_{G}}
defines a perfect pairing between

\operatorname{Shv}(\operatorname{Bun}_{G})
(resp.,
\operatorname{Shv}_{\operatorname{Nilp}}(\operatorname{Bun}_{G})
) and its “co” version, denoted

\operatorname{Shv}(\operatorname{Bun}_{G})_{\operatorname{co}}
(resp.,
\operatorname{Shv}_{\operatorname{Nilp}}(\operatorname{Bun}_{G})_{\operatorname{co}}
), see Sect.
2.5
.

The relationship between
\operatorname{ev}^{l}_{\operatorname{Bun}_{G}}
and
\operatorname{ev}_{\operatorname{Bun}_{G}}
is the second main
point of this paper, to be discussed below.

0.1.5.

The assertion that
\operatorname{ev}^{l}_{\operatorname{Bun}_{G}}
defines a perfect pairing may seem innocuous enough,
but it is actually very non-trivial. In fact, it uses the full strength of some of the key results of the
paper
[
AGKRRV
]
.

Namely, consider the embedding

\operatorname{Shv}_{{\operatorname{Nilp}}\times{\operatorname{Nilp}}}(\operatorname{Bun}_{G}\times\operatorname{Bun}_{G})\hookrightarrow\operatorname{Shv}(\operatorname{Bun}_{G}\times\operatorname{Bun}_{G});

let

\operatorname{ps-u}_{\operatorname{Bun}_{G},{\operatorname{Nilp}}}\in\operatorname{Shv}_{{\operatorname{Nilp}}\times{\operatorname{Nilp}}}(\operatorname{Bun}_{G}\times\operatorname{Bun}_{G})

be the object equal to the value of the spectral projector
{\mathsf{P}}_{\operatorname{Bun}_{G},{\operatorname{Nilp}}}\boxtimes\operatorname{Id}_{\operatorname{Bun}_{G}}

(see Sect.
1.6.1
) on the object

(\Delta_{\operatorname{Bun}_{G}})_{!}(\underline{{\mathsf{e}}}_{\operatorname{Bun}_{G}})\in\operatorname{Shv}(\operatorname{Bun}_{G}\times\operatorname{Bun}_{G}),

where
\underline{{\mathsf{e}}}_{\operatorname{Bun}_{G}}\in\operatorname{Shv}(\operatorname{Bun}_{G})
is the constant sheaf.

The assertion of Theorem
3.2.2
is easily deduced from the fact that the above object
\operatorname{ps-u}_{\operatorname{Bun}_{G},{\operatorname{Nilp}}}

belongs to the essential image of (what is a priori just a fully faithful functor):

(0.2)

\operatorname{Shv}_{\operatorname{Nilp}}(\operatorname{Bun}_{G})\otimes\operatorname{Shv}_{\operatorname{Nilp}}(\operatorname{Bun}_{G})\to\operatorname{Shv}_{{\operatorname{Nilp}}\times{\operatorname{Nilp}}}(\operatorname{Bun}_{G}\times\operatorname{Bun}_{G}),

thereby providing the unit for the sought-for self-duality of
\operatorname{Shv}_{\operatorname{Nilp}}(\operatorname{Bun}_{G})
.

Now, it turns out that the functor (
0.2
) is actually an equivalence; this is the
highly non-trivial
[
AGKRRV
, Theorem 16.3.3]
. We give a slightly different proof of this
theorem in the present paper (see Theorem
1.6.7
), but one which still uses the key results of
[
AGKRRV
]
.

0.2.
Relation to miraculous duality and the Serre functor

0.2.1.

Recall that there
is
a way to define a self-duality on the entire category
\operatorname{Shv}(\operatorname{Bun}_{G})
,
as well as its subcategory
\operatorname{Shv}_{\operatorname{Nilp}}(\operatorname{Bun}_{G})
.

Namely, we consider the category
\operatorname{Shv}(\operatorname{Bun}_{G})_{\operatorname{co}}
(see Sect.
2.5
for the definition),
and Verdier duality defines an identification

\operatorname{Shv}(\operatorname{Bun}_{G})^{\vee}\simeq\operatorname{Shv}(\operatorname{Bun}_{G})_{\operatorname{co}},

with the counit defined by the pairing
\operatorname{ev}_{\operatorname{Bun}_{G}}
mentioned above.

Now, in the paper
[
Ga1
]
, it is shown that a certain canonically defined functor,
denoted in this note by

\operatorname{Mir}_{\operatorname{Bun}_{G}}:\operatorname{Shv}(\operatorname{Bun}_{G})_{\operatorname{co}}\to\operatorname{Shv}(\operatorname{Bun}_{G}),

is an equivalence. The functor
\operatorname{Mir}_{\operatorname{Bun}_{G}}
, which makes sense for any algebraic stack, was proposed by Drinfeld;
we refer to it as the
miraculous functor
.

Combining Verdier duality with the miraculous functor, we obtain an identification

(0.3)

\operatorname{Shv}(\operatorname{Bun}_{G})^{\vee}\simeq\operatorname{Shv}(\operatorname{Bun}_{G}).

Following Drinfeld, we refer to (
0.3
) as the
miraculous self-duality
of
\operatorname{Shv}(\operatorname{Bun}_{G})
.

0.2.2.

One shows (see Corollary
2.9.7
) that the identification (
0.3
)
induces an identification

(0.4)

\operatorname{Shv}_{\operatorname{Nilp}}(\operatorname{Bun}_{G})^{\vee}\simeq\operatorname{Shv}_{\operatorname{Nilp}}(\operatorname{Bun}_{G}).

Now, the second main result of this note, Corollary
3.3.7
, says that the identification
(
0.4
) equals (
0.1
) introduced in Sect.
0.1.3
.

0.2.3.

In the process of identifying (
0.4
) with (
0.1
) we relate

\operatorname{Mir}_{\operatorname{Bun}_{G}}
to the
pseudo-identity
and
Serre
functors
(see Sects.
5.2.3
and
5.1.4
for what we mean by the latter functors).

Namely, let
{\mathcal{U}}
be a universally
{\operatorname{Nilp}}
-
contruncative
quasi-compact open substack of
\operatorname{Bun}_{G}

(see Sect.
C.1.5
for what this means; such substacks form a cofinal subset among all quasi-compact open substacks of
\operatorname{Bun}_{G}
).
Consider the endofunctor

\operatorname{Mir}_{\mathcal{U}}:\operatorname{Shv}({\mathcal{U}})\to\operatorname{Shv}({\mathcal{U}}),

and let us restrict it to
\operatorname{Shv}_{\operatorname{Nilp}}({\mathcal{U}})\subset\operatorname{Shv}({\mathcal{U}})
.

We show that this restriction sends
\operatorname{Shv}_{\operatorname{Nilp}}({\mathcal{U}})
to
\operatorname{Shv}_{\operatorname{Nilp}}({\mathcal{U}})
. We show that
the resulting endofunctor of
\operatorname{Shv}_{\operatorname{Nilp}}({\mathcal{U}})
is an equivalence, which
identifies with
\operatorname{Ps-Id}_{\operatorname{Shv}_{\operatorname{Nilp}}({\mathcal{U}})}
,
and also with the
inverse
of the Serre functor
\operatorname{Se}_{\operatorname{Shv}_{\operatorname{Nilp}}({\mathcal{U}})}
. In particular, the categories

\operatorname{Shv}_{\operatorname{Nilp}}({\mathcal{U}})
are Serre (see Sect.
5.1.6
for what this means).

We should emphasize that the idea that for a Serre category, the Serre and pseudo-identity endofunctors
are mutually inverse goes back to A. Yom Din (it was recorded in the paper
[
GaYo
]
).

0.2.4.

Returning to the entire
\operatorname{Bun}_{G}
, we show that the Serre functor on
\operatorname{Shv}_{\operatorname{Nilp}}(\operatorname{Bun}_{G})
canonically
factors as

\operatorname{Shv}_{\operatorname{Nilp}}(\operatorname{Bun}_{G})\overset{\operatorname{Se}_{\operatorname{Shv}_{\operatorname{Nilp}}(\operatorname{Bun}_{G}),\operatorname{co}}}{\longrightarrow}\operatorname{Shv}_{\operatorname{Nilp}}(\operatorname{Bun}_{G})_{\operatorname{co}}\overset{\operatorname{Id}^{\operatorname{naive}}_{\operatorname{Bun}_{G}}}{\longrightarrow}\operatorname{Shv}_{\operatorname{Nilp}}(\operatorname{Bun}_{G}),

where the first arrow is an equivalence inverse to

\operatorname{Mir}_{\operatorname{Bun}_{G}}|_{\operatorname{Shv}_{\operatorname{Nilp}}(\operatorname{Bun}_{G})_{\operatorname{co}}}:\operatorname{Shv}_{\operatorname{Nilp}}(\operatorname{Bun}_{G})_{\operatorname{co}}\to\operatorname{Shv}_{\operatorname{Nilp}}(\operatorname{Bun}_{G}),

and the second arrow is the tautological functor, denoted
\operatorname{Id}^{\operatorname{naive}}_{\operatorname{Bun}_{G}}
, defined for any
non-quasi-compact stack (see Sect.
C.2.3
).

Thus, we obtain that
\operatorname{Shv}_{\operatorname{Nilp}}(\operatorname{Bun}_{G})
is “Serre, up to the issue of non-quasi-compactness”, which is compensated
by replacing the target
\operatorname{Shv}_{\operatorname{Nilp}}(\operatorname{Bun}_{G})
with
\operatorname{Shv}_{\operatorname{Nilp}}(\operatorname{Bun}_{G})_{\operatorname{co}}
.

Remark 0.2.5
.

The functor

\operatorname{Mir}_{\operatorname{Bun}_{G}}^{-1}:\operatorname{Shv}(\operatorname{Bun}_{G})\to\operatorname{Shv}(\operatorname{Bun}_{G})_{\operatorname{co}}

has been recently studied by L. Chen in
[
Ch
]
. Namely, in
loc. cit.

it was shown that it is canonically isomorphic to the
Deligne-Lusztig
functor,
the latter being an explicit complex whose terms are composites of Eisenstein and
Constant Term functors.

A spectral counterpart of the Deligne-Lusztig functor, which is an endofunctor of
the category
{\operatorname{IndCoh}}_{\operatorname{Nilp}}(\operatorname{LocSys}_{\check{G}}(X))
has been studied by D. Beraldo in
[
Be
]
.
In
loc. cit.
it is shown that the spectral Deligne-Lusztig functor is the composition

{\operatorname{IndCoh}}_{\operatorname{Nilp}}(\operatorname{LocSys}_{\check{G}}(X))\twoheadrightarrow{\operatorname{QCoh}}(\operatorname{LocSys}_{\check{G}}(X))\to{\operatorname{QCoh}}(\operatorname{LocSys}_{\check{G}}(X))\hookrightarrow{\operatorname{IndCoh}}_{\operatorname{Nilp}}(\operatorname{LocSys}_{\check{G}}(X)),

where the middle arrow
{\operatorname{QCoh}}(\operatorname{LocSys}_{\check{G}}(X))\to{\operatorname{QCoh}}(\operatorname{LocSys}_{\check{G}}(X))
is given by tensor product with
an explicit object of
{\operatorname{QCoh}}(\operatorname{LocSys}_{\check{G}}(X))
, called the Steinberg object, which is in some sense
“the structure sheaf of the locus of semi-simple local systems”.

0.3.
An intrinsic characterization of
\operatorname{Shv}_{\operatorname{Nilp}}(\operatorname{Bun}_{G})

0.3.1.

The isomorphism between the functors (
0.4
) with (
0.1
) has another,
rather unexpected consequence:

It turns out that the subcategory

\operatorname{Shv}_{\operatorname{Nilp}}(\operatorname{Bun}_{G})^{\operatorname{constr}}
can be characterized intrinsically, without mentioning the singular support condition.

0.3.2.

In Sect.
B
we recall the construction of a 2-category, whose objects are algebraic stacks,
and whose category of morphisms for a given pair of stacks
{\mathcal{Y}}_{1}
and
{\mathcal{Y}}_{2}
is

\operatorname{Shv}({\mathcal{Y}}_{1}\times{\mathcal{Y}}_{2}).

An object
{\mathcal{Q}}\in\operatorname{Shv}({\mathcal{Y}}_{1}\times{\mathcal{Y}}_{2})
gives rise to a functor

{\mathsf{Q}}:\operatorname{Shv}({\mathcal{Y}}_{1})\to\operatorname{Shv}({\mathcal{Y}}_{2}),

but the data of
{\mathcal{Q}}
carries more information. Namely,
{\mathcal{Q}}
gives rise to functors denoted

\operatorname{Id}_{\mathcal{Z}}\boxtimes{\mathsf{Q}}:\operatorname{Shv}({\mathcal{Z}}\times{\mathcal{Y}}_{1})\to\operatorname{Shv}({\mathcal{Z}}\times{\mathcal{Y}}_{2})

for any algebraic stack
{\mathcal{Z}}
that commute appropriately with functors that relate different
{\mathcal{Z}}
’s
(see Sect.
B.1.5
).

0.3.3.

Thus, given
{\mathcal{Q}}\in\operatorname{Shv}({\mathcal{Y}}_{1}\times{\mathcal{Y}}_{2})
one can ask whether it admits a right or a left adjoint
within the above 2-category.

For example,
{\mathcal{Q}}
admits a right adjoint if and only if the above functors
\operatorname{Id}_{\mathcal{Z}}\boxtimes{\mathsf{Q}}
admit right
adjoints for every
{\mathcal{Z}}
(this is not very restrictive),
and
if these right adjoints, denoted

(\operatorname{Id}\otimes{\mathsf{Q}})^{R}
, themselves commute appropriately with functors that relate different
{\mathcal{Z}}
’s
(this is a really non-trivial condition).

0.3.4.

Let us consider the case when
{\mathcal{Y}}_{1}=Y
is a connected separated scheme and
{\mathcal{Y}}_{2}=\operatorname{pt}
. Then an
object

{\mathcal{F}}\in\operatorname{Shv}(Y)=\operatorname{Shv}({\mathcal{Y}}_{1}\times{\mathcal{Y}}_{2})

admits a right adjoint if and only if the following conditions are satisfied: (i)
Y
is smooth and proper;
(ii)
{\mathcal{F}}\in\operatorname{Lisse}(Y)
(i.e.,
{\mathcal{F}}
is bounded, and each of its cohomologies is a locally system of finite rank).

0.3.5.

The third main result of this paper (Theorem
4.5.4
) says that an object

{\mathcal{F}}\in\operatorname{Shv}(\operatorname{Bun}_{G})^{\operatorname{constr}}
admits a right adjoint if and only if it belongs to
\operatorname{Shv}_{\operatorname{Nilp}}(\operatorname{Bun}_{G})
.

This result suggests that in a certain sense, the stack
\operatorname{Bun}_{G}
(or any
{\operatorname{Nilp}}
-cotruncative open substack
thereof) together with the subset
{\operatorname{Nilp}}
of its cotangent bundle, behaves similarly to a proper smooth
scheme
Y
, with
\{0\}\subset T^{*}(Y)
, see also Sects.
5.5.6
and
4.6.8
.

0.4.
Organization of the paper

Let us briefly review the contents of this work.

In Sect.
1
we extend the results of
[
AGKRRV
, Part III]
concerning
\operatorname{Shv}_{\operatorname{Nilp}}(\operatorname{Bun}_{G})

to the case when instead of
\operatorname{Bun}_{G}
, we consider the relative situation, i.e.,
{\mathcal{Z}}\times\operatorname{Bun}_{G}
,
where
{\mathcal{Z}}
is an arbitrary algebraic stack.

In Sect.
2
we show that the category
\operatorname{Shv}_{\operatorname{Nilp}}(\operatorname{Bun}_{G})
is self-dual via a combination
of Verdier duality and the miraculous functor.

In Sect.
3
we prove the main result relevant to the trace calculation, namely, that the
pairing
\operatorname{ev}^{l}_{\operatorname{Bun}_{G}}
defines the counit of a self-duality on
\operatorname{Shv}_{\operatorname{Nilp}}(\operatorname{Bun}_{G})
.

In Sect.
4
we establish the intrinsic characterization of the subcategory

\operatorname{Shv}_{\operatorname{Nilp}}(\operatorname{Bun}_{G})^{\operatorname{constr}}\subset\operatorname{Shv}(\operatorname{Bun}_{G})
as consisting of objects that admit
right adjoints when viewed as kernels.

In Sect.
5
we relate the miraculous functor on
\operatorname{Shv}_{\operatorname{Nilp}}(\operatorname{Bun}_{G})
to the Serre functor,
and establish the Serre property of categories
\operatorname{Shv}_{\operatorname{Nilp}}({\mathcal{U}})
, where
{\mathcal{U}}

is a universally
{\operatorname{Nilp}}
-contruncative quasi-compact open substack of
\operatorname{Bun}_{G}
.

In Sect.
A
we collect some basic facts pertaining to the category of sheaves
on algebraic stacks; in particular to Verdier duality in this situation and the functor of
renormalized direct image.

In Sect.
B
we review the theory of functors defined by kernels,
their behavior with respect to adjunctions and relation to the miraculous functor.

In Sect.
C
we review the theory of sheaves on non quasi-compact stacks,
the “co”- categories, and functors defined by kernels in this situation.

0.5.
Conventions

0.5.1.
Algebraic geometry

Throughout the note we will work over a ground field
k
, assumed algebraically closed.
The algebraic geometry over
k
will be
classical
, i.e., non-derived.

Our algebro-geometric objects will be either schemes of finite type or algebraic stacks locally
of finite type over
k
. In this note we will not need more general prestacks.

All
quasi-compact
algebraic stacks that appear in this paper will be of the form

Z/H
, where
Z
is a scheme (of finite type) and
H
a linear algebraic group. All
non quasi-compact algebraic stacks that appear in this paper will be unions of
quasi-compact stacks of the above form.

0.5.2.
Higher algebra

Let
{\mathsf{e}}
be a field of coefficients, assumed algebraically closed and of characteristic
0
.
Our main objects of study are DG categories over
{\mathsf{e}}
. In our treatment of DG categories
we follow the conventions of
[
AGKRRV
]
.

In particular, we will denote by
\operatorname{DGCat}
the
(\infty,1)
-category, whose objects are
cocomplete DG categories, and whose 1-morphisms are colimit-preserving functors.

The category
\operatorname{DGCat}
carries a symmetric monoidal structure, given by the Lurie tensor
product; we denote it by

{\mathbf{C}}_{1},{\mathbf{C}}_{2}\mapsto{\mathbf{C}}_{1}\otimes{\mathbf{C}}_{2}.

The unit object for this symmetric monoidal structure is the DG category of chain complexes
of
{\mathsf{e}}
-vector spaces, denoted
\operatorname{Vect}
.

In particular, we can talk about dualizable objects in
\operatorname{DGCat}
. These are

dualizable
DG categories. For a dualizable DG category
{\mathbf{C}}
we will denote
by
{\mathbf{C}}^{\vee}
its dual.

0.5.3.
Compact generation

For a given DG category
{\mathbf{C}}
and objects
{\mathbf{c}}_{1},{\mathbf{c}}_{2}\in{\mathbf{C}}
we will denote by

{\mathop{\operatorname{{\mathcal{H}}\it om}}}_{\mathbf{C}}({\mathbf{c}}_{1},{\mathbf{c}}_{2})\in\operatorname{Vect}

the object corresponding to the canonical enrichment of
{\mathbf{C}}
over
\operatorname{Vect}
.

For a given
{\mathbf{C}}
we will denote by
{\mathbf{C}}^{c}
the subcategory consisting of compact objects, i.e.,
those objects for which the functor

{\mathop{\operatorname{{\mathcal{H}}\it om}}}_{\mathbf{C}}({\mathbf{c}},-):{\mathbf{C}}\to\operatorname{Vect}

preserves colimits.

A category
{\mathbf{C}}
is said to be compactly generated if
{\mathop{\operatorname{{\mathcal{H}}\it om}}}_{\mathbf{C}}({\mathbf{c}},{\mathbf{c}}^{\prime})=0
for all
{\mathbf{c}}\in{\mathbf{C}}^{c}
implies

{\mathbf{c}}^{\prime}=0
. In this case,
{\mathbf{C}}
can be recovered as the
ind-completion
of its subcategory
{\mathbf{C}}^{c}
.

Furthermore, if
{\mathbf{C}}
is compactly generated, it is dualizable, and its dual
{\mathbf{C}}^{\vee}
can be described
explicitly as follows:
{\mathbf{C}}^{\vee}
is also compactly generated and its subcategory of compact objects

({\mathbf{C}}^{\vee})^{c}
identifies with
({\mathbf{C}}^{c})^{\operatorname{op}}
.

0.6.

[

]
,
about the relationship between the Serre and pseudo-identity functors.

The work of Y.V. was partially supported by ISF grants 822/17 and 2019/21.

1.
The category
\operatorname{Shv}_{\operatorname{Nilp}}(\operatorname{Bun}_{G})
and is variants

In this section we extend the results of
[
AGKRRV
, Part III]
concerning
\operatorname{Shv}_{\operatorname{Nilp}}(\operatorname{Bun}_{G})

to the case when instead of
\operatorname{Bun}_{G}
, we consider the relative situation, i.e.,
{\mathcal{Z}}\times\operatorname{Bun}_{G}
,
where
{\mathcal{Z}}
is an arbitrary algebraic stack.

1.1.
Hecke action in the relative situation

1.1.1.

For the duration of the paper we fix a smooth projective curve
X
,
and a reductive group
G
(both over
k
).

We let
\operatorname{Bun}_{G}
denote the moduli stack of principal
G
-bundles on
X
.

1.1.2.

For a finite set
I
, we consider the
I
-legged Hecke stack
{\mathcal{H}}_{G,X^{I}}

\operatorname{Bun}_{G}\overset{\overset{\leftarrow}{h}{}}{\leftarrow}{\mathcal{H}}_{G,X^{I}}\overset{\overset{\rightarrow}{h}{}\times s}{\longrightarrow}\operatorname{Bun}_{G}\times X^{I}.

Let

\operatorname{Sat}_{I}:{\mathop{\operatorname{\rm Rep}}}({\check{G}})^{\otimes I}\to\operatorname{Shv}({\mathcal{H}}_{G,X^{I}})

denote the geometric Satake functor.

We normalize it so that the value of
\operatorname{Sat}_{I}
on the trivial representation

({\mathbf{1}}_{{\mathop{\operatorname{\rm Rep}}}({\check{G}})})^{\otimes I}
is (the direct image of)
\omega_{\operatorname{Bun}_{G}\times X^{I}}
,
where

\operatorname{Bun}_{G}\times X^{I}\hookrightarrow{\mathcal{H}}_{G,X^{I}}

is the unit section.

Remark 1.1.3
.

The entire
{\mathcal{H}}_{G,X^{I}}
is an ind-algebraic stack, i.e., a filtered union of algebraic stacks
that map to each other by means of closed embeddings.

For each compact object
V\in{\mathop{\operatorname{\rm Rep}}}({\check{G}})^{\otimes I}
, the object
\operatorname{Sat}_{I}(V)\in\operatorname{Shv}({\mathcal{H}}_{G,X^{I}})

is supported on one of the closed algebraic substacks of
{\mathcal{H}}_{G,X^{I}}
.

Hence, in the discussion below,
one can start by working with compact objects of
{\mathop{\operatorname{\rm Rep}}}({\check{G}})^{\otimes I}
, and thus deal with usual
algebraic stacks, and then ind-extend to all of
{\mathop{\operatorname{\rm Rep}}}({\check{G}})^{\otimes I}
.

1.1.4.

Let
{\mathcal{Z}}
be an arbitrary algebraic stack. We can consider Hecke functors, denoted

(1.1)

\operatorname{Id}_{\mathcal{Z}}\boxtimes{\mathsf{H}}(-,-):{\mathop{\operatorname{\rm Rep}}}({\check{G}})^{\otimes I}\otimes\operatorname{Shv}({\mathcal{Z}}\times\operatorname{Bun}_{G})\to\operatorname{Shv}({\mathcal{Z}}\times\operatorname{Bun}_{G}\times X^{I}),\quad I\in\operatorname{fSet}.

Namely, for
V\in{\mathop{\operatorname{\rm Rep}}}({\check{G}})^{\otimes I}
, the Hecke functor
\operatorname{Id}_{\mathcal{Z}}\boxtimes{\mathsf{H}}(V,-)
is given by

(1.2)

\operatorname{Id}_{\mathcal{Z}}\boxtimes{\mathsf{H}}(V,{\mathcal{F}}):=(\operatorname{id}_{\mathcal{Z}}\times(\overset{\rightarrow}{h}{}\times s))_{*}\left((\operatorname{id}_{\mathcal{Z}}\times\overset{\leftarrow}{h}{})^{!}({\mathcal{F}})\overset{!}{\otimes}(\omega_{\mathcal{Z}}\boxtimes\operatorname{Sat}_{I}(V))\right),\quad{\mathcal{F}}\in\operatorname{Shv}({\mathcal{Z}}\times\operatorname{Bun}_{G}).

Note that in the above formula, the functor
(\operatorname{id}_{\mathcal{Z}}\times(\overset{\rightarrow}{h}{}\times s))_{*}
is canonically isomorphic
to the functor
(\operatorname{id}_{\mathcal{Z}}\times(\overset{\rightarrow}{h}{}\times s))_{\blacktriangle}
, since the morphism
\overset{\rightarrow}{h}{}\times s
is (ind)-schematic
(it is actually (ind)-proper).

The functors
\operatorname{Id}_{\mathcal{Z}}\boxtimes{\mathsf{H}}(V,-)
introduced above fall into the paradigm of functors
defined
by a kernel,
see Sect.
B.1
for what this means (see also Sect.
C.4
for
the formalism of functors defined by kernels on non quasi-compact stacks).

Thus, the notation
\operatorname{Id}_{\mathcal{Z}}\boxtimes{\mathsf{H}}(V,-)
is consistent with one in Sect.
B.1.3
.

1.1.5.

A key property of the objects of the form
\operatorname{Sat}_{I}(V)
, for
V\in({\mathop{\operatorname{\rm Rep}}}({\check{G}})^{\otimes I})^{c}
,
is that they are ULA with respect to the
map
\overset{\leftarrow}{h}{}\times s
(and, by symmetry, also with respect to the map
\overset{\rightarrow}{h}{}\times s
).

In particular, since
X^{I}
is smooth, the objects
\operatorname{Sat}_{I}(V)
, for
V\in({\mathop{\operatorname{\rm Rep}}}({\check{G}})^{\otimes I})^{c}
,
are ULA with respect to the projection
\overset{\leftarrow}{h}{}
. This implies that there are canonical isomorphisms

(\operatorname{Id}_{\mathcal{Z}}\times\overset{\leftarrow}{h}{})^{!}({\mathcal{F}})\overset{!}{\otimes}(\omega_{\mathcal{Z}}\boxtimes\operatorname{Sat}_{I}(V))\simeq(\operatorname{Id}_{\mathcal{Z}}\times\overset{\leftarrow}{h}{})^{*}({\mathcal{F}})\overset{*}{\otimes}(\underline{{\mathsf{e}}}_{\mathcal{Z}}\boxtimes(\operatorname{Sat}_{I}(V)\overset{!}{\otimes}\overset{\leftarrow}{h}{}^{!}(\underline{{\mathsf{e}}}_{\operatorname{Bun}_{G}}))),\quad{\mathcal{F}}\in\operatorname{Shv}({\mathcal{Z}}\times\operatorname{Bun}_{G}),

where we note that the operation
-\overset{!}{\otimes}\overset{\leftarrow}{h}{}^{!}(\underline{{\mathsf{e}}}_{\operatorname{Bun}_{G}})
amounts to
the cohomological shift
[-2\dim(\operatorname{Bun}_{G})]
.

Furthermore, the map
\overset{\rightarrow}{h}{}\times s
is ind-proper. This implies that the functor
\operatorname{Id}_{\mathcal{Z}}\boxtimes{\mathsf{H}}(V,-)

can be rewritten as

(\operatorname{Id}_{\mathcal{Z}}\times(\overset{\rightarrow}{h}{}\times s))_{!}\left((\operatorname{id}_{\mathcal{Z}}\times\overset{\leftarrow}{h}{})^{*}({\mathcal{F}})\overset{*}{\otimes}(\underline{{\mathsf{e}}}_{\mathcal{Z}}\boxtimes\operatorname{Sat}_{I}(V))\right)[-2\dim(\operatorname{Bun}_{G})].

This implies that the functors
\operatorname{Id}_{\mathcal{Z}}\boxtimes{\mathsf{H}}(V,-)
are
both defined and codefined by kernels
, see Sect.
B.3.6

for what this means. In particular, the functors
\operatorname{Id}_{\mathcal{Z}}\boxtimes{\mathsf{H}}(V,-)
commute with
*
-pullbacks and

!
-pushforwards along the
{\mathcal{Z}}
variable.

1.1.6.

Recall the monoidal category
{\mathop{\operatorname{\rm Rep}}}({\check{G}})_{{\operatorname{Ran}}}
, see
[
AGKRRV
, Sect. 11.1]
. As in

[
AGKRRV
, Sect. 15.1]
, the functors (
1.1
) gives rise to a monoidal action of

{\mathop{\operatorname{\rm Rep}}}({\check{G}})_{{\operatorname{Ran}}}
on
\operatorname{Shv}({\mathcal{Z}}\times\operatorname{Bun}_{G})
.

We will denote this action by

{\mathcal{V}}\in{\mathop{\operatorname{\rm Rep}}}({\check{G}})_{{\operatorname{Ran}}}\,\rightsquigarrow{\mathcal{V}}\star-.

For a fixed
{\mathcal{V}}
, we will also denote the above functor by

(1.3)

\operatorname{Id}_{\mathcal{Z}}\boxtimes{\mathsf{H}}_{\mathcal{V}}.

This is again consistent with the notation of Sect.
B.1
, since these functors
are defined by kernels, by construction.

We will refer to functors (
1.3
) as
integral Hecke functors
.

1.1.7.

The next observation will play an important role in the sequel:

Proposition 1.1.8
.

The functors
{\mathsf{H}}_{\mathcal{V}}
are
defined and codefined
by kernels.

The proof is given in the next subsection.

1.2.
Proof of Proposition
1.1.8

1.2.1.

We need to show that the functors
\operatorname{Id}_{\mathcal{Z}}\boxtimes{\mathsf{H}}_{\mathcal{V}}
of
(
1.3
) commute with
*
-pullbacks and
!
-pushforwards along the
{\mathcal{Z}}
variable.
It is enough to check this assertion on the generators of the category
{\mathop{\operatorname{\rm Rep}}}({\check{G}})_{{\operatorname{Ran}}}
.

Thus,
we fix a finite set
I
, an object
V\in({\mathop{\operatorname{\rm Rep}}}({\check{G}})^{\otimes I})^{c}
and
{\mathcal{M}}\in\operatorname{Shv}(X^{I})^{c}
. The corresponding
functor
\operatorname{Id}_{\mathcal{Z}}\boxtimes{\mathsf{H}}_{\mathcal{V}}
is given by

(1.4)

{\mathcal{F}}\mapsto(p_{{\mathcal{Z}}\times\operatorname{Bun}_{G}})_{*}((\operatorname{Id}_{\mathcal{Z}}\boxtimes{\mathsf{H}}(V,-))({\mathcal{F}})\overset{!}{\otimes}p_{X^{I}}^{!}({\mathcal{M}})),

where

{\mathcal{Z}}\times\operatorname{Bun}_{G}\overset{p_{{\mathcal{Z}}\times\operatorname{Bun}_{G}}}{\longleftarrow}{\mathcal{Z}}\times\operatorname{Bun}_{G}\times X^{I}\overset{p_{X^{I}}}{\longrightarrow}X^{I}

are the two projections.

We need to show that these functors commute with
*
-pullbacks and
!
-pushforwards along the
{\mathcal{Z}}
variable. We will
prove this by showing that the functor (
1.4
) is canonically isomorphic to the functor

(1.5)

{\mathcal{F}}\mapsto(p_{{\mathcal{Z}}\times\operatorname{Bun}_{G}})_{!}((\operatorname{Id}_{\mathcal{Z}}\boxtimes{\mathsf{H}}(V,-))({\mathcal{F}})\overset{*}{\otimes}p_{X^{I}}^{*}({\mathcal{M}}))[-2|I|].

From here, the commutation with
*
-pullbacks and
!
-pushforwards would then follow from the fact that the functor

{\mathsf{H}}(V,-)
is defined and codefined by a kernel, see Sect.
1.1.5
above.

We can assume that
{\mathcal{F}}
is compact. Since the map
p_{{\mathcal{Z}}\times\operatorname{Bun}_{G}}
is proper, the isomorphism between
(
1.4
) and (
1.5
) follows from the next assertion:

Proposition 1.2.2
.

An object of the form

(\operatorname{Id}_{\mathcal{Z}}\boxtimes{\mathsf{H}}(V,-))({\mathcal{F}})\in\operatorname{Shv}({\mathcal{Z}}\times\operatorname{Bun}_{G}\times X^{I}),\quad{\mathcal{F}}\in\operatorname{Shv}({\mathcal{Z}}\times\operatorname{Bun}_{G})^{c}

is ULA with respect to the projection
p_{X^{I}}:{\mathcal{Z}}\times\operatorname{Bun}_{G}\times X^{I}\to X^{I}
.

∎[Proposition
1.1.8
]

1.2.3.
Proof of Proposition
1.2.2

Since the map

\operatorname{id}_{\mathcal{Z}}\times(\overset{\rightarrow}{h}{}\times s):{\mathcal{Z}}\times{\mathcal{H}}_{G,X^{I}}\to{\mathcal{Z}}\times\operatorname{Bun}_{G}\times X^{I}

is ind-proper, it suffices to show that the object

(1.6)

(\operatorname{Id}_{\mathcal{Z}}\times\overset{\leftarrow}{h}{})^{!}({\mathcal{F}})\overset{!}{\otimes}(\omega_{\mathcal{Z}}\boxtimes\operatorname{Sat}_{I}(V))\in\operatorname{Shv}({\mathcal{Z}}\times{\mathcal{H}}_{G,X^{I}})

is ULA with respect to the map

p_{X_{I}}\circ(\operatorname{id}_{\mathcal{Z}}\times(\overset{\rightarrow}{h}{}\times s)),

where we note that

p_{X_{I}}\circ(\operatorname{id}_{\mathcal{Z}}\times(\overset{\rightarrow}{h}{}\times s))=s=p_{X_{I}}\circ(\operatorname{id}_{\mathcal{Z}}\times(\overset{\leftarrow}{h}{}\times s)).

Thus, it suffices to show that (
1.6
) is ULA with respect to the projection

p_{X_{I}}\circ(\operatorname{id}_{\mathcal{Z}}\times(\overset{\leftarrow}{h}{}\times s))
. However, this follows from the fact that

\operatorname{Sat}_{I}(V)
is ULA with respect to
\overset{\leftarrow}{h}{}\times s
using the following general lemma:

Lemma 1.2.4
.

Let

{\mathcal{Y}}_{1}\overset{p_{1}}{\leftarrow}{\mathcal{Y}}\overset{p_{2}}{\to}{\mathcal{Y}}_{2}

be a diagram of stacks. Let

{\mathcal{G}}\in\operatorname{Shv}({\mathcal{Y}})^{c}
be ULA with respect to the map

(p_{1},p_{2}):{\mathcal{Y}}\to{\mathcal{Y}}_{1}\times{\mathcal{Y}}_{2}.

Then for any
{\mathcal{G}}_{1}\in\operatorname{Shv}({\mathcal{Y}}_{1})^{\operatorname{constr}}
, the object

{\mathcal{G}}\overset{!}{\otimes}p_{1}^{!}({\mathcal{G}}_{1})\in\operatorname{Shv}({\mathcal{Y}})^{\operatorname{constr}}

is ULA with respect to
p_{2}
.

∎[Proposition
1.2.2
]

1.3.
Spectral decomposition in a relative situation

Let

{\operatorname{Nilp}}\subset T^{*}(\operatorname{Bun}_{G})

be the nilpotent cone. It is known to be half-dimensional, under very mild assumptions on
\operatorname{char}(k)
,
see
[
AGKRRV
, Sect. D]
, which we impose from now on.

In
[
AGKRRV
, Theorem 14.4.3]
, it was shown that the subcategory
\operatorname{Shv}_{\operatorname{Nilp}}(\operatorname{Bun}_{G})\subset\operatorname{Shv}(\operatorname{Bun}_{G})

can be characterized as that of “Hecke-lisse” objects. In this subsection we will establish an analog of this result
in the relative situation.

1.3.1.

For
V\in{\mathop{\operatorname{\rm Rep}}}({\check{G}})
, consider the corresponding Hecke functor

\operatorname{Id}_{\mathcal{Z}}\boxtimes{\mathsf{H}}(V,-):\operatorname{Shv}({\mathcal{Z}}\times\operatorname{Bun}_{G})\to\operatorname{Shv}({\mathcal{Z}}\times\operatorname{Bun}_{G}\times X).

Let

\operatorname{Shv}({\mathcal{Z}}\times\operatorname{Bun}_{G})^{\operatorname{Hecke-lisse}}

denote the full subcategory of
\operatorname{Shv}({\mathcal{Z}}\times\operatorname{Bun}_{G})
that consists of objects
{\mathcal{F}}
such that for
every
V\in{\mathop{\operatorname{\rm Rep}}}({\check{G}})
,

{\mathsf{H}}(V,{\mathcal{F}})\in\operatorname{Shv}({\mathcal{Z}}\times\operatorname{Bun}_{G})\otimes\operatorname{QLisse}(X)\subset\operatorname{Shv}({\mathcal{Z}}\times\operatorname{Bun}_{G}\times X).

As in
[
GKRV
, Proposition C.2.5]
, we obtain that the Hecke functors (
1.1
)
give rise to a compatible family of functors

(1.7)

{\mathop{\operatorname{\rm Rep}}}({\check{G}})^{\otimes I}\to{\mathop{\operatorname{\rm End}}}(\operatorname{Shv}({\mathcal{Z}}\times\operatorname{Bun}_{G})^{\operatorname{Hecke-lisse}})\otimes\operatorname{QLisse}(X)^{\otimes I}.

Thus, in the terminology of
[
AGKRRV
, Sect. 8.4.2]
, we obtain that
\operatorname{Shv}({\mathcal{Z}}\times\operatorname{Bun}_{G})^{\operatorname{Hecke-lisse}}

acquires an action of the (symmetric) monoidal category
{\mathop{\operatorname{\rm Rep}}}({\check{G}})^{\otimes X\operatorname{-lisse}}
.

1.3.2.

Let
\operatorname{LocSys}_{\check{G}}^{\operatorname{restr}}(X)
be the prestack introduced in
[
AGKRRV
, Sect. 1.4.2]
,
and recall now (see
[
AGKRRV
, Equation (8.10)]
) that we have a canonically defined functor

(1.8)

{\mathop{\operatorname{\rm Rep}}}({\check{G}})^{\otimes X\operatorname{-lisse}}\to{\operatorname{QCoh}}(\operatorname{LocSys}_{\check{G}}^{\operatorname{restr}}(X)).

A key result
[
AGKRRV
, Theorem 8.3.7]
says that the functor (
1.8
) is an equivalence.

Thus, we obtain that the above
{\mathop{\operatorname{\rm Rep}}}({\check{G}})^{\otimes X\operatorname{-lisse}}
-action on
\operatorname{Shv}({\mathcal{Z}}\times\operatorname{Bun}_{G})^{\operatorname{Hecke-lisse}}

is obtained from a uniquely defined monoidal action of the category

{\operatorname{QCoh}}(\operatorname{LocSys}_{\check{G}}^{\operatorname{restr}}(X))

on
\operatorname{Shv}({\mathcal{Z}}\times\operatorname{Bun}_{G})^{\operatorname{Hecke-lisse}}
.

We will refer to this phenomenon as the
spectral decomposition
of
\operatorname{Shv}({\mathcal{Z}}\times\operatorname{Bun}_{G})^{\operatorname{Hecke-lisse}}

along
{\operatorname{QCoh}}(\operatorname{LocSys}_{\check{G}}^{\operatorname{restr}}(X))
.

1.3.3.

Recall the symmetric monoidal functor

(1.9)

{\mathop{\operatorname{\rm Rep}}}({\check{G}})_{\operatorname{Ran}}\to{\mathop{\operatorname{\rm Rep}}}({\check{G}})^{\otimes X\operatorname{-lisse}},

see
[
AGKRRV
, Sect. 11.2.3]
. Its composition with (
1.8
) is the functor

\operatorname{Loc}:{\mathop{\operatorname{\rm Rep}}}({\check{G}})_{\operatorname{Ran}}\to{\operatorname{QCoh}}(\operatorname{LocSys}_{\check{G}}^{\operatorname{restr}}(X))

of
[
AGKRRV
, Sect. 12.7.1]
.

Following
[
AGKRRV
, Sect. 13.3.1]
, set

\operatorname{Shv}({\mathcal{Z}}\times\operatorname{Bun}_{G})^{\operatorname{spec}}:=\operatorname{Funct}_{{\mathop{\operatorname{\rm Rep}}}({\check{G}})_{\operatorname{Ran}}}({\operatorname{QCoh}}(\operatorname{LocSys}_{\check{G}}^{\operatorname{restr}}(X)),\operatorname{Shv}({\mathcal{Z}}\times\operatorname{Bun}_{G})).

Pre-composition with
\operatorname{Loc}
defines a functor

\operatorname{Shv}({\mathcal{Z}}\times\operatorname{Bun}_{G})^{\operatorname{spec}}\to\operatorname{Funct}_{{\mathop{\operatorname{\rm Rep}}}({\check{G}})_{\operatorname{Ran}}}({\mathop{\operatorname{\rm Rep}}}({\check{G}})_{\operatorname{Ran}},\operatorname{Shv}({\mathcal{Z}}\times\operatorname{Bun}_{G}))=\operatorname{Shv}({\mathcal{Z}}\times\operatorname{Bun}_{G}),

which is fully faithful, according to
[
AGKRRV
, Proposition 13.3.4]
.

Thus, we can view
\operatorname{Shv}({\mathcal{Z}}\times\operatorname{Bun}_{G})^{\operatorname{spec}}
as a full subcategory of
\operatorname{Shv}({\mathcal{Z}}\times\operatorname{Bun}_{G})
.

1.3.4.

By construction, the action of
{\mathop{\operatorname{\rm Rep}}}({\check{G}})_{\operatorname{Ran}}
on
\operatorname{Shv}({\mathcal{Z}}\times\operatorname{Bun}_{G})^{\operatorname{Hecke-lisse}}
factors
via (
1.9
).

Hence, we obtain

(1.10)

\operatorname{Shv}({\mathcal{Z}}\times\operatorname{Bun}_{G})^{\operatorname{Hecke-lisse}}\subset\operatorname{Shv}({\mathcal{Z}}\times\operatorname{Bun}_{G})^{\operatorname{spec}}.

1.3.5.

Consider also the subcategory

\operatorname{Shv}_{\frac{1}{2}\operatorname{-dim}\times{\operatorname{Nilp}}}({\mathcal{Z}}\times\operatorname{Bun}_{G})\subset\operatorname{Shv}({\mathcal{Z}}\times\operatorname{Bun}_{G}),

see Sect.
A.6.2
for the notation.

1.3.6.

We will prove:

Theorem 1.3.7
.

For any stack
{\mathcal{Z}}
the following four full subcategories of
\operatorname{Shv}({\mathcal{Z}}\times\operatorname{Bun}_{G})
coincide:

(i) The essential image of

\operatorname{Shv}({\mathcal{Z}})\otimes\operatorname{Shv}_{{\operatorname{Nilp}}}(\operatorname{Bun}_{G})\to\operatorname{Shv}({\mathcal{Z}}\times\operatorname{Bun}_{G})
.

(ii)
\operatorname{Shv}_{\frac{1}{2}\operatorname{-dim}\times{\operatorname{Nilp}}}({\mathcal{Z}}\times\operatorname{Bun}_{G})
;

(iii)
\operatorname{Shv}({\mathcal{Z}}\times\operatorname{Bun}_{G})^{\operatorname{Hecke-lisse}}
;

(iv)
\operatorname{Shv}({\mathcal{Z}}\times\operatorname{Bun}_{G})^{\operatorname{spec}}
;

Remark 1.3.8
.

We will eventually prove also that for a conical half-dimensional
closed subset
{\mathcal{N}}\subset T^{*}({\mathcal{Z}})
, the fully
faithful functor

\operatorname{Shv}_{\mathcal{N}}({\mathcal{Z}})\otimes\operatorname{Shv}_{{\operatorname{Nilp}}}(\operatorname{Bun}_{G})\to\operatorname{Shv}_{{\mathcal{N}}\times{\operatorname{Nilp}}}({\mathcal{Z}}\times\operatorname{Bun}_{G})

is an equivalence, see Theorem
4.4.2
.

1.4.
Proof of Theorem
1.3.7

1.4.1.

The inclusion (i)
\subset
(ii) is evident. Let us establish (ii)
\subset
(iii).

Let
{\mathcal{F}}
be an object in
\operatorname{Shv}_{\frac{1}{2}\operatorname{-dim}\times{\operatorname{Nilp}}}({\mathcal{Z}}\times\operatorname{Bun}_{G})
,
and let
V
be an object of
{\mathop{\operatorname{\rm Rep}}}({\check{G}})
. It follows
by the argument of
[
GKRV
, Theorem B.5.2]
that

{\mathsf{H}}(V,{\mathcal{F}})\in\operatorname{Shv}_{\frac{1}{2}\operatorname{-dim}\times{\operatorname{Nilp}}\times\{0\}}({\mathcal{Z}}\times\operatorname{Bun}_{G}\times X).

However, the subcategory
\operatorname{Shv}_{\frac{1}{2}\operatorname{-dim}\times{\operatorname{Nilp}}\times\{0\}}({\mathcal{Z}}\times\operatorname{Bun}_{G}\times X)

is contained in the essential image of

\operatorname{Shv}({\mathcal{Z}}\times\operatorname{Bun}_{G})\otimes\operatorname{QLisse}(X)\subset\operatorname{Shv}({\mathcal{Z}}\times\operatorname{Bun}_{G}\times X),

by Theorem
A.6.5
(applied to
Y_{1}=X
).

The inclusion (iii)
\subset
(iv) has been noted in Sect.
1.3.4
.
Thus, it remains to prove (iv)
\subset
(i).

1.4.2.

Let

Z_{n}\overset{f_{n}}{\to}\operatorname{LocSys}_{\check{G}}^{\operatorname{restr}}(X)

be as in
[
AGKRRV
, Sect. 16.1.2]
. Consider the category

\operatorname{Shv}({\mathcal{Z}}\times\operatorname{Bun}_{G})^{\operatorname{spec}}\underset{{\operatorname{QCoh}}(\operatorname{LocSys}_{\check{G}}^{\operatorname{restr}}(X))}{\otimes}{\operatorname{QCoh}}(Z_{n}),

equipped with the forgetful functor, denoted,
{\mathbf{oblv}}_{\operatorname{Hecke}}
:

\operatorname{Shv}({\mathcal{Z}}\times\operatorname{Bun}_{G})^{\operatorname{spec}}\underset{{\operatorname{QCoh}}(\operatorname{LocSys}_{\check{G}}^{\operatorname{restr}}(X))}{\otimes}{\operatorname{QCoh}}(Z_{n})\overset{\operatorname{Id}\otimes(f_{n})_{*}}{\longrightarrow}\\
\longrightarrow\operatorname{Shv}({\mathcal{Z}}\times\operatorname{Bun}_{G})^{\operatorname{spec}}\underset{{\operatorname{QCoh}}(\operatorname{LocSys}_{\check{G}}^{\operatorname{restr}}(X))}{\otimes}{\operatorname{QCoh}}(\operatorname{LocSys}_{\check{G}}^{\operatorname{restr}}(X))\simeq\\
\simeq\operatorname{Shv}({\mathcal{Z}}\times\operatorname{Bun}_{G})^{\operatorname{spec}}\hookrightarrow\operatorname{Shv}({\mathcal{Z}}\times\operatorname{Bun}_{G}).

The functor
{\mathbf{oblv}}_{\operatorname{Hecke}}
admits a left adjoint, to be denoted

(1.11)

(\operatorname{Id}_{\mathcal{Z}}\boxtimes{\mathsf{P}}_{Z_{n}})^{\operatorname{enh}},

see
[
AGKRRV
, Corollary 13.5.4]
.

1.4.3.

We will prove:

Proposition 1.4.4
.

Objects of the form

(\operatorname{Id}_{\mathcal{Z}}\boxtimes{\mathsf{P}}_{Z_{n}})^{\operatorname{enh}}({\mathcal{F}}_{\mathcal{Z}}\boxtimes\delta_{y}),\quad{\mathcal{F}}_{\mathcal{Z}}\in\operatorname{Shv}({\mathcal{Z}}),\quad y\in\operatorname{Bun}_{G}(k)

generate the category
\operatorname{Shv}({\mathcal{Z}}\times\operatorname{Bun}_{G})^{\operatorname{spec}}\underset{{\operatorname{QCoh}}(\operatorname{LocSys}_{\check{G}}^{\operatorname{restr}}(X))}{\otimes}{\operatorname{QCoh}}(Z_{n})
.

Let us assume this proposition temporarily and finish the proof of the containment (iv)
\subset
(i) in Theorem
1.3.7
.

1.4.5.

Consider the functor

\operatorname{Shv}({\mathcal{Z}}\times\operatorname{Bun}_{G})^{\operatorname{spec}}\underset{{\operatorname{QCoh}}(\operatorname{LocSys}_{\check{G}}^{\operatorname{restr}}(X))}{\otimes}{\operatorname{QCoh}}(Z_{n})\overset{\operatorname{Id}\otimes(f_{n})_{*}}{\longrightarrow}\\
\longrightarrow\operatorname{Shv}({\mathcal{Z}}\times\operatorname{Bun}_{G})^{\operatorname{spec}}\underset{{\operatorname{QCoh}}(\operatorname{LocSys}_{\check{G}}^{\operatorname{restr}}(X))}{\otimes}{\operatorname{QCoh}}(\operatorname{LocSys}_{\check{G}}^{\operatorname{restr}}(X))\simeq\operatorname{Shv}({\mathcal{Z}}\times\operatorname{Bun}_{G})^{\operatorname{spec}}.

As in
[
AGKRRV
, Sect. 16.1.4]
, one shows that the union (over the index
n
) of the essential images of these
functors generates
\operatorname{Shv}({\mathcal{Z}}\times\operatorname{Bun}_{G})^{\operatorname{spec}}
.

Hence, applying Proposition
1.4.4
, we obtain that in order to prove the containment (iv)
\subset
(i), it suffices to show that
for a fixed index
n
, the functor

(1.12)

\operatorname{Shv}({\mathcal{Z}}\times\operatorname{Bun}_{G})\overset{(\operatorname{Id}_{\mathcal{Z}}\boxtimes{\mathsf{P}}_{Z_{n}})^{\operatorname{enh}}}{\longrightarrow}\\
\longrightarrow\operatorname{Shv}({\mathcal{Z}}\times\operatorname{Bun}_{G})^{\operatorname{spec}}\underset{{\operatorname{QCoh}}(\operatorname{LocSys}_{\check{G}}^{\operatorname{restr}}(X))}{\otimes}{\operatorname{QCoh}}(Z_{n})\overset{{\mathbf{oblv}}_{\operatorname{Hecke}}}{\longrightarrow}\operatorname{Shv}({\mathcal{Z}}\times\operatorname{Bun}_{G})

applied to objects of the form

{\mathcal{F}}_{\mathcal{Z}}\boxtimes\delta_{y},\quad{\mathcal{F}}_{\mathcal{Z}}\in\operatorname{Shv}({\mathcal{Z}}),\quad y\in\operatorname{Bun}_{G}(k)

maps to the essential image of

\operatorname{Shv}({\mathcal{Z}})\otimes\operatorname{Shv}_{{\operatorname{Nilp}}}(\operatorname{Bun}_{G})\to\operatorname{Shv}({\mathcal{Z}}\times\operatorname{Bun}_{G}).

1.4.6.

Note that, as in
[
AGKRRV
, Equations (15.15) and (15.16)]
, the composite functor (
1.12
) is given by

\operatorname{Id}_{\mathcal{Z}}\boxtimes{\mathsf{H}}_{\mathcal{V}}

(see Sect.
1.1.6
for the notation), for

(1.13)

{\mathcal{V}}:=\left(\operatorname{Id}_{{\mathop{\operatorname{\rm Rep}}}({\check{G}})_{\operatorname{Ran}}}\otimes\Gamma(Z_{n},-)\right)({\mathsf{R}}_{Z_{n}})\in{\mathop{\operatorname{\rm Rep}}}({\check{G}})_{\operatorname{Ran}},

where

{\mathsf{R}}_{Z_{n}}\in{\mathop{\operatorname{\rm Rep}}}({\check{G}})_{\operatorname{Ran}}\otimes{\operatorname{QCoh}}(Z_{n})

is the object of
[
AGKRRV
, Sect. 15.3.1]
.

However, for any
{\mathcal{V}}\in{\mathop{\operatorname{\rm Rep}}}({\check{G}})_{\operatorname{Ran}}
,

(\operatorname{Id}_{\mathcal{Z}}\boxtimes{\mathsf{H}}_{\mathcal{V}})({\mathcal{F}}_{\mathcal{Z}}\boxtimes\delta_{y})\simeq{\mathcal{F}}_{\mathcal{Z}}\boxtimes{\mathsf{H}}_{\mathcal{V}}(\delta_{y}).

Now, for
{\mathcal{V}}
given by (
1.13
), we have

{\mathsf{H}}_{\mathcal{V}}(\delta_{y})\in\operatorname{Shv}_{{\operatorname{Nilp}}}(\operatorname{Bun}_{G}),

(e.g., by
[
AGKRRV
, Equations (15.15) and (15.16) and Corollary 15.5.4]
), which implies the desired containment.

∎[Theorem
1.3.7
]

1.5.
Proof of Proposition
1.4.4

1.5.1.

We will prove a slightly stronger assertion. Namely, let
y_{i}\in\operatorname{Bun}_{G}(k)
be points
chosen as in
[
AGKRRV
, Sect. 16.2.1-16.2.2]
. We will show that the objects

(\operatorname{Id}_{\mathcal{Z}}\boxtimes{\mathsf{P}}_{Z_{n}})^{\operatorname{enh}}({\mathcal{F}}_{\mathcal{Z}}\boxtimes\delta_{y_{i}})

generate

\operatorname{Shv}({\mathcal{Z}}\times\operatorname{Bun}_{G})^{\operatorname{spec}}\underset{{\operatorname{QCoh}}(\operatorname{LocSys}_{\check{G}}^{\operatorname{restr}}(X))}{\otimes}{\operatorname{QCoh}}(Z_{n}).

1.5.2.

By adjunction, it suffices to show that for every
0\neq{\mathcal{F}}\in\operatorname{Shv}({\mathcal{Z}}\times\operatorname{Bun}_{G})^{\operatorname{spec}}
, we can find

{\mathcal{F}}_{\mathcal{Z}}\in\operatorname{Shv}({\mathcal{Z}})
and an index
i
such that

(1.14)

{\mathop{\operatorname{{\mathcal{H}}\it om}}}({\mathcal{F}}_{\mathcal{Z}}\boxtimes\delta_{y_{i}},{\mathcal{F}})\neq 0.

Let
{\mathbf{i}}_{y_{i}}
be the map
\operatorname{pt}\to\operatorname{Bun}_{G}
corresponding to the point
y_{i}
. Our assertion is equivalent to saying that
there exists an index
i
such that

(\operatorname{id}_{\mathcal{Z}}\times{\mathbf{i}}_{y_{i}})^{!}({\mathcal{F}})\neq 0.

1.5.3.

Let
g:{\mathcal{Z}}_{1}\to{\mathcal{Z}}_{2}
be a map between algebraic stacks. The pullback functor

\operatorname{Shv}({\mathcal{Z}}_{2}\times\operatorname{Bun}_{G})\overset{(g\times\operatorname{id})^{!}}{\longrightarrow}\operatorname{Shv}({\mathcal{Z}}_{1}\times\operatorname{Bun}_{G})

is compatible with the Hecke action. In particular, it sends

\operatorname{Shv}({\mathcal{Z}}_{2}\times\operatorname{Bun}_{G})^{\operatorname{spec}}\to\operatorname{Shv}({\mathcal{Z}}_{1}\times\operatorname{Bun}_{G})^{\operatorname{spec}}.

1.5.4.

Let
\operatorname{Spec}(k^{\prime})\overset{{\mathbf{i}}_{z}}{\to}{\mathcal{Z}}
be a geometric point such that the !-pullback of
{\mathcal{F}}
to

\operatorname{Bun}^{\prime}_{G}:=\operatorname{Spec}(k^{\prime})\times\operatorname{Bun}_{G}\to{\mathcal{Z}}\times\operatorname{Bun}_{G}

is non-zero (such a point exists by a Cousin argument).

We can view the above !-pullback as the !-pullback along

\operatorname{Bun}^{\prime}_{G}\overset{{\mathbf{i}}^{\prime}_{z}}{\to}{\mathcal{Z}}^{\prime}\underset{\operatorname{Spec}(k^{\prime})}{\times}\operatorname{Bun}^{\prime}_{G},\quad{\mathcal{Z}}^{\prime}:=\operatorname{Spec}(k^{\prime})\times{\mathcal{Z}}

of the base change
{\mathcal{F}}^{\prime}
of
{\mathcal{F}}
to
{\mathcal{Z}}^{\prime}\underset{\operatorname{Spec}(k^{\prime})}{\times}\operatorname{Bun}^{\prime}_{G}
.

Tautologically,

{\mathcal{F}}^{\prime}\in\operatorname{Shv}({\mathcal{Z}}^{\prime}\underset{\operatorname{Spec}(k^{\prime})}{\times}\operatorname{Bun}^{\prime}_{G})^{\operatorname{spec}}.

By assumption,
({\mathbf{i}}^{\prime}_{z})^{!}({\mathcal{F}}^{\prime})\neq 0
, and by Sect.
1.5.3
,

({\mathbf{i}}^{\prime}_{z})^{!}({\mathcal{F}}^{\prime})\in\operatorname{Shv}(\operatorname{Bun}^{\prime}_{G})^{\operatorname{spec}}.

1.5.5.

Let

{\mathbf{i}}^{\prime}_{y_{i}}:\operatorname{Spec}(k^{\prime})\to\operatorname{Bun}^{\prime}_{G}

denote the base change of the map
{\mathbf{i}}_{y_{i}}
. We have

({\mathbf{i}}_{z})^{!}\circ(\operatorname{id}_{\mathcal{Z}}\times{\mathbf{i}}_{y_{i}})^{!}({\mathcal{F}})\simeq({\mathbf{i}}^{\prime}_{y_{i}})^{!}\circ({\mathbf{i}}^{\prime}_{z})^{!}({\mathcal{F}}^{\prime}).

Hence, it suffices to show that there exists an index
i
, such that

({\mathbf{i}}^{\prime}_{y_{i}})^{!}\circ({\mathbf{i}}^{\prime}_{z})^{!}({\mathcal{F}}^{\prime})\neq 0.

However, by
[
AGKRRV
, Proposition 15.4.4]
,

\operatorname{Shv}(\operatorname{Bun}^{\prime}_{G})^{\operatorname{spec}}=\operatorname{Shv}_{\operatorname{Nilp}}(\operatorname{Bun}^{\prime}_{G}),

so

0\neq({\mathbf{i}}^{\prime}_{z})^{!}({\mathcal{F}}^{\prime})\in\operatorname{Shv}_{\operatorname{Nilp}}(\operatorname{Bun}^{\prime}_{G}),

and the assertion follows from
[
AGKRRV
, Sect. 16.2.2-16.2.3]
.

∎[Proposition
1.4.4
]

1.6.
The projector onto the category with nilpotent singular support

In
[
AGKRRV
, Sect. 15.4.5]
, a particular object of
{\mathop{\operatorname{\rm Rep}}}({\check{G}})_{\operatorname{Ran}}
was introduced whose action on

\operatorname{Shv}(\operatorname{Bun}_{G})
effects a projection onto the full subcategory
\operatorname{Shv}_{\operatorname{Nilp}}(\operatorname{Bun}_{G})\subset\operatorname{Shv}(\operatorname{Bun}_{G})
.

In this subsection, we will establish an analog of this result in the relative situation.

1.6.1.

Let

{\mathsf{R}}\in{\mathop{\operatorname{\rm Rep}}}({\check{G}})_{\operatorname{Ran}}

be the object introduced in
[
AGKRRV
, Sect. 13.4.1]
.

Denote by
\operatorname{Id}_{\mathcal{Z}}\boxtimes{\mathsf{P}}
the resulting endofunctor of
\operatorname{Shv}({\mathcal{Z}}\times\operatorname{Bun}_{G})
, i.e.,

{\mathsf{P}}:={\mathsf{H}}_{\mathsf{R}},

as functors defined by kernels.

In what follows we will also use the notation

{\mathsf{P}}_{\operatorname{Bun}_{G},{\operatorname{Nilp}}}:={\mathsf{P}}.

Remark 1.6.2
.

For
{\mathcal{Z}}=\operatorname{pt}
, the resulting functor endofunctor
{\mathsf{P}}
of
\operatorname{Shv}(\operatorname{Bun}_{G})

is the one considered in
[
AGKRRV
, Sect. 15.4.7]
.

1.6.3.

We claim:

Theorem 1.6.4
.

The endofunctor
\operatorname{Id}_{\mathcal{Z}}\boxtimes{\mathsf{P}}
of
\operatorname{Shv}({\mathcal{Z}}\times\operatorname{Bun}_{G})
is a projector
onto the full subcategory

\operatorname{Shv}({\mathcal{Z}})\otimes\operatorname{Shv}_{\operatorname{Nilp}}(\operatorname{Bun}_{G})\simeq\operatorname{Shv}_{\frac{1}{2}\operatorname{-dim}\times{\operatorname{Nilp}}}({\mathcal{Z}}\times\operatorname{Bun}_{G})\subset\operatorname{Shv}({\mathcal{Z}}\times\operatorname{Bun}_{G}).

Proof.

Interpreting
\operatorname{Shv}_{\frac{1}{2}\operatorname{-dim}\times{\operatorname{Nilp}}}({\mathcal{Z}}\times\operatorname{Bun}_{G})
as
\operatorname{Shv}({\mathcal{Z}}\times\operatorname{Bun}_{G})^{\operatorname{spec}}

(see Theorem
1.3.7
), the assertion follows by
[
AGKRRV
, Remark 13.4.8]
.

∎

Corollary 1.6.5
.

The endofunctor
{\mathsf{P}}\boxtimes{\mathsf{P}}
of
\operatorname{Shv}(\operatorname{Bun}_{G}\times\operatorname{Bun}_{G})

is a projector onto the full subcategory

\operatorname{Shv}_{\operatorname{Nilp}}(\operatorname{Bun}_{G})\otimes\operatorname{Shv}_{\operatorname{Nilp}}(\operatorname{Bun}_{G})\subset\operatorname{Shv}(\operatorname{Bun}_{G}\times\operatorname{Bun}_{G}).

Proof.

Since
{\mathsf{P}}
acts as identity on
\operatorname{Shv}_{\operatorname{Nilp}}(\operatorname{Bun}_{G})
, it follows from (
B.3
) that
{\mathsf{P}}\boxtimes{\mathsf{P}}

acts as identity on
\operatorname{Shv}_{\operatorname{Nilp}}(\operatorname{Bun}_{G})\otimes\operatorname{Shv}_{\operatorname{Nilp}}(\operatorname{Bun}_{G})
.

We need to show that the essential image of
{\mathsf{P}}\boxtimes{\mathsf{P}}
is contained in
\operatorname{Shv}_{\operatorname{Nilp}}(\operatorname{Bun}_{G})\otimes\operatorname{Shv}_{\operatorname{Nilp}}(\operatorname{Bun}_{G})
.
We write

(1.15)

{\mathsf{P}}\boxtimes{\mathsf{P}}=({\mathsf{P}}\boxtimes\operatorname{Id}_{\operatorname{Bun}_{G}})\circ(\operatorname{Id}_{\operatorname{Bun}_{G}}\boxtimes{\mathsf{P}}).

By Theorems
1.6.4
and
1.3.7
, the essential image of
\operatorname{Id}_{\operatorname{Bun}_{G}}\boxtimes{\mathsf{P}}
is contained in

\operatorname{Shv}(\operatorname{Bun}_{G})\otimes\operatorname{Shv}_{\operatorname{Nilp}}(\operatorname{Bun}_{G})\subset\operatorname{Shv}(\operatorname{Bun}_{G})\otimes\operatorname{Shv}(\operatorname{Bun}_{G})\subset\operatorname{Shv}(\operatorname{Bun}_{G}\times\operatorname{Bun}_{G}).

By (
B.3
), the functor
{\mathsf{P}}\boxtimes\operatorname{Id}_{\operatorname{Bun}_{G}}
, restricted to
\operatorname{Shv}(\operatorname{Bun}_{G})\otimes\operatorname{Shv}(\operatorname{Bun}_{G})

acts as
{\mathsf{P}}\otimes\operatorname{Id}
. Hence, the essential image of its further restriction to
\operatorname{Shv}(\operatorname{Bun}_{G})\otimes\operatorname{Shv}_{\operatorname{Nilp}}(\operatorname{Bun}_{G})

is contained in the subcategory
\operatorname{Shv}_{\operatorname{Nilp}}(\operatorname{Bun}_{G})\otimes\operatorname{Shv}_{\operatorname{Nilp}}(\operatorname{Bun}_{G})
.

∎

1.6.6.

Finally, we observe that Theorem
1.6.4
gives us a (somewhat) alternative proof of
the following result (
[
AGKRRV
, Theorem 16.3.3]
):

Theorem 1.6.7
.

The functor

\operatorname{Shv}_{\operatorname{Nilp}}(\operatorname{Bun}_{G})\otimes\operatorname{Shv}_{\operatorname{Nilp}}(\operatorname{Bun}_{G})\to\operatorname{Shv}_{{\operatorname{Nilp}}\times{\operatorname{Nilp}}}(\operatorname{Bun}_{G}\times\operatorname{Bun}_{G})

is an equivalence.

Proof.

It is enough to show that the functor
{\mathsf{P}}\boxtimes{\mathsf{P}}
acts as identity on
\operatorname{Shv}_{{\operatorname{Nilp}}\times{\operatorname{Nilp}}}(\operatorname{Bun}_{G}\times\operatorname{Bun}_{G})
.
Writing
{\mathsf{P}}\boxtimes{\mathsf{P}}
as in (
1.15
), it suffices to show that both
\operatorname{Id}_{\operatorname{Bun}_{G}}\boxtimes{\mathsf{P}}
and

{\mathsf{P}}\boxtimes\operatorname{Id}_{\operatorname{Bun}_{G}}
act as identity on
\operatorname{Shv}_{{\operatorname{Nilp}}\times{\operatorname{Nilp}}}(\operatorname{Bun}_{G}\times\operatorname{Bun}_{G})
.

We have

\operatorname{Shv}_{{\operatorname{Nilp}}\times{\operatorname{Nilp}}}(\operatorname{Bun}_{G}\times\operatorname{Bun}_{G})\subset\operatorname{Shv}_{\frac{1}{2}\operatorname{-dim}\times{\operatorname{Nilp}}}(\operatorname{Bun}_{G}\times\operatorname{Bun}_{G})\cap\operatorname{Shv}_{{\operatorname{Nilp}}\times\frac{1}{2}\operatorname{-dim}}(\operatorname{Bun}_{G}\times\operatorname{Bun}_{G}).

Now, by Theorem
1.6.4
,
\operatorname{Id}_{\operatorname{Bun}_{G}}\boxtimes{\mathsf{P}}
acts as identity on
\operatorname{Shv}_{\frac{1}{2}\operatorname{-dim}\times{\operatorname{Nilp}}}(\operatorname{Bun}_{G}\times\operatorname{Bun}_{G})

and
{\mathsf{P}}\boxtimes\operatorname{Id}_{\operatorname{Bun}_{G}}
acts as identity on
\operatorname{Shv}_{{\operatorname{Nilp}}\times\frac{1}{2}\operatorname{-dim}}(\operatorname{Bun}_{G}\times\operatorname{Bun}_{G})
.

∎

1.7.
(Universally) Nilp-cotruncative substacks

In
[
AGKRRV
, Theorem 14.1.5]
, there was exhibited a collection of quasi-compact open substacks
{\mathcal{U}}\subset\operatorname{Bun}_{G}

with particularly favorable properties vis-a-vis the subcategory
\operatorname{Shv}_{\operatorname{Nilp}}(-)
.

In this subsection, we will show that these
properties carry over to the relative situation.

1.7.1.

We shall say that an open substack
{\mathcal{U}}\overset{j}{\hookrightarrow}\operatorname{Bun}_{G}
is
{\operatorname{Nilp}}
-cotruncative if:

•

It is
cotruncative
, i.e., if the functor
j_{!}
is defined by a kernel
(see Sect.
C.1.1
);

•

The functor
j_{!}
(equivalently,
j_{*}
) sends
\operatorname{Shv}_{\operatorname{Nilp}}({\mathcal{U}})
to
\operatorname{Shv}_{\operatorname{Nilp}}(\operatorname{Bun}_{G})
.

We shall say that an open substack
{\mathcal{U}}\overset{j}{\hookrightarrow}\operatorname{Bun}_{G}
is
universally

{\operatorname{Nilp}}
-cotruncative if,
moreover:

•

For any algebraic stack
{\mathcal{Z}}
, the functor

(\operatorname{id}\times j)_{!}:\operatorname{Shv}({\mathcal{Z}}\times{\mathcal{U}})\to\operatorname{Shv}({\mathcal{Z}}\times\operatorname{Bun}_{G})

(equivalently,
(\operatorname{id}\times j)_{*}
) sends

\operatorname{Shv}_{{\mathcal{N}}\times{\operatorname{Nilp}}}({\mathcal{Z}}\times{\mathcal{U}})\to\operatorname{Shv}_{{\mathcal{N}}\times{\operatorname{Nilp}}}({\mathcal{Z}}\times\operatorname{Bun}_{G})

for any closed conical subset
{\mathcal{N}}\subset T^{*}({\mathcal{Z}})
.

We refer the reader to Sect.
C.1.5
where the general notion of (universal) cotruncativeness relative
to a subset in the cotangent bundle is discussed.

1.7.2.

The following is an extension of the combination of
[
DrGa1
, Theorem 9.1.2]

and
[
AGKRRV
, Theorem 14.1.5]
(in fact, the arguments in
loc. cit.

prove this strengthened statement):

Theorem 1.7.3
.

The stack
\operatorname{Bun}_{G}
can be written as a filtered union of its quasi-compact
universally
{\operatorname{Nilp}}
-cotruncative open substacks.

In the terminology of Sect.
C.1.6
, the assertion of Theorem
1.7.3
is that
\operatorname{Bun}_{G}
is universally
{\operatorname{Nilp}}
-truncatable.

1.7.4.

For the remainder of this subsection, we fix a universally Nilp-cotruncative quasi-compact open substack

{\mathcal{U}}\overset{j}{\hookrightarrow}\operatorname{Bun}_{G}.

Let

\operatorname{Shv}_{\frac{1}{2}\operatorname{-dim}\times{\operatorname{Nilp}}}({\mathcal{Z}}\times{\mathcal{U}})\subset\operatorname{Shv}({\mathcal{Z}}\times{\mathcal{U}})

be as in Sect.
A.6.2
.

The assumption that
{\mathcal{U}}
is universally Nilp-cotruncative implies that the functors

(\operatorname{id}\times j)_{!}
and
(\operatorname{id}\times j)_{*}
map

\operatorname{Shv}_{\frac{1}{2}\operatorname{-dim}\times{\operatorname{Nilp}}}({\mathcal{Z}}\times{\mathcal{U}})\to\operatorname{Shv}_{\frac{1}{2}\operatorname{-dim}\times{\operatorname{Nilp}}}({\mathcal{Z}}\times\operatorname{Bun}_{G}).

1.7.5.

We claim:

Corollary 1.7.6
.

Let
{\mathcal{U}}\overset{j}{\hookrightarrow}\operatorname{Bun}_{G}
be a universally
{\operatorname{Nilp}}
-cotruncative open substack. Then for
any stack
{\mathcal{Z}}
, the functor

\operatorname{Shv}({\mathcal{Z}})\otimes\operatorname{Shv}_{{\operatorname{Nilp}}}({\mathcal{U}})\to\operatorname{Shv}_{\frac{1}{2}\operatorname{-dim}\times{\operatorname{Nilp}}}({\mathcal{Z}}\times{\mathcal{U}})

is an equivalence.

Proof.

We only have to show that the functor in question is essentially surjective. This
follows from the equivalence (i)
\Leftrightarrow
(ii) in Theorem
1.3.7

and the fact that in the commutative diagram

\begin{CD}\operatorname{Shv}({\mathcal{Z}})\otimes\operatorname{Shv}_{{\operatorname{Nilp}}}(\operatorname{Bun}_{G})@>{}>{}>\operatorname{Shv}_{\frac{1}{2}\operatorname{-dim}\times{\operatorname{Nilp}}}({\mathcal{Z}}\times\operatorname{Bun}_{G})\\
@V{\operatorname{Id}\otimes j^{*}}V{}V@V{}V{(\operatorname{id}\times j)^{*}}V\\
\operatorname{Shv}({\mathcal{Z}})\otimes\operatorname{Shv}_{{\operatorname{Nilp}}}({\mathcal{U}})@>{}>{}>\operatorname{Shv}_{\frac{1}{2}\operatorname{-dim}\times{\operatorname{Nilp}}}({\mathcal{Z}}\times{\mathcal{U}})\end{CD}

the vertical arrows are essentially surjective (which is in turn implied by the fact that they admit fully
faithful left adjoints, due to the universal
{\operatorname{Nilp}}
-cotruncativeness assumption).

∎

1.8.
The projector on universally Nilp-cotruncative substacks

In this subsection we fix a universally Nilp-cotruncative quasi-compact open substack

{\mathcal{U}}\overset{j}{\hookrightarrow}\operatorname{Bun}_{G}.

We will show how the results pertaining to the projector
{\mathsf{P}}
can be applied to
{\mathcal{U}}
.

1.8.1.

Denote by
{\mathsf{P}}_{{\mathcal{U}},{\operatorname{Nilp}}}
the endofunctor of
\operatorname{Shv}({\mathcal{U}})
defined by the kernel equal to

j^{*}\circ{\mathsf{P}}_{\operatorname{Bun}_{G},{\operatorname{Nilp}}}\circ j_{*},

where we remind that
{\mathsf{P}}_{\operatorname{Bun}_{G},{\operatorname{Nilp}}}
is the same as the functor defined by a kernel, denoted
{\mathsf{P}}
,
in Sect.
1.6.1
.

1.8.2.

We claim:

Corollary 1.8.3
.

For a stack
{\mathcal{Z}}
, the endofunctor
\operatorname{Id}_{\mathcal{Z}}\boxtimes{\mathsf{P}}_{{\mathcal{U}},{\operatorname{Nilp}}}
of

\operatorname{Shv}({\mathcal{Z}}\times{\mathcal{U}})
is a projector onto the full subcategory

\operatorname{Shv}({\mathcal{Z}})\otimes\operatorname{Shv}_{\operatorname{Nilp}}(\operatorname{Bun}_{G})\simeq\operatorname{Shv}_{\frac{1}{2}\operatorname{-dim}\times{\operatorname{Nilp}}}({\mathcal{Z}}\times\operatorname{Bun}_{G})\subset\operatorname{Shv}({\mathcal{Z}}\times{\mathcal{U}}).

Proof.

The fact that the endofunctor functor
\operatorname{Id}_{\mathcal{Z}}\boxtimes{\mathsf{P}}_{{\mathcal{U}},{\operatorname{Nilp}}}
maps

\operatorname{Shv}({\mathcal{Z}}\times{\mathcal{U}})
to
\operatorname{Shv}_{\frac{1}{2}\operatorname{-dim}\times{\operatorname{Nilp}}}({\mathcal{Z}}\times\operatorname{Bun}_{G})
follows
from the fact that
{\mathsf{P}}_{\operatorname{Bun}_{G},{\operatorname{Nilp}}}
maps

\operatorname{Shv}({\mathcal{Z}}\times\operatorname{Bun}_{G})
to
\operatorname{Shv}_{\frac{1}{2}\operatorname{-dim}\times{\operatorname{Nilp}}}({\mathcal{Z}}\times\operatorname{Bun}_{G})

(see Theorem
1.6.4
).

The fact that
\operatorname{Id}_{\mathcal{Z}}\boxtimes{\mathsf{P}}_{{\mathcal{U}},{\operatorname{Nilp}}}
acts as identity on
\operatorname{Shv}_{\frac{1}{2}\operatorname{-dim}\times{\operatorname{Nilp}}}({\mathcal{Z}}\times{\mathcal{U}})

follows from the fact that
(\operatorname{id}\times j)_{*}
maps
\operatorname{Shv}_{\frac{1}{2}\operatorname{-dim}\times{\operatorname{Nilp}}}({\mathcal{Z}}\times{\mathcal{U}})

to
\operatorname{Shv}_{\frac{1}{2}\operatorname{-dim}\times{\operatorname{Nilp}}}({\mathcal{Z}}\times\operatorname{Bun}_{G})
and the fact that
{\mathsf{P}}_{\operatorname{Bun}_{G},{\operatorname{Nilp}}}
acts as identity
on
\operatorname{Shv}_{\frac{1}{2}\operatorname{-dim}\times{\operatorname{Nilp}}}({\mathcal{Z}}\times\operatorname{Bun}_{G})
.

∎

Furthermore, from Corollary
1.6.5
we obtain:

Corollary 1.8.4
.

The endofunctor
{\mathsf{P}}_{{\mathcal{U}},{\operatorname{Nilp}}}\boxtimes{\mathsf{P}}_{{\mathcal{U}},{\operatorname{Nilp}}}
of
\operatorname{Shv}({\mathcal{U}}\times{\mathcal{U}})

is a projector onto the full subcategory

\operatorname{Shv}_{\operatorname{Nilp}}({\mathcal{U}})\otimes\operatorname{Shv}_{\operatorname{Nilp}}({\mathcal{U}})\subset\operatorname{Shv}_{{\operatorname{Nilp}}\times{\operatorname{Nilp}}}({\mathcal{U}}\times{\mathcal{U}})

Proof.

Indeed, the functor
{\mathsf{P}}_{{\mathcal{U}},{\operatorname{Nilp}}}\boxtimes{\mathsf{P}}_{{\mathcal{U}},{\operatorname{Nilp}}}
is the composition

\operatorname{Shv}({\mathcal{U}}\times{\mathcal{U}})\overset{(j\times j)_{*}}{\longrightarrow}\operatorname{Shv}(\operatorname{Bun}_{G}\times\operatorname{Bun}_{G})\overset{{\mathsf{P}}\boxtimes{\mathsf{P}}}{\longrightarrow}\\
\to\operatorname{Shv}_{\operatorname{Nilp}}(\operatorname{Bun}_{G})\otimes\operatorname{Shv}_{\operatorname{Nilp}}(\operatorname{Bun}_{G})\overset{j^{*}\otimes j^{*}}{\longrightarrow}\operatorname{Shv}_{\operatorname{Nilp}}({\mathcal{U}})\otimes\operatorname{Shv}_{\operatorname{Nilp}}({\mathcal{U}}).

∎

1.8.5.

Finally, from Theorem
1.6.7
we obtain:

Corollary 1.8.6
.

The functor

\operatorname{Shv}_{\operatorname{Nilp}}({\mathcal{U}})\otimes\operatorname{Shv}_{\operatorname{Nilp}}({\mathcal{U}})\to\operatorname{Shv}_{{\operatorname{Nilp}}\times{\operatorname{Nilp}}}({\mathcal{U}}\times{\mathcal{U}})

is an equivalence.

Proof.

Follows from Theorem
1.6.7
in the same way as Corollary
1.7.6
follows
from Theorem
1.3.7
.
∎

2.
Verdier duality for
\operatorname{Shv}_{\operatorname{Nilp}}(\operatorname{Bun}_{G})

This section is devoted to the study of the pattern of Verdier (i.e., usual) self-duality
on
\operatorname{Bun}_{G}
(and its quasi-compact open substacks) and how this self-duality interacts
with the subcategory
\operatorname{Shv}_{\operatorname{Nilp}}(\operatorname{Bun}_{G})\subset\operatorname{Shv}(\operatorname{Bun}_{G})
.

Since
\operatorname{Bun}_{G}
is not quasi-compact, the category that is naturally
dual to
\operatorname{Shv}(\operatorname{Bun}_{G})
is
\operatorname{Shv}(\operatorname{Bun}_{G})_{co}
(see Sect.
2.5
). That said,
the self-duality on
\operatorname{Shv}(\operatorname{Bun}_{G})
by identifying
\operatorname{Shv}(\operatorname{Bun}_{G})_{co}
using
the miraculous functor
\operatorname{Mir}_{\operatorname{Bun}_{G}}
, see Sect.
2.9
.

This leads us to study the corresponding subcategory

\operatorname{Shv}_{\operatorname{Nilp}}(\operatorname{Bun}_{G})_{\operatorname{co}}\subset\operatorname{Shv}(\operatorname{Bun}_{G})_{co}
.

2.1.
The diagonal object

We begin this section by establishing the behavior of the diagonal object on
\operatorname{Bun}_{G}

with respect to the Hecke action. The result is quite obvious (Proposition
2.1.6
),
but it serves as a basis for our further study.

2.1.1.

Let
{\mathcal{Y}}
be a quasi-compact algebraic stack. Throughout this paper, we use the following notation:

\operatorname{u}_{\mathcal{Y}}:=(\Delta_{\mathcal{Y}})_{*}(\omega_{\mathcal{Y}})\in\operatorname{Shv}({\mathcal{Y}}\times{\mathcal{Y}}).

2.1.2.

This object should not be confused with the unit of the Verdier self-duality on
\operatorname{Shv}({\mathcal{Y}})
,

\operatorname{u}_{\operatorname{Shv}({\mathcal{Y}})}\in\operatorname{Shv}({\mathcal{Y}})\otimes\operatorname{Shv}({\mathcal{Y}}),

see Sect.
A.4.3
.

When we view
\operatorname{Shv}({\mathcal{Y}})\otimes\operatorname{Shv}({\mathcal{Y}})
as a full subcategory of
\operatorname{Shv}({\mathcal{Y}}\times{\mathcal{Y}})

via (
A.15
), we have a tautological map

(2.1)

\operatorname{u}_{\operatorname{Shv}({\mathcal{Y}})}\to\operatorname{u}_{\mathcal{Y}}.

In fact, according to
[
AGKRRV
, Sect. 22.2.4]
,

\operatorname{u}_{\operatorname{Shv}({\mathcal{Y}})}
isomorphic to the value on
\operatorname{u}_{\mathcal{Y}}
of the right adjoint functor to (
A.15
),
and (
2.1
) is given by the counit of the adjunction.

2.1.3.

Let now
{\mathcal{Y}}
be not necessarily quasi-compact, but
truncatable
(see Sect.
C.1.4
for what this means).
In this case, one can still consider the object

(\Delta_{\mathcal{Y}})_{*}(\omega_{\mathcal{Y}})\in\operatorname{Shv}({\mathcal{Y}}\times{\mathcal{Y}}),

but we will denote it by

\operatorname{u}^{\operatorname{naive}}_{\mathcal{Y}}.

It has more refined variants, namely

(2.2)

\operatorname{u}_{{\mathcal{Y}},\operatorname{co}_{1}}\text{ and }\operatorname{u}_{{\mathcal{Y}},\operatorname{co}_{2}},

which are objects of the categories

(2.3)

\operatorname{Shv}({\mathcal{Y}}\times{\mathcal{Y}})_{\operatorname{co}_{1}}\text{ and }\operatorname{Shv}({\mathcal{Y}}\times{\mathcal{Y}})_{\operatorname{co}_{2}},

respectively, see Sects.
C.4.5
and
C.4.6
, where the above categories
and objects are defined, respectively.

By Sect.
C.4.4
, the categories in (
2.3
) correspond to functors defined by kernels

\operatorname{Shv}({\mathcal{Y}})\to\operatorname{Shv}({\mathcal{Y}})\text{ and }\operatorname{Shv}({\mathcal{Y}})_{\operatorname{co}}\to\operatorname{Shv}({\mathcal{Y}})_{\operatorname{co}},

respectively. The objects (
2.2
) correspond to the identity functor in both cases.

By contrast, objects of
\operatorname{Shv}({\mathcal{Y}}\times{\mathcal{Y}})
correspond to functors defined by kernels

\operatorname{Shv}({\mathcal{Y}})_{\operatorname{co}}\to\operatorname{Shv}({\mathcal{Y}}),

and the object
\operatorname{u}^{\operatorname{naive}}_{\mathcal{Y}}
corresponds to the functor

\operatorname{Id}^{\operatorname{naive}}_{\mathcal{Y}}:\operatorname{Shv}({\mathcal{Y}})_{\operatorname{co}}\to\operatorname{Shv}({\mathcal{Y}}),

see Sect.
C.2.3
.

2.1.4.

The key player in this section is the object

\operatorname{u}^{\operatorname{naive}}_{\operatorname{Bun}_{G}}\in\operatorname{Shv}(\operatorname{Bun}_{G}\times\operatorname{Bun}_{G}).

2.1.5.

In what follows, we will need the following property of
\operatorname{u}^{\operatorname{naive}}_{\operatorname{Bun}_{G}}
vis-a-vis the
Hecke action:

The category
{\mathop{\operatorname{\rm Rep}}}({\check{G}})
carries a canonical involution, denoted
\tau
, given by the Chevalley involution on
{\check{G}}
.
We will denote it by
V\mapsto V^{\tau}
. Let

{\mathcal{V}}\mapsto{\mathcal{V}}^{\tau}

denote the induced involution on
{\mathop{\operatorname{\rm Rep}}}({\check{G}})_{\operatorname{Ran}}
.

We claim:

Proposition 2.1.6
.

For
{\mathcal{V}}\in{\mathop{\operatorname{\rm Rep}}}({\check{G}})_{\operatorname{Ran}}
,

\sigma((\operatorname{Id}_{\operatorname{Bun}_{G}}\boxtimes{\mathsf{H}}_{\mathcal{V}})(\operatorname{u}^{\operatorname{naive}}_{\operatorname{Bun}_{G}}))\simeq(\operatorname{Id}_{\operatorname{Bun}_{G}}\boxtimes{\mathsf{H}}_{{\mathcal{V}}^{\tau}})(\operatorname{u}^{\operatorname{naive}}_{\operatorname{Bun}_{G}}),

where
\sigma
is the transposition acting on
\operatorname{Bun}_{G}\times\operatorname{Bun}_{G}
.

Proof.

Unwinding the construction of the functors
{\mathsf{H}}_{\mathcal{V}}
, we obtain that we need
to show that for every
V\in{\mathop{\operatorname{\rm Rep}}}({\check{G}})^{\otimes I}
, we have

\sigma((\operatorname{Id}_{\operatorname{Bun}_{G}}\boxtimes{\mathsf{H}}(V,-))(\operatorname{u}^{\operatorname{naive}}_{\operatorname{Bun}_{G}}))\simeq(\operatorname{Id}_{\operatorname{Bun}_{G}}\boxtimes{\mathsf{H}}(V^{\tau},-))(\operatorname{u}^{\operatorname{naive}}_{\operatorname{Bun}_{G}})

as objects of

\operatorname{Shv}(\operatorname{Bun}_{G}\times\operatorname{Bun}_{G}\times X^{I}),

where
\sigma
is the transposition of the
\operatorname{Bun}_{G}
factors.

By the definition of the Hecke action, the object

(\operatorname{Id}_{\operatorname{Bun}_{G}}\boxtimes{\mathsf{H}}(V,-))(\operatorname{u}^{\operatorname{naive}}_{\operatorname{Bun}_{G}})\in\operatorname{Shv}(\operatorname{Bun}_{G}\times\operatorname{Bun}_{G}\times X^{I})

identifies with

(\overset{\leftarrow}{h}{}\times\overset{\rightarrow}{h}{}\times s)_{*}(\operatorname{Sat}_{I}(V)).

The assertion now follows from the fact that there is a canonical isomorphism

(2.4)

\sigma(\operatorname{Sat}_{I}(V))\simeq\operatorname{Sat}_{I}(V^{\tau}),\quad V\in{\mathop{\operatorname{\rm Rep}}}({\check{G}})^{\otimes I}.

∎

2.2.
The projector and the diagonal

In this subsection we establish the behavior of the diagonal object

\operatorname{u}^{\operatorname{naive}}_{\operatorname{Bun}_{G}}
vis-a-vis the projector
{\mathsf{P}}
onto
the subcategory with nilpotent singular support.

2.2.1.

We claim:

Proposition 2.2.2
.

We have canonical isomorphisms

({\mathsf{P}}_{\operatorname{Bun}_{G},{\operatorname{Nilp}}}\boxtimes\operatorname{Id}_{\operatorname{Bun}_{G}})(\operatorname{u}^{\operatorname{naive}}_{\operatorname{Bun}_{G}})\simeq({\mathsf{P}}_{\operatorname{Bun}_{G},{\operatorname{Nilp}}}\boxtimes{\mathsf{P}}_{\operatorname{Bun}_{G},{\operatorname{Nilp}}})(\operatorname{u}^{\operatorname{naive}}_{\operatorname{Bun}_{G}})\simeq(\operatorname{Id}_{\operatorname{Bun}_{G}}\boxtimes{\mathsf{P}}_{\operatorname{Bun}_{G},{\operatorname{Nilp}}})(\operatorname{u}^{\operatorname{naive}}_{\operatorname{Bun}_{G}}).

Proof.

We will prove the second isomorphism; the first one would follow by symmetry.

In the notation of Sect.
1.6.1
, we have

(2.5)

({\mathsf{P}}_{\operatorname{Bun}_{G},{\operatorname{Nilp}}}\boxtimes{\mathsf{P}}_{\operatorname{Bun}_{G},{\operatorname{Nilp}}})(\operatorname{u}^{\operatorname{naive}}_{\operatorname{Bun}_{G}})=({\mathsf{H}}_{\mathsf{R}}\boxtimes{\mathsf{H}}_{\mathsf{R}})(\operatorname{u}^{\operatorname{naive}}_{\operatorname{Bun}_{G}})\simeq(\operatorname{Id}_{\operatorname{Bun}_{G}}\boxtimes{\mathsf{H}}_{\mathsf{R}})\circ({\mathsf{H}}_{\mathsf{R}}\boxtimes\operatorname{Id}_{\operatorname{Bun}_{G}})(\operatorname{u}^{\operatorname{naive}}_{\operatorname{Bun}_{G}}).

Note that

({\mathsf{H}}_{\mathsf{R}}\boxtimes\operatorname{Id}_{\operatorname{Bun}_{G}})(\operatorname{u}^{\operatorname{naive}}_{\operatorname{Bun}_{G}})\simeq\sigma((\operatorname{Id}_{\operatorname{Bun}_{G}}\boxtimes{\mathsf{H}}_{\mathsf{R}})(\operatorname{u}^{\operatorname{naive}}_{\operatorname{Bun}_{G}})),

and by Proposition
2.1.6
we can rewrite the latter as

(\operatorname{Id}_{\operatorname{Bun}_{G}}\boxtimes{\mathsf{H}}_{{\mathsf{R}}^{\tau}})(\operatorname{u}^{\operatorname{naive}}_{\operatorname{Bun}_{G}}).

Hence, the expression in (
2.5
) can be rewritten as

(2.6)

(\operatorname{Id}_{\operatorname{Bun}_{G}}\boxtimes{\mathsf{H}}_{\mathsf{R}})\circ(\operatorname{Id}_{\operatorname{Bun}_{G}}\boxtimes{\mathsf{H}}_{{\mathsf{R}}^{\tau}})(\operatorname{u}^{\operatorname{naive}}_{\operatorname{Bun}_{G}}).

Now, the canonicity of the construction of the object
{\mathsf{R}}
implies that we have a canonical
identification

{\mathsf{R}}^{\tau}\simeq{\mathsf{R}}.

Hence, we can further rewrite the expression in (
2.6
) as

(\operatorname{Id}_{\operatorname{Bun}_{G}}\boxtimes{\mathsf{H}}_{\mathsf{R}})\circ(\operatorname{Id}_{\operatorname{Bun}_{G}}\boxtimes{\mathsf{H}}_{\mathsf{R}})(\operatorname{u}^{\operatorname{naive}}_{\operatorname{Bun}_{G}})=(\operatorname{Id}_{\operatorname{Bun}_{G}}\boxtimes{\mathsf{P}}_{\operatorname{Bun}_{G},{\operatorname{Nilp}}})\circ(\operatorname{Id}_{\operatorname{Bun}_{G}}\boxtimes{\mathsf{P}}_{\operatorname{Bun}_{G},{\operatorname{Nilp}}})(\operatorname{u}^{\operatorname{naive}}_{\operatorname{Bun}_{G}}).

Now, the isomorphism with
(\operatorname{Id}_{\operatorname{Bun}_{G}}\boxtimes{\mathsf{P}}_{\operatorname{Bun}_{G},{\operatorname{Nilp}}})(\operatorname{u}^{\operatorname{naive}}_{\operatorname{Bun}_{G}})

follows from Theorem
1.6.4
.

∎

2.2.3.

In what follows we will denote the object that appears in Proposition
2.2.2
by

\operatorname{u}^{\operatorname{naive}}_{\operatorname{Bun}_{G},{\operatorname{Nilp}}}.

By Corollary
1.6.5
, it belongs to the full subcategory

\operatorname{Shv}_{\operatorname{Nilp}}(\operatorname{Bun}_{G})\otimes\operatorname{Shv}_{\operatorname{Nilp}}(\operatorname{Bun}_{G})\subset\operatorname{Shv}(\operatorname{Bun}_{G}\times\operatorname{Bun}_{G}).

2.2.4.

Let
{\mathcal{U}}\overset{j}{\hookrightarrow}\operatorname{Bun}_{G}
be a universally Nilp-cotruncative quasi-compact open prestack.
Denote by
\operatorname{u}_{{\mathcal{U}},{\operatorname{Nilp}}}
the object

(j\times j)^{*}(\operatorname{u}^{\operatorname{naive}}_{\operatorname{Bun}_{G},{\operatorname{Nilp}}})\in\operatorname{Shv}({\mathcal{U}}\times{\mathcal{U}}).

Since
\operatorname{u}^{\operatorname{naive}}_{\operatorname{Bun}_{G},{\operatorname{Nilp}}}\in\operatorname{Shv}_{\operatorname{Nilp}}(\operatorname{Bun}_{G})\otimes\operatorname{Shv}_{\operatorname{Nilp}}(\operatorname{Bun}_{G})
, we obtain that

\operatorname{u}_{{\mathcal{U}},{\operatorname{Nilp}}}
belongs to the full subcategory

\operatorname{Shv}_{\operatorname{Nilp}}({\mathcal{U}})\otimes\operatorname{Shv}_{\operatorname{Nilp}}({\mathcal{U}})\subset\operatorname{Shv}({\mathcal{U}}\times{\mathcal{U}}).

Proposition 2.2.5
.

The object
\operatorname{u}_{{\mathcal{U}},{\operatorname{Nilp}}}
is isomorphic to

({\mathsf{P}}_{{\mathcal{U}},{\operatorname{Nilp}}}\boxtimes\operatorname{Id}_{{\mathcal{U}}})(\operatorname{u}_{{\mathcal{U}}}),\,\,({\mathsf{P}}_{{\mathcal{U}},{\operatorname{Nilp}}}\boxtimes{\mathsf{P}}_{{\mathcal{U}},{\operatorname{Nilp}}})(\operatorname{u}_{{\mathcal{U}}})\text{ and }(\operatorname{Id}_{{\mathcal{U}}}\boxtimes{\mathsf{P}}_{{\mathcal{U}},{\operatorname{Nilp}}})(\operatorname{u}_{{\mathcal{U}}}).

Proof.

We have

({\mathsf{P}}_{{\mathcal{U}},{\operatorname{Nilp}}}\boxtimes\operatorname{Id}_{{\mathcal{U}}})(\operatorname{u}_{{\mathcal{U}}})\simeq((j^{*}\circ{\mathsf{P}}_{\operatorname{Bun}_{G},{\operatorname{Nilp}}}\circ j_{*})\boxtimes\operatorname{Id}_{{\mathcal{U}}})(\operatorname{u}_{{\mathcal{U}}})\simeq((j^{*}\circ{\mathsf{P}}_{\operatorname{Bun}_{G},{\operatorname{Nilp}}})\boxtimes\operatorname{Id}_{{\mathcal{U}}})\circ(j_{*}\boxtimes\operatorname{Id}_{{\mathcal{U}}})(\operatorname{u}_{{\mathcal{U}}})\simeq\\
\simeq((j^{*}\circ{\mathsf{P}}_{\operatorname{Bun}_{G},{\operatorname{Nilp}}})\boxtimes\operatorname{Id}_{{\mathcal{U}}})\circ(\operatorname{Id}_{{\mathcal{U}}}\boxtimes j^{*})(\operatorname{u}^{\operatorname{naive}}_{\operatorname{Bun}_{G}})\simeq(j\times j)^{*}\circ({\mathsf{P}}_{\operatorname{Bun}_{G},{\operatorname{Nilp}}}\boxtimes\operatorname{Id}_{\operatorname{Bun}_{G}})(\operatorname{u}^{\operatorname{naive}}_{\operatorname{Bun}_{G}})\simeq\\
\simeq(j\times j)^{*}(\operatorname{u}^{\operatorname{naive}}_{\operatorname{Bun}_{G},{\operatorname{Nilp}}})=\operatorname{u}_{{\mathcal{U}},{\operatorname{Nilp}}}.

This proves the first isomorphism. The third isomorphism follows by symmetry. To prove the middle isomorphism,
we note that

({\mathsf{P}}_{{\mathcal{U}},{\operatorname{Nilp}}}\boxtimes{\mathsf{P}}_{{\mathcal{U}},{\operatorname{Nilp}}})(\operatorname{u}_{{\mathcal{U}}})\simeq(\operatorname{Id}_{\mathcal{U}}\boxtimes{\mathsf{P}}_{{\mathcal{U}},{\operatorname{Nilp}}})\circ({\mathsf{P}}_{{\mathcal{U}},{\operatorname{Nilp}}}\boxtimes\operatorname{Id}_{\mathcal{U}})(\operatorname{u}_{{\mathcal{U}}})\overset{\text{1st isomorphism}}{\simeq}\\
\simeq(\operatorname{Id}_{\mathcal{U}}\boxtimes{\mathsf{P}}_{{\mathcal{U}},{\operatorname{Nilp}}})(\operatorname{u}_{{\mathcal{U}},{\operatorname{Nilp}}})\overset{\text{Corollary~\ref{c:projector U}}}{\simeq}\operatorname{u}_{{\mathcal{U}},{\operatorname{Nilp}}}.

∎

2.3.
Verdier self-duality for a universally Nilp-cotruncative substack

Let
{\mathcal{U}}\overset{j}{\hookrightarrow}\operatorname{Bun}_{G}
be a universally Nilp-cotruncative quasi-compact open substack.

In this subsection we will show that the Verdier self-duality on
\operatorname{Shv}({\mathcal{U}})

restricts to a self-duality of the full subcategory
\operatorname{Shv}_{\operatorname{Nilp}}({\mathcal{U}})\subset\operatorname{Shv}({\mathcal{U}})
.

2.3.1.

Throughout this paper, for a quasi-compact algebraic stack
{\mathcal{Y}}
we denote by
\operatorname{ev}_{\mathcal{Y}}
the functor

(2.7)

\operatorname{Shv}({\mathcal{Y}})\otimes\operatorname{Shv}({\mathcal{Y}})\overset{\overset{!}{\otimes}}{\to}\operatorname{Shv}({\mathcal{Y}})\overset{\operatorname{C}^{\cdot}_{\blacktriangle}({\mathcal{Y}},-)}{\longrightarrow}\operatorname{Vect}.

Recall that according to Sect.
A.4
, the functor (
2.7
) defines the counit
of a self-duality on
\operatorname{Shv}({\mathcal{Y}})
, which we refer to as
Verdier self-duality
.

2.3.2.

We claim:

Proposition 2.3.3
.

The functors

(2.8)

\operatorname{Shv}_{\operatorname{Nilp}}({\mathcal{U}})\otimes\operatorname{Shv}_{\operatorname{Nilp}}({\mathcal{U}})\to\operatorname{Shv}({\mathcal{U}})\otimes\operatorname{Shv}({\mathcal{U}})\overset{\operatorname{ev}_{\mathcal{U}}}{\longrightarrow}\operatorname{Vect}

and
