\operatorname{u}_{{\mathcal{U}},{\operatorname{Nilp}}}\in\operatorname{Shv}_{\operatorname{Nilp}}({\mathcal{U}})\otimes\operatorname{Shv}_{\operatorname{Nilp}}({\mathcal{U}})

define a datum of self-duality on
\operatorname{Shv}_{\operatorname{Nilp}}({\mathcal{U}})
.

Proof.

Let
{\mathcal{F}}
be an object of
\operatorname{Shv}_{\operatorname{Nilp}}({\mathcal{U}})
. We have to construct a canonical isomorphism

(p_{2})_{\blacktriangle}(p_{1}^{!}({\mathcal{F}})\overset{!}{\otimes}\operatorname{u}_{{\mathcal{U}},{\operatorname{Nilp}}})\simeq{\mathcal{F}}.

Using Proposition
2.2.5
and interpreting
\operatorname{u}_{{\mathcal{U}},{\operatorname{Nilp}}}
as
(\operatorname{Id}_{{\mathcal{U}}}\boxtimes{\mathsf{P}}_{{\mathcal{U}},{\operatorname{Nilp}}})(\operatorname{u}_{{\mathcal{U}}})
, we rewrite

(p_{2})_{\blacktriangle}(p_{1}^{!}({\mathcal{F}})\overset{!}{\otimes}\operatorname{u}_{{\mathcal{U}},{\operatorname{Nilp}}})\simeq{\mathsf{P}}_{{\mathcal{U}},{\operatorname{Nilp}}}((p_{2})_{\blacktriangle}(p_{1}^{!}({\mathcal{F}})\overset{!}{\otimes}\operatorname{u}_{{\mathcal{U}}}))\simeq{\mathsf{P}}_{{\mathcal{U}},{\operatorname{Nilp}}}({\mathcal{F}}),

while the latter is isomorphic to
{\mathcal{F}}
by Corollary
1.8.3
.

∎

Corollary 2.3.4
.

The pair
({\mathcal{U}},{\operatorname{Nilp}})
is duality-adapted
1
1

1

See Sect.
A.5.4
for what this means.
.

2.3.5.

We now claim:

Proposition 2.3.6
.

With respect to the self-duality

\operatorname{Shv}({\mathcal{U}})^{\vee}\simeq\operatorname{Shv}({\mathcal{U}})

of (
A.12
) and the self-duality

\operatorname{Shv}_{\operatorname{Nilp}}({\mathcal{U}})^{\vee}\simeq\operatorname{Shv}_{\operatorname{Nilp}}({\mathcal{U}})

of Proposition
2.3.3
, the functor

{\mathsf{P}}_{{\mathcal{U}},{\operatorname{Nilp}}}:\operatorname{Shv}({\mathcal{U}})\to\operatorname{Shv}_{\operatorname{Nilp}}({\mathcal{U}})

identifies with the dual of the tautological embedding

\iota_{\mathcal{U}}:\operatorname{Shv}_{\operatorname{Nilp}}({\mathcal{U}})\hookrightarrow\operatorname{Shv}({\mathcal{U}})
.

Proof.

We need to establish a canonical isomorphism

(2.9)

(\operatorname{Id}_{\operatorname{Shv}({\mathcal{U}})}\otimes{\mathsf{P}}_{{\mathcal{U}},{\operatorname{Nilp}}})(\operatorname{u}_{\operatorname{Shv}({\mathcal{U}})})\simeq\operatorname{u}_{{\mathcal{U}},{\operatorname{Nilp}}}

(here
\operatorname{u}_{\operatorname{Shv}({\mathcal{U}})}
is as in Sect.
2.1.2
) as objects of

\operatorname{Shv}({\mathcal{U}})\otimes\operatorname{Shv}_{\operatorname{Nilp}}({\mathcal{U}})\subset\operatorname{Shv}({\mathcal{U}})\otimes\operatorname{Shv}({\mathcal{U}}).

Let
{\mathcal{F}}_{1},{\mathcal{F}}_{2}
be a pair of compact objects of
\operatorname{Shv}({\mathcal{U}})
, and let
us calculate

{\mathop{\operatorname{{\mathcal{H}}\it om}}}_{\operatorname{Shv}({\mathcal{U}})\otimes\operatorname{Shv}({\mathcal{U}})}({\mathcal{F}}_{1}\otimes{\mathcal{F}}_{2},-)

into both sides of (
2.9
).

Note that since
{\mathcal{F}}_{1}
is compact, the functor

{\mathop{\operatorname{{\mathcal{H}}\it om}}}_{\operatorname{Shv}({\mathcal{U}})}({\mathcal{F}}_{1},-):\operatorname{Shv}({\mathcal{U}})\to\operatorname{Vect}

is continuous. Furthermore, the functor

{\mathop{\operatorname{{\mathcal{H}}\it om}}}_{\operatorname{Shv}({\mathcal{U}})}({\mathcal{F}}_{1},-)\otimes\operatorname{Id}_{\operatorname{Shv}({\mathcal{U}})}:\operatorname{Shv}({\mathcal{U}})\otimes\operatorname{Shv}({\mathcal{U}})\to\operatorname{Shv}({\mathcal{U}})

sends
\operatorname{u}_{\operatorname{Shv}({\mathcal{U}})}
to
{\mathbb{D}}^{\operatorname{Verdier}}({\mathcal{F}}_{1})
.

Hence, for the left-hand side of (
2.9
), we obtain

{\mathop{\operatorname{{\mathcal{H}}\it om}}}_{\operatorname{Shv}({\mathcal{U}})\otimes\operatorname{Shv}({\mathcal{U}})}({\mathcal{F}}_{1}\otimes{\mathcal{F}}_{2},(\operatorname{Id}_{\operatorname{Shv}({\mathcal{U}})}\otimes{\mathsf{P}}_{{\mathcal{U}},{\operatorname{Nilp}}})(\operatorname{u}_{\operatorname{Shv}({\mathcal{U}})}))\simeq\\
\simeq{\mathop{\operatorname{{\mathcal{H}}\it om}}}_{\operatorname{Shv}({\mathcal{U}})}\left({\mathcal{F}}_{2},({\mathop{\operatorname{{\mathcal{H}}\it om}}}_{\operatorname{Shv}({\mathcal{U}})}({\mathcal{F}}_{1},-)\otimes\operatorname{Id}_{\operatorname{Shv}({\mathcal{U}})})\circ(\operatorname{Id}_{\operatorname{Shv}({\mathcal{U}})}\otimes{\mathsf{P}}_{{\mathcal{U}},{\operatorname{Nilp}}})(\operatorname{u}_{\operatorname{Shv}({\mathcal{U}})})\right)\simeq\\
\simeq{\mathop{\operatorname{{\mathcal{H}}\it om}}}_{\operatorname{Shv}({\mathcal{U}})}\left({\mathcal{F}}_{2},{\mathsf{P}}_{{\mathcal{U}},{\operatorname{Nilp}}}\circ({\mathop{\operatorname{{\mathcal{H}}\it om}}}_{\operatorname{Shv}({\mathcal{U}})}({\mathcal{F}}_{1},-)\otimes\operatorname{Id}_{\operatorname{Shv}({\mathcal{U}})})(\operatorname{u}_{\operatorname{Shv}({\mathcal{U}})})\right)\simeq\\
\simeq{\mathop{\operatorname{{\mathcal{H}}\it om}}}_{\operatorname{Shv}({\mathcal{U}})}({\mathcal{F}}_{2},{\mathsf{P}}_{{\mathcal{U}},{\operatorname{Nilp}}}({\mathbb{D}}^{\operatorname{Verdier}}({\mathcal{F}}_{1}))).

Let us interpret
\operatorname{u}_{{\mathcal{U}},{\operatorname{Nilp}}}
as
(\operatorname{Id}_{\mathcal{U}}\boxtimes{\mathsf{P}}_{{\mathcal{U}},{\operatorname{Nilp}}})(\operatorname{u}_{\mathcal{U}})
. Note that since

{\mathcal{F}}_{1}
and
{\mathcal{F}}_{2}
are compact, for any
{\mathcal{F}}\in\operatorname{Shv}({\mathcal{U}}\times{\mathcal{U}})
, we have

{\mathop{\operatorname{{\mathcal{H}}\it om}}}_{\operatorname{Shv}({\mathcal{U}}\times{\mathcal{U}})}({\mathcal{F}}_{1}\boxtimes{\mathcal{F}}_{2},{\mathcal{F}})\simeq{\mathop{\operatorname{{\mathcal{H}}\it om}}}_{\operatorname{Shv}({\mathcal{U}})}\left({\mathcal{F}}_{2},(p_{2})_{\blacktriangle}(p_{1}^{!}({\mathbb{D}}^{\operatorname{Verdier}}({\mathcal{F}}_{1}))\overset{!}{\otimes}{\mathcal{F}})\right).

Hence, for the right-hand side of (
2.9
), we obtain

{\mathop{\operatorname{{\mathcal{H}}\it om}}}_{\operatorname{Shv}({\mathcal{U}})\otimes\operatorname{Shv}({\mathcal{U}})}({\mathcal{F}}_{1}\otimes{\mathcal{F}}_{2},\operatorname{u}_{{\mathcal{U}},{\operatorname{Nilp}}})\simeq{\mathop{\operatorname{{\mathcal{H}}\it om}}}_{\operatorname{Shv}({\mathcal{U}}\times{\mathcal{U}})}({\mathcal{F}}_{1}\boxtimes{\mathcal{F}}_{2},(\operatorname{Id}_{\mathcal{U}}\boxtimes{\mathsf{P}}_{{\mathcal{U}},{\operatorname{Nilp}}})(\operatorname{u}_{\mathcal{U}}))\simeq\\
\simeq{\mathop{\operatorname{{\mathcal{H}}\it om}}}_{\operatorname{Shv}({\mathcal{U}})}\left({\mathcal{F}}_{2},(p_{2})_{\blacktriangle}\left(p_{1}^{!}({\mathbb{D}}^{\operatorname{Verdier}}({\mathcal{F}}_{1}))\overset{!}{\otimes}(\operatorname{Id}_{\mathcal{U}}\boxtimes{\mathsf{P}}_{{\mathcal{U}},{\operatorname{Nilp}}})(\operatorname{u}_{\mathcal{U}})\right)\right)\simeq\\
\simeq{\mathop{\operatorname{{\mathcal{H}}\it om}}}_{\operatorname{Shv}({\mathcal{U}})}\left({\mathcal{F}}_{2},{\mathsf{P}}_{{\mathcal{U}},{\operatorname{Nilp}}}\left((p_{2})_{\blacktriangle}(p_{1}^{!}({\mathbb{D}}^{\operatorname{Verdier}}({\mathcal{F}}_{1}))\overset{!}{\otimes}\operatorname{u}_{\mathcal{U}})\right)\right)\simeq\\
\simeq{\mathop{\operatorname{{\mathcal{H}}\it om}}}_{\operatorname{Shv}({\mathcal{U}})}({\mathcal{F}}_{2},{\mathsf{P}}_{{\mathcal{U}},{\operatorname{Nilp}}}({\mathbb{D}}^{\operatorname{Verdier}}({\mathcal{F}}_{1}))),$$

as required.

∎

Corollary 2.3.7
.

For
{\mathcal{F}}_{1}\in\operatorname{Shv}_{\operatorname{Nilp}}({\mathcal{U}})
and
{\mathcal{F}}_{2}\in\operatorname{Shv}({\mathcal{U}})
, we have a canonical isomorphism

\operatorname{C}^{\cdot}_{\blacktriangle}({\mathcal{U}},{\mathcal{F}}_{1}\overset{!}{\otimes}{\mathcal{F}}_{2})\simeq\operatorname{C}^{\cdot}_{\blacktriangle}({\mathcal{U}},{\mathcal{F}}_{1}\overset{!}{\otimes}{\mathsf{P}}_{{\mathcal{U}},{\operatorname{Nilp}}}({\mathcal{F}}_{2})).

2.4.
Constraccessibility

The material in this subsection is not needed for the main results of this paper,
so it can be skipped on the first pass.

2.4.1.

We claim:

Proposition 2.4.2
.

Let
{\mathcal{U}}\subset\operatorname{Bun}_{G}
be a universally
{\operatorname{Nilp}}
-cotrancative quasi-compact open substack.
The following statements are equivalent:

(i) The functor
\iota_{\mathcal{U}}^{R}
is continuous;

(ii) The functor
{\mathsf{P}}_{{\mathcal{U}},{\operatorname{Nilp}}}
provides a right adjoint to
\iota_{\mathcal{U}}
.

Proof.

Clearly, (ii)
\Rightarrow
(i). We will prove that (i) implies (ii) using the following general assertion:

Lemma 2.4.3
.

Let
\iota:{\mathbf{C}}_{1}\to{\mathbf{C}}
be a fully faithful functor between compactly generated DG categories.
Suppose that
\iota
preserves compactness, and let

\iota^{\operatorname{fake-op}}:{\mathbf{C}}_{1}^{\vee}\to{\mathbf{C}}^{\vee},

be the ind-extension of
\iota^{\operatorname{op}}:({\mathbf{C}}_{1}^{c})^{\operatorname{op}}\to({\mathbf{C}}^{c})^{\operatorname{op}}
.
Then
(\iota^{\operatorname{fake-op}})^{\vee}
is the right adjoint of
\iota
.

We apply this lemma as follows: take
\iota:{\mathbf{C}}_{1}\to{\mathbf{C}}
to be
\iota_{\mathcal{U}}:\operatorname{Shv}_{\operatorname{Nilp}}({\mathcal{U}})\hookrightarrow\operatorname{Shv}({\mathcal{U}})
.

Assumption (i) means that
\operatorname{Shv}_{\operatorname{Nilp}}({\mathcal{U}})
is constraccessible, and the self-duality of
\operatorname{Shv}_{\operatorname{Nilp}}({\mathcal{U}})
with counit
(
2.8
), at the level of compact objects is induced by the Verdier duality functor (
A.11
). This implies
that with respect to this self-duality of
\operatorname{Shv}_{\operatorname{Nilp}}({\mathcal{U}})
and the self-duality of
\operatorname{Shv}({\mathcal{U}})
given by
(
A.12
), the functor
\iota_{\mathcal{U}}^{\operatorname{fake-op}}
identifies again with
\iota_{\mathcal{U}}
.

Hence,

\iota_{\mathcal{U}}^{R}\overset{\text{Lemma~\ref{l:right adjoint and proj}}}{\simeq}(\iota^{\operatorname{fake-op}}_{\mathcal{U}})^{\vee}\simeq\iota_{\mathcal{U}}^{\vee}\overset{\text{Proposition~\ref{p:dual of projector U}}}{\simeq}{\mathsf{P}}_{{\mathcal{U}},{\operatorname{Nilp}}}.

∎

2.4.4.

From now on, for the duration of this subsection we will assume
[
AGKRRV
, Conjecture 14.1.8]
. This conjecture says
that the category
\operatorname{Shv}_{\operatorname{Nilp}}(\operatorname{Bun}_{G})
is generated by objects that are compact in the ambient
category
\operatorname{Shv}(\operatorname{Bun}_{G})
. (Recall also that this conjecture
is known to hold in the de Rham and Betti contexts, see
[
AGKRRV
, Theorems 16.4.3 and 16.4.10]
.)

Given Theorem
1.7.3
, this conjecture
is equivalent to the statement that for every universally Nilp-cotruncative quasi-compact open substack

{\mathcal{U}}\subset\operatorname{Bun}_{G}
, the pair
({\mathcal{U}},{\operatorname{Nilp}})
is constraccessible
2
2

2

See Sect.
A.5.2
for what this means.

(see
[
AGKRRV
, Lemma F.8.10]
).

Since each
\operatorname{Shv}_{\operatorname{Nilp}}({\mathcal{U}})
is compactly generated (
[
AGKRRV
, Corollary 16.1.8]
), the latter
statement is equivalent to saying that the right adjoint of the tautological embedding

\iota_{\mathcal{U}}:\operatorname{Shv}_{\operatorname{Nilp}}({\mathcal{U}})\hookrightarrow\operatorname{Shv}({\mathcal{U}})

is continuous.

Hence, by Proposition
2.4.2
, we obtain that the functor
{\mathsf{P}}_{{\mathcal{U}},{\operatorname{Nilp}}}
identifies with the
right adjoint of the embedding
\iota_{\mathcal{U}}
.

In addition, according to
[
AGKRRV
, Proposition 17.2.3]
, the functor

{\mathsf{P}}_{\operatorname{Bun}_{G},{\operatorname{Nilp}}}
, viewed as a functor
\operatorname{Shv}(\operatorname{Bun}_{G})\to\operatorname{Shv}_{\operatorname{Nilp}}(\operatorname{Bun}_{G})
, is the right adjoint
to the tautological embedding
\iota
.

2.4.5.

Let
{\mathcal{Z}}
be an algebraic stack. Recall that the functors

\operatorname{Id}_{\mathcal{Z}}\boxtimes{\mathsf{P}}_{\operatorname{Bun}_{G},{\operatorname{Nilp}}}\text{ and }\operatorname{Id}_{\mathcal{Z}}\boxtimes{\mathsf{P}}_{{\mathcal{U}},{\operatorname{Nilp}}}

takes values in the subcategories

\operatorname{Shv}({\mathcal{Z}})\otimes\operatorname{Shv}_{{\operatorname{Nilp}}}(\operatorname{Bun}_{G})\subset\operatorname{Shv}({\mathcal{Z}}\times\operatorname{Bun}_{G})\text{ and }\operatorname{Shv}({\mathcal{Z}})\otimes\operatorname{Shv}_{{\operatorname{Nilp}}}({\mathcal{U}})\subset\operatorname{Shv}({\mathcal{Z}}\times{\mathcal{U}}),

respectively (see Theorem
1.6.4
and Corollary
1.8.3
).

We claim:

Proposition 2.4.6
.

Assuming
[
AGKRRV
, Conjecture 14.1.8]
, for any algebraic stack
{\mathcal{Z}}
, the functors

\operatorname{Id}_{\mathcal{Z}}\boxtimes{\mathsf{P}}_{\operatorname{Bun}_{G},{\operatorname{Nilp}}}\text{ and }\operatorname{Id}_{\mathcal{Z}}\boxtimes{\mathsf{P}}_{{\mathcal{U}},{\operatorname{Nilp}}}

are the right adjoints of the embeddings

\operatorname{Shv}({\mathcal{Z}})\otimes\operatorname{Shv}_{{\operatorname{Nilp}}}(\operatorname{Bun}_{G})\hookrightarrow\operatorname{Shv}({\mathcal{Z}}\times\operatorname{Bun}_{G})\text{ and }\operatorname{Shv}({\mathcal{Z}})\otimes\operatorname{Shv}_{{\operatorname{Nilp}}}({\mathcal{U}})\hookrightarrow\operatorname{Shv}({\mathcal{Z}}\times{\mathcal{U}}),

respectively.

Proof.

We will prove the assertion for
\operatorname{Bun}_{G}
; the case of
{\mathcal{U}}
is similar.

First, it is easy to reduce the assertion to the case when
{\mathcal{Z}}
is quasi-compact, which
we will from now on assume.

We need to show that for
{\mathcal{F}}_{\mathcal{Z}}\in\operatorname{Shv}({\mathcal{Z}})^{c}
,
{\mathcal{F}}_{\operatorname{Nilp}}\in\operatorname{Shv}_{{\operatorname{Nilp}}}(\operatorname{Bun}_{G})

and
{\mathcal{F}}\in\operatorname{Shv}({\mathcal{Z}}\times\operatorname{Bun}_{G})
we have a canonical isomorphism

(2.10)

{\mathop{\operatorname{{\mathcal{H}}\it om}}}({\mathcal{F}}_{\mathcal{Z}}\boxtimes{\mathcal{F}}_{\operatorname{Nilp}},{\mathcal{F}})\simeq{\mathop{\operatorname{{\mathcal{H}}\it om}}}({\mathcal{F}}_{\mathcal{Z}}\boxtimes{\mathcal{F}}_{\operatorname{Nilp}},(\operatorname{Id}_{\mathcal{Z}}\boxtimes{\mathsf{P}}_{\operatorname{Bun}_{G},{\operatorname{Nilp}}})({\mathcal{F}})).

Set

{\mathcal{F}}^{\prime}:=(p_{\operatorname{Bun}_{G}})_{*}(p_{\mathcal{Z}}^{!}({\mathbb{D}}^{\operatorname{verdier}}({\mathcal{F}}_{\mathcal{Z}}))\overset{!}{\otimes}{\mathcal{F}})\in\operatorname{Shv}(\operatorname{Bun}_{G}).

Then the left-hand side in (
2.10
) identifies with

{\mathop{\operatorname{{\mathcal{H}}\it om}}}_{\operatorname{Shv}(\operatorname{Bun}_{G})}({\mathcal{F}}_{\operatorname{Nilp}},{\mathcal{F}}^{\prime}).

Set

{\mathcal{F}}^{\prime\prime}:=(p_{\operatorname{Bun}_{G}})_{*}(p_{\mathcal{Z}}^{!}({\mathbb{D}}^{\operatorname{verdier}}({\mathcal{F}}_{\mathcal{Z}}))\overset{!}{\otimes}(\operatorname{Id}_{\mathcal{Z}}\boxtimes{\mathsf{P}}_{\operatorname{Bun}_{G},{\operatorname{Nilp}}})({\mathcal{F}}))\in\operatorname{Shv}(\operatorname{Bun}_{G}).

Then the right-hand side in (
2.10
) identifies with

{\mathop{\operatorname{{\mathcal{H}}\it om}}}_{\operatorname{Shv}(\operatorname{Bun}_{G})}({\mathcal{F}}_{\operatorname{Nilp}},{\mathcal{F}}^{\prime\prime}).

However, since
{\mathcal{F}}_{\mathcal{Z}}
is compact (and hence so is
{\mathbb{D}}^{\operatorname{verdier}}({\mathcal{F}}_{\mathcal{Z}})
), the maps

(p_{\operatorname{Bun}_{G}})_{\blacktriangle}(p_{\mathcal{Z}}^{!}({\mathbb{D}}^{\operatorname{verdier}}({\mathcal{F}}_{\mathcal{Z}}))\overset{!}{\otimes}{\mathcal{F}})\to(p_{\operatorname{Bun}_{G}})_{*}(p_{\mathcal{Z}}^{!}({\mathbb{D}}^{\operatorname{verdier}}({\mathcal{F}}_{\mathcal{Z}}))\overset{!}{\otimes}{\mathcal{F}})

and

(p_{\operatorname{Bun}_{G}})_{\blacktriangle}(p_{\mathcal{Z}}^{!}({\mathbb{D}}^{\operatorname{verdier}}({\mathcal{F}}_{\mathcal{Z}}))\overset{!}{\otimes}(\operatorname{Id}_{\mathcal{Z}}\boxtimes{\mathsf{P}}_{\operatorname{Bun}_{G},{\operatorname{Nilp}}})({\mathcal{F}}))\to

\to(p_{\operatorname{Bun}_{G}})_{*}(p_{\mathcal{Z}}^{!}({\mathbb{D}}^{\operatorname{verdier}}({\mathcal{F}}_{\mathcal{Z}}))\overset{!}{\otimes}(\operatorname{Id}_{\mathcal{Z}}\boxtimes{\mathsf{P}}_{\operatorname{Bun}_{G},{\operatorname{Nilp}}})({\mathcal{F}}))

are isomorphisms.

This implies that

{\mathcal{F}}^{\prime\prime}\simeq{\mathsf{P}}_{\operatorname{Bun}_{G},{\operatorname{Nilp}}}({\mathcal{F}}^{\prime}).

Hence, the assertion follows from the
(\iota,{\mathsf{P}}_{\operatorname{Bun}_{G},{\operatorname{Nilp}}})
adjunction.

∎

Iterating, from Proposition
2.4.6
we obtain:

Corollary 2.4.7
.

Assuming
[
AGKRRV
, Conjecture 14.1.8]
, the functor

{\mathsf{P}}_{\operatorname{Bun}_{G},{\operatorname{Nilp}}}\boxtimes{\mathsf{P}}_{\operatorname{Bun}_{G},{\operatorname{Nilp}}}:\operatorname{Shv}(\operatorname{Bun}_{G}\times\operatorname{Bun}_{G})\to\operatorname{Shv}_{{\operatorname{Nilp}}}(\operatorname{Bun}_{G})\otimes\operatorname{Shv}_{{\operatorname{Nilp}}}(\operatorname{Bun}_{G})

is the right adjoint of

\operatorname{Shv}_{{\operatorname{Nilp}}}(\operatorname{Bun}_{G})\otimes\operatorname{Shv}_{{\operatorname{Nilp}}}(\operatorname{Bun}_{G})\overset{\iota\otimes\iota}{\longrightarrow}\operatorname{Shv}(\operatorname{Bun}_{G})\boxtimes\operatorname{Shv}(\operatorname{Bun}_{G})\overset{\boxtimes}{\hookrightarrow}\operatorname{Shv}(\operatorname{Bun}_{G}\times\operatorname{Bun}_{G}).

2.4.8.

Note that by passing to right adjoints in the commutative diagrams

\begin{CD}\operatorname{Shv}_{\operatorname{Nilp}}({\mathcal{U}})@>{\iota_{\mathcal{U}}}>{}>\operatorname{Shv}({\mathcal{U}})\\
@V{j_{!}}V{}V@V{}V{j_{!}}V\\
\operatorname{Shv}_{\operatorname{Nilp}}(\operatorname{Bun}_{G})@>{\iota}>{}>\operatorname{Shv}(\operatorname{Bun}_{G})\end{CD}

and

\begin{CD}\operatorname{Shv}_{\operatorname{Nilp}}({\mathcal{U}})@>{\iota_{\mathcal{U}}}>{}>\operatorname{Shv}({\mathcal{U}})\\
@A{j^{*}}A{}A@A{}A{j^{*}}A\\
\operatorname{Shv}_{\operatorname{Nilp}}(\operatorname{Bun}_{G})@>{\iota}>{}>\operatorname{Shv}(\operatorname{Bun}_{G}),\end{CD}

we obtain the isomorphisms

(2.11)

j^{*}\circ{\mathsf{P}}_{\operatorname{Bun}_{G},{\operatorname{Nilp}}}\simeq{\mathsf{P}}_{{\mathcal{U}},{\operatorname{Nilp}}}\circ j^{*}

and

(2.12)

j_{*}\circ{\mathsf{P}}_{{\mathcal{U}},{\operatorname{Nilp}}}\simeq{\mathsf{P}}_{\operatorname{Bun}_{G},{\operatorname{Nilp}}}\circ j_{*}.

We claim that a stronger assertion holds:

Corollary 2.4.9
.

Assuming
[
AGKRRV
, Conjecture 14.1.8]
, the isomorphisms (
2.11
) and (
2.12
) hold as
functors defined by kernels, where we view
{\mathsf{P}}_{\operatorname{Bun}_{G},{\operatorname{Nilp}}}
and
{\mathsf{P}}_{{\mathcal{U}},{\operatorname{Nilp}}}

as endofunctors defined by kernels of
\operatorname{Shv}(\operatorname{Bun}_{G})
and
\operatorname{Shv}({\mathcal{U}})
,
respectively.

Proof.

We will prove the assertion for (
2.11
); the assertion for (
2.12
)
is similar.

We have to construct an isomorphism

\operatorname{Id}_{\mathcal{Z}}\boxtimes(j^{*}\circ{\mathsf{P}}_{\mathcal{U}})\simeq\operatorname{Id}_{\mathcal{Z}}\boxtimes({\mathsf{P}}_{\mathcal{U}}\circ j^{*})

for any algebraic stack
{\mathcal{Z}}
.

This follows formally from Proposition
2.4.6
by passing to right adjoints of the composition

(2.13)

\operatorname{Shv}({\mathcal{Z}})\otimes\operatorname{Shv}_{{\operatorname{Nilp}}}({\mathcal{U}})\overset{\operatorname{Id}\otimes j_{!}}{\longrightarrow}\operatorname{Shv}({\mathcal{Z}})\otimes\operatorname{Shv}_{{\operatorname{Nilp}}}(\operatorname{Bun}_{G})\hookrightarrow\operatorname{Shv}({\mathcal{Z}}\times\operatorname{Bun}_{G}),

which is the same as

(2.14)

\operatorname{Shv}({\mathcal{Z}})\otimes\operatorname{Shv}_{{\operatorname{Nilp}}}({\mathcal{U}})\hookrightarrow\operatorname{Shv}({\mathcal{Z}}\times{\mathcal{U}})\overset{(\operatorname{id}\times j)_{!}}{\longrightarrow}\operatorname{Shv}({\mathcal{Z}}\times\operatorname{Bun}_{G}).

∎

2.5.
The “co” category for
\operatorname{Bun}_{G}
with nilpotent singular support

In order to talk about Verdier duality on the (non quasi-compact) algebraic stack

\operatorname{Bun}_{G}
, we need to study the category
\operatorname{Shv}(\operatorname{Bun}_{G})_{\operatorname{co}}
, see Sect.
C.2
.

In this subsection we will discuss the pattern of Hecke action on
\operatorname{Shv}(\operatorname{Bun}_{G})_{\operatorname{co}}
.

We also introduce the “co”-version of the subcategory with nilpotent singular support

\operatorname{Shv}_{\operatorname{Nilp}}(\operatorname{Bun}_{G})_{\operatorname{co}}\subset\operatorname{Shv}(\operatorname{Bun}_{G})_{\operatorname{co}}.

2.5.1.

Recall the category
\operatorname{Shv}(\operatorname{Bun}_{G})_{\operatorname{co}}
, defined as

\underset{{\mathcal{U}}}{\operatorname{colim}_{*}}\,\operatorname{Shv}({\mathcal{U}}),

where the colimit is taken over the poset of cotruncative quasi-compact
open substacks of
\operatorname{Bun}_{G}
, see Sect.
C.2.2
.

2.5.2.

The Hecke action on
\operatorname{Shv}(\operatorname{Bun}_{G})
gives rise to a Hecke action on
\operatorname{Shv}(\operatorname{Bun}_{G})_{\operatorname{co}}
:

Let
V
be an object of
{\mathop{\operatorname{\rm Rep}}}({\check{G}})^{\otimes I}
. Note that the Hecke functor

{\mathsf{H}}(V,-):\operatorname{Shv}(\operatorname{Bun}_{G})\to\operatorname{Shv}(\operatorname{Bun}_{G}\times X^{I})

is defined by the kernel

(\operatorname{Id}_{\operatorname{Bun}_{G}}\boxtimes{\mathsf{H}}(V,-))(\operatorname{u}_{\operatorname{Bun}_{G},\operatorname{co}_{1}})\in\operatorname{Shv}(\operatorname{Bun}_{G}\times\operatorname{Bun}_{G}\times X^{I})_{\operatorname{co}_{1}}.

Consider now the object

(2.15)

\sigma\left((\operatorname{Id}_{\operatorname{Bun}_{G}}\boxtimes{\mathsf{H}}(V^{\tau},-))(\operatorname{u}_{\operatorname{Bun}_{G},\operatorname{co}_{1}})\right)\in\operatorname{Shv}(\operatorname{Bun}_{G}\times\operatorname{Bun}_{G}\times X^{I})_{\operatorname{co}_{2}},

where
\sigma
is the transposition of the two factors of
\operatorname{Bun}_{G}
.

We let

{\mathsf{H}}(V,-)_{\operatorname{co}}:\operatorname{Shv}(\operatorname{Bun}_{G})_{\operatorname{co}}\to\operatorname{Shv}(\operatorname{Bun}_{G}\times X^{I})_{\operatorname{co}}

be the functor defined by the kernel (
2.15
).

2.5.3.

Recall the functor

\operatorname{Id}^{\operatorname{naive}}_{\operatorname{Bun}_{G}}:\operatorname{Shv}(\operatorname{Bun}_{G})_{\operatorname{co}}\to\operatorname{Shv}(\operatorname{Bun}_{G}),

see Sect.
2.1.3
. We claim:

Lemma 2.5.4
.

The diagram (of functors defined by kernels)

\begin{CD}\operatorname{Shv}(\operatorname{Bun}_{G})_{\operatorname{co}}@>{{\mathsf{H}}(V,-)_{\operatorname{co}}}>{}>\operatorname{Shv}(\operatorname{Bun}_{G}\times X^{I})_{\operatorname{co}}\\
@V{\operatorname{Id}^{\operatorname{naive}}_{\operatorname{Bun}_{G}}}V{}V@V{}V{\operatorname{Id}^{\operatorname{naive}}_{\operatorname{Bun}_{G}}\boxtimes\operatorname{Id}_{X^{I}}}V\\
\operatorname{Shv}(\operatorname{Bun}_{G})@>{{\mathsf{H}}(V,-)}>{}>\operatorname{Shv}(\operatorname{Bun}_{G}\times X^{I})\end{CD}

commutes.

Proof.

Follows by diagram chase from Proposition
2.1.6
.

∎

2.5.5.

A similar discussion applies to the action of
{\mathop{\operatorname{\rm Rep}}}({\check{G}})_{\operatorname{Ran}}
. Namely, for
{\mathcal{V}}\in{\mathop{\operatorname{\rm Rep}}}({\check{G}})_{\operatorname{Ran}}

we have a functor defined by a kernel

{\mathsf{H}}_{{\mathcal{V}},\operatorname{co}}:\operatorname{Shv}(\operatorname{Bun}_{G})_{\operatorname{co}}\to\operatorname{Shv}(\operatorname{Bun}_{G})_{\operatorname{co}},

and the diagram (of functors defined by kernels)

(2.16)

\begin{CD}\operatorname{Shv}(\operatorname{Bun}_{G})_{\operatorname{co}}@>{{\mathsf{H}}_{{\mathcal{V}},\operatorname{co}}}>{}>\operatorname{Shv}(\operatorname{Bun}_{G})_{\operatorname{co}}\\
@V{\operatorname{Id}^{\operatorname{naive}}_{\operatorname{Bun}_{G}}}V{}V@V{}V{\operatorname{Id}^{\operatorname{naive}}_{\operatorname{Bun}_{G}}}V\\
\operatorname{Shv}(\operatorname{Bun}_{G})@>{{\mathsf{H}}_{\mathcal{V}}}>{}>\operatorname{Shv}(\operatorname{Bun}_{G})\end{CD}

commutes (indeed, this formally follows from Lemma
2.5.4
).

2.5.6.

We claim:

Lemma 2.5.7
.

For
{\mathcal{V}}\in{\mathop{\operatorname{\rm Rep}}}({\check{G}})_{\operatorname{Ran}}
, we have a canonical isomorphism

({\mathsf{H}}_{{\mathcal{V}},\operatorname{co}}\boxtimes\operatorname{Id}_{\operatorname{Bun}_{G}})(\operatorname{u}_{\operatorname{Bun}_{G},\operatorname{co}_{1}})\simeq(\operatorname{Id}_{\operatorname{Bun}_{G}}\boxtimes{\mathsf{H}}_{{\mathcal{V}}^{\tau}})(\operatorname{u}_{\operatorname{Bun}_{G},\operatorname{co}_{1}})

as objects of
\operatorname{Shv}(\operatorname{Bun}_{G}\times\operatorname{Bun}_{G})_{\operatorname{co}_{1}}
.

Proof.

The assertion of the lemma is equivalent to an isomorphism

\sigma((\operatorname{Id}_{\operatorname{Bun}_{G}}\boxtimes{\mathsf{H}}_{{\mathcal{V}},\operatorname{co}})(\operatorname{u}_{\operatorname{Bun}_{G},\operatorname{co}_{2}}))\simeq(\operatorname{Id}_{\operatorname{Bun}_{G}}\boxtimes{\mathsf{H}}_{{\mathcal{V}}^{\tau}})(\operatorname{u}_{\operatorname{Bun}_{G},\operatorname{co}_{1}}).

The fact that the images of both sides under the forgetful functor

\operatorname{Id}^{\operatorname{naive}}_{\operatorname{Bun}_{G}}\boxtimes\operatorname{Id}_{\operatorname{Bun}_{G}}:\operatorname{Shv}(\operatorname{Bun}_{G}\times\operatorname{Bun}_{G})_{\operatorname{co}_{1}}\to\operatorname{Shv}(\operatorname{Bun}_{G}\times\operatorname{Bun}_{G})

are canonically isomorphic is the content of Proposition
2.1.6
(using the commutative diagram (
2.16
)
as functors defined by kernels).

In order to upgrade this isomorphism to an isomorphism that takes place in
\operatorname{Shv}(\operatorname{Bun}_{G}\times\operatorname{Bun}_{G})_{\operatorname{co}_{1}}

we argue as follows:

We can assume that the object
{\mathcal{V}}\in{\mathop{\operatorname{\rm Rep}}}({\check{G}})_{\operatorname{Ran}}
is compact. It suffices to establish a compatible
family of isomorphisms

(2.17)

(\operatorname{Id}_{\operatorname{Bun}_{G}}\times j)^{*}(({\mathsf{H}}_{{\mathcal{V}},\operatorname{co}}\boxtimes\operatorname{Id}_{\operatorname{Bun}_{G}})(\operatorname{u}_{\operatorname{Bun}_{G},\operatorname{co}_{1}}))\simeq(\operatorname{Id}_{\operatorname{Bun}_{G}}\times j)^{*}((\operatorname{Id}_{\operatorname{Bun}_{G}}\boxtimes{\mathsf{H}}_{{\mathcal{V}}^{\tau}})(\operatorname{u}_{\operatorname{Bun}_{G},\operatorname{co}_{1}}))

as objects in
\operatorname{Shv}(\operatorname{Bun}_{G}\times{\mathcal{U}})_{\operatorname{co}}
for quasi-compact open substacks
{\mathcal{U}}\overset{j}{\hookrightarrow}\operatorname{Bun}_{G}
.

However, for
{\mathcal{V}}
compact and a fixed
{\mathcal{U}}
, both sides in (
2.17
) lie in the essential image
of the (fully faithful) functor

(j^{\prime}\times\operatorname{id}_{\mathcal{U}})_{*,\operatorname{co}}:\operatorname{Shv}({\mathcal{U}}^{\prime}\times{\mathcal{U}})\to\operatorname{Shv}(\operatorname{Bun}_{G}\times{\mathcal{U}})_{\operatorname{co}}

for some quasi-compact open
{\mathcal{U}}^{\prime}\overset{j^{\prime}}{\hookrightarrow}\operatorname{Bun}_{G}
.

Hence, it is enough to show that both sides in (
2.17
) become isomorphic after applying the
functor

(j^{\prime}\times\operatorname{id}_{\mathcal{U}})^{*}_{\operatorname{co}}:\operatorname{Shv}(\operatorname{Bun}_{G}\times{\mathcal{U}})_{\operatorname{co}}\to\operatorname{Shv}({\mathcal{U}}^{\prime}\times{\mathcal{U}}).

However,

(j^{\prime}\times\operatorname{id}_{\mathcal{U}})^{*}_{\operatorname{co}}\circ(\operatorname{Id}_{\operatorname{Bun}_{G}}\times j)^{*}\simeq(j^{\prime}\times j)^{*}\circ(\operatorname{Id}^{\operatorname{naive}}_{\operatorname{Bun}_{G}}\boxtimes\operatorname{Id}_{\operatorname{Bun}_{G}})

and the assertion follows.

∎

2.5.8.

Following Sect.
C.2.4
, we define the category
\operatorname{Shv}_{{\operatorname{Nilp}}}(\operatorname{Bun}_{G})_{\operatorname{co}}
as

\underset{{\mathcal{U}}}{\operatorname{colim}_{*}}\,\operatorname{Shv}_{\operatorname{Nilp}}({\mathcal{U}}),

where the colimit is taken over the poset of universally
{\operatorname{Nilp}}
-cotruncative quasi-compact
open substacks of
\operatorname{Bun}_{G}
, and the transition functors are given by
-_{*}
.

Since the above poset is filtered, the functor

\iota_{\operatorname{co}}:\operatorname{Shv}_{{\operatorname{Nilp}}}(\operatorname{Bun}_{G})_{\operatorname{co}}\to\operatorname{Shv}(\operatorname{Bun}_{G})_{\operatorname{co}}

comprised of the functors

\iota_{\mathcal{U}}:\operatorname{Shv}_{\operatorname{Nilp}}({\mathcal{U}})\to\operatorname{Shv}({\mathcal{U}})
, is fully faithful.

2.5.9.

The following results from the definitions:

Lemma 2.5.10
.

The functor
\operatorname{Id}^{\operatorname{naive}}_{\operatorname{Bun}_{G}}
sends

\operatorname{Shv}_{\operatorname{Nilp}}(\operatorname{Bun}_{G})_{\operatorname{co}}
to
\operatorname{Shv}_{\operatorname{Nilp}}(\operatorname{Bun}_{G})
.

2.6.
Duality between
\operatorname{Shv}_{\operatorname{Nilp}}(\operatorname{Bun}_{G})
and
\operatorname{Shv}_{\operatorname{Nilp}}(\operatorname{Bun}_{G})_{\operatorname{co}}

In this subsection we will extend the Verdier self-duality of
\operatorname{Shv}_{\operatorname{Nilp}}({\mathcal{U}})

(where
{\mathcal{U}}
is a universally Nilp-contruncative quasi-compact open substack
of
\operatorname{Bun}_{G}
) to obtain a duality between
\operatorname{Shv}_{\operatorname{Nilp}}(\operatorname{Bun}_{G})
and
\operatorname{Shv}_{\operatorname{Nilp}}(\operatorname{Bun}_{G})_{\operatorname{co}}
.

2.6.1.

Recall that according to Sect.
C.3.3
, there is a canonical identification

(2.18)

\operatorname{Shv}(\operatorname{Bun}_{G})_{\operatorname{co}}\simeq\operatorname{Shv}(\operatorname{Bun}_{G})^{\vee},

so that the pairing

(2.19)

\operatorname{Shv}(\operatorname{Bun}_{G})\otimes\operatorname{Shv}(\operatorname{Bun}_{G})_{\operatorname{co}}\to\operatorname{Vect},

denoted
\operatorname{ev}_{\operatorname{Bun}_{G}}
, is given by

{\mathcal{F}}_{1}\in\operatorname{Shv}(\operatorname{Bun}_{G}),\,{\mathcal{F}}_{2}\in\operatorname{Shv}(\operatorname{Bun}_{G})_{\operatorname{co}}\mapsto\operatorname{C}^{\cdot}_{\blacktriangle}(\operatorname{Bun}_{G},{\mathcal{F}}_{1}\overset{!}{\otimes}{\mathcal{F}}_{2}),

where we view
{\mathcal{F}}_{1}\overset{!}{\otimes}{\mathcal{F}}_{2}
as an object of
\operatorname{Shv}(\operatorname{Bun}_{G})_{\operatorname{co}}
, and

\operatorname{C}^{\cdot}_{\blacktriangle}(\operatorname{Bun}_{G},-)
denotes the corresponding functor

\operatorname{Shv}(\operatorname{Bun}_{G})_{\operatorname{co}}\to\operatorname{Vect},

see Sect.
C.3.4
.

2.6.2.

From Lemma
2.5.7
and Sect.
B.1.2
we obtain:

Corollary 2.6.3
.

With respect to the duality (
2.18
), the dual of the functor

{\mathsf{H}}_{\mathcal{V}}
identifies with
{\mathsf{H}}_{{\mathcal{V}}^{\tau},\operatorname{co}}
.

2.6.4.

Combining Sect.
C.3.5
and Corollary
2.3.4
, we obtain:

Corollary 2.6.5
.

The restriction of the pairing (
2.19
) along

\operatorname{Shv}_{\operatorname{Nilp}}(\operatorname{Bun}_{G})_{\operatorname{co}}\otimes\operatorname{Shv}_{\operatorname{Nilp}}(\operatorname{Bun}_{G})\to\operatorname{Shv}(\operatorname{Bun}_{G})_{\operatorname{co}}\otimes\operatorname{Shv}(\operatorname{Bun}_{G})

defines an equivalence

(2.20)

\operatorname{Shv}_{\operatorname{Nilp}}(\operatorname{Bun}_{G})_{\operatorname{co}}\simeq\operatorname{Shv}_{\operatorname{Nilp}}(\operatorname{Bun}_{G})^{\vee}.

2.6.6.

Our next step it to define a projector from
\operatorname{Shv}(\operatorname{Bun}_{G})_{\operatorname{co}}

onto
\operatorname{Shv}_{\operatorname{Nilp}}(\operatorname{Bun}_{G})_{\operatorname{co}}
.

Let
{\mathsf{P}}_{\operatorname{co}}
denote the endofunctor of
\operatorname{Shv}(\operatorname{Bun}_{G})_{\operatorname{co}}
equal to
{\mathsf{H}}_{{\mathsf{R}},\operatorname{co}}

(see Sect.
2.5.5
for the notation), where
{\mathsf{R}}\in{\mathop{\operatorname{\rm Rep}}}({\check{G}})_{\operatorname{Ran}}
is as in Sect.
1.6.1
. We claim:

Proposition 2.6.7
.

With respect to the identifications (
2.18
) and (
2.20
),
we have:

(i) The functor dual to
{\mathsf{P}}:\operatorname{Shv}(\operatorname{Bun}_{G})\to\operatorname{Shv}_{\operatorname{Nilp}}(\operatorname{Bun}_{G})
identifies with

\iota_{\operatorname{co}}:\operatorname{Shv}_{\operatorname{Nilp}}(\operatorname{Bun}_{G})_{\operatorname{co}}\to\operatorname{Shv}(\operatorname{Bun}_{G})_{\operatorname{co}}.

(ii) The essential image of the functor
{\mathsf{P}}_{\operatorname{co}}
lies in
\operatorname{Shv}_{\operatorname{Nilp}}(\operatorname{Bun}_{G})_{\operatorname{co}}
,
and the resulting functor

{\mathsf{P}}_{\operatorname{co}}:\operatorname{Shv}(\operatorname{Bun}_{G})_{\operatorname{co}}\to\operatorname{Shv}_{\operatorname{Nilp}}(\operatorname{Bun}_{G})_{\operatorname{co}}

is
the functor dual of
\iota:\operatorname{Shv}_{\operatorname{Nilp}}(\operatorname{Bun}_{G})\to\operatorname{Shv}(\operatorname{Bun}_{G})
.

Proof.

Point (i) follows by unwinding the definitions from Corollary
2.3.7
.

For point (ii),
given point (i), it suffices to show that the dual of
{\mathsf{P}}
, viewed as an endofunctor of

\operatorname{Shv}(\operatorname{Bun}_{G})
, is
{\mathsf{P}}_{\operatorname{co}}
, viewed as an endofunctor of

\operatorname{Shv}(\operatorname{Bun}_{G})_{\operatorname{co}}
.

Since
{\mathsf{P}}
is defined by the kernel

(\operatorname{Id}_{\operatorname{Bun}_{G}}\boxtimes{\mathsf{H}}_{\mathsf{R}})(\operatorname{u}_{\operatorname{Bun}_{G},\operatorname{co}_{1}})\in\operatorname{Shv}(\operatorname{Bun}_{G}\times\operatorname{Bun}_{G})_{\operatorname{co}_{1}},

from Corollary
2.6.3
, we obtain that its dual is the functor
{\mathsf{H}}_{{\mathsf{R}}^{\tau},\operatorname{co}}
.

Now the required assertion follows from the isomorphism
{\mathsf{R}}^{\tau}\simeq{\mathsf{R}}
.

∎

Corollary 2.6.8
.

The endofunctor
{\mathsf{P}}_{\operatorname{co}}
of
\operatorname{Shv}(\operatorname{Bun}_{G})_{\operatorname{co}}
is the projector onto the full subcategory

\operatorname{Shv}_{{\operatorname{Nilp}}}(\operatorname{Bun}_{G})_{\operatorname{co}}\subset\operatorname{Shv}(\operatorname{Bun}_{G})_{\operatorname{co}}.

2.7.
The projector and the diagonal, revisited

In this subsection, we will refine the results of Sect.
2.2
, when instead of
the object

\operatorname{u}^{\operatorname{naive}}_{\operatorname{Bun}_{G}}\in\operatorname{Shv}(\operatorname{Bun}_{G}\times\operatorname{Bun}_{G}),

we consider its refined version, namely,

\operatorname{u}_{\operatorname{Bun}_{G},\operatorname{co}_{2}}\in\operatorname{Shv}(\operatorname{Bun}_{G}\times\operatorname{Bun}_{G})_{\operatorname{co}_{2}}.

2.7.1.

First, we claim:

Proposition 2.7.2
.

The objects

(\operatorname{Id}_{\operatorname{Bun}_{G}}\boxtimes{\mathsf{P}}_{\operatorname{co}})(\operatorname{u}_{\operatorname{Bun}_{G},\operatorname{co}_{2}}),\,\,({\mathsf{P}}\boxtimes{\mathsf{P}}_{\operatorname{co}})(\operatorname{u}_{\operatorname{Bun}_{G},\operatorname{co}_{2}}),\,\,({\mathsf{P}}\boxtimes\operatorname{Id}_{\operatorname{Bun}_{G}})(\operatorname{u}_{\operatorname{Bun}_{G},\operatorname{co}_{2}})

are isomorphic.

Proof.

The isomorphism follows from Lemma
2.5.7
using the isomorphism

{\mathsf{R}}^{\tau}\simeq{\mathsf{R}}
in the same way as Proposition
2.2.2
follows
from Proposition
2.1.6
.
∎

2.7.3.

Denote the object appearing in Proposition
2.7.2
by
\operatorname{u}_{\operatorname{Bun}_{G},{\operatorname{Nilp}},\operatorname{co}_{2}}
.
We claim:

Proposition 2.7.4
.

The object
\operatorname{u}_{\operatorname{Bun}_{G},{\operatorname{Nilp}},\operatorname{co}_{2}}
belongs to

\operatorname{Shv}_{{\operatorname{Nilp}}}(\operatorname{Bun}_{G})\otimes\operatorname{Shv}_{\operatorname{Nilp}}(\operatorname{Bun}_{G})_{\operatorname{co}}\subset\operatorname{Shv}(\operatorname{Bun}_{G})\otimes\operatorname{Shv}(\operatorname{Bun}_{G})_{\operatorname{co}}\subset\operatorname{Shv}(\operatorname{Bun}_{G}\times\operatorname{Bun}_{G})_{\operatorname{co}_{2}}.

Proof.

We first show that
({\mathsf{P}}\boxtimes\operatorname{Id}_{\operatorname{Bun}_{G}})(\operatorname{u}_{\operatorname{Bun}_{G},\operatorname{co}_{2}})
belongs to

\operatorname{Shv}_{{\operatorname{Nilp}}}(\operatorname{Bun}_{G})\otimes\operatorname{Shv}(\operatorname{Bun}_{G})_{\operatorname{co}}\subset\operatorname{Shv}(\operatorname{Bun}_{G}\times\operatorname{Bun}_{G})_{\operatorname{co}_{2}}.

For that, it suffices to show that for every quasi-compact cotruncative open substack

{\mathcal{U}}\overset{j}{\hookrightarrow}\operatorname{Bun}_{G}
, the object

(\operatorname{Id}_{\operatorname{Bun}_{G}}\boxtimes j^{?})\circ({\mathsf{P}}\boxtimes\operatorname{Id}_{\operatorname{Bun}_{G}})(\operatorname{u}_{\operatorname{Bun}_{G},\operatorname{co}_{2}})\simeq({\mathsf{P}}\boxtimes j^{?})(\operatorname{u}_{\operatorname{Bun}_{G},\operatorname{co}_{2}})\simeq({\mathsf{P}}\boxtimes\operatorname{Id}_{\operatorname{Bun}_{G}})\circ(\operatorname{Id}_{\operatorname{Bun}_{G}}\boxtimes j^{?})(\operatorname{u}_{\operatorname{Bun}_{G},\operatorname{co}_{2}})

belongs to

\operatorname{Shv}_{{\operatorname{Nilp}}}(\operatorname{Bun}_{G})\otimes\operatorname{Shv}({\mathcal{U}})\subset\operatorname{Shv}(\operatorname{Bun}_{G}\times{\mathcal{U}}),

where
j^{?}
is the functor defined by a kernel as in Sect.
C.1.1
.
However, this follows from Theorems
1.6.4
and
1.3.7
.

Now, the fact that

({\mathsf{P}}\boxtimes{\mathsf{P}}_{\operatorname{co}})(\operatorname{u}_{\operatorname{Bun}_{G},\operatorname{co}_{2}})\simeq(\operatorname{Id}_{\operatorname{Bun}_{G}}\boxtimes{\mathsf{P}}_{\operatorname{co}})\circ({\mathsf{P}}\boxtimes\operatorname{Id}_{\operatorname{Bun}_{G}})(\operatorname{u}_{\operatorname{Bun}_{G},\operatorname{co}_{2}})

belongs to

\operatorname{Shv}_{{\operatorname{Nilp}}}(\operatorname{Bun}_{G})\otimes\operatorname{Shv}_{\operatorname{Nilp}}(\operatorname{Bun}_{G})_{\operatorname{co}}\subset\operatorname{Shv}_{{\operatorname{Nilp}}}(\operatorname{Bun}_{G})\otimes\operatorname{Shv}(\operatorname{Bun}_{G})_{\operatorname{co}}

follows from Corollary
2.6.8
.

∎

2.7.5.

We now claim:

Proposition 2.7.6
.

The object

\operatorname{u}_{\operatorname{Bun}_{G},{\operatorname{Nilp}},\operatorname{co}_{2}}\in\operatorname{Shv}_{{\operatorname{Nilp}}}(\operatorname{Bun}_{G})\otimes\operatorname{Shv}_{\operatorname{Nilp}}(\operatorname{Bun}_{G})_{\operatorname{co}}

is the unit of the duality (
2.20
).

Proof.

Follows formally from Proposition
2.7.2
in the same way as in the proof of
Proposition
2.3.3
.

∎

2.8.
The miraculous functor on
\operatorname{Bun}_{G}

We now input another ingredient into our story, the
miraculous functor
,
see Sects.
B.4
and
C.4.7
.

2.8.1.

Consider the object

\operatorname{ps-u}_{\operatorname{Bun}_{G}}:=(\Delta_{\operatorname{Bun}_{G}})_{!}(\underline{{\mathsf{e}}}_{\operatorname{Bun}_{G}})\in\operatorname{Shv}(\operatorname{Bun}_{G}\times\operatorname{Bun}_{G}).

Following Sect.
C.4.7
, we denote the resulting functor

\operatorname{Shv}(\operatorname{Bun}_{G})_{\operatorname{co}}\to\operatorname{Shv}(\operatorname{Bun}_{G})

by
\operatorname{Mir}_{\operatorname{Bun}_{G}}
.

2.8.2.

First, we claim:

Lemma 2.8.3
.

For a finite set
I
and
V\in{\mathop{\operatorname{\rm Rep}}}({\check{G}})^{\otimes I}
, the diagram

(2.21)

\begin{CD}\operatorname{Shv}(\operatorname{Bun}_{G})_{\operatorname{co}}@>{{\mathsf{H}}(V,-)_{\operatorname{co}}}>{}>\operatorname{Shv}(\operatorname{Bun}_{G}\times X^{I})_{\operatorname{co}}\\
@V{\operatorname{Mir}_{\operatorname{Bun}_{G}}}V{}V@V{}V{\operatorname{Mir}_{\operatorname{Bun}_{G}}\boxtimes\operatorname{Id}_{X^{I}}}V\\
\operatorname{Shv}(\operatorname{Bun}_{G})@>{{\mathsf{H}}(V,-)}>{}>\operatorname{Shv}(\operatorname{Bun}_{G}\times X^{I})\end{CD}

commutes, as functors defined by kernels.

Proof.

Using Sect.
1.1.5
and (
2.4
), we obtain that both circuits of the diagram are functors defined by the kernel

(\overset{\leftarrow}{h}{}\times\overset{\rightarrow}{h}{}\times s)_{!}(\operatorname{Sat}_{I}(V))[-2\dim(\operatorname{Bun}_{G})]\in\operatorname{Shv}(\operatorname{Bun}_{G}\times\operatorname{Bun}_{G}\times X^{I}).

∎

Corollary 2.8.4
.

The functor
\operatorname{Mir}_{\operatorname{Bun}_{G}}
intertwines the actions of
{\mathop{\operatorname{\rm Rep}}}({\check{G}})_{\operatorname{Ran}}
on

\operatorname{Shv}(\operatorname{Bun}_{G})_{\operatorname{co}}
and
\operatorname{Shv}(\operatorname{Bun}_{G})
, respectively, as functors defined by kernels.

In particular, we obtain:

Corollary 2.8.5
.

The functor
\operatorname{Mir}_{\operatorname{Bun}_{G}}
intertwines the action of
{\mathsf{P}}_{\operatorname{co}}
on

\operatorname{Shv}(\operatorname{Bun}_{G})_{\operatorname{co}}
and the action of
{\mathsf{P}}
on
\operatorname{Shv}(\operatorname{Bun}_{G})
.

2.8.6.

We now claim:

Proposition 2.8.7
.

The functor
\operatorname{Mir}_{\operatorname{Bun}_{G}}
sends
\operatorname{Shv}_{\operatorname{Nilp}}(\operatorname{Bun}_{G})_{\operatorname{co}}
to
\operatorname{Shv}_{\operatorname{Nilp}}(\operatorname{Bun}_{G})
.

Proof.

Follows from Corollary
2.8.5
, combined with Corollary
2.6.8
and
Theorem
1.6.4
(for
{\mathcal{Z}}=\operatorname{pt}
).

∎

2.8.8.

Let
{\mathcal{U}}\overset{j}{\hookrightarrow}\operatorname{Bun}_{G}
be a quasi-compact open substack. Consider the
corresponding endofunctor

\operatorname{Mir}_{\mathcal{U}}:\operatorname{Shv}({\mathcal{U}})\to\operatorname{Shv}({\mathcal{U}}),

see Sect.
B.4.1
.

Assume that
{\mathcal{U}}
is cotruncative. Then, according to Sect.
C.4.8
, we have

(2.22)

\operatorname{Mir}_{\operatorname{Bun}_{G}}\circ j_{*,\operatorname{co}}\simeq j_{!}\circ\operatorname{Mir}_{\mathcal{U}}.

2.8.9.

We claim:

Proposition 2.8.10
.

Assume that
{\mathcal{U}}
is
{\operatorname{Nilp}}
-cotruncative. Then the functor
\operatorname{Mir}_{\mathcal{U}}
sends

\operatorname{Shv}_{\operatorname{Nilp}}({\mathcal{U}})\to\operatorname{Shv}_{\operatorname{Nilp}}({\mathcal{U}})
.

Proof.

We have

{\mathcal{F}}\in\operatorname{Shv}_{\operatorname{Nilp}}({\mathcal{U}})\,\Leftrightarrow\,j_{*,\operatorname{co}}({\mathcal{F}})\in\operatorname{Shv}_{\operatorname{Nilp}}(\operatorname{Bun}_{G})_{\operatorname{co}}

and

\operatorname{Mir}_{\mathcal{U}}({\mathcal{F}})\in\operatorname{Shv}_{\operatorname{Nilp}}({\mathcal{U}})\,\Leftrightarrow\,j_{!}\circ\operatorname{Mir}_{\mathcal{U}}({\mathcal{F}})\in\operatorname{Shv}_{\operatorname{Nilp}}(\operatorname{Bun}_{G}).

Using (
2.22
), the assertion follows from Proposition
2.8.7
.

∎

2.9.
The miraculous property of
\operatorname{Bun}_{G}

2.9.1.

We now recall that in the paper
[
Ga1
]
it was shown that the functor

\operatorname{Mir}_{\operatorname{Bun}_{G}}:\operatorname{D-mod}(\operatorname{Bun}_{G})_{\operatorname{co}}\to\operatorname{D-mod}(\operatorname{Bun}_{G})

is an equivalence.

However, the proof in
loc. cit.
applies in any sheaf-theoretic context.
Furthermore, the same proof shows that

\operatorname{Id}_{\mathcal{Z}}\boxtimes\operatorname{Mir}_{\operatorname{Bun}_{G}}:\operatorname{Shv}({\mathcal{Z}}\times\operatorname{Bun}_{G})_{\operatorname{co}_{2}}\to\operatorname{Shv}({\mathcal{Z}}\times\operatorname{Bun}_{G})

is an equivalence for any algebraic stack
{\mathcal{Z}}
.

This implies that
\operatorname{Mir}_{\operatorname{Bun}_{G}}
admits an inverse
as a functor defined by a kernel
.
Hence, it makes sense to consider

(\operatorname{Id}_{\mathcal{Z}}\boxtimes\operatorname{Mir}_{\operatorname{Bun}_{G}}^{-1}):\operatorname{Shv}({\mathcal{Z}}\times\operatorname{Bun}_{G})\to\operatorname{Shv}({\mathcal{Z}}\times\operatorname{Bun}_{G})_{\operatorname{co}_{2}},

for any algebraic stack
{\mathcal{Z}}
. In other words,
\operatorname{Bun}_{G}
is
miraculous
in
the terminology of Sect.
C.5.1
.

2.9.2.

Following Sect.
C.5.4
, let us denote by
\operatorname{ev}^{\operatorname{Mir}}_{\operatorname{Bun}_{G}}

the composite functor

(2.23)

\operatorname{Shv}(\operatorname{Bun}_{G})\otimes\operatorname{Shv}(\operatorname{Bun}_{G})\overset{\operatorname{Id}\otimes\operatorname{Mir}_{\operatorname{Bun}_{G}}^{-1}}{\longrightarrow}\operatorname{Shv}(\operatorname{Bun}_{G})\otimes\operatorname{Shv}(\operatorname{Bun}_{G})_{\operatorname{co}}\overset{\operatorname{ev}_{\operatorname{Bun}_{G}}}{\longrightarrow}\operatorname{Vect},

Combining Sects.
2.9.1
and
2.6.1
, we obtain that the functor
(
2.23
) is the counit of a self-duality on
\operatorname{Shv}(\operatorname{Bun}_{G})
.

We refer to it as the
miraculous self-duality
of
\operatorname{Shv}(\operatorname{Bun}_{G})
.

2.9.3.

We claim:

Proposition 2.9.4
.

The functors
\operatorname{Mir}_{\operatorname{Bun}_{G}}
and
\operatorname{Mir}_{\operatorname{Bun}_{G}}^{-1}
send the subcategories

\operatorname{Shv}_{\operatorname{Nilp}}(\operatorname{Bun}_{G})_{\operatorname{co}}\subset\operatorname{Shv}(\operatorname{Bun}_{G})_{\operatorname{co}}\text{ and }\operatorname{Shv}_{\operatorname{Nilp}}(\operatorname{Bun}_{G})\subset\operatorname{Shv}(\operatorname{Bun}_{G})

to one another.

Proof.

Follows from the fact that the functor
\operatorname{Mir}_{\operatorname{Bun}_{G}}
intertwines the functors
{\mathsf{P}}
and

{\mathsf{P}}_{\operatorname{co}}
(see Corollary
2.8.5
), combined with Theorem
1.6.4
(for
{\mathcal{Z}}=\operatorname{pt}
)
and Corollary
2.6.8
.

∎

Corollary 2.9.5
.

The functor
\operatorname{Mir}_{\operatorname{Bun}_{G}}
induces an equivalence

\operatorname{Shv}_{\operatorname{Nilp}}(\operatorname{Bun}_{G})_{\operatorname{co}}\to\operatorname{Shv}_{\operatorname{Nilp}}(\operatorname{Bun}_{G}).

2.9.6.

Combining Corollaries
2.9.5
with
2.6.5
, we obtain:

Corollary 2.9.7
.

The composition

\operatorname{Shv}_{\operatorname{Nilp}}(\operatorname{Bun}_{G})\otimes\operatorname{Shv}_{\operatorname{Nilp}}(\operatorname{Bun}_{G})\hookrightarrow\operatorname{Shv}(\operatorname{Bun}_{G})\otimes\operatorname{Shv}(\operatorname{Bun}_{G})\overset{\operatorname{ev}^{\operatorname{Mir}}_{\operatorname{Bun}_{G}}}{\longrightarrow}\operatorname{Vect}

is the counit of a self-duality on
\operatorname{Shv}_{\operatorname{Nilp}}(\operatorname{Bun}_{G})
.

We refer to the self-duality of
\operatorname{Shv}_{\operatorname{Nilp}}(\operatorname{Bun}_{G})
established in Corollary
2.9.7
as
the
miraculous self-duality
of
\operatorname{Shv}_{\operatorname{Nilp}}(\operatorname{Bun}_{G})
.

2.9.8.

The fact that
\operatorname{Bun}_{G}
is miraculous formally implies that any cotruncative
quasi-compact open substack
{\mathcal{U}}\subset\operatorname{Bun}_{G}
is also miraculous, see
Sect.
C.5.2
.

In particular, the functor

\operatorname{Mir}_{\mathcal{U}}:\operatorname{Shv}({\mathcal{U}})\to\operatorname{Shv}({\mathcal{U}})

is invertible also as a functor defined by a kernel, and so we can consider the functor

(\operatorname{Id}_{\mathcal{Z}}\boxtimes\operatorname{Mir}_{\mathcal{U}}^{-1}):\operatorname{Shv}({\mathcal{Z}}\times{\mathcal{U}})\to\operatorname{Shv}({\mathcal{Z}}\times{\mathcal{U}}),

for any
{\mathcal{Z}}
.

Similarly to Sect.
2.9.2
, we obtain that the functor, denoted
\operatorname{ev}^{\operatorname{Mir}}_{\mathcal{U}}
,

\operatorname{Shv}({\mathcal{U}})\otimes\operatorname{Shv}({\mathcal{U}})\overset{\operatorname{Id}\otimes\operatorname{Mir}_{{\mathcal{U}}}^{-1}}{\longrightarrow}\operatorname{Shv}({\mathcal{U}})\otimes\operatorname{Shv}({\mathcal{U}})\overset{\operatorname{ev}_{\mathcal{U}}}{\longrightarrow}\operatorname{Vect}

is the counit of a self-duality on
\operatorname{Shv}({\mathcal{U}})
.

2.9.9.

We claim:

Proposition 2.9.10
.

For a quasi-compact Nilp-cotruncative
{\mathcal{U}}\overset{j}{\hookrightarrow}\operatorname{Bun}_{G}
, the functor

\operatorname{Mir}_{\mathcal{U}}^{-1}
sends
\operatorname{Shv}_{\operatorname{Nilp}}({\mathcal{U}})
to
\operatorname{Shv}_{\operatorname{Nilp}}({\mathcal{U}})
.

Proof.

Follows from Proposition
2.9.4
in the same way as Proposition
2.8.10

follows from Proposition
2.8.7
.

∎

Finally, similarly to Corollary
2.9.7
, we obtain:

Corollary 2.9.11
.

The composition

\operatorname{Shv}_{\operatorname{Nilp}}({\mathcal{U}})\otimes\operatorname{Shv}_{\operatorname{Nilp}}({\mathcal{U}})\hookrightarrow\operatorname{Shv}({\mathcal{U}})\otimes\operatorname{Shv}({\mathcal{U}})\overset{\operatorname{ev}^{\operatorname{Mir}}_{{\mathcal{U}}}}{\longrightarrow}\operatorname{Vect}

is the counit of a self-duality on
\operatorname{Shv}_{\operatorname{Nilp}}({\mathcal{U}})
.

We refer to the self-duality of
\operatorname{Shv}_{\operatorname{Nilp}}({\mathcal{U}})
established in Corollary
2.9.7
as
the
miraculous self-duality
of
\operatorname{Shv}_{\operatorname{Nilp}}({\mathcal{U}})
.

3.
The non-standard duality for
\operatorname{Shv}_{\operatorname{Nilp}}(\operatorname{Bun}_{G})

In this section we will prove what can be considered as the main result of this paper, Theorem
3.2.2
.
It states that the category
\operatorname{Shv}_{\operatorname{Nilp}}(\operatorname{Bun}_{G})
is self-dual via a certain non-standard procedure.

The proof of this theorem will be rather straightforward, modulo the work done in
[
AGKRRV
]
.

We will then connect this non-standard duality with the miraculous self-duality of Sect.
2.9.6
.

3.1.
The projection of the pseudo-diagonal object

In this subsection we study the object obtained by applying the projector
{\mathsf{P}}={\mathsf{P}}_{\operatorname{Bun}_{G},{\operatorname{Nilp}}}
to the

pseudo-diagonal
object on
\operatorname{Bun}_{G}
.

3.1.1.

Let
{\mathsf{P}}_{\operatorname{Bun}_{G},{\operatorname{Nilp}}}
is as in Sect.
1.6.1
. I.e., this is the idempotent on
\operatorname{Shv}(\operatorname{Bun}_{G})
that corresponds to the
precomposition of the fully faithful embedding

\operatorname{Shv}_{\operatorname{Nilp}}(\operatorname{Bun}_{G})\hookrightarrow\operatorname{Shv}(\operatorname{Bun}_{G})

with its left inverse.

Recall that
{\mathsf{P}}_{\operatorname{Bun}_{G},{\operatorname{Nilp}}}
is a functor defined by a kernel. In particular, it makes sense to consider the
endofunctors of
\operatorname{Shv}(\operatorname{Bun}\times\operatorname{Bun}_{G})
given by

{\mathsf{P}}_{\operatorname{Bun}_{G},{\operatorname{Nilp}}}\boxtimes\operatorname{Id}_{\operatorname{Bun}_{G}},\,\,{\mathsf{P}}_{\operatorname{Bun}_{G},{\operatorname{Nilp}}}\boxtimes{\mathsf{P}}_{\operatorname{Bun}_{G},{\operatorname{Nilp}}}\text{ and }\operatorname{Id}_{\operatorname{Bun}_{G}}\boxtimes{\mathsf{P}}_{\operatorname{Bun}_{G},{\operatorname{Nilp}}}.

3.1.2.

Let

\operatorname{ps-u}_{\operatorname{Bun}_{G},{\operatorname{Nilp}}}\in\operatorname{Shv}(\operatorname{Bun}_{G}\times\operatorname{Bun}_{G})

denote the object

({\mathsf{P}}_{\operatorname{Bun}_{G},{\operatorname{Nilp}}}\boxtimes{\mathsf{P}}_{\operatorname{Bun}_{G},{\operatorname{Nilp}}})(\operatorname{ps-u}_{\operatorname{Bun}_{G}}),

where

\operatorname{ps-u}_{\operatorname{Bun}_{G}}:=(\Delta_{\operatorname{Bun}_{G}})_{!}(\underline{{\mathsf{e}}}_{\operatorname{Bun}_{G}}),

see Sect.
2.8.1
.

Note that by Corollary
1.6.5
, the functor
{\mathsf{P}}_{\operatorname{Bun}_{G},{\operatorname{Nilp}}}\boxtimes{\mathsf{P}}_{\operatorname{Bun}_{G},{\operatorname{Nilp}}}
is the idempotent corresponding to the
embedding

\operatorname{Shv}_{\operatorname{Nilp}}(\operatorname{Bun}_{G})\otimes\operatorname{Shv}_{\operatorname{Nilp}}(\operatorname{Bun}_{G})\to\operatorname{Shv}(\operatorname{Bun}_{G}\times\operatorname{Bun}_{G}).

Hence, we can think of
\operatorname{ps-u}_{\operatorname{Bun}_{G},{\operatorname{Nilp}}}
as an object of

\operatorname{Shv}_{\operatorname{Nilp}}(\operatorname{Bun}_{G})\otimes\operatorname{Shv}_{\operatorname{Nilp}}(\operatorname{Bun}_{G}).

3.1.3.

We claim:

Proposition 3.1.4
.

We have canonical isomorphisms

({\mathsf{P}}_{\operatorname{Bun}_{G},{\operatorname{Nilp}}}\otimes\operatorname{Id}_{\operatorname{Bun}_{G}})(\operatorname{ps-u}_{\operatorname{Bun}_{G}})\simeq({\mathsf{P}}_{\operatorname{Bun}_{G},{\operatorname{Nilp}}}\otimes{\mathsf{P}}_{\operatorname{Bun}_{G},{\operatorname{Nilp}}})(\operatorname{ps-u}_{\operatorname{Bun}_{G}})\simeq\\
\simeq(\operatorname{Id}_{\operatorname{Bun}_{G}}\otimes{\mathsf{P}}_{\operatorname{Bun}_{G},{\operatorname{Nilp}}})(\operatorname{ps-u}_{\operatorname{Bun}_{G}}).

Proof.

Repeats the proof of Proposition
2.2.2
, taking into account Proposition
1.1.8
.

∎

3.2.
The main result

In this subsection we state and prove the main result of this paper, Theorem
3.2.2
.

3.2.1.

Let
\operatorname{ev}^{l}_{\operatorname{Bun}_{G}}
denote the functor

\operatorname{Shv}(\operatorname{Bun}_{G})\otimes\operatorname{Shv}(\operatorname{Bun}_{G})\to\operatorname{Vect},\quad{\mathcal{F}}_{1},{\mathcal{F}}_{2}\mapsto\operatorname{C}^{\cdot}_{c}({\operatorname{Bun}_{G}},{\mathcal{F}}_{1}\overset{*}{\otimes}{\mathcal{F}}_{2}).

The main result of this paper is:

Theorem 3.2.2
.

The functors

\operatorname{Shv}_{{\operatorname{Nilp}}}(\operatorname{Bun}_{G})\otimes\operatorname{Shv}_{{\operatorname{Nilp}}}(\operatorname{Bun}_{G})\hookrightarrow\operatorname{Shv}(\operatorname{Bun}_{G})\otimes\operatorname{Shv}(\operatorname{Bun}_{G})\overset{\operatorname{ev}^{l}_{\operatorname{Bun}_{G}}}{\longrightarrow}\operatorname{Vect}

and

\operatorname{ps-u}_{{\operatorname{Bun}_{G}},{\operatorname{Nilp}}}\in\operatorname{Shv}_{{\operatorname{Nilp}}}({\operatorname{Bun}_{G}})\otimes\operatorname{Shv}_{{\operatorname{Nilp}}}({\operatorname{Bun}_{G}})

define a duality datum.

Remark 3.2.3
.

As we shall see, the proof of Theorem
3.2.2
will be essentially
a formal manipulation (modulo Proposition
3.1.4
),
given the highly non-trivial fact that
\operatorname{ps-u}_{\operatorname{Bun}_{G},{\operatorname{Nilp}}}
actually belongs to

\operatorname{Shv}_{\operatorname{Nilp}}(\operatorname{Bun}_{G})\otimes\operatorname{Shv}_{\operatorname{Nilp}}(\operatorname{Bun}_{G})
. The latter result uses the full strength of
the main results of
[
AGKRRV
]
.

Remark 3.2.4
.

The peculiarity of Theorem
3.2.2
is that is straddles two different paradigms in which duality takes
place:

In the constructible sheaf-theoretic contexts
and
for D-modules, for a scheme
{\mathcal{Y}}

we have the perfect pairing

(3.1)

\operatorname{ev}_{\mathcal{Y}}:\operatorname{Shv}({\mathcal{Y}})\otimes\operatorname{Shv}({\mathcal{Y}})\to\operatorname{Vect},\quad{\mathcal{F}}_{1},{\mathcal{F}}_{2}\mapsto\operatorname{C}^{\cdot}({\mathcal{Y}},{\mathcal{F}}_{1}\overset{!}{\otimes}{\mathcal{F}}_{2}),

where the corresponding contravariant equivalence

(\operatorname{Shv}({\mathcal{Y}})^{c})^{\operatorname{op}}\to\operatorname{Shv}({\mathcal{Y}})^{c}

is given by Verdier duality. (The same is valid when
{\mathcal{Y}}
is a Verdier-compatible
quasi-compact algebraic stack,
but we need to replace
\operatorname{C}^{\cdot}({\mathcal{Y}},-)
by
\operatorname{C}^{\cdot}_{\blacktriangle}({\mathcal{Y}},-)
.)

In the Betti context, for the category
\operatorname{Shv}^{\operatorname{all}}({\mathcal{Y}})
of all sheaves of
{\mathsf{e}}
-vector spaces (not necessarily constructible ones), we have
a perfect pairing

(3.2)

\operatorname{ev}^{l}_{\mathcal{Y}}:\operatorname{Shv}^{\operatorname{all}}({\mathcal{Y}})\otimes\operatorname{Shv}^{\operatorname{all}}({\mathcal{Y}})\to\operatorname{Vect},\quad{\mathcal{F}}_{1},{\mathcal{F}}_{2}\mapsto\operatorname{C}^{\cdot}_{c}({\mathcal{Y}},{\mathcal{F}}_{1}\overset{*}{\otimes}{\mathcal{F}}_{2}).

The content of Theorem
3.2.2
is that an analog of the latter pairing defines a self-duality on
\operatorname{Shv}_{{\operatorname{Nilp}}}({\operatorname{Bun}_{G}})
.
We will see that this non-standard duality actually coincides with the miraculous duality of Sect.
2.9.6
.

Remark 3.2.5
.

It is easy to see that if
{\mathcal{Y}}
is a smooth and proper scheme, then the pairing (
3.2
) does induce a perfect pairing

\operatorname{Shv}_{\{0\}}({\mathcal{Y}})\otimes\operatorname{Shv}_{\{0\}}({\mathcal{Y}})\to\operatorname{Vect},

where
\operatorname{Shv}_{\{0\}}(-)
is the same as
\operatorname{QLisse}(-)
.

Further, in Sect.
5.5.6
we will see that if
{\mathcal{Y}}
is a (quasi-compact) stack with finitely many
isomorphism classes of
k
-points, then (
3.2
)
is
a perfect perfect

\operatorname{Shv}({\mathcal{Y}})\otimes\operatorname{Shv}({\mathcal{Y}})\to\operatorname{Vect}.

Thus, the pair
(\operatorname{Bun}_{G},{\operatorname{Nilp}})
behaves in a way analogous to both
({\mathcal{Y}},\{0\})
for a smooth and proper
{\mathcal{Y}}

and
({\mathcal{Y}},T^{*}({\mathcal{Y}}))
for
{\mathcal{Y}}
with finitely many
isomorphism classes of
k
-points.

In fact, this analogy is even stronger, see Sect.
4.6.8
.

3.2.6.
Proof of Theorem
3.2.2

In order to prove Theorem
3.2.2
, we need to show that for
{\mathcal{F}}\in\operatorname{Shv}_{{\operatorname{Nilp}}}({\operatorname{Bun}_{G}})
, the object

(3.3)

(\operatorname{Id}\otimes\operatorname{ev}^{l}_{\operatorname{Bun}_{G}})(\operatorname{ps-u}_{{\operatorname{Bun}_{G}},{\operatorname{Nilp}}}\otimes{\mathcal{F}})\simeq\\
\simeq(\operatorname{Id}\otimes\operatorname{C}^{\cdot}_{c}({\operatorname{Bun}_{G}},-))\circ(\operatorname{Id}\otimes(\Delta_{{\operatorname{Bun}_{G}}})^{*})(\operatorname{ps-u}_{{\operatorname{Bun}_{G}},{\operatorname{Nilp}}}\boxtimes{\mathcal{F}})\in\operatorname{Shv}_{{\operatorname{Nilp}}}({\operatorname{Bun}_{G}})

identifies canonically with
{\mathcal{F}}
, where in the right-hand side of the above formula we consider
\operatorname{ps-u}_{{\operatorname{Bun}_{G}},{\operatorname{Nilp}}}\boxtimes{\mathcal{F}}

as an object of the category

\operatorname{Shv}(\operatorname{Bun}_{G})\otimes\operatorname{Shv}(\operatorname{Bun}_{G}\times\operatorname{Bun}_{G}).

Using Proposition
3.1.4
, we interpret
\operatorname{ps-u}_{\operatorname{Bun}_{G},{\operatorname{Nilp}}}
as
({\mathsf{P}}_{\operatorname{Bun}_{G},{\operatorname{Nilp}}}\boxtimes\operatorname{Id}_{\operatorname{Bun}_{G}})(\operatorname{ps-u}_{\operatorname{Bun}_{G}})
.
We now use the fact that the functor
{\mathsf{P}}_{\operatorname{Bun}_{G},{\operatorname{Nilp}}}
is
co
defined by a kernel, see Proposition
1.1.8
.

Let us view
\operatorname{C}^{\cdot}_{c}({\operatorname{Bun}_{G}},-)
and
(\Delta_{{\operatorname{Bun}_{G}}})^{*}
also as functors codefined by kernels.
Hence, we can rewrite
the right-hand side in (
3.3
) as

(\operatorname{Id}_{\operatorname{Bun}_{G}}\boxtimes\operatorname{C}^{\cdot}_{c}({\operatorname{Bun}_{G}},-))\circ(\operatorname{Id}_{\operatorname{Bun}_{G}}\boxtimes(\Delta_{{\operatorname{Bun}_{G}}})^{*})\circ({\mathsf{P}}_{\operatorname{Bun}_{G},{\operatorname{Nilp}}}\boxtimes\operatorname{Id}_{\operatorname{Bun}_{G}}\boxtimes\operatorname{Id}_{\operatorname{Bun}_{G}})(\operatorname{ps-u}_{\operatorname{Bun}_{G}}\boxtimes{\mathcal{F}}),

where we now view
\operatorname{ps-u}_{\operatorname{Bun}_{G}}\boxtimes{\mathcal{F}}

as an object of
\operatorname{Shv}(\operatorname{Bun}_{G}\times\operatorname{Bun}_{G}\times\operatorname{Bun}_{G})
.

We can further rewrite the above expression as

{\mathsf{P}}_{\operatorname{Bun}_{G},{\operatorname{Nilp}}}\circ(\operatorname{Id}_{\operatorname{Bun}_{G}}\boxtimes\operatorname{C}^{\cdot}_{c}({\operatorname{Bun}_{G}},-))\circ(\operatorname{Id}_{\operatorname{Bun}_{G}}\boxtimes(\Delta_{{\operatorname{Bun}_{G}}})^{*})(\operatorname{ps-u}_{\operatorname{Bun}_{G}}\boxtimes{\mathcal{F}}).

We note that we have, tautologically,

(\operatorname{Id}_{\operatorname{Bun}_{G}}\boxtimes\operatorname{C}^{\cdot}_{c}({\operatorname{Bun}_{G}},-))\circ(\operatorname{Id}_{\operatorname{Bun}_{G}}\boxtimes(\Delta_{{\operatorname{Bun}_{G}}})^{*})(\operatorname{ps-u}_{\operatorname{Bun}_{G}}\boxtimes{\mathcal{F}})\simeq{\mathcal{F}}.

Finally, we have

{\mathsf{P}}_{\operatorname{Bun}_{G},{\operatorname{Nilp}}}({\mathcal{F}})\simeq{\mathcal{F}},

since
{\mathcal{F}}\in\operatorname{Shv}_{{\operatorname{Nilp}}}({\operatorname{Bun}_{G}})
.

∎[Theorem
3.2.2
]

3.3.
Relation to the miraculous functor

In this subsection, we will relate the non-standard duality of Theorem
3.2.2
with the
miraculous duality of Sect.
2.9.6
.

3.3.1.

On the one hand, according to Theorem
3.2.2
, we have a canonical identification

(3.4)

\operatorname{Shv}_{{\operatorname{Nilp}}}({\operatorname{Bun}_{G}})\simeq\operatorname{Shv}_{{\operatorname{Nilp}}}({\operatorname{Bun}_{G}})^{\vee}.

On the other hand, according to Corollary
2.6.5

we have a canonical identification

(3.5)

\operatorname{Shv}_{{\operatorname{Nilp}}}({\operatorname{Bun}_{G}})_{\operatorname{co}}\simeq\operatorname{Shv}_{{\operatorname{Nilp}}}({\operatorname{Bun}_{G}})^{\vee}.

3.3.2.

We claim:

Theorem 3.3.3
.

The identifications (
3.4
) and (
3.5
)
are intertwined by the miraculous functor

\operatorname{Mir}_{\operatorname{Bun}_{G}}:\operatorname{Shv}_{{\operatorname{Nilp}}}({\operatorname{Bun}_{G}})_{\operatorname{co}}\overset{\sim}{\to}\operatorname{Shv}_{{\operatorname{Nilp}}}({\operatorname{Bun}_{G}}).

Proof.

It suffices to show that the functor

\operatorname{Id}_{\operatorname{Shv}_{\operatorname{Nilp}}(\operatorname{Bun}_{G})}\otimes\operatorname{Mir}_{\operatorname{Bun}_{G}}:\operatorname{Shv}_{{\operatorname{Nilp}}}({\operatorname{Bun}_{G}})\otimes\operatorname{Shv}_{{\operatorname{Nilp}}}({\operatorname{Bun}_{G}})_{\operatorname{co}}\to\operatorname{Shv}_{{\operatorname{Nilp}}}({\operatorname{Bun}_{G}})\otimes\operatorname{Shv}_{{\operatorname{Nilp}}}({\operatorname{Bun}_{G}})

sends the unit of the (
3.5
) duality to the unit of the (
3.4
)
duality.

By Proposition
2.7.6
, the unit of the (
3.5
) duality is the object

\operatorname{u}_{\operatorname{Bun}_{G},{\operatorname{Nilp}},\operatorname{co}_{2}}\simeq({\mathsf{P}}\boxtimes\operatorname{Id}_{\operatorname{Bun}_{G}})(\operatorname{u}_{\operatorname{Bun}_{G},\operatorname{co}_{2}}),

and the unit of the duality (
3.4
) is the object

\operatorname{ps-u}_{{\operatorname{Bun}_{G}},{\operatorname{Nilp}}}\simeq({\mathsf{P}}\boxtimes\operatorname{Id}_{\operatorname{Bun}_{G}})(\operatorname{ps-u}_{\operatorname{Bun}_{G}}).

Hence, it suffices to establish an isomorphism

(\operatorname{Id}_{\operatorname{Bun}_{G}}\boxtimes\operatorname{Mir}_{\operatorname{Bun}_{G}})\circ({\mathsf{P}}\boxtimes\operatorname{Id}_{\operatorname{Bun}_{G}})(\operatorname{u}_{\operatorname{Bun}_{G},\operatorname{co}_{2}})\simeq({\mathsf{P}}\boxtimes\operatorname{Id}_{\operatorname{Bun}_{G}})(\operatorname{ps-u}_{\operatorname{Bun}_{G}}).

The latter isomorphism follows from the tautological isomorphism

(\operatorname{Id}_{\operatorname{Bun}_{G}}\boxtimes\operatorname{Mir}_{\operatorname{Bun}_{G}})(\operatorname{u}_{\operatorname{Bun}_{G},\operatorname{co}_{2}})\simeq\operatorname{ps-u}_{\operatorname{Bun}_{G}}.

∎

3.3.4.

From Theorem
3.3.3
, we obtain:

Corollary 3.3.5
.

The diagram

\begin{CD}\operatorname{Shv}_{{\operatorname{Nilp}}}(\operatorname{Bun}_{G})\otimes\operatorname{Shv}_{{\operatorname{Nilp}}}(\operatorname{Bun}_{G})_{\operatorname{co}}@>{}>{}>\operatorname{Shv}(\operatorname{Bun}_{G})\otimes\operatorname{Shv}(\operatorname{Bun}_{G})_{\operatorname{co}}@>{\operatorname{ev}_{\operatorname{Bun}_{G}}}>{}>\operatorname{Vect}\\
@V{\operatorname{Id}\otimes\operatorname{Mir}_{\operatorname{Bun}_{G}}}V{}V&&@V{}V{\operatorname{Id}}V\\
\operatorname{Shv}_{{\operatorname{Nilp}}}(\operatorname{Bun}_{G})\otimes\operatorname{Shv}_{{\operatorname{Nilp}}}(\operatorname{Bun}_{G})@>{}>{}>\operatorname{Shv}(\operatorname{Bun}_{G})\otimes\operatorname{Shv}(\operatorname{Bun}_{G})@>{\operatorname{ev}^{l}_{\operatorname{Bun}_{G}}}>{}>\operatorname{Vect}\end{CD}

commutes.

3.3.6.

From Corollary
3.3.5
we obtain:

Corollary 3.3.7
.

The pairing

\operatorname{Shv}_{{\operatorname{Nilp}}}(\operatorname{Bun}_{G})\otimes\operatorname{Shv}_{\operatorname{Nilp}}(\operatorname{Bun}_{G})\hookrightarrow\operatorname{Shv}(\operatorname{Bun}_{G})\otimes\operatorname{Shv}(\operatorname{Bun}_{G})\overset{\operatorname{ev}^{l}_{\operatorname{Bun}_{G}}}{\longrightarrow}\operatorname{Vect}

identifies canonically with

\operatorname{Shv}_{\operatorname{Nilp}}(\operatorname{Bun}_{G})\otimes\operatorname{Shv}_{\operatorname{Nilp}}(\operatorname{Bun}_{G})\hookrightarrow\operatorname{Shv}(\operatorname{Bun}_{G})\otimes\operatorname{Shv}(\operatorname{Bun}_{G})\overset{\operatorname{ev}^{\operatorname{Mir}}_{\operatorname{Bun}_{G}}}{\longrightarrow}\operatorname{Vect}.

In other words, this corollary says that the non-standard duality of Theorem
3.2.2

identifies canonically with the miraculous duality of Sect.
2.9.6
.

3.4.
Pairings against sheaves with nilpotent singular support

In this subsection we will amplify the assertion of Corollary
3.3.7
.

3.4.1.

We claim:

Theorem 3.4.2
.

The pairings

\operatorname{ev}^{l}_{\operatorname{Bun}_{G}}
and
\operatorname{ev}^{\operatorname{Mir}}_{\operatorname{Bun}_{G}}

\operatorname{Shv}(\operatorname{Bun}_{G})\otimes\operatorname{Shv}(\operatorname{Bun}_{G})\to\operatorname{Vect}

agree on the subcategories

\operatorname{Shv}_{\operatorname{Nilp}}(\operatorname{Bun}_{G})\otimes\operatorname{Shv}(\operatorname{Bun}_{G})\subset\operatorname{Shv}(\operatorname{Bun}_{G})\otimes\operatorname{Shv}(\operatorname{Bun}_{G})\supset\operatorname{Shv}(\operatorname{Bun}_{G})\otimes\operatorname{Shv}_{\operatorname{Nilp}}(\operatorname{Bun}_{G}).

Remark 3.4.3
.

We can reformulate Theorem
3.4.2
as saying that the following diagrams commute:

\begin{CD}\operatorname{Shv}(\operatorname{Bun}_{G})\otimes\operatorname{Shv}_{{\operatorname{Nilp}}}(\operatorname{Bun}_{G})_{\operatorname{co}}@>{}>{}>\operatorname{Shv}(\operatorname{Bun}_{G})\otimes\operatorname{Shv}(\operatorname{Bun}_{G})_{\operatorname{co}}@>{\operatorname{ev}_{\operatorname{Bun}_{G}}}>{}>\operatorname{Vect}\\
@V{\operatorname{Id}\otimes\operatorname{Mir}_{\operatorname{Bun}_{G}}}V{}V&&@V{}V{\operatorname{Id}}V\\
\operatorname{Shv}(\operatorname{Bun}_{G})\otimes\operatorname{Shv}_{{\operatorname{Nilp}}}(\operatorname{Bun}_{G})@>{}>{}>\operatorname{Shv}(\operatorname{Bun}_{G})\otimes\operatorname{Shv}(\operatorname{Bun}_{G})@>{\operatorname{ev}^{l}_{\operatorname{Bun}_{G}}}>{}>\operatorname{Vect}.\end{CD}

and

\begin{CD}\operatorname{Shv}_{{\operatorname{Nilp}}}(\operatorname{Bun}_{G})\otimes\operatorname{Shv}(\operatorname{Bun}_{G})_{\operatorname{co}}@>{}>{}>\operatorname{Shv}(\operatorname{Bun}_{G})\otimes\operatorname{Shv}(\operatorname{Bun}_{G})_{\operatorname{co}}@>{\operatorname{ev}_{\operatorname{Bun}_{G}}}>{}>\operatorname{Vect}\\
@V{\operatorname{Id}\otimes\operatorname{Mir}_{\operatorname{Bun}_{G}}}V{}V&&@V{}V{\operatorname{Id}}V\\
\operatorname{Shv}_{{\operatorname{Nilp}}}(\operatorname{Bun}_{G})\otimes\operatorname{Shv}(\operatorname{Bun}_{G})@>{}>{}>\operatorname{Shv}(\operatorname{Bun}_{G})\otimes\operatorname{Shv}(\operatorname{Bun}_{G})@>{\operatorname{ev}^{l}_{\operatorname{Bun}_{G}}}>{}>\operatorname{Vect}.\end{CD}

I.e., unlike the commutative diagram in Corollary
3.3.5
,
we only need one of the factors to have singular support in
{\operatorname{Nilp}}
.

3.4.4.

The rest of this subsection is devoted to the proof of Theorem
3.4.2
. Since
both pairings are swap-equivariant (see Sects.
B.7.3
and
C.5.4
),
it suffices to show that the two pairings agree on

\operatorname{Shv}_{\operatorname{Nilp}}(\operatorname{Bun}_{G})\otimes\operatorname{Shv}(\operatorname{Bun}_{G})\subset\operatorname{Shv}(\operatorname{Bun}_{G})\otimes\operatorname{Shv}(\operatorname{Bun}_{G}).

3.4.5.

By Proposition
2.6.7
(i), for
{\mathcal{F}}_{1}\in\operatorname{Shv}_{\operatorname{Nilp}}(\operatorname{Bun}_{G})
and

{\mathcal{F}}_{2}\in\operatorname{Shv}(\operatorname{Bun}_{G})
, we have

\operatorname{ev}^{\operatorname{Mir}}_{\operatorname{Bun}_{G}}({\mathcal{F}}_{1},{\mathcal{F}}_{2}):=\operatorname{ev}_{\operatorname{Bun}_{G}}(\operatorname{Mir}^{-1}_{\operatorname{Bun}_{G}}({\mathcal{F}}_{1}),{\mathcal{F}}_{2})\simeq\operatorname{ev}_{\operatorname{Bun}_{G}}(\operatorname{Mir}^{-1}_{\operatorname{Bun}_{G}}({\mathcal{F}}_{1}),{\mathsf{P}}({\mathcal{F}}_{2})),

and by Corollary
3.3.7
, the latter is canonically isomorphic to

\operatorname{ev}^{l}_{\operatorname{Bun}_{G}}({\mathcal{F}}_{1},{\mathsf{P}}({\mathcal{F}}_{2})).

Hence, in order to prove Theorem
3.4.2
, it suffices to show that for

{\mathcal{F}}_{1}\in\operatorname{Shv}_{\operatorname{Nilp}}(\operatorname{Bun}_{G})
and
{\mathcal{F}}_{2}\in\operatorname{Shv}(\operatorname{Bun}_{G})
, we have a canonical
isomorphism

\operatorname{ev}^{l}_{\operatorname{Bun}_{G}}({\mathcal{F}}_{1},{\mathcal{F}}_{2})\simeq\operatorname{ev}^{l}_{\operatorname{Bun}_{G}}({\mathcal{F}}_{1},{\mathsf{P}}({\mathcal{F}}_{2})).

This would follow from the next assertion:

Proposition 3.4.6
.

For any
{\mathcal{F}}_{1},{\mathcal{F}}_{2}\in\operatorname{Shv}(\operatorname{Bun}_{G})
, we have a canonical isomorphism

\operatorname{ev}^{l}_{\operatorname{Bun}_{G}}({\mathsf{P}}({\mathcal{F}}_{1}),{\mathcal{F}}_{2})\simeq\operatorname{ev}^{l}_{\operatorname{Bun}_{G}}({\mathcal{F}}_{1},{\mathsf{P}}({\mathcal{F}}_{2})).

3.4.7.
Proof of Proposition
3.4.6

Recall that
{\mathsf{P}}={\mathsf{H}}_{\mathsf{R}}
for
{\mathsf{R}}
as in Sect.
1.6.1
. Recall also that

{\mathsf{R}}\simeq{\mathsf{R}}^{\tau}
. Hence, Proposition
3.4.6
follows from the next
general assertion:

Lemma 3.4.8
.

For
{\mathcal{F}}_{1},{\mathcal{F}}_{2}\in\operatorname{Shv}(\operatorname{Bun}_{G})
and
{\mathcal{V}}\in{\mathop{\operatorname{\rm Rep}}}({\check{G}})_{\operatorname{Ran}}
, we have a canonical
isomorphism

\operatorname{ev}^{l}_{\operatorname{Bun}_{G}}({\mathsf{H}}_{{\mathcal{V}}}({\mathcal{F}}_{1}),{\mathcal{F}}_{2})\simeq\operatorname{ev}^{l}_{\operatorname{Bun}_{G}}({\mathcal{F}}_{1},{\mathsf{H}}_{{\mathcal{V}}^{\tau}}({\mathcal{F}}_{2})).

Proof.

We can assume that
{\mathcal{F}}_{1},{\mathcal{F}}_{2},{\mathcal{V}}
are compact, so both sides in the lemma are finite-dimensional
vector spaces. Hence, it is sufficient to identify the corresponding dual vector spaces.

For
{\mathcal{F}}^{\prime}_{1},{\mathcal{F}}^{\prime}_{2}\in\operatorname{Shv}(\operatorname{Bun}_{G})
, we have

\operatorname{ev}^{l}_{\operatorname{Bun}_{G}}({\mathcal{F}}^{\prime}_{1},{\mathcal{F}}^{\prime}_{2})^{\vee}:=\operatorname{C}^{\cdot}_{c}(\operatorname{Bun}_{G},{\mathcal{F}}^{\prime}_{1}\overset{*}{\otimes}{\mathcal{F}}^{\prime}_{2})^{\vee}\simeq{\mathop{\operatorname{{\mathcal{H}}\it om}}}_{\operatorname{Shv}(\operatorname{Bun}_{G}\times\operatorname{Bun}_{G})}({\mathcal{F}}^{\prime}_{1}\boxtimes{\mathcal{F}}^{\prime}_{2},\operatorname{u}^{\operatorname{naive}}_{\operatorname{Bun}_{G}}).

Recall (see
[
AGKRRV
, Sect. 11.3]
) that the category
{\mathop{\operatorname{\rm Rep}}}({\check{G}})_{\operatorname{Ran}}
is rigid. Let
{\mathcal{V}}^{\vee}
denote the
monoidal dual of
{\mathcal{V}}
.

We have

{\mathop{\operatorname{{\mathcal{H}}\it om}}}_{\operatorname{Shv}(\operatorname{Bun}_{G}\times\operatorname{Bun}_{G})}({\mathsf{H}}_{\mathcal{V}}({\mathcal{F}}_{1})\boxtimes{\mathcal{F}}_{2},\operatorname{u}^{\operatorname{naive}}_{\operatorname{Bun}_{G}})\simeq{\mathop{\operatorname{{\mathcal{H}}\it om}}}_{\operatorname{Shv}(\operatorname{Bun}_{G}\times\operatorname{Bun}_{G})}({\mathcal{F}}_{1}\boxtimes{\mathcal{F}}_{2},({\mathsf{H}}_{{\mathcal{V}}^{\vee}}\boxtimes\operatorname{Id}_{\operatorname{Bun}_{G}})(\operatorname{u}^{\operatorname{naive}}_{\operatorname{Bun}_{G}}))

and

{\mathop{\operatorname{{\mathcal{H}}\it om}}}_{\operatorname{Shv}(\operatorname{Bun}_{G}\times\operatorname{Bun}_{G})}({\mathcal{F}}_{1}\boxtimes{\mathsf{H}}_{{\mathcal{V}}^{\tau}}({\mathcal{F}}_{2}),\operatorname{u}^{\operatorname{naive}}_{\operatorname{Bun}_{G}})\simeq{\mathop{\operatorname{{\mathcal{H}}\it om}}}_{\operatorname{Shv}(\operatorname{Bun}_{G}\times\operatorname{Bun}_{G})}({\mathcal{F}}_{1}\boxtimes{\mathcal{F}}_{2},(\operatorname{Id}_{\operatorname{Bun}_{G}}\boxtimes{\mathsf{H}}_{{\mathcal{V}}^{\tau}{}^{\vee}})(\operatorname{u}^{\operatorname{naive}}_{\operatorname{Bun}_{G}})).

Note also that

{\mathcal{V}}^{\tau}{}^{\vee}\simeq{\mathcal{V}}^{\vee}{}^{\tau}.

Hence, the assertion of the lemma follows from the isomorphism

({\mathsf{H}}_{{\mathcal{V}}^{\vee}}\boxtimes\operatorname{Id}_{\operatorname{Bun}_{G}})(\operatorname{u}^{\operatorname{naive}}_{\operatorname{Bun}_{G}})\simeq(\operatorname{Id}_{\operatorname{Bun}_{G}}\boxtimes{\mathsf{H}}_{{\mathcal{V}}^{\vee}{}^{\tau}})(\operatorname{u}^{\operatorname{naive}}_{\operatorname{Bun}_{G}})

of Proposition
2.1.6
.

∎

3.5.
Pairings of cuspidal sheaves

In this subsection we will discuss an application of Corollary
3.3.5
to two ways to define
pairings on cuspidal objects of
\operatorname{Shv}(\operatorname{Bun}_{G})
.

3.5.1.

Let

\operatorname{Shv}(\operatorname{Bun}_{G})_{\operatorname{cusp}}\overset{\jmath}{\hookrightarrow}\operatorname{Shv}(\operatorname{Bun}_{G})

be the full subcategory of
cuspidal
objects, see
[
DrGa2
, Sect. 1.4]
.

Recall that every object
of
\operatorname{Shv}(\operatorname{Bun}_{G})_{\operatorname{cusp}}
is a clean extension from a particular open substack

{\mathcal{U}}_{0}\subset\operatorname{Bun}_{G}
, whose intersection with every connected component of

\operatorname{Bun}_{G}
is quasi-compact (see
[
DrGa2
, Proposition 1.4.6]
).
In particular, we have a canonical fully faithful embedding

\operatorname{Shv}(\operatorname{Bun}_{G})_{\operatorname{cusp}}\overset{\jmath_{\operatorname{co}}}{\hookrightarrow}\operatorname{Shv}(\operatorname{Bun}_{G})_{\operatorname{co}}.

Furthermore, in
[
Ga1
, Theorem 2.2.7 and Corollary 3.3.2]
, the following isomorphism is established:

(3.6)

\operatorname{Mir}_{\operatorname{Bun}_{G}}\circ\jmath_{\operatorname{co}}\simeq\jmath[-2(\dim(\operatorname{Bun}_{G}))-\dim(Z_{G})],

where
\dim(Z_{G})
is the center of
G
.

3.5.2.

Combining (
3.6
) with Corollary
3.3.5
we obtain

Corollary 3.5.3
.

Let

{\mathcal{F}}_{1},{\mathcal{F}}_{2}\in\operatorname{Shv}_{{\operatorname{Nilp}}}(\operatorname{Bun}_{G})
be two objects with

{\mathcal{F}}_{1}=\jmath({\mathcal{F}}_{1,\operatorname{cusp}}),\quad{\mathcal{F}}_{1,\operatorname{cusp}}\in\operatorname{Shv}(\operatorname{Bun}_{G})_{\operatorname{cusp}}.

Then there is a natural isomorphism

\operatorname{C}^{\cdot}_{c}(\operatorname{Bun}_{G},{\mathcal{F}}_{1}\overset{*}{\otimes}{\mathcal{F}}_{2})[-2(\dim(\operatorname{Bun}_{G}))-\dim(Z_{G})]\simeq\operatorname{C}^{\cdot}_{\blacktriangle}(\operatorname{Bun}_{G},\jmath_{\operatorname{co}}({\mathcal{F}}_{1,\operatorname{cusp}})\overset{!}{\otimes}{\mathcal{F}}_{2}).

Remark 3.5.4
.

Corollary
3.5.3
justifies a degree of agnosticism that some authors used when considering
pairings on automorphic sheaves: do we want to use

\operatorname{C}^{\cdot}_{c}(\operatorname{Bun}_{G},-\overset{*}{\otimes}-),

which is more natural in the Betti setting, or

\operatorname{C}^{\cdot}_{\blacktriangle}(\operatorname{Bun}_{G},-\overset{!}{\otimes}-),

which is more natural in the de Rham setting?

Now, Corollary
3.5.3
says that as long as one of the objects is cuspidal, the two give
the same result (up to a cohomological shift).

Remark 3.5.5
.

Similarly to Remark
3.4.3
, for the validity of Corollary
3.5.3
, it is sufficient
to require that only one of the objects
{\mathcal{F}}_{1},{\mathcal{F}}_{2}
have nilpotent singular support.

3.6.
Restricting to quasi-compact substacks of
\operatorname{Bun}_{G}

In this subsection we will discuss a variant of Corollary
3.3.5
for
quasi-compact open substacks of
\operatorname{Bun}_{G}
.

3.6.1.

Let
{\mathcal{U}}\overset{j}{\hookrightarrow}\operatorname{Bun}_{G}
be a Nilp-cotruncative quasi-compact open substack
\operatorname{Bun}_{G}
.

Set

\operatorname{ps-u}_{{\mathcal{U}},{\operatorname{Nilp}}}:=(j\times j)^{*}(\operatorname{ps-u}_{\operatorname{Bun}_{G},{\operatorname{Nilp}}})\in\operatorname{Shv}({\mathcal{U}}\times{\mathcal{U}}).

Since
\operatorname{ps-u}_{\operatorname{Bun}_{G},{\operatorname{Nilp}}}
belongs to
\operatorname{Shv}_{\operatorname{Nilp}}(\operatorname{Bun}_{G})\otimes\operatorname{Shv}_{\operatorname{Nilp}}(\operatorname{Bun}_{G})
, we obtain that

\operatorname{ps-u}_{{\mathcal{U}},{\operatorname{Nilp}}}
belongs to the full subcategory

\operatorname{Shv}_{\operatorname{Nilp}}({\mathcal{U}})\otimes\operatorname{Shv}_{\operatorname{Nilp}}({\mathcal{U}})\subset\operatorname{Shv}({\mathcal{U}}\times{\mathcal{U}}).

3.6.2.

Let

\operatorname{ev}^{l}_{{\mathcal{U}}}:\operatorname{Shv}({\mathcal{U}})\otimes\operatorname{Shv}({\mathcal{U}})\to\operatorname{Vect}

denote the functor

{\mathcal{F}}_{1},{\mathcal{F}}_{2}\mapsto\operatorname{C}^{\cdot}_{c}({{\mathcal{U}}},{\mathcal{F}}_{1}\overset{*}{\otimes}{\mathcal{F}}_{2}).

From Theorem
3.2.2
, we obtain:

Corollary 3.6.3
.

The functors

\operatorname{Shv}_{{\operatorname{Nilp}}}({\mathcal{U}})\otimes\operatorname{Shv}_{{\operatorname{Nilp}}}({\mathcal{U}})\to\operatorname{Shv}({\mathcal{U}})\otimes\operatorname{Shv}({\mathcal{U}})\overset{\operatorname{ev}^{l}_{{\mathcal{U}}}}{\longrightarrow}\operatorname{Vect}

and

\operatorname{ps-u}_{{\mathcal{U}},{\operatorname{Nilp}}}\in\operatorname{Shv}_{{\operatorname{Nilp}}}({\mathcal{U}})\otimes\operatorname{Shv}_{{\operatorname{Nilp}}}({\mathcal{U}})

define a duality datum.

Proof.

We need to show that for
{\mathcal{F}}\in\operatorname{Shv}_{{\operatorname{Nilp}}}({\mathcal{U}})
,

(\operatorname{ev}^{l}_{\mathcal{U}}\otimes\operatorname{Id})({\mathcal{F}}\otimes\operatorname{ps-u}_{{\mathcal{U}},{\operatorname{Nilp}}})\simeq{\mathcal{F}}.

We have

(\operatorname{ev}^{l}_{\mathcal{U}}\otimes\operatorname{Id})({\mathcal{F}}\otimes\operatorname{ps-u}_{{\mathcal{U}},{\operatorname{Nilp}}})=(\operatorname{ev}^{l}_{\mathcal{U}}\otimes\operatorname{Id})({\mathcal{F}}\otimes(j^{*}\otimes j^{*})(\operatorname{ps-u}_{\operatorname{Bun}_{G},{\operatorname{Nilp}}}))\simeq\\
\simeq(\operatorname{ev}^{l}_{\operatorname{Bun}_{G}}\otimes\operatorname{Id})(j_{!}({\mathcal{F}})\otimes(\operatorname{Id}\otimes j^{*})(\operatorname{ps-u}_{\operatorname{Bun}_{G},{\operatorname{Nilp}}}))\simeq\\
\simeq j^{*}\left((\operatorname{ev}^{l}_{\operatorname{Bun}_{G}}\otimes\operatorname{Id})(j_{!}({\mathcal{F}})\otimes\operatorname{ps-u}_{\operatorname{Bun}_{G},{\operatorname{Nilp}}})\right)\overset{\text{Theorem~\ref{t:non-standard duality}}}{\simeq}j^{*}\circ j_{!}({\mathcal{F}})\simeq{\mathcal{F}}.

∎

3.6.4.

Parallel to Theorem
3.3.3
, we have:

Corollary 3.6.5
.

Suppose that
{\mathcal{U}}
is universally
{\operatorname{Nilp}}
-contruncative (in particular, the category
\operatorname{Shv}_{\operatorname{Nilp}}({\mathcal{U}})

is self-dual via Verdier duality). Then:

(a)
The two identifications

\operatorname{Shv}_{\operatorname{Nilp}}({\mathcal{U}})\underset{\sim}{\overset{\sim}{\rightrightarrows}}\operatorname{Shv}_{\operatorname{Nilp}}({\mathcal{U}})^{\vee}

of Proposition
2.3.3
and Corollary
3.6.3
, respectively,
are intertwined by the miraculous functor
\operatorname{Mir}_{\mathcal{U}}
.

(b)
The diagram

\begin{CD}\operatorname{Shv}_{{\operatorname{Nilp}}}({\mathcal{U}})\otimes\operatorname{Shv}_{{\operatorname{Nilp}}}({\mathcal{U}})@>{}>{}>\operatorname{Shv}({\mathcal{U}})\otimes\operatorname{Shv}({\mathcal{U}})@>{\operatorname{ev}_{\mathcal{U}}}>{}>\operatorname{Vect}\\
@V{\operatorname{Id}\otimes\operatorname{Mir}_{{\mathcal{U}}}}V{}V&&@V{}V{\operatorname{Id}}V\\
\operatorname{Shv}_{{\operatorname{Nilp}}}({\mathcal{U}})\otimes\operatorname{Shv}_{{\operatorname{Nilp}}}({\mathcal{U}})@>{}>{}>\operatorname{Shv}({\mathcal{U}})\otimes\operatorname{Shv}({\mathcal{U}})@>{\operatorname{ev}^{l}_{\mathcal{U}}}>{}>\operatorname{Vect}\end{CD}

commutes.

(c) The self-duality of point (a) identifies with the miraculous self-duality of
Corollary
2.9.11
.

Proof.

Point (a) follows in the same way as Theorem
3.3.3
. Point (b)
follows formally from point (a). Point (c) follows formally from point (b).

∎

3.6.6.

Finally, we claim that we have the following analog of Theorem
3.4.2
:

Corollary 3.6.7
.

The pairings

\operatorname{ev}^{l}_{{\mathcal{U}}}
and
\operatorname{ev}^{\operatorname{Mir}}_{{\mathcal{U}}}

\operatorname{Shv}({\mathcal{U}})\otimes\operatorname{Shv}({\mathcal{U}})\to\operatorname{Vect}

agree on the subcategory
