\operatorname{Shv}_{\operatorname{Nilp}}({\mathcal{U}})\otimes\operatorname{Shv}({\mathcal{U}})\subset\operatorname{Shv}({\mathcal{U}})\otimes\operatorname{Shv}({\mathcal{U}}).

Proof.

Consider objects
{\mathcal{F}}_{1}\in\operatorname{Shv}_{\operatorname{Nilp}}({\mathcal{U}})
and
{\mathcal{F}}_{2}\in\operatorname{Shv}({\mathcal{U}})
. We have

\operatorname{ev}^{l}_{{\mathcal{U}}}({\mathcal{F}}_{1},{\mathcal{F}}_{2})\simeq\operatorname{ev}^{l}_{\operatorname{Bun}_{G}}(j_{!}({\mathcal{F}}_{1}),j_{!}({\mathcal{F}}_{2}))\overset{\text{\eqref{t:two pairings amplified}}}{\simeq}\operatorname{ev}_{\operatorname{Bun}_{G}}(j_{!}({\mathcal{F}}_{1}),\operatorname{Mir}^{-1}_{\operatorname{Bun}_{G}}\circ j_{!}({\mathcal{F}}_{2}))\simeq\\
\simeq\operatorname{ev}_{\operatorname{Bun}_{G}}(j_{!}({\mathcal{F}}_{1}),j_{*,\operatorname{co}}\circ\operatorname{Mir}^{-1}_{{\mathcal{U}}}({\mathcal{F}}_{2}))\simeq\operatorname{ev}_{{\mathcal{U}}}(j^{*}\circ j_{!}({\mathcal{F}}_{1}),\operatorname{Mir}^{-1}_{{\mathcal{U}}}({\mathcal{F}}_{2}))\simeq\operatorname{ev}_{{\mathcal{U}}}({\mathcal{F}}_{1},\operatorname{Mir}^{-1}_{{\mathcal{U}}}({\mathcal{F}}_{2})),

as required.

∎

Remark 3.6.8
.

As in Remark
3.4.3
, we can reformulate Corollary
3.6.7
as saying that the following diagram commutes:

\begin{CD}\operatorname{Shv}_{\operatorname{Nilp}}({\mathcal{U}})\otimes\operatorname{Shv}({\mathcal{U}})@>{}>{}>\operatorname{Shv}({\mathcal{U}})\otimes\operatorname{Shv}({\mathcal{U}})@>{\operatorname{ev}_{{\mathcal{U}}}}>{}>\operatorname{Vect}\\
@V{\operatorname{Id}\otimes\operatorname{Mir}_{{\mathcal{U}}}}V{}V&&@V{}V{\operatorname{Id}}V\\
\operatorname{Shv}_{\operatorname{Nilp}}({\mathcal{U}})\otimes\operatorname{Shv}({\mathcal{U}})@>{}>{}>\operatorname{Shv}({\mathcal{U}})\otimes\operatorname{Shv}({\mathcal{U}})@>{\operatorname{ev}^{l}_{{\mathcal{U}}}}>{}>\operatorname{Vect}.\end{CD}

I.e., unlike the commutative diagram in Corollary
3.6.5
(b),
we only need one of the factors to have singular support in
{\operatorname{Nilp}}
.

4.
An intrinsic characterization of sheaves with nilpotent singular support

The goal of this section is to show that the full subcategory

\operatorname{Shv}_{\operatorname{Nilp}}(\operatorname{Bun}_{G})^{\operatorname{constr}}\subset\operatorname{Shv}(\operatorname{Bun}_{G})
can be characterized by an intrinsic
categorical property (see Theorem
4.5.4
):

This theorem says that a constructible object of
\operatorname{Shv}(\operatorname{Bun}_{G})
belongs to
\operatorname{Shv}_{\operatorname{Nilp}}(\operatorname{Bun}_{G})
if and only if
the functor

{\mathcal{F}}^{\prime}\mapsto\operatorname{C}^{\cdot}_{\blacktriangle}(\operatorname{Bun}_{G},{\mathcal{F}}\overset{!}{\otimes}{\mathcal{F}}^{\prime})

admits a right adjoint
as a functor defined by a kernel
3
3

3

In the case of D-modules, the above condition
is equivalent to the functor in question preserving compactness.
.

4.1.
Comparison of two pairings, revisited

In this subsection we revisit the isomorphism of Corollary
3.6.5
(b) from the point of view
of the material in Sect.
B
.

4.1.1.

Recall that, according to Sect.
B.4.5
, if
{\mathcal{Y}}
is a quasi-compact algebraic stack, we have
a natural transformation

(4.1)

\operatorname{ev}^{l}_{\mathcal{Y}}({\mathcal{F}}_{1},\operatorname{Mir}_{\mathcal{Y}}({\mathcal{F}}_{2}))\to\operatorname{ev}_{\mathcal{Y}}({\mathcal{F}}_{1},{\mathcal{F}}_{2})

as functors

\operatorname{Shv}({\mathcal{Y}})\otimes\operatorname{Shv}({\mathcal{Y}})\to\operatorname{Vect}.

Using Sect.
C.4.8
, this natural transformation automatically extends to the case when
{\mathcal{Y}}
is not necessarily
quasi-compact, when we view both sides as functors

\operatorname{Shv}({\mathcal{Y}})\otimes\operatorname{Shv}({\mathcal{Y}})_{\operatorname{co}}\to\operatorname{Vect}.

In particular, we obtain a natural transformation

(4.2)

\operatorname{ev}^{l}_{\operatorname{Bun}_{G}}({\mathcal{F}}_{1},\operatorname{Mir}_{\operatorname{Bun}_{G}}({\mathcal{F}}_{2}))\to\operatorname{ev}_{\operatorname{Bun}_{G}}({\mathcal{F}}_{1},{\mathcal{F}}_{2}),\quad{\mathcal{F}}_{1}\in\operatorname{Shv}(\operatorname{Bun}_{G}),\,{\mathcal{F}}_{2}\in\operatorname{Shv}(\operatorname{Bun}_{G})_{\operatorname{co}}.

4.1.2.

Recall now that according to Corollary
3.3.5
, we have a canonical isomorphism

(4.3)

\operatorname{C}^{\cdot}_{c}(\operatorname{Bun}_{G},{\mathcal{F}}_{1}\overset{*}{\otimes}\operatorname{Mir}_{\mathcal{U}}({\mathcal{F}}_{2}))\simeq\operatorname{C}^{\cdot}_{\blacktriangle}(\operatorname{Bun}_{G},{\mathcal{F}}_{1}\overset{!}{\otimes}{\mathcal{F}}_{2}),\quad{\mathcal{F}}_{1}\in\operatorname{Shv}_{\operatorname{Nilp}}(\operatorname{Bun}_{G}),\,\,{\mathcal{F}}_{2}\in\operatorname{Shv}_{\operatorname{Nilp}}(\operatorname{Bun}_{G})_{\operatorname{co}}.

The goal of this subsection is to prove the following assertion:

Proposition 4.1.3
.

For
{\mathcal{F}}_{1}\in\operatorname{Shv}_{\operatorname{Nilp}}(\operatorname{Bun}_{G})
and
{\mathcal{F}}_{2}\in\operatorname{Shv}_{\operatorname{Nilp}}(\operatorname{Bun}_{G})_{\operatorname{co}}
, the
map (
4.2
) identifies with the
isomorphism (
4.3
).

Note that Proposition
4.1.3
formally implies:

Corollary 4.1.4
.

Let
{\mathcal{U}}
be a universally
{\operatorname{Nilp}}
-cotruncative quasi-compact open substack of
\operatorname{Bun}_{G}
.
Then for
{\mathcal{F}}_{1},{\mathcal{F}}_{2}\in\operatorname{Shv}_{\operatorname{Nilp}}({\mathcal{U}})
, the map

(4.4)

\operatorname{ev}^{l}_{\mathcal{U}}({\mathcal{F}}_{1},\operatorname{Mir}_{\mathcal{Y}}({\mathcal{F}}_{2}))\to\operatorname{ev}_{\mathcal{U}}({\mathcal{F}}_{1},{\mathcal{F}}_{2})

of (
4.1
) identifies with the
isomorphism of Corollary
3.6.5
(b).

The rest of this subsection is devoted to the proof of Proposition
4.1.3
.

4.1.5.

According to Sect.
B.4.5
, the map (
4.2
) is given by

\operatorname{ev}^{l}_{\operatorname{Bun}_{G}}\circ(\operatorname{Id}_{\operatorname{Bun}_{G}\times\operatorname{Bun}_{G}}\boxtimes\operatorname{ev}_{\operatorname{Bun}_{G}})({\mathcal{F}}_{1}\boxtimes\operatorname{ps-u}_{\operatorname{Bun}_{G}}\boxtimes{\mathcal{F}}_{2})\to\\
\to\operatorname{ev}_{\operatorname{Bun}_{G}}\circ(\operatorname{ev}^{l}_{\operatorname{Bun}_{G}}\boxtimes\operatorname{Id}_{\operatorname{Bun}_{G}\times\operatorname{Bun}_{G}})({\mathcal{F}}_{1}\boxtimes\operatorname{ps-u}_{\operatorname{Bun}_{G}}\boxtimes{\mathcal{F}}_{2})

where

\operatorname{ev}^{l}_{\operatorname{Bun}_{G}}:=\operatorname{C}^{\cdot}_{c}(\operatorname{Bun}_{G},-)\circ\Delta^{*}_{\operatorname{Bun}_{G}}\text{ and }\operatorname{ev}_{\operatorname{Bun}_{G}}:=\operatorname{C}^{\cdot}_{\blacktriangle}(\operatorname{Bun}_{G},-)\circ\Delta^{!}_{\operatorname{Bun}_{G}},

as functors

\operatorname{Shv}(\operatorname{Bun}_{G}\times\operatorname{Bun}_{G})\to\operatorname{Shv}(\operatorname{pt})\text{ and }\operatorname{Shv}(\operatorname{Bun}_{G}\times\operatorname{Bun}_{G})_{\operatorname{co}_{2}}\to\operatorname{Shv}(\operatorname{pt}),

codefined and defined by kernels, respectively.

Consider now the corresponding map

\operatorname{ev}^{l}_{\operatorname{Bun}_{G}}\circ(\operatorname{Id}_{\operatorname{Bun}_{G}\times\operatorname{Bun}_{G}}\boxtimes\operatorname{ev}_{\operatorname{Bun}_{G}})({\mathcal{F}}_{1}\boxtimes\operatorname{ps-u}_{\operatorname{Bun}_{G},{\operatorname{Nilp}}}\boxtimes{\mathcal{F}}_{2})\to\\
\to\operatorname{ev}_{\operatorname{Bun}_{G}}\circ(\operatorname{ev}^{l}_{\operatorname{Bun}_{G}}\boxtimes\operatorname{Id}_{\operatorname{Bun}_{G}\times\operatorname{Bun}_{G}})({\mathcal{F}}_{1}\boxtimes\operatorname{ps-u}_{\operatorname{Bun}_{G},{\operatorname{Nilp}}}\boxtimes{\mathcal{F}}_{2}).

We claim that we have a commutative diagram

(4.5)

\textstyle{\operatorname{ev}^{l}_{\operatorname{Bun}_{G}}\circ(\operatorname{Id}_{\operatorname{Bun}_{G}\times\operatorname{Bun}_{G}}\boxtimes\operatorname{ev}_{\operatorname{Bun}_{G}})({\mathcal{F}}_{1}\boxtimes\operatorname{ps-u}_{\operatorname{Bun}_{G}}\boxtimes{\mathcal{F}}_{2})}
\textstyle{\operatorname{ev}_{\operatorname{Bun}_{G}}\circ(\operatorname{ev}^{l}_{\operatorname{Bun}_{G}}\boxtimes\operatorname{Id}_{\operatorname{Bun}_{G}\times\operatorname{Bun}_{G}})({\mathcal{F}}_{1}\boxtimes\operatorname{ps-u}_{\operatorname{Bun}_{G}}\boxtimes{\mathcal{F}}_{2})}
\textstyle{\operatorname{ev}^{l}_{\operatorname{Bun}_{G}}\circ(\operatorname{Id}_{\operatorname{Bun}_{G}\times\operatorname{Bun}_{G}}\boxtimes\operatorname{ev}_{\operatorname{Bun}_{G}})({\mathcal{F}}_{1}\boxtimes\operatorname{ps-u}_{\operatorname{Bun}_{G},{\operatorname{Nilp}}}\boxtimes{\mathcal{F}}_{2})}
\textstyle{\operatorname{ev}_{\operatorname{Bun}_{G}}\circ(\operatorname{ev}^{l}_{\operatorname{Bun}_{G}}\boxtimes\operatorname{Id}_{\operatorname{Bun}_{G}\times\operatorname{Bun}_{G}})({\mathcal{F}}_{1}\boxtimes\operatorname{ps-u}_{\operatorname{Bun}_{G},{\operatorname{Nilp}}}\boxtimes{\mathcal{F}}_{2})}

]

Remark 4.1.7
.

Note that the same argument shows that the map (
4.2
) identifies with the isomorphism of
Theorem
3.4.2
when one of the objects
{\mathcal{F}}_{1}
and
{\mathcal{F}}_{2}
has nilpotent singular support.

The same remark applies when instead of
\operatorname{Bun}_{G}
we take a universally contruncative open substack

{\mathcal{U}}\subset\operatorname{Bun}_{G}
.

4.2.
Kernels defined by objects from
\operatorname{Shv}_{\operatorname{Nilp}}(\operatorname{Bun}_{G})

In this subsection we will combine Proposition
4.1.3
with Theorem
B.8.8
and deduce
that (constructible) objects from
\operatorname{Shv}_{\operatorname{Nilp}}(\operatorname{Bun}_{G})
admit right adjoints, when viewed as functors
defined by kernels.

4.2.1.

Let
{\mathcal{U}}\subset\operatorname{Bun}_{G}
be a universally
{\operatorname{Nilp}}
-contruncative quasi-compact open substack. We are going to prove:

Theorem 4.2.2
.

Let
{\mathcal{F}}\in\operatorname{Shv}({\mathcal{U}})
be an object contained in
\operatorname{Shv}_{\operatorname{Nilp}}({\mathcal{U}})^{\operatorname{constr}}
.
Then
{\mathcal{F}}
admits a right adjoint, viewed as a functor

\operatorname{Shv}({\mathcal{U}})\to\operatorname{Vect},

defined by a kernel.

Proof.

We will apply the criterion of Theorem
B.8.8
to
{\mathcal{F}}
. (Note that the condition in Sect.
B.8.3

holds automatically, since
{\mathcal{Y}}_{2}=\operatorname{pt}
).

By the equivalence (i)
\Leftrightarrow
(iii) in Theorem
B.8.8
, it suffices to show that for any

{\mathcal{F}}^{\prime}\in\operatorname{Shv}_{\operatorname{Nilp}}({\mathcal{U}})
, the map

\operatorname{ev}_{\mathcal{U}}^{l}({\mathcal{F}},{\mathcal{F}}^{\prime})\overset{\text{\eqref{e:left-to-right trans bis}}}{\longrightarrow}\operatorname{ev}_{\mathcal{U}}({\mathcal{F}},\operatorname{Id}^{l}_{\mathcal{U}}({\mathcal{F}}^{\prime}))

is an isomorphism.

Recall that the stack
{\mathcal{U}}
is miraculous and that the functor
\operatorname{Mir}_{\mathcal{U}}
defines a self-equivalence
on
\operatorname{Shv}_{\operatorname{Nilp}}({\mathcal{U}})
. Hence, we can assume that
{\mathcal{F}}^{\prime}
is of the form
\operatorname{Mir}_{\mathcal{U}}({\mathcal{F}}^{\prime\prime})
for

{\mathcal{F}}^{\prime\prime}\in\operatorname{Shv}_{\operatorname{Nilp}}({\mathcal{U}})
.

Consider the composition

\operatorname{ev}^{l}_{\mathcal{U}}({\mathcal{F}},{\mathcal{F}}^{\prime})=\operatorname{ev}^{l}_{\mathcal{U}}({\mathcal{F}},\operatorname{Mir}_{\mathcal{U}}({\mathcal{F}}^{\prime\prime}))\overset{\text{\eqref{e:left-to-right trans bis}}}{\longrightarrow}\operatorname{ev}_{\mathcal{U}}({\mathcal{F}},\operatorname{Id}^{l}_{\mathcal{U}}\circ\operatorname{Mir}_{\mathcal{U}}({\mathcal{F}}^{\prime\prime}))\overset{\text{\eqref{e:back to Q}}}{\longrightarrow}\operatorname{ev}_{\mathcal{U}}({\mathcal{F}},{\mathcal{F}}^{\prime\prime}).

By Sect.
B.4.9
, this composite map identifies with the map (
4.4
), and hence
is an isomorphism by Corollary
4.1.4
. Now, the map

\operatorname{Id}^{l}_{\mathcal{U}}\circ\operatorname{Mir}_{\mathcal{U}}({\mathcal{F}}^{\prime\prime})\to{\mathcal{F}}^{\prime\prime}

is an isomorphism since
{\mathcal{U}}
is miraculous (see Sect.
B.7.4
).

This implies that the map

\operatorname{ev}^{l}_{\mathcal{U}}({\mathcal{F}},\operatorname{Mir}_{\mathcal{U}}({\mathcal{F}}^{\prime\prime}))\overset{\text{\eqref{e:left-to-right trans bis}}}{\longrightarrow}\operatorname{ev}_{\mathcal{U}}({\mathcal{F}},\operatorname{Id}^{l}_{\mathcal{U}}\circ\operatorname{Mir}_{\mathcal{U}}({\mathcal{F}}^{\prime\prime}))

is an isomorphism, as required.

∎

4.2.3.

Let
{\mathcal{F}}
be as in Theorem
4.2.2
. Let us describe the right adjoint
{\mathcal{F}}^{R}
explicitly.
Namely, by Theorem
B.8.8
, we have

{\mathcal{F}}^{R}\simeq\operatorname{Id}^{l}_{\mathcal{U}}\circ{\mathbb{D}}^{\operatorname{Verdier}}({\mathcal{F}})\simeq\operatorname{Mir}_{\mathcal{U}}^{-1}\circ{\mathbb{D}}^{\operatorname{Verdier}}({\mathcal{F}}),

which also identifies with

{\mathbb{D}}^{\operatorname{Verdier}}\circ\operatorname{Mir}_{\mathcal{U}}({\mathcal{F}}),

see Remark
B.8.2
.

The unit of the adjunction is the map

(4.7)

\operatorname{u}_{\mathcal{U}}\to{\mathcal{F}}\boxtimes(\operatorname{Id}^{l}_{\mathcal{U}}\circ{\mathbb{D}}^{\operatorname{Verdier}}({\mathcal{F}}))

obtained from the tautological map

\operatorname{ps-u}_{\mathcal{U}}\to{\mathcal{F}}\boxtimes{\mathbb{D}}^{\operatorname{Verdier}}({\mathcal{F}})

by applying the functor
\operatorname{Id}_{\mathcal{U}}\boxtimes\operatorname{Id}^{l}_{\mathcal{U}}
.

The counit is the map

(4.8)

\operatorname{ev}_{\mathcal{U}}({\mathcal{F}},\operatorname{Mir}_{\mathcal{U}}^{-1}\circ{\mathbb{D}}^{\operatorname{Verdier}}({\mathcal{F}}))\overset{\text{Corollary~\ref{c:two pairings U}(b)}}{\simeq}\\
\simeq\operatorname{ev}^{l}_{\mathcal{U}}({\mathcal{F}},\operatorname{Mir}_{\mathcal{U}}\circ\operatorname{Mir}_{\mathcal{U}}^{-1}\circ{\mathbb{D}}^{\operatorname{Verdier}}({\mathcal{F}}))=\operatorname{ev}^{l}_{\mathcal{U}}({\mathcal{F}},{\mathbb{D}}^{\operatorname{Verdier}}({\mathcal{F}}))\to{\mathsf{e}},

where the last arrow is obtained by adjunction.

4.2.4.

We now claim:

Corollary 4.2.5
.

For any
{\mathcal{F}}\in\operatorname{Shv}_{{\operatorname{Nilp}}}({\mathcal{U}})
, the functor

{\mathsf{F}}:\operatorname{Shv}_{{\operatorname{Nilp}}}({\mathcal{U}})\to\operatorname{Vect},\quad{\mathcal{F}}^{\prime}\mapsto\operatorname{C}^{\cdot}_{\blacktriangle}({\mathcal{U}},{\mathcal{F}}\overset{!}{\otimes}{\mathcal{F}}^{\prime})

is defined and codefined by a kernel. The codefining object identifies canonically with

{\mathcal{G}}:=\operatorname{Mir}_{\mathcal{U}}({\mathcal{F}})\in\operatorname{Shv}_{{\operatorname{Nilp}}}({\mathcal{U}})\subset\operatorname{Shv}({\mathcal{U}}).

Proof.

If
{\mathcal{F}}
is constructible, the assertion follows by combining Theorems
4.2.2
and

B.6.3
(a).

We will now show how to reduce the assertion to the case when
{\mathcal{F}}
is constructible.

We need check that the map

(4.9)

(\operatorname{Id}_{\mathcal{Z}}\boxtimes{\mathsf{G}}^{l})({\mathcal{F}}^{\prime})\to\operatorname{Id}_{\mathcal{Z}}\boxtimes{\mathsf{F}}({\mathcal{F}}^{\prime}),

is an isomorphism for
{\mathcal{F}}^{\prime}\in\operatorname{Shv}({\mathcal{Z}}\times{\mathcal{U}})
.

Note that both sides in (
4.9
) commute with colimits in
{\mathcal{F}}
and
{\mathcal{F}}^{\prime}
.
Hence, we can assume that
{\mathcal{F}}^{\prime}
is bounded above.

If
{\mathcal{F}}^{\prime}
is bounded above, both sides of (
4.9
) maps isomorphically to the limits
over
n\in{\mathbb{N}}
of the corresponding expressions, when we replace
{\mathcal{F}}
by
\tau^{\geq-n}({\mathcal{F}})
.

Thus, we can assume that
{\mathcal{F}}
is bounded below. In this case,
{\mathcal{F}}
is a colimit of
constructible objects in
\operatorname{Shv}_{{\operatorname{Nilp}}}({\mathcal{U}})
. Hence, the assertion that (
4.9
)
is an isomorphism follows from the constructible case, by passing to colimits in the
{\mathcal{F}}

argument.

∎

4.2.6.

We now consider the situation with all of
\operatorname{Bun}_{G}
. We claim:

Theorem 4.2.7
.

Let
{\mathcal{F}}\in\operatorname{Shv}(\operatorname{Bun}_{G})
be an object contained in
\operatorname{Shv}_{\operatorname{Nilp}}(\operatorname{Bun}_{G})^{\operatorname{constr}}
.
Then it admits a right adjoint, when viewed as a functor

\operatorname{Shv}(\operatorname{Bun}_{G})_{\operatorname{co}}\to\operatorname{Vect}.

Proof.

Set

{\mathcal{F}}^{R}:=\operatorname{Mir}^{-1}_{\operatorname{Bun}_{G}}\circ{\mathbb{D}}^{\operatorname{Verdier}}({\mathcal{F}})\in\operatorname{Shv}(\operatorname{Bun}_{G})_{\operatorname{co}},

where we view
{\mathbb{D}}^{\operatorname{Verdier}}
as a functor

(\operatorname{Shv}(\operatorname{Bun}_{G})^{\operatorname{constr}})^{\operatorname{op}}\to\operatorname{Shv}(\operatorname{Bun}_{G})^{\operatorname{constr}}.

We will show that
{\mathcal{F}}^{R}
provides an adjoint of
{\mathcal{F}}
. The unit of the adjunction is a map

(4.10)

\operatorname{u}_{\operatorname{Bun}_{G},\operatorname{co}_{2}}\to{\mathcal{F}}\boxtimes{\mathcal{F}}^{R}

constructed as follows:

For every universally
{\operatorname{Nilp}}
-contruncative quasi-compact open substack
{\mathcal{U}}\overset{j}{\hookrightarrow}\operatorname{Bun}_{G}
, the

j^{*}\boxtimes j^{?}
restriction of (
4.10
) is the map

(j^{*}\boxtimes j^{?})(\operatorname{u}_{\operatorname{Bun}_{G},\operatorname{co}_{2}})\simeq(\operatorname{Id}_{\operatorname{Bun}_{G}}\boxtimes j^{?})\circ(j^{*}\boxtimes\operatorname{Id}_{\operatorname{Bun}_{G}})(\operatorname{u}_{\operatorname{Bun}_{G},\operatorname{co}_{2}})\simeq(\operatorname{Id}_{\operatorname{Bun}_{G}}\boxtimes j^{?})\circ(\operatorname{Id}_{\mathcal{U}}\boxtimes j_{*,\operatorname{co}})(\operatorname{u}_{\mathcal{U}})\simeq\\
\simeq(\operatorname{Id}_{\operatorname{Bun}_{G}}\boxtimes(j^{?}\circ j_{*}))(\operatorname{u}_{\mathcal{U}})\simeq\operatorname{u}_{\mathcal{U}}\overset{\text{\eqref{e:unit adj U}}}{\longrightarrow}j^{*}({\mathcal{F}})\boxtimes(\operatorname{Mir}_{\mathcal{U}}^{-1}\circ{\mathbb{D}}^{\operatorname{Verdier}}\circ j^{*}({\mathcal{F}}))\simeq\\
\simeq j^{*}({\mathcal{F}})\boxtimes(\operatorname{Mir}_{\mathcal{U}}^{-1}\circ j^{*}\circ{\mathbb{D}}^{\operatorname{Verdier}}({\mathcal{F}}))\simeq j^{*}({\mathcal{F}})\boxtimes(j^{?}\circ\operatorname{Mir}^{-1}_{\operatorname{Bun}_{G}}\circ{\mathbb{D}}^{\operatorname{Verdier}}({\mathcal{F}}))\simeq(j^{*}\boxtimes j^{?})({\mathcal{F}}\boxtimes{\mathcal{F}}^{R}).

The counit of the adjunction is a map

(4.11)

\operatorname{C}^{\cdot}_{\blacktriangle}(\operatorname{Bun}_{G},{\mathcal{F}}\overset{!}{\otimes}{\mathcal{F}}^{R})\to{\mathsf{e}}

is equal to the composition

\operatorname{C}^{\cdot}_{\blacktriangle}(\operatorname{Bun}_{G},{\mathcal{F}}\overset{!}{\otimes}{\mathcal{F}}^{R})\overset{\text{Corollary~\ref{c:two pairings BunG}}}{\simeq}\operatorname{C}^{\cdot}_{c}(\operatorname{Bun}_{G},{\mathcal{F}}\overset{*}{\otimes}{\mathbb{D}}^{\operatorname{Verdier}}({\mathcal{F}}))\to{\mathsf{e}},

where the latter map is obtained by adjunction from the map

{\mathcal{F}}\boxtimes{\mathbb{D}}^{\operatorname{Verdier}}({\mathcal{F}})\to(\Delta_{\operatorname{Bun}_{G}})_{*}(\omega_{\operatorname{Bun}_{G}}).

∎

Corollary 4.2.8
.

For any
{\mathcal{F}}\in\operatorname{Shv}_{{\operatorname{Nilp}}}(\operatorname{Bun}_{G})_{\operatorname{co}}
, the functor

{\mathsf{F}}:\operatorname{Shv}_{{\operatorname{Nilp}}}(\operatorname{Bun}_{G})\to\operatorname{Vect},\quad{\mathcal{F}}^{\prime}\mapsto\operatorname{C}^{\cdot}_{\blacktriangle}(\operatorname{Bun}_{G},{\mathcal{F}}\overset{!}{\otimes}{\mathcal{F}}^{\prime})

is defined and codefined by a kernel. The codefining object identifies canonically with

{\mathcal{G}}:=\operatorname{Mir}_{\operatorname{Bun}_{G}}({\mathcal{F}})\in\operatorname{Shv}_{{\operatorname{Nilp}}}(\operatorname{Bun}_{G})\subset\operatorname{Shv}(\operatorname{Bun}_{G}).

Proof.

Repeats that of Corollary
4.2.5
(indeed, it is easy to see that
Theorem
B.6.3
extends from the case of quasi-compact algebraic stacks to truncatable ones).

∎

4.3.
Pairings against sheaves with nilpotent singular support, revisited

4.3.1.

Let
{\mathcal{U}}
be a universally
{\operatorname{Nilp}}
-contruncative quasi-compact open substack of
\operatorname{Bun}_{G}
.

Let
{\mathcal{Y}}
be an algebraic stack. Note that for
{\mathcal{F}}\in\operatorname{Shv}({\mathcal{U}})
and
{\mathcal{F}}^{\prime}\in\operatorname{Shv}({\mathcal{Y}}\times{\mathcal{U}})
, we have a canonically defined map

(4.12)

(p_{\mathcal{Y}})_{!}({\mathcal{F}}^{\prime}\overset{*}{\otimes}p_{\mathcal{U}}^{*}(\operatorname{Mir}_{\mathcal{U}}({\mathcal{F}})))\to(p_{\mathcal{Y}})_{\blacktriangle}({\mathcal{F}}^{\prime}\overset{!}{\otimes}p_{\mathcal{U}}^{!}({\mathcal{F}}))

as functors

\operatorname{Shv}({\mathcal{Y}}\times{\mathcal{U}})\to\operatorname{Shv}({\mathcal{Y}}),

see (
B.17
).

From Corollary
4.2.5
we obtain:

Corollary 4.3.2
.

The natural transformation (
4.12
) is an isomorphism if
{\mathcal{F}}\in\operatorname{Shv}_{\operatorname{Nilp}}({\mathcal{U}})
.

Remark 4.3.3
.

Recall that Corollary
3.6.7
says that we have
a
canonical isomorphism

(4.13)

(p_{\mathcal{Y}})_{!}({\mathcal{F}}^{\prime}\overset{*}{\otimes}p_{\mathcal{U}}^{*}(\operatorname{Mir}_{\mathcal{U}}({\mathcal{F}})))\to(p_{\mathcal{Y}})_{\blacktriangle}({\mathcal{F}}^{\prime}\overset{!}{\otimes}p_{\mathcal{U}}^{!}({\mathcal{F}})),

when
{\mathcal{Y}}=\operatorname{pt}
. In fact, the same proof shows that there exists an isomorphism as in (
4.13
)
for any
{\mathcal{Y}}
.

The additional information provided by Corollary
4.3.2
is that this isomorphism comes from the generally
defined natural transformation (
B.17
).

4.3.4.

We now claim:

Corollary 4.3.5
.

There exists a canonical isomorphism

(p_{\mathcal{Y}})_{!}((\operatorname{Id}_{\mathcal{Y}}\boxtimes\operatorname{Mir}_{\mathcal{U}})({\mathcal{F}}^{\prime})\overset{*}{\otimes}p_{\mathcal{U}}^{*}({\mathcal{F}}))\simeq(p_{\mathcal{Y}})_{\blacktriangle}({\mathcal{F}}^{\prime}\overset{!}{\otimes}p_{\mathcal{U}}^{!}({\mathcal{F}})),\quad{\mathcal{F}}^{\prime}\in\operatorname{Shv}({\mathcal{Y}}\times{\mathcal{U}}),\,{\mathcal{F}}\in\operatorname{Shv}_{\operatorname{Nilp}}({\mathcal{U}}).

Proof.

Write

(p_{\mathcal{Y}})_{!}((\operatorname{Id}_{\mathcal{Y}}\boxtimes\operatorname{Mir}_{\mathcal{U}})({\mathcal{F}}^{\prime})\overset{*}{\otimes}p_{\mathcal{U}}^{*}({\mathcal{F}}))=(p_{\mathcal{Y}})_{!}\left((\operatorname{Id}_{\mathcal{Y}}\boxtimes\operatorname{Mir}_{\mathcal{U}})({\mathcal{F}}^{\prime})\overset{*}{\otimes}p_{\mathcal{U}}^{*}(\operatorname{Mir}_{\mathcal{U}}(\operatorname{Mir}^{-1}_{\mathcal{U}}({\mathcal{F}})))\right)\overset{\text{Corollary~\ref{c:pairing Y U}}}{\simeq}\\
\simeq(p_{\mathcal{Y}})_{\blacktriangle}((\operatorname{Id}_{\mathcal{Y}}\boxtimes\operatorname{Mir}_{\mathcal{U}})({\mathcal{F}}^{\prime})\overset{!}{\otimes}p_{\mathcal{U}}^{!}(\operatorname{Mir}^{-1}_{\mathcal{U}}({\mathcal{F}}))).

However, since the object
\operatorname{ps-u}_{{\mathcal{U}}}
is swap-equivariant, the latter expression identifies with

(p_{\mathcal{Y}})_{\blacktriangle}\left({\mathcal{F}}^{\prime}\overset{!}{\otimes}p_{\mathcal{U}}^{!}(\operatorname{Mir}^{-1}_{\mathcal{U}}(\operatorname{Mir}_{\mathcal{U}}({\mathcal{F}})))\right)\simeq(p_{\mathcal{Y}})_{\blacktriangle}({\mathcal{F}}^{\prime}\overset{!}{\otimes}p_{\mathcal{U}}^{!}({\mathcal{F}})),

as required.

∎

4.3.6.

We now revisit the pairings on all of
\operatorname{Bun}_{G}
. Let
{\mathcal{Y}}
be as above. For
{\mathcal{F}}\in\operatorname{Shv}(\operatorname{Bun}_{G})_{\operatorname{co}}

consider the natural transformation

(4.14)

(p_{\mathcal{Y}})_{!}({\mathcal{F}}^{\prime}\overset{*}{\otimes}p_{\operatorname{Bun}_{G}}^{*}(\operatorname{Mir}_{\operatorname{Bun}_{G}}({\mathcal{F}})))\to(p_{\mathcal{Y}})_{\blacktriangle}({\mathcal{F}}^{\prime}\overset{!}{\otimes}p_{\operatorname{Bun}_{G}}^{!}({\mathcal{F}})),

as functors
\operatorname{Shv}({\mathcal{Y}}\times\operatorname{Bun}_{G})\to\operatorname{Shv}({\mathcal{Y}})
, see (
B.17
).

From Corollary
4.2.8
we obtain:

Corollary 4.3.7
.

The natural transformation (
4.14
) is an isomorphism if
{\mathcal{F}}\in\operatorname{Shv}_{\operatorname{Nilp}}(\operatorname{Bun}_{G})
.

4.3.8.

Finally, we claim:

Corollary 4.3.9
.

For
{\mathcal{F}}\in\operatorname{Shv}_{\operatorname{Nilp}}(\operatorname{Bun}_{G})
and any algebraic stack
{\mathcal{Y}}
, there is a canonical isomorphism
isomorphism of functors
\operatorname{Shv}({\mathcal{Y}}\times\operatorname{Bun}_{G})_{\operatorname{co}}\to\operatorname{Shv}({\mathcal{Y}})

(4.15)

(p_{\mathcal{Y}})_{!}((\operatorname{Id}_{\mathcal{Y}}\boxtimes\operatorname{Mir}_{\operatorname{Bun}_{G}})({\mathcal{F}}^{\prime})\overset{*}{\otimes}p_{\operatorname{Bun}_{G}}^{*}({\mathcal{F}}))\simeq(p_{\mathcal{Y}})_{\blacktriangle}({\mathcal{F}}^{\prime}\overset{!}{\otimes}p_{\operatorname{Bun}_{G}}^{!}({\mathcal{F}})),\quad{\mathcal{F}}^{\prime}\in\operatorname{Shv}({\mathcal{Y}}\times\operatorname{Bun}_{G}).

Proof.

Follows from Corollary
4.3.7
in the same way as Corollary
4.3.5
follows from Corollary
4.3.2
.

∎

Remark 4.3.10
.

Note that in the case when
{\mathcal{Y}}=\operatorname{pt}
,
the assertion of Corollaries
4.3.9
and
4.3.7

has been already established in Theorem
3.4.2
.

Similarly to Remark
4.3.3
, the proof of Theorem
3.4.2

can be extended to include the case of an arbitrary
{\mathcal{Y}}
. Furthermore, one can
show that the resulting isomorphisms identify with those of Corollaries
4.3.9
and
4.3.7
.

4.4.
A refined version of Theorem
1.3.7

In this subsection we discuss an amplification of the categorical Künneth formula,
Theorem
1.3.7
.

4.4.1.

In this subsection, we will use Theorem
4.2.2
to prove the following result:

Theorem 4.4.2
.

Let
{\mathcal{Z}}
be an algebraic stack, and let
{\mathcal{N}}
be a conical half-dimensional closed subset of
T^{*}({\mathcal{Z}})
. Then the
functor

\operatorname{Shv}_{\mathcal{N}}({\mathcal{Z}})\otimes\operatorname{Shv}_{\operatorname{Nilp}}(\operatorname{Bun}_{G})\to\operatorname{Shv}_{{\mathcal{N}}\times{\operatorname{Nilp}}}({\mathcal{Z}}\times\operatorname{Bun}_{G})

is an equivalence.

By passing to the limit, the statement of Theorem
4.4.2
is obtained from the following:

Theorem 4.4.3
.

Let
{\mathcal{Z}}
be an algebraic stack, and let
{\mathcal{N}}
be a half-dimensional closed
subset of
T^{*}({\mathcal{Z}})
. Then for
any universally
{\operatorname{Nilp}}
-cotruncative open substack
{\mathcal{U}}\subset\operatorname{Bun}_{G}
, the
functor

\operatorname{Shv}_{\mathcal{N}}({\mathcal{Z}})\otimes\operatorname{Shv}_{\operatorname{Nilp}}({\mathcal{U}})\to\operatorname{Shv}_{{\mathcal{N}}\times{\operatorname{Nilp}}}({\mathcal{Z}}\times{\mathcal{U}})

is an equivalence.

We proceed to the proof of Theorem
4.4.3
.

4.4.4.

Given Corollary
1.7.6
, in order to prove Theorem
4.4.3
, we have to show
the following:

Let
{\mathcal{F}}^{\prime}
be an object in
\operatorname{Shv}({\mathcal{Z}})\otimes\operatorname{Shv}_{\operatorname{Nilp}}({\mathcal{U}})
, whose image along the functor

(4.16)

\operatorname{Shv}({\mathcal{Z}})\otimes\operatorname{Shv}_{\operatorname{Nilp}}({\mathcal{U}})\overset{\boxtimes}{\to}\operatorname{Shv}({\mathcal{Z}}\times{\mathcal{U}})

is contained in
\operatorname{Shv}_{{\mathcal{N}}\times{\operatorname{Nilp}}}({\mathcal{Z}}\times{\mathcal{U}})
. Then
{\mathcal{F}}^{\prime}\in\operatorname{Shv}_{\mathcal{N}}({\mathcal{Z}})\otimes\operatorname{Shv}_{\operatorname{Nilp}}({\mathcal{U}})
.

To prove this, by Proposition
2.3.3
, it suffices to show that for every
{\mathcal{F}}\in\operatorname{Shv}_{\operatorname{Nilp}}({\mathcal{U}})
, the object

(\operatorname{Id}\otimes\operatorname{ev}_{\mathcal{U}})({\mathcal{F}}^{\prime}\otimes{\mathcal{F}})\in\operatorname{Shv}({\mathcal{Z}})

belongs to
\operatorname{Shv}_{\mathcal{N}}({\mathcal{Z}})
.

We rewrite

(\operatorname{Id}\otimes\operatorname{ev}_{\mathcal{U}})({\mathcal{F}}^{\prime}\otimes{\mathcal{F}})\simeq(p_{1})_{\blacktriangle}({\mathcal{F}}^{\prime}\overset{!}{\otimes}p_{2}^{!}({\mathcal{F}})),

where in the right-hand side, we denote by the same character
{\mathcal{F}}^{\prime}

the image of
{\mathcal{F}}^{\prime}
along (
4.16
).

Thus, it suffices to show that for
{\mathcal{F}}^{\prime}\in\operatorname{Shv}_{{\mathcal{N}}\times T^{*}(\operatorname{Bun}_{G})}({\mathcal{Z}}\times{\mathcal{U}})
and

{\mathcal{F}}\in\operatorname{Shv}_{\operatorname{Nilp}}({\mathcal{U}})
, the object

(\operatorname{Id}_{\mathcal{Z}}\boxtimes{\mathsf{F}})({\mathcal{F}}^{\prime})\in\operatorname{Shv}({\mathcal{Z}})

belongs to
\operatorname{Shv}_{\mathcal{N}}({\mathcal{Z}})
, where
{\mathsf{F}}
is the functor defined by
{\mathcal{F}}
as a kernel.

According to Corollary
4.2.5
, the functor
{\mathsf{F}}
is defined and codefined by a kernel.
The required assertion follows now from Corollary
B.10.10
.

∎[Theorem
4.4.3
]

4.5.
A converse statement

In this subsection we will state an assertion that provides a converse to Corollary
4.2.8
,
and as a result also to Theorem
4.2.7
.

4.5.1.

We claim:

Theorem 4.5.2
.

Let
{\mathcal{F}}
be an object of
\operatorname{Shv}(\operatorname{Bun}_{G})_{\operatorname{co}}
such that the corresponding functor

(4.17)

{\mathsf{F}}:=\operatorname{C}^{\cdot}_{\blacktriangle}(\operatorname{Bun}_{G},{\mathcal{F}}\overset{!}{\otimes}-),\quad\operatorname{Shv}(\operatorname{Bun}_{G})\to\operatorname{Vect}

is defined and codefined by a kernel. Then

{\mathcal{F}}\in\operatorname{Shv}_{\operatorname{Nilp}}(\operatorname{Bun}_{G})_{\operatorname{co}}
.

This theorem will be proved in Sect.
4.7
. In the rest of this subsection we will derive some corollaries.

4.5.3.

As a consequence, we obtain:

Theorem 4.5.4
.

Let
{\mathcal{F}}
be an object of
\operatorname{Shv}(\operatorname{Bun}_{G})^{\operatorname{constr}}
such that the corresponding functor

{\mathsf{F}}:=\operatorname{C}^{\cdot}_{\blacktriangle}(\operatorname{Bun}_{G},{\mathcal{F}}\overset{!}{\otimes}-),\quad\operatorname{Shv}(\operatorname{Bun}_{G})_{\operatorname{co}}\to\operatorname{Vect}

admits a right adjoint
as a functor defined by a kernel
. Then

{\mathcal{F}}\in\operatorname{Shv}_{\operatorname{Nilp}}(\operatorname{Bun}_{G})
.

Proof.

Consider the object

{\mathcal{G}}:=\operatorname{Mir}^{-1}_{\operatorname{Bun}_{G}}({\mathcal{F}})\in\operatorname{Shv}(\operatorname{Bun}_{G})_{\operatorname{co}}.

The assumption on
{\mathcal{F}}
implies that
{\mathcal{G}}
, viewed as a functor
\operatorname{Shv}(\operatorname{Bun}_{G})\to\operatorname{Vect}

defined by a kernel, also admits a right adjoint.
By Theorem
B.6.3
(a) (see Remark
B.6.10
), the resulting functor

{\mathsf{G}}:\operatorname{Shv}(\operatorname{Bun}_{G})\to\operatorname{Vect}

is defined and codefined by a kernel.

Hence, from Theorem
4.5.2
, we obtain that
{\mathcal{G}}\in\operatorname{Shv}_{\operatorname{Nilp}}(\operatorname{Bun}_{G})_{\operatorname{co}}
.
Hence,

{\mathcal{F}}=\operatorname{Mir}_{\operatorname{Bun}_{G}}({\mathcal{G}})\in\operatorname{Shv}_{\operatorname{Nilp}}(\operatorname{Bun}_{G}).

∎

4.5.5.

Combining Theorems
4.5.4
,
4.2.7
and
B.8.8

(see Remark
B.8.9
), we obtain:

Corollary 4.5.6
.

For
{\mathcal{F}}\in\operatorname{Shv}(\operatorname{Bun}_{G})^{\operatorname{constr}}
the following conditions are equivalent:

(i)
{\mathcal{F}}\in\operatorname{Shv}_{\operatorname{Nilp}}(\operatorname{Bun}_{G})
;

(ii)
The functor
{\mathsf{F}}
admits
a
right adjoint as a functor defined by a kernel.

(iii) The map

\operatorname{C}^{\cdot}_{c}(\operatorname{Bun}_{G},{\mathcal{F}}\overset{*}{\otimes}{\mathbb{D}}({\mathcal{F}}))\to\operatorname{C}^{\cdot}_{\blacktriangle}(\operatorname{Bun}_{G},{\mathcal{F}}\overset{!}{\otimes}(\operatorname{Mir}^{-1}_{\operatorname{Bun}_{G}}\circ{\mathbb{D}}^{\operatorname{Verdier}}({\mathcal{F}})))

of (
4.2
) is an isomorphism, where
{\mathbb{D}}^{\operatorname{Verdier}}
is understood as a functor

(\operatorname{Shv}(\operatorname{Bun}_{G})^{\operatorname{constr}})^{\operatorname{op}}\to\operatorname{Shv}(\operatorname{Bun}_{G})^{\operatorname{constr}}.

4.5.7.

Let us momentarily place ourselves in the context of (non-necessarily holonomic) D-modules. I.e.,
\operatorname{Shv}(-)

will denote the category of ind-holonoimic D-modules, viewed as a subcategory of the category
\operatorname{D-mod}(-)

of all D-modules. Note that the inclusion

\operatorname{Shv}_{\operatorname{Nilp}}(\operatorname{Bun}_{G})\subset\operatorname{D-mod}_{\operatorname{Nilp}}(\operatorname{Bun}_{G})

is an equivalence, since
{\operatorname{Nilp}}\subset T^{*}(\operatorname{Bun}_{G})
is Lagrangian.

In this case, the condition that the functor

{\mathsf{F}}
admits a right adjoint as a functor defined by a kernel is equivalent to the condition that it admits a
continuous right adjoint as a plain functor

\operatorname{D-mod}(\operatorname{Bun}_{G})_{\operatorname{co}}\to\operatorname{Vect},

which is in turn equivalent to the condition that it sends compact objects to finite-dimensional vector spaces.

4.5.8.

We clam:

Theorem 4.5.9
.

For
{\mathcal{F}}\in\operatorname{Shv}(\operatorname{Bun}_{G})^{\operatorname{constr}}\subset\operatorname{D-mod}(\operatorname{Bun}_{G})
the following conditions are equivalent:

(i)
{\mathcal{F}}\in\operatorname{Shv}_{\operatorname{Nilp}}(\operatorname{Bun}_{G})
;

(ii) For any
{\mathcal{F}}^{\prime}\in\operatorname{D-mod}(\operatorname{Bun}_{G})^{c}_{\operatorname{co}}
, the object

\operatorname{C}_{\blacktriangle}(\operatorname{Bun}_{G},{\mathcal{F}}\overset{!}{\otimes}{\mathcal{F}}^{\prime})\in\operatorname{Vect}

is finite-dimensional.

(ii’) For any
{\mathcal{F}}^{\prime}\in\operatorname{D-mod}(\operatorname{Bun}_{G})^{c}
, the object

{\mathop{\operatorname{{\mathcal{H}}\it om}}}_{\operatorname{D-mod}(\operatorname{Bun}_{G})}({\mathcal{F}}^{\prime},{\mathcal{F}})\in\operatorname{Vect}

is finite-dimensional.

(ii”) For any
{\mathcal{F}}^{\prime}\in\operatorname{D-mod}(\operatorname{Bun}_{G})^{c}_{\operatorname{co}}
, the object

{\mathop{\operatorname{{\mathcal{H}}\it om}}}_{\operatorname{D-mod}(\operatorname{Bun}_{G})}({\mathcal{F}},\operatorname{Id}^{\operatorname{naive}}_{\operatorname{Bun}_{G}}({\mathcal{F}}^{\prime}))\in\operatorname{Vect}

is finite-dimensional.

Proof.

The equivalence of (i) and (ii) follows from the combination of Theorems
4.2.7
and
4.5.4
.
The equivalence of (ii) and (ii’) is formal: for
{\mathcal{F}}^{\prime}\in\operatorname{D-mod}(\operatorname{Bun}_{G})^{c}

{\mathop{\operatorname{{\mathcal{H}}\it om}}}_{\operatorname{D-mod}(\operatorname{Bun}_{G})}({\mathcal{F}}^{\prime},{\mathcal{F}})\simeq\operatorname{C}_{\blacktriangle}(\operatorname{Bun}_{G},{\mathcal{F}}\overset{!}{\otimes}{\mathbb{D}}^{\operatorname{Verdier}}({\mathcal{F}}^{\prime})),

where
{\mathbb{D}}^{\operatorname{Verdier}}
is understood as an equivalence

(\operatorname{D-mod}(\operatorname{Bun}_{G})^{c})^{\operatorname{op}}\to\operatorname{D-mod}(\operatorname{Bun}_{G})^{c}_{\operatorname{co}}.

The equivalence of (ii’) and (ii”) follows by viewing
{\mathbb{D}}^{\operatorname{Verdier}}
as an equivalence

(\operatorname{Shv}(\operatorname{Bun}_{G})^{\operatorname{constr}})^{\operatorname{op}}\simeq\operatorname{Shv}(\operatorname{Bun}_{G})^{\operatorname{constr}}.

∎

4.6.
A version for a universally
{\operatorname{Nilp}}
-cotruncative quasi-compact open substack

4.6.1.

Let
{\mathcal{U}}
be a universally
{\operatorname{Nilp}}
-cotruncative quasi-compact open substack of
\operatorname{Bun}_{G}
.
As a formal corollary of Theorem
4.5.2
, we obtain:

Corollary 4.6.2
.

Let
{\mathcal{F}}
be an object of
\operatorname{Shv}({\mathcal{U}})
such that the corresponding functor

{\mathsf{F}}:=\operatorname{C}^{\cdot}_{\blacktriangle}({\mathcal{U}},{\mathcal{F}}\overset{!}{\otimes}-),\quad\operatorname{Shv}({\mathcal{U}})\to\operatorname{Vect}

is defined and codefined by a kernel. Then

{\mathcal{F}}\in\operatorname{Shv}_{\operatorname{Nilp}}({\mathcal{U}})
.

From Theorem
4.5.4
, we obtain:

Corollary 4.6.3
.

Let
{\mathcal{F}}
be an object of
\operatorname{Shv}({\mathcal{U}})^{\operatorname{constr}}
such that the corresponding functor

{\mathsf{F}}:=\operatorname{C}^{\cdot}_{\blacktriangle}({\mathcal{U}},{\mathcal{F}}\overset{!}{\otimes}-),\quad\operatorname{Shv}({\mathcal{U}})\to\operatorname{Vect}

admits a right adjoint defined by a kernel. Then

{\mathcal{F}}\in\operatorname{Shv}_{\operatorname{Nilp}}({\mathcal{U}})
.

4.6.4.

Observe that by combining Corollary
4.6.3
and Theorems
4.2.2
and
B.8.8
,
we obtain:

Corollary 4.6.5
.

Let
{\mathcal{U}}\subset\operatorname{Bun}_{G}
be a universally
{\operatorname{Nilp}}
-cotruncative quasi-compact open substack.
Then for
{\mathcal{F}}\in\operatorname{Shv}({\mathcal{U}})^{\operatorname{constr}}
the following conditions are equivalent:

(i)
{\mathcal{F}}\in\operatorname{Shv}_{\operatorname{Nilp}}({\mathcal{U}})
;

(ii)
The functor
{\mathsf{F}}
admits
a
right adjoint as a functor defined by a kernel.

(iii) The map

\operatorname{C}^{\cdot}_{c}({\mathcal{U}},{\mathcal{F}}\overset{*}{\otimes}{\mathbb{D}}({\mathcal{F}}))\to\operatorname{C}^{\cdot}_{\blacktriangle}({\mathcal{U}},{\mathcal{F}}\overset{!}{\otimes}(\operatorname{Mir}^{-1}_{{\mathcal{U}}}\circ{\mathbb{D}}({\mathcal{F}})))

of (
4.1
) is an isomorphism.

4.6.6.

Let
{\mathcal{Y}}
be an arbitrary quasi-compact algebraic stack. Let
{\mathcal{F}}
be an object of
\operatorname{Shv}({\mathcal{Y}})^{\operatorname{constr}}
.

First, recall that according to Theorem
B.8.8
, conditions (ii) and (iii) in Corollary
4.6.5
are
equivalent.

The statement of Corollary
4.6.5
suggests the following question:

Question 4.6.7
.

Under what conditions on
{\mathcal{Y}}
does there exist a subset
{\mathcal{N}}\subset T^{*}({\mathcal{Y}})
so that
conditions (ii) or/and (iii) as in Corollary
4.6.5
are equivalent to the condition that

{\mathcal{F}}\in\operatorname{Shv}_{\mathcal{N}}({\mathcal{Y}})
?

4.6.8.
Example

Let
{\mathcal{Y}}=Y
be a proper smooth scheme. Then it is easy to see that the assertion of Corollary
4.6.5

holds for
{\mathcal{N}}=\{0\}
.

So, in some ways the subset
{\operatorname{Nilp}}\subset T^{*}(\operatorname{Bun}_{G})
plays the same role as the zero-section

\{0\}\subset T^{*}(Y)
for a proper smooth scheme
Y
.

The other extreme case is when
{\mathcal{Y}}
is an algebraic stack with finitely many isomorphism
classes of points. Then the assertion of Corollary
4.6.5
holds for
{\mathcal{N}}=T^{*}({\mathcal{Y}})
.

Note that in both of the above examples, the pair
({\mathcal{Y}},{\mathcal{N}})
is Serre; see Definition
5.5.4

for what this means.

4.7.
Proof of Theorem
4.5.2

4.7.1.

We will deduce Theorem
4.5.2
from the combination of the following two statements:

Proposition 4.7.2
.

Assume that
{\mathcal{F}}
is such that the functor (
4.17
) is defined and codefined by a kernel.
Then for any
{\mathcal{F}}^{\prime}\in\operatorname{Shv}(\operatorname{Bun}_{G})
and
V\in{\mathop{\operatorname{\rm Rep}}}({\check{G}})
,

(4.18)

(p_{2})_{\blacktriangle}({\mathsf{H}}(V,{\mathcal{F}})_{\operatorname{co}}\overset{!}{\otimes}p_{1}^{!}({\mathcal{F}}^{\prime}))\in\operatorname{Shv}_{\{0\}}(X)=\operatorname{QLisse}(X).

Proposition 4.7.3
.

Let
{\mathcal{Y}}
be an algebraic stack, and let
{\mathcal{F}}_{\mathcal{Y}}\in\operatorname{Shv}({\mathcal{Y}}\times X)
be an object, such that for every
geometric point
{\mathbf{i}}_{y}:\operatorname{Spec}(k^{\prime})\to{\mathcal{Y}}
, the object

({\mathbf{i}}_{y}\times\operatorname{id})^{!}({\mathcal{F}}_{\mathcal{Y}})\in\operatorname{Shv}(X^{\prime}),\quad X^{\prime}:=\operatorname{Spec}(k^{\prime})\underset{\operatorname{Spec}(k)}{\times}X

belongs to
\operatorname{QLisse}(X^{\prime})
. Then
{\mathcal{F}}_{\mathcal{Y}}
lies in the essential image of the functor

\operatorname{Shv}({\mathcal{Y}})\otimes\operatorname{QLisse}(X)\hookrightarrow\operatorname{Shv}({\mathcal{Y}}\times X).

4.7.4.

Let us assume these propositions temporarily, and prove Theorem
4.5.2
.

Let

{\mathcal{G}}:=\operatorname{Mir}_{\operatorname{Bun}_{G}}({\mathcal{F}})\in\operatorname{Shv}(\operatorname{Bun}_{G}).

By Theorem
1.3.7
for
{\mathcal{Z}}=\operatorname{pt}
(which is
[
AGKRRV
, Theorem 14.4.3]
),
it suffices to show that
for every
V\in{\mathop{\operatorname{\rm Rep}}}({\check{G}})
, the object
{\mathsf{H}}(V,{\mathcal{G}})
belongs to the essential image of

(4.19)

\operatorname{Shv}(\operatorname{Bun}_{G})\otimes\operatorname{QLisse}(X)\hookrightarrow\operatorname{Shv}(\operatorname{Bun}_{G}\times X).

By Proposition
4.7.3
, it suffices to show that for every
geometric point
{\mathbf{i}}_{y}:\operatorname{Spec}(k^{\prime})\to{\mathcal{Y}}
, the object

({\mathbf{i}}_{y}\times\operatorname{id})^{!}({\mathsf{H}}(V,{\mathcal{G}}))\in\operatorname{Shv}(X^{\prime}),\quad X^{\prime}:=\operatorname{Spec}(k^{\prime})\underset{\operatorname{Spec}(k)}{\times}X

belongs to
\operatorname{QLisse}(X^{\prime})
.

Changing base from
k
and
k^{\prime}
, we can assume that
y
is a closed point. We can rewrite

({\mathbf{i}}_{y}\times\operatorname{id})^{!}({\mathsf{H}}(V,{\mathcal{G}}))\simeq(p_{2})_{\blacktriangle}({\mathsf{H}}(V,{\mathcal{G}})\overset{!}{\otimes}p_{1}^{!}(({\mathbf{i}}_{y})_{*}({\mathsf{e}}))),

and further as

(p_{2})_{\blacktriangle}\left((\operatorname{Mir}_{\operatorname{Bun}_{G}}\boxtimes\operatorname{Id})({\mathsf{H}}(V,{\mathcal{F}})_{\operatorname{co}})\overset{!}{\otimes}p_{1}^{!}(({\mathbf{i}}_{y})_{*}({\mathsf{e}}))\right)\simeq(p_{2})_{\blacktriangle}\left({\mathsf{H}}(V,{\mathcal{F}})_{\operatorname{co}}\overset{!}{\otimes}p_{1}^{!}\left(\operatorname{Mir}_{\operatorname{Bun}_{G}}(({\mathbf{i}}_{y})_{*}({\mathsf{e}}))\right)\right).

The required assertion follows now from Proposition
4.7.2
, applied to

{\mathcal{F}}^{\prime}:=\operatorname{Mir}_{\operatorname{Bun}_{G}}(({\mathbf{i}}_{y})_{*}({\mathsf{e}})).

∎[Theorem
4.5.2
]

4.7.5.
Proof of Proposition
4.7.2

We rewrite

(p_{2})_{\blacktriangle}({\mathsf{H}}(V,{\mathcal{F}})_{\operatorname{co}}\overset{!}{\otimes}p_{1}^{!}({\mathcal{F}}^{\prime}))\simeq(p_{2})_{\blacktriangle}(p_{1}^{!}({\mathcal{F}})\overset{!}{\otimes}{\mathsf{H}}(V^{\tau},{\mathcal{F}}^{\prime})).

With no restriction of generality, we can assume that
V\in{\mathop{\operatorname{\rm Rep}}}({\check{G}})^{c}
and
{\mathcal{F}}^{\prime}\in\operatorname{Shv}(\operatorname{Bun}_{G})^{c}
.
In particular,
{\mathcal{F}}^{\prime}
is constructible, and so is
{\mathsf{H}}(V^{\tau},{\mathcal{F}}^{\prime})
.

Recall now that objects of the form

{\mathsf{H}}(V^{\prime},{\mathcal{F}}^{\prime})\in\operatorname{Shv}(\operatorname{Bun}_{G}\times X),\quad V^{\prime}\in{\mathop{\operatorname{\rm Rep}}}({\check{G}})^{c},\,{\mathcal{F}}^{\prime}\in\operatorname{Shv}(\operatorname{Bun}_{G})^{\operatorname{constr}}

are ULA with respect to the projection

p_{2}:\operatorname{Bun}_{G}\times X\to X.

Applying Corollary
B.10.10
, we obtain that

(p_{2})_{\blacktriangle}(p_{1}^{!}({\mathcal{F}})\overset{!}{\otimes}{\mathsf{H}}(V^{\tau},{\mathcal{F}}^{\prime}))\in\operatorname{QLisse}(X),

as required.

∎[Proof of Proposition
4.7.2
]

4.7.6.
Proof of Proposition
4.7.3

The functor (
4.19
) is fully faithful, and admits a continuous right adjoint, explicitly
described as follows:

Identify
\operatorname{QLisse}(X)
with its own dual via pairing
\operatorname{ev}_{X}

(here we use the fact that the pair
(X,\{0\})
is duality-adapted, see Sects.
A.5.4
and
A.5.5
).
Then the resulting functor

\operatorname{Shv}({\mathcal{Y}}\times X)\otimes\operatorname{QLisse}(X)\simeq\operatorname{Shv}({\mathcal{Y}}\times X)\otimes\operatorname{QLisse}(X)^{\vee}\to\operatorname{Shv}({\mathcal{Y}})

is given by

{\mathcal{F}}_{\mathcal{Y}}\otimes E_{X}\in\operatorname{Shv}({\mathcal{Y}}\times X)\otimes\operatorname{QLisse}(X)\mapsto(p_{1})_{*}({\mathcal{F}}_{\mathcal{Y}}\overset{!}{\otimes}p_{2}^{!}(E_{X}))\in\operatorname{Shv}({\mathcal{Y}}).

Note also that every object in the essential image of (
4.19
) satisfies the condition of
Proposition
4.7.3
. Hence, it suffices to show that if
{\mathcal{F}}_{\mathcal{Y}}

is such that it satisfies the condition of Proposition
4.7.3
and

(p_{1})_{*}({\mathcal{F}}_{\mathcal{Y}}\overset{!}{\otimes}p_{2}^{!}(E_{X}))=0,\,\,\forall E_{X}\in\operatorname{QLisse}(X),

then
{\mathcal{F}}_{\mathcal{Y}}=0
.

To check this, it suffices to show that

({\mathbf{i}}_{y}\times\operatorname{id})^{!}({\mathcal{F}}_{\mathcal{Y}})=0

for every geometric point
{\mathbf{i}}_{y}:\operatorname{Spec}(k^{\prime})\to{\mathcal{Y}}
. By the assumption on
{\mathcal{F}}_{\mathcal{Y}}
, it suffices to show that

\operatorname{C}^{\cdot}(X^{\prime},({\mathbf{i}}_{y}\times\operatorname{id})^{!}({\mathcal{F}}_{\mathcal{Y}})\overset{!}{\otimes}E_{X^{\prime}})=0

for every
E_{X^{\prime}}\in\operatorname{QLisse}(X^{\prime})
.

Note that the base change functor
\operatorname{QLisse}(X)\to\operatorname{QLisse}(X^{\prime})
is an equivalence. Hence, the object
E_{X^{\prime}}\in\operatorname{QLisse}(X^{\prime})

in the above formula is the base change of some
E_{X}\in\operatorname{QLisse}(X)
, and hence

\operatorname{C}^{\cdot}(X^{\prime},({\mathbf{i}}_{y}\times\operatorname{id})^{!}({\mathcal{F}}_{\mathcal{Y}})\overset{!}{\otimes}E_{X^{\prime}})\simeq{\mathbf{i}}_{y}^{!}\left((p_{1})_{*}({\mathcal{F}}_{\mathcal{Y}}\overset{!}{\otimes}p_{2}^{!}(E_{X}))\right),

while the latter vanishes, by assumption.

∎[Proof of Proposition
4.7.3
]

5.
Serre functor on
\operatorname{Shv}_{\operatorname{Nilp}}(\operatorname{Bun}_{G})

In this section we will recast the results of Sect.
3
in a different light, by relating
the miraculous functor on
\operatorname{Shv}_{\operatorname{Nilp}}(\operatorname{Bun}_{G})
to the
Serre functor
.

We will show that the category
\operatorname{Shv}_{\operatorname{Nilp}}(\operatorname{Bun}_{G})
is Serre (see Sect.
5.1.6
for what this means),
up to
the

\operatorname{Shv}_{\operatorname{Nilp}}(\operatorname{Bun}_{G})\rightsquigarrow\operatorname{Shv}_{\operatorname{Nilp}}(\operatorname{Bun}_{G})_{\operatorname{co}}
replacement.

5.1.
The Serre functor

In this subsection we recall the basic definitions pertaining to the Serre functor.

5.1.1.

Let
{\mathbf{C}}
be a dualizable category. Throughout this paper we denote by

\operatorname{u}_{\mathbf{C}}\in{\mathbf{C}}\otimes{\mathbf{C}}^{\vee}

the unit of the duality.

We denote by

\operatorname{ev}_{\mathbf{C}}:{\mathbf{C}}\otimes{\mathbf{C}}^{\vee}\to\operatorname{Vect}

the canonical pairing.

5.1.2.

From now on we will assume that
{\mathbf{C}}
is compactly generated. We will identify the dual
{\mathbf{C}}^{\vee}
of
{\mathbf{C}}
with

\operatorname{Ind}(({\mathbf{C}}^{c})^{\operatorname{op}})
. We will denote by
{\mathbb{D}}
the resulting contravariant equivalence

({\mathbf{C}}^{c})^{\operatorname{op}}\to({\mathbf{C}}^{\vee})^{c}.

Under this identification, for
{\mathbf{c}}\in{\mathbf{C}}^{c}
and
{\mathbf{c}}^{\prime}\in{\mathbf{C}}
,

\operatorname{ev}_{\mathbf{C}}({\mathbf{c}}^{\prime},{\mathbb{D}}({\mathbf{c}}))\simeq{\mathop{\operatorname{{\mathcal{H}}\it om}}}_{\mathbf{C}}({\mathbf{c}},{\mathbf{c}}^{\prime}).

5.1.3.

Recall that
{\mathbf{C}}
is said to be proper if the functor
\operatorname{ev}_{\mathbf{C}}

preserves compactness
4
4

4

The more familiar formulation of this is that
\operatorname{Hom}_{\mathbf{C}}(-,-)

between compact objects is compact
.
This is equivalent to the assumption that the functor

\operatorname{Vect}\overset{\operatorname{u}_{\mathbf{C}}}{\to}{\mathbf{C}}\otimes{\mathbf{C}}^{\vee}

admits a
left
adjoint, to be denoted

(5.1)

\operatorname{u}_{\mathbf{C}}^{L}:{\mathbf{C}}\otimes{\mathbf{C}}^{\vee}\to\operatorname{Vect}.

5.1.4.

Assume that
{\mathbf{C}}
is proper. The Serre endofunctor of
{\mathbf{C}}
, denoted
\operatorname{Se}_{\mathbf{C}}
,
is defined by the formula

{\mathop{\operatorname{{\mathcal{H}}\it om}}}_{\mathbf{C}}({\mathbf{c}}_{1},\operatorname{Se}_{\mathbf{C}}({\mathbf{c}})):={\mathop{\operatorname{{\mathcal{H}}\it om}}}_{\mathbf{C}}({\mathbf{c}},{\mathbf{c}}_{1})^{\vee},\quad{\mathbf{c}},{\mathbf{c}}_{1}\in{\mathbf{C}}^{c}.

The following results from the definitions:

Lemma 5.1.5
.

Let
{\mathbf{C}}
be proper.
Then the functor
\operatorname{u}_{\mathbf{C}}^{L}
identifies canonically with

{\mathbf{C}}\otimes{\mathbf{C}}^{\vee}\overset{\operatorname{Se}_{\mathbf{C}}\otimes\operatorname{Id}}{\longrightarrow}{\mathbf{C}}\otimes{\mathbf{C}}^{\vee}\overset{\operatorname{ev}_{\mathbf{C}}}{\to}\operatorname{Vect}.

5.1.6.

A DG category
{\mathbf{C}}
is said to be
Serre
if the functor
\operatorname{Se}_{\mathbf{C}}
is a self-equivalence.

From Lemma
5.1.5
, we obtain:

Corollary 5.1.7
.

The following conditions are equivalent:

(i)
{\mathbf{C}}
is Serre;

(ii) The functor
\operatorname{u}^{L}_{\mathbf{C}}
is the counit of
another
duality
between
{\mathbf{C}}
and
{\mathbf{C}}^{\vee}
.

Corollary 5.1.8
.

Let
{\mathbf{C}}
be proper (and hence so is
{\mathbf{C}}^{\vee}
). Then
{\mathbf{C}}
is Serre if and only if
{\mathbf{C}}^{\vee}
is Serre.

5.1.9.

Let
{\mathbf{C}}
be Serre. Thus, we obtain a
new
identification between
the dual of
{\mathbf{C}}
and
{\mathbf{C}}^{\vee}
. In particular, we obtain a
new
contravariant equivalence

({\mathbf{C}}^{c})^{\operatorname{op}}\to({\mathbf{C}}^{\vee})^{c},

which we will denote by
{\mathbb{D}}^{\operatorname{new}}
.

By definition,

(5.2)

{\mathbb{D}}^{\operatorname{new}}\simeq{\mathbb{D}}\circ\operatorname{Se}_{\mathbf{C}}.

5.1.10.
Example

Let
{\mathbf{C}}={\operatorname{QCoh}}(Y)
, where
Y
is a smooth and proper scheme. We identify

{\operatorname{QCoh}}(Y)\simeq{\operatorname{QCoh}}(Y)^{\vee}

via the
Serre pairing
. I.e., the unit is given by

(\Delta_{Y})_{*}(\omega_{Y})\in{\operatorname{QCoh}}(Y\times Y)\simeq{\operatorname{QCoh}}(Y)\otimes{\operatorname{QCoh}}(Y)

(here
\omega_{Y}
is the dualizing sheaf) and the counit by

{\operatorname{QCoh}}(Y)\otimes{\operatorname{QCoh}}(Y)\simeq{\operatorname{QCoh}}(Y\times Y)\overset{\Delta_{Y}^{!}}{\longrightarrow}{\operatorname{QCoh}}(Y)\overset{\Gamma(Y,-)}{\to}\operatorname{Vect}.

Then the functor

{\mathbb{D}}:(\operatorname{Perf}(Y))^{\operatorname{op}}\to\operatorname{Perf}(Y)

is

{\mathcal{F}}\mapsto{\mathcal{F}}^{\vee}\otimes\omega_{Y},

where

{\mathcal{F}}^{\vee}:=\underline{\operatorname{Hom}}({\mathcal{F}},{\mathcal{O}}_{Y}).

The Serre functor on
{\operatorname{QCoh}}(Y)
is given by

{\mathcal{F}}\mapsto{\mathcal{F}}\otimes\omega_{Y},

in particular
{\operatorname{QCoh}}(Y)
is Serre.

The functor
{\mathbb{D}}^{\operatorname{new}}
is given by

{\mathcal{F}}\mapsto{\mathcal{F}}^{\vee},

and the
new
self-duality on
{\operatorname{QCoh}}(Y)
has as counit

{\operatorname{QCoh}}(Y)\otimes{\operatorname{QCoh}}(Y)\simeq{\operatorname{QCoh}}(Y\times Y)\overset{\Delta_{Y}^{*}}{\longrightarrow}{\operatorname{QCoh}}(Y)\overset{\Gamma(Y,-)}{\to}\operatorname{Vect},

and as unit

(\Delta_{Y})_{*}({\mathcal{O}}_{Y})\in{\operatorname{QCoh}}(Y\times Y)\simeq{\operatorname{QCoh}}(Y)\otimes{\operatorname{QCoh}}(Y).

5.2.
Serre vs pseudo-identity

In this subsection we reproduce some of the results of
[
GaYo
]
, which relate the Serre
functor to the
pseudo-identity
functor.

5.2.1.

Let
{\mathbf{C}}
be a compactly generated category.

Applying the right Kan extension to
{\mathbb{D}}
along
({\mathbf{C}}^{c})^{\operatorname{op}}\hookrightarrow{\mathbf{C}}^{\operatorname{op}}
,
and extend
{\mathbb{D}}
to a
discontinuous
functor

{\mathbf{C}}^{\operatorname{op}}\to{\mathbf{C}}^{\vee}.

Explicitly, for

{\mathbf{c}}=\underset{\alpha}{\operatorname{colim}}\,{\mathbf{c}}_{\alpha_{i}},

we have

{\mathbb{D}}({\mathbf{c}})=\underset{\alpha}{\operatorname{lim}}\,{\mathbb{D}}({\mathbf{c}}_{\alpha_{i}}).

5.2.2.

We identify

({\mathbf{C}}\otimes{\mathbf{C}}^{\vee})^{\vee}\simeq{\mathbf{C}}^{\vee}\otimes{\mathbf{C}},

and define the object

(5.3)

\operatorname{ps-u}_{\mathbf{C}}\in{\mathbf{C}}\otimes{\mathbf{C}}^{\vee}

to be
{\mathbb{D}}(\operatorname{u}_{\mathbf{C}})
.

5.2.3.

We identify

(5.4)

{\mathbf{C}}\otimes{\mathbf{C}}^{\vee}\simeq\operatorname{Funct}_{\operatorname{cont}}({\mathbf{C}},{\mathbf{C}}).

Under the identification the object
\operatorname{u}_{\mathbf{C}}\in{\mathbf{C}}\otimes{\mathbf{C}}^{\vee}

corresponds to
\operatorname{Id}_{\mathbf{C}}\in\operatorname{Funct}_{\operatorname{cont}}({\mathbf{C}},{\mathbf{C}})
.

We define the pseudo-identity endofunctor of
{\mathbf{C}}

\operatorname{Ps-Id}_{\mathbf{C}}\in\operatorname{Funct}_{\operatorname{cont}}({\mathbf{C}},{\mathbf{C}})

as the object corresponding under (
5.4
) to

\operatorname{ps-u}_{\mathbf{C}}\in{\mathbf{C}}\otimes{\mathbf{C}}^{\vee}
.

Note that we have:

(\operatorname{Ps-Id}_{\mathbf{C}})^{\vee}\simeq\operatorname{Ps-Id}_{{\mathbf{C}}^{\vee}}.

5.2.4.

Let
{\mathbf{C}}
be Serre. In particular, by Corollary
5.1.7
, the functor

(5.5)

{\mathbf{C}}\otimes{\mathbf{C}}^{\vee}\overset{\operatorname{u}^{L}_{\mathbf{C}}}{\to}\operatorname{Vect}

is the counit of a duality.

We claim:

Proposition 5.2.5
.

The unit of the duality (
5.5
) is given by
\operatorname{ps-u}_{\mathbf{C}}
.

Proof.

We have to show that there exists a canonical isomorphism

{\mathop{\operatorname{{\mathcal{H}}\it om}}}_{{\mathbf{C}}\otimes{\mathbf{C}}^{\vee}}({\mathbf{c}}_{1}\otimes{\mathbb{D}}(\operatorname{Se}_{\mathbf{C}}({\mathbf{c}}_{2})),\operatorname{ps-u}_{\mathbf{C}})\simeq{\mathop{\operatorname{{\mathcal{H}}\it om}}}_{\mathbf{C}}({\mathbf{c}}_{1},{\mathbf{c}}_{2}),\quad{\mathbf{c}}_{1},{\mathbf{c}}_{2}\in{\mathbf{C}}^{c}.

The left-hand side identifies, by definition, with

{\mathop{\operatorname{{\mathcal{H}}\it om}}}_{{\mathbf{C}}\otimes{\mathbf{C}}^{\vee}}(\operatorname{u}_{\mathbf{C}},\operatorname{Se}_{\mathbf{C}}({\mathbf{c}}_{2})\otimes{\mathbb{D}}({\mathbf{c}}_{1})),

which is the same as the space of natural transformations

{\mathbf{c}}\to{\mathop{\operatorname{{\mathcal{H}}\it om}}}_{{\mathbf{C}}}({\mathbf{c}}_{1},{\mathbf{c}})\otimes\operatorname{Se}_{\mathbf{C}}({\mathbf{c}}_{2}),\quad{\mathbf{c}}\in{\mathbf{C}}^{c}

We rewrite the latter as the space of natural transformations

{\mathop{\operatorname{{\mathcal{H}}\it om}}}_{{\mathbf{C}}}({\mathbf{c}}_{1},{\mathbf{c}})^{\vee}\to{\mathop{\operatorname{{\mathcal{H}}\it om}}}_{{\mathbf{C}}}({\mathbf{c}},\operatorname{Se}_{\mathbf{C}}({\mathbf{c}}_{2})),\quad{\mathbf{c}}\in{\mathbf{C}}^{c},

i.e.,

{\mathop{\operatorname{{\mathcal{H}}\it om}}}_{{\mathbf{C}}}({\mathbf{c}}_{1},{\mathbf{c}})^{\vee}\to{\mathop{\operatorname{{\mathcal{H}}\it om}}}_{{\mathbf{C}}}({\mathbf{c}}_{2},{\mathbf{c}})^{\vee},\quad{\mathbf{c}}\in{\mathbf{C}}^{c},

which is the same as

{\mathop{\operatorname{{\mathcal{H}}\it om}}}_{{\mathbf{C}}}({\mathbf{c}}_{2},{\mathbf{c}})\to{\mathop{\operatorname{{\mathcal{H}}\it om}}}_{{\mathbf{C}}}({\mathbf{c}}_{1},{\mathbf{c}}),\quad{\mathbf{c}}\in{\mathbf{C}}^{c}.

By Yoneda, the latter is the same as
{\mathop{\operatorname{{\mathcal{H}}\it om}}}_{\mathbf{C}}({\mathbf{c}}_{1},{\mathbf{c}}_{2})
, as required.

∎

5.2.6.

To summarize, if
{\mathbf{C}}
is Serre, we have the commutative diagrams

(5.6)

\begin{CD}{\mathbf{C}}\otimes{\mathbf{C}}^{\vee}@>{\operatorname{ev}_{\mathbf{C}}}>{}>\operatorname{Vect}\\
@A{\operatorname{Se}_{\mathbf{C}}\otimes\operatorname{Id}}A{}A@A{}A{\operatorname{Id}}A\\
{\mathbf{C}}\otimes{\mathbf{C}}^{\vee}@>{\operatorname{u}^{L}_{\mathbf{C}}}>{}>\operatorname{Vect}\end{CD}

and

(5.7)

\begin{CD}\operatorname{Vect}@>{\operatorname{u}_{\mathbf{C}}}>{}>{\mathbf{C}}\otimes{\mathbf{C}}^{\vee}\\
@A{\operatorname{Id}}A{}A@A{}A{\operatorname{Se}_{\mathbf{C}}\otimes\operatorname{Id}}A\\
\operatorname{Vect}@>{\operatorname{ps-u}_{\mathbf{C}}}>{}>{\mathbf{C}}\otimes{\mathbf{C}}^{\vee},\end{CD}

where the horizontal arrows are the counits and units of the corresponding dualities,
respectively.

In particular, we recover the following result of
[
GaYo
, Proposition 1.5.2]
:

Corollary 5.2.7
.

Assume that
{\mathbf{C}}
is Serre. Then the functor
\operatorname{Ps-Id}_{\mathbf{C}}
is the inverse of
\operatorname{Se}_{\mathbf{C}}
.

5.2.8.

Thus, if
{\mathbf{C}}
is Serre, along with diagram (
5.6
)
we also obtain the commutative diagram

(5.8)

\begin{CD}{\mathbf{C}}\otimes{\mathbf{C}}^{\vee}@>{\operatorname{u}^{L}_{\mathbf{C}}}>{}>\operatorname{Vect}\\
@A{\operatorname{Ps-Id}_{\mathbf{C}}\otimes\operatorname{Id}}A{}A@A{}A{\operatorname{Id}}A\\
{\mathbf{C}}\otimes{\mathbf{C}}^{\vee}@>{\operatorname{ev}_{\mathbf{C}}}>{}>\operatorname{Vect},\end{CD}

5.2.9.

Recall the functor

{\mathbb{D}}^{\operatorname{new}}:({\mathbf{C}}^{c})^{\operatorname{op}}\to({\mathbf{C}}^{\vee})^{c},

see (
5.2
). We have

(5.9)

{\mathbb{D}}\simeq{\mathbb{D}}^{\operatorname{new}}\circ\operatorname{Ps-Id}_{\mathbf{C}}.

5.3.
Duality-adapted pairs, complements

In this subsection we collect some background material on the notion of

duality-adapted pair
, see Sect.
A.5.4
.

5.3.1.

Let
{\mathcal{Y}}
be a quasi-compact algebraic stack (see Sect.
0.5.1
for our assumptions). Let
{\mathcal{N}}
be a
closed conical subset in
T^{*}({\mathcal{Y}})
.
Consider the corresponding full subcategory

\operatorname{Shv}_{\mathcal{N}}({\mathcal{Y}})\overset{\iota_{\mathcal{Y}}}{\hookrightarrow}\operatorname{Shv}({\mathcal{Y}}).

We will assume that the pair
({\mathcal{Y}},{\mathcal{N}})
is
duality-adapted
, see Sect.
A.5.4
,
i.e., the functor

\operatorname{Shv}_{\mathcal{N}}({\mathcal{Y}})\otimes\operatorname{Shv}_{\mathcal{N}}({\mathcal{Y}})\to\operatorname{Shv}({\mathcal{Y}})\otimes\operatorname{Shv}({\mathcal{Y}})\overset{\operatorname{ev}_{\mathcal{Y}}}{\longrightarrow}\operatorname{Vect}

is the counit of a self-duality

(5.10)

\operatorname{Shv}_{\mathcal{N}}({\mathcal{Y}})^{\vee}\simeq\operatorname{Shv}_{\mathcal{N}}({\mathcal{Y}})

5.3.2.

Let

{\mathsf{P}}_{{\mathcal{Y}},{\mathcal{N}}}:\operatorname{Shv}({\mathcal{Y}})\to\operatorname{Shv}_{\mathcal{N}}({\mathcal{Y}})

denote the functor dual to the embedding

\iota_{\mathcal{Y}}:\operatorname{Shv}_{\mathcal{N}}({\mathcal{Y}})\hookrightarrow\operatorname{Shv}({\mathcal{Y}})

with respect the self-dualities

\operatorname{Shv}({\mathcal{Y}})^{\vee}\simeq\operatorname{Shv}({\mathcal{Y}})\text{ and }\operatorname{Shv}_{\mathcal{N}}({\mathcal{Y}})^{\vee}\simeq\operatorname{Shv}_{\mathcal{N}}({\mathcal{Y}})

of (
A.12
) and (
5.10
), respectively.

We will sometimes view
{\mathsf{P}}_{{\mathcal{Y}},{\mathcal{N}}}
as an endofunctor of
\operatorname{Shv}({\mathcal{Y}})
, by composing it
with the embedding
\iota_{\mathcal{Y}}
.

5.3.3.

Let

\operatorname{u}_{{\mathcal{Y}},{\mathcal{N}}}\in\operatorname{Shv}_{\mathcal{N}}({\mathcal{Y}})\otimes\operatorname{Shv}_{\mathcal{N}}({\mathcal{Y}})

be the unit of the self-duality (
5.10
) on
\operatorname{Shv}_{\mathcal{N}}({\mathcal{Y}})
.

We will sometimes view
\operatorname{u}_{{\mathcal{Y}},{\mathcal{N}}}
as an object of
\operatorname{Shv}({\mathcal{Y}}\times{\mathcal{Y}})
via
the embedding

\operatorname{Shv}_{\mathcal{N}}({\mathcal{Y}})\otimes\operatorname{Shv}_{\mathcal{N}}({\mathcal{Y}})\overset{\iota_{\mathcal{Y}}\otimes\iota_{\mathcal{Y}}}{\longrightarrow}\operatorname{Shv}({\mathcal{Y}})\otimes\operatorname{Shv}({\mathcal{Y}})\overset{\boxtimes}{\longrightarrow}\operatorname{Shv}({\mathcal{Y}}\times{\mathcal{Y}}).

Remark 5.3.4
.

Note that when
{\mathcal{N}}=T^{*}({\mathcal{Y}})
, we have

\operatorname{u}_{{\mathcal{Y}},{\mathcal{N}}}=\operatorname{u}_{\operatorname{Shv}({\mathcal{Y}})}.

5.3.5.

It follows from the definitions that
{\mathsf{P}}_{{\mathcal{Y}},{\mathcal{N}}}
, viewed as an endofunctor of
\operatorname{Shv}({\mathcal{Y}})
,
identifies with

{\mathcal{F}}\mapsto(p_{2})_{\blacktriangle}(p_{1}^{!}({\mathcal{F}})\overset{!}{\otimes}\operatorname{u}_{{\mathcal{Y}},{\mathcal{N}}}).

Thus, we extend
{\mathsf{P}}_{{\mathcal{Y}},{\mathcal{N}}}
to a functor defined by a kernel in the sense of
Sect.
B.1
(with the kernel being
\operatorname{u}_{{\mathcal{Y}},{\mathcal{N}}}
, viewed as an object of
\operatorname{Shv}({\mathcal{Y}}\times{\mathcal{Y}})
).
In particular, we obtain the endofunctor

\operatorname{Id}_{\mathcal{Z}}\boxtimes{\mathsf{P}}_{{\mathcal{Y}},{\mathcal{N}}}

of
\operatorname{Shv}({\mathcal{Z}}\times{\mathcal{Y}})
for any algebraic stack
{\mathcal{Z}}
, so that for
{\mathcal{Z}}=\operatorname{pt}
we recover
the original
{\mathsf{P}}_{{\mathcal{Y}},{\mathcal{N}}}
.

Proposition 5.3.6
.

The endofunctor
\operatorname{Id}_{\mathcal{Z}}\boxtimes{\mathsf{P}}_{{\mathcal{Y}},{\mathcal{N}}}

is the projector onto the full subcategory

\operatorname{Shv}({\mathcal{Z}})\otimes\operatorname{Shv}_{\mathcal{N}}({\mathcal{Y}})\hookrightarrow\operatorname{Shv}({\mathcal{Z}})\otimes\operatorname{Shv}({\mathcal{Y}})\overset{\boxtimes}{\hookrightarrow}\operatorname{Shv}({\mathcal{Z}}\times{\mathcal{Y}}).

Proof.

The fact that
\operatorname{u}_{{\mathcal{Y}},{\mathcal{N}}}
belongs to

\operatorname{Shv}_{\mathcal{N}}({\mathcal{Y}})\otimes\operatorname{Shv}_{\mathcal{N}}({\mathcal{Y}})\subset\operatorname{Shv}({\mathcal{Y}})\otimes\operatorname{Shv}_{\mathcal{N}}({\mathcal{Y}})

implies that the essential image of
\operatorname{Id}_{\mathcal{Z}}\boxtimes{\mathsf{P}}_{{\mathcal{Y}},{\mathcal{N}}}
belongs to

\operatorname{Shv}({\mathcal{Z}})\otimes\operatorname{Shv}_{\mathcal{N}}({\mathcal{Y}})\subset\operatorname{Shv}({\mathcal{Z}}\times{\mathcal{Y}}).

For any functor
{\mathsf{Q}}
defined by a kernel
{\mathcal{Q}}\in\operatorname{Shv}({\mathcal{Y}}_{1}\times{\mathcal{Y}}_{2})
,
the restriction of
\operatorname{Id}_{\mathcal{Z}}\boxtimes{\mathsf{Q}}
to

\operatorname{Shv}({\mathcal{Z}})\otimes\operatorname{Shv}({\mathcal{Y}}_{1})\subset\operatorname{Shv}({\mathcal{Z}}\times{\mathcal{Y}}_{2})

identifies with

\operatorname{Id}_{\operatorname{Shv}({\mathcal{Z}})}\otimes{\mathsf{Q}},

viewed as a functor

\operatorname{Shv}({\mathcal{Z}})\otimes\operatorname{Shv}({\mathcal{Y}}_{1})\to\operatorname{Shv}({\mathcal{Z}})\otimes\operatorname{Shv}({\mathcal{Y}}_{2})\subset\operatorname{Shv}({\mathcal{Z}}\times{\mathcal{Y}}_{2}),

see (
B.3
).

This implies that
\operatorname{Id}_{\mathcal{Z}}\boxtimes{\mathsf{P}}_{{\mathcal{Y}},{\mathcal{N}}}
acts as identity when restricted
to
\operatorname{Shv}({\mathcal{Z}})\otimes\operatorname{Shv}_{\mathcal{N}}({\mathcal{Y}})
.

∎

Corollary 5.3.7
.

The endofunctor
{\mathsf{P}}_{{\mathcal{Y}},{\mathcal{N}}}\boxtimes{\mathsf{P}}_{{\mathcal{Y}},{\mathcal{N}}}
of
\operatorname{Shv}({\mathcal{Y}}\times{\mathcal{Y}})

is a projector onto

\operatorname{Shv}_{\mathcal{N}}({\mathcal{Y}})\otimes\operatorname{Shv}_{\mathcal{N}}({\mathcal{Y}})\subset\operatorname{Shv}({\mathcal{Y}}\times{\mathcal{Y}}).

Proof.

Same as that of Corollary
1.6.5
.
∎

5.3.8.

We now claim:

Lemma 5.3.9
.

The object
\operatorname{u}_{{\mathcal{Y}},{\mathcal{N}}}
identifies with each of the following:

({\mathsf{P}}_{{\mathcal{Y}},{\mathcal{N}}}\boxtimes\operatorname{Id}_{\mathcal{Y}})(\operatorname{u}_{\mathcal{Y}}),\,\,({\mathsf{P}}_{{\mathcal{Y}},{\mathcal{N}}}\boxtimes{\mathsf{P}}_{{\mathcal{Y}},{\mathcal{N}}})(\operatorname{u}_{\mathcal{Y}}),\,\,(\operatorname{Id}_{\mathcal{Y}}\boxtimes{\mathsf{P}}_{{\mathcal{Y}},{\mathcal{N}}})(\operatorname{u}_{\mathcal{Y}}).

Proof.

The isomorphism

\operatorname{u}_{{\mathcal{Y}},{\mathcal{N}}}\simeq(\operatorname{Id}_{\mathcal{Y}}\boxtimes{\mathsf{P}}_{{\mathcal{Y}},{\mathcal{N}}})(\operatorname{u}_{\mathcal{Y}})

is tautological, and the isomorphism with
({\mathsf{P}}_{{\mathcal{Y}},{\mathcal{N}}}\boxtimes\operatorname{Id}_{\mathcal{Y}})(\operatorname{u}_{\mathcal{Y}})
follows by symmetry.

We have

({\mathsf{P}}_{{\mathcal{Y}},{\mathcal{N}}}\boxtimes{\mathsf{P}}_{{\mathcal{Y}},{\mathcal{N}}})(\operatorname{u}_{\mathcal{Y}})\simeq({\mathsf{P}}_{{\mathcal{Y}},{\mathcal{N}}}\boxtimes\operatorname{Id}_{\mathcal{Y}})\circ(\operatorname{Id}_{\mathcal{Y}}\boxtimes{\mathsf{P}}_{{\mathcal{Y}},{\mathcal{N}}})(\operatorname{u}_{\mathcal{Y}})\simeq({\mathsf{P}}_{{\mathcal{Y}},{\mathcal{N}}}\boxtimes\operatorname{Id}_{\mathcal{Y}})(\operatorname{u}_{{\mathcal{Y}},{\mathcal{N}}}),

and the latter is isomorphic to
\operatorname{u}_{{\mathcal{Y}},{\mathcal{N}}}
, since
{\mathsf{P}}_{{\mathcal{Y}},{\mathcal{N}}}\boxtimes\operatorname{Id}_{\mathcal{Y}}
acts
as identity on

\operatorname{Shv}_{\mathcal{N}}({\mathcal{Y}})\otimes\operatorname{Shv}({\mathcal{Y}})\subset\operatorname{Shv}({\mathcal{Y}}\times{\mathcal{Y}}).

∎

5.4.
Constraccessible pairs, complements

In this subsection we collect some background material on the notion of

constraccessible pair
, see Sect.
A.5.2
.

In this subsection we assume that
{\mathcal{Y}}
is quasi-compact.

5.4.1.

Assume now that the pair
({\mathcal{Y}},{\mathcal{N}})
is
constraccessible
, see Sect.
A.5.2
.
In particular, it is duality-adapted, and the equivalence

(\operatorname{Shv}_{\mathcal{N}}({\mathcal{Y}})^{c})^{\operatorname{op}}\to\operatorname{Shv}_{\mathcal{N}}({\mathcal{Y}})^{c},

corresponding to (
5.10
), is obtained by restriction from the Verdier duality functor

{\mathbb{D}}^{\operatorname{Verdier}}:(\operatorname{Shv}({\mathcal{Y}})^{c})^{\operatorname{op}}\to\operatorname{Shv}({\mathcal{Y}})^{c}.

5.4.2.

We claim:

Lemma 5.4.3
.

For a quasi-compact algebraic stack
{\mathcal{Z}}
, the functor
\operatorname{Id}_{\mathcal{Z}}\boxtimes{\mathsf{P}}_{{\mathcal{Y}},{\mathcal{N}}}
, viewed as a functor

\operatorname{Shv}({\mathcal{Z}}\times{\mathcal{Y}})\to\operatorname{Shv}({\mathcal{Z}})\otimes\operatorname{Shv}_{\mathcal{N}}({\mathcal{Y}}),

is the right adjoint of

\operatorname{Shv}({\mathcal{Z}})\otimes\operatorname{Shv}_{\mathcal{N}}({\mathcal{Y}})\overset{\operatorname{Id}\otimes\iota_{\mathcal{Y}}}{\longrightarrow}\operatorname{Shv}({\mathcal{Z}})\otimes\operatorname{Shv}({\mathcal{Y}})\hookrightarrow\operatorname{Shv}({\mathcal{Z}}\times{\mathcal{Y}}).

Proof.

Follows from Lemma
2.4.3
, since the constraccessibility assumption
implies that

(\boxtimes\circ(\operatorname{Id}\otimes\iota_{\mathcal{Y}}))^{\operatorname{fake-op}}\simeq(\boxtimes\circ(\operatorname{Id}\otimes\iota_{\mathcal{Y}})).

∎

Corollary 5.4.4
.

The endofunctor
{\mathsf{P}}_{{\mathcal{Y}},{\mathcal{N}}}\boxtimes{\mathsf{P}}_{{\mathcal{Y}},{\mathcal{N}}}
of
\operatorname{Shv}({\mathcal{Y}}\times{\mathcal{Y}})
,
viewed as a functor

\operatorname{Shv}({\mathcal{Y}}\times{\mathcal{Y}})\to\operatorname{Shv}_{\mathcal{N}}({\mathcal{Y}})\otimes\operatorname{Shv}_{\mathcal{N}}({\mathcal{Y}})

identifies with the right adjoint of the tautological embedding.

Proof.

Obtained by applying Lemma
5.4.3
to

\operatorname{Id}_{\mathcal{Y}}\boxtimes{\mathsf{P}}_{{\mathcal{Y}},{\mathcal{N}}}:\operatorname{Shv}({\mathcal{Y}}\times{\mathcal{Y}})\to\operatorname{Shv}({\mathcal{Y}})\otimes\operatorname{Shv}_{\mathcal{N}}({\mathcal{Y}})

and

{\mathsf{P}}_{{\mathcal{Y}},{\mathcal{N}}}\otimes\operatorname{Id}:\operatorname{Shv}({\mathcal{Y}})\otimes\operatorname{Shv}_{\mathcal{N}}({\mathcal{Y}})\to\operatorname{Shv}_{\mathcal{N}}({\mathcal{Y}})\otimes\operatorname{Shv}_{\mathcal{N}}({\mathcal{Y}}).

∎

Corollary 5.4.5
.

The object
\operatorname{u}_{{\mathcal{Y}},{\mathcal{N}}}
identifies with the value on
\operatorname{u}_{\mathcal{Y}}
of the right adjoint to the embedding

(5.11)

\operatorname{Shv}_{\mathcal{N}}({\mathcal{Y}})\otimes\operatorname{Shv}_{\mathcal{N}}({\mathcal{Y}})\hookrightarrow\operatorname{Shv}({\mathcal{Y}}\times{\mathcal{Y}}).

5.5.
Serre pairs

In this subsection we introduce the notion of what it means for a pair
({\mathcal{Y}},{\mathcal{N}})
to be
Serre
.

5.5.1.

Let
{\mathcal{Y}}
be an algebraic stack (for now we are
not
assuming that
{\mathcal{Y}}
is quasi-compact).
Let
{\mathcal{N}}
be a subset in
T^{*}({\mathcal{Y}})
.

Recall the notation

\operatorname{ps-u}_{\mathcal{Y}}:=\Delta_{!}(\underline{{\mathsf{e}}}_{\mathcal{Y}})\in\operatorname{Shv}({\mathcal{Y}}\times{\mathcal{Y}}).

Let

\operatorname{ps-u}_{{\mathcal{Y}},{\mathcal{N}}}\in\operatorname{Shv}_{\mathcal{N}}({\mathcal{Y}})\otimes\operatorname{Shv}_{\mathcal{N}}({\mathcal{Y}})

be the value on
\operatorname{ps-u}_{\mathcal{Y}}
of the right adjoint of the embedding

\operatorname{Shv}_{\mathcal{N}}({\mathcal{Y}})\otimes\operatorname{Shv}_{\mathcal{N}}({\mathcal{Y}})\hookrightarrow\operatorname{Shv}({\mathcal{Y}})\otimes\operatorname{Shv}({\mathcal{Y}})\overset{\boxtimes}{\hookrightarrow}\operatorname{Shv}({\mathcal{Y}}\times{\mathcal{Y}}).

The counit of the adjunction defines a map

(5.12)

\operatorname{ps-u}_{{\mathcal{Y}},{\mathcal{N}}}\to\operatorname{ps-u}_{\mathcal{Y}}.

5.5.2.

Recall that

\operatorname{ev}^{l}_{\mathcal{Y}}:\operatorname{Shv}({\mathcal{Y}})\otimes\operatorname{Shv}({\mathcal{Y}})\to\operatorname{Vect}

denoted the pairing

{\mathcal{F}}_{1},{\mathcal{F}}_{2}\mapsto\operatorname{C}^{\cdot}_{c}({\mathcal{Y}},{\mathcal{F}}_{1}\overset{*}{\otimes}{\mathcal{F}}_{2}).

By a slight abuse of notation, we will denote by the same symbol
\operatorname{ev}^{l}_{\mathcal{Y}}

its restriction along

\operatorname{Shv}_{\mathcal{N}}({\mathcal{Y}})\otimes\operatorname{Shv}_{\mathcal{N}}({\mathcal{Y}})\to\operatorname{Shv}({\mathcal{Y}})\otimes\operatorname{Shv}({\mathcal{Y}}).

5.5.3.

The map (
5.12
) gives rise to a natural transformation from the functor

(5.13)

\operatorname{Shv}({\mathcal{Y}})\overset{\operatorname{Id}\boxtimes\operatorname{ps-u}_{{\mathcal{Y}},{\mathcal{N}}}}{\longrightarrow}\operatorname{Shv}({\mathcal{Y}}\times{\mathcal{Y}}\times{\mathcal{Y}})\overset{\operatorname{ev}_{\mathcal{Y}}^{l}\boxtimes\operatorname{Id}_{\mathcal{Y}}}{\longrightarrow}\operatorname{Shv}({\mathcal{Y}})

to the composition

\operatorname{Shv}({\mathcal{Y}})\overset{\operatorname{Id}\boxtimes\operatorname{ps-u}_{{\mathcal{Y}}}}{\longrightarrow}\operatorname{Shv}({\mathcal{Y}}\times{\mathcal{Y}}\times{\mathcal{Y}})\overset{\operatorname{ev}_{\mathcal{Y}}^{l}\boxtimes\operatorname{Id}_{\mathcal{Y}}}{\longrightarrow}\operatorname{Shv}({\mathcal{Y}}),

which is the identity functor.

Restricting to
\operatorname{Shv}_{\mathcal{N}}({\mathcal{Y}})
, we obtain a natural transformation

(5.14)

(\operatorname{ev}_{\mathcal{Y}}^{l}\otimes\operatorname{Id})\circ(\operatorname{Id}_{\mathcal{Y}}\boxtimes\operatorname{ps-u}_{{\mathcal{Y}},{\mathcal{N}}})\to\operatorname{Id}

Definition 5.5.4
.

We shall say that the pair
({\mathcal{Y}},{\mathcal{N}})
is
Serre
if the natural transformation (
5.14
)
becomes an isomorphism when restricted to
\operatorname{Shv}_{\mathcal{N}}({\mathcal{Y}})
.

5.5.5.

Thus, by definition, if
({\mathcal{Y}},{\mathcal{N}})
is Serre, then the object

\operatorname{ps-u}_{{\mathcal{Y}},{\mathcal{N}}}\in\operatorname{Shv}_{\mathcal{N}}({\mathcal{Y}})\otimes\operatorname{Shv}_{\mathcal{N}}({\mathcal{Y}})

and the pairing

\operatorname{ev}^{l}_{\mathcal{Y}}:\operatorname{Shv}_{\mathcal{N}}({\mathcal{Y}})\otimes\operatorname{Shv}_{\mathcal{N}}({\mathcal{Y}})\to\operatorname{Vect}

define the unit and the counit for a self-duality on
\operatorname{Shv}_{\mathcal{N}}({\mathcal{Y}})
.

5.5.6.
Examples

Let
{\mathcal{Y}}
be a smooth proper scheme. Assume that the pair
({\mathcal{Y}},\{0\})

is constraccessible. Then the pair
({\mathcal{Y}},\{0\})
is Serre.

Let us now consider another extreme: let
{\mathcal{Y}}
be an algebraic stack with finitely many isomorphism
classes of points (e.g., we can take
{\mathcal{Y}}
to be
N\backslash G/B
or an open substack
\operatorname{Bun}_{G}

for a curve of genus
0
). We claim that in case the pair
({\mathcal{Y}},T^{*}({\mathcal{Y}}))
is Serre. Indeed, this follows
from the fact that for
{\mathcal{Y}}
of the above form the functor

\operatorname{Shv}({\mathcal{Y}})\otimes\operatorname{Shv}({\mathcal{Y}})\to\operatorname{Shv}({\mathcal{Y}}\times{\mathcal{Y}})

is an equivalence, so

\operatorname{ps-u}_{{\mathcal{Y}},{\mathcal{N}}}\simeq\operatorname{ps-u}_{\mathcal{Y}}.

5.5.7.

Recall that the category
\operatorname{Shv}({\mathcal{Y}})
is compactly generated by objects of the form
g_{!}({\mathcal{F}})
, for

g:S\to{\mathcal{Y}},

where
S
is an affine scheme,
g
is smooth and
{\mathcal{F}}\in\operatorname{Shv}(Z)^{c}=\operatorname{Shv}(Z)^{\operatorname{constr}}

(see Sect.
A.1.6
). It is easy to see that the
{\mathop{\operatorname{{\mathcal{H}}\it om}}}
space between objects of the above form
is compact. This implies that the category
\operatorname{Shv}({\mathcal{Y}})
is proper.

Assume that the pair

({\mathcal{Y}},{\mathcal{N}})
is constraccessible, i.e.,
\operatorname{Shv}_{\mathcal{N}}({\mathcal{Y}})
is generated by objects that are compact in
\operatorname{Shv}({\mathcal{Y}})
.
We obtain that the category
\operatorname{Shv}_{\mathcal{N}}({\mathcal{Y}})
is also proper. Hence, we can consider the Serre functor

\operatorname{Se}_{\operatorname{Shv}_{\mathcal{N}}({\mathcal{Y}})}
.

5.5.8.

We claim:

Theorem 5.5.9
.

Assume that
{\mathcal{Y}}
is quasi-compact, and that
({\mathcal{Y}},{\mathcal{N}})
is constraccessible and
Serre. Then the category
\operatorname{Shv}_{\mathcal{N}}({\mathcal{Y}})
is Serre. Moreover
under the identification

\operatorname{Shv}_{\mathcal{N}}({\mathcal{Y}})^{\vee}\simeq\operatorname{Shv}_{\mathcal{N}}({\mathcal{Y}})

of (
5.10
), the object

\operatorname{ps-u}_{{\mathcal{Y}},{\mathcal{N}}}\in\operatorname{Shv}_{\mathcal{N}}({\mathcal{Y}})\otimes\operatorname{Shv}_{\mathcal{N}}({\mathcal{Y}})

corresponds to the object

\operatorname{ps-u}_{\operatorname{Shv}_{\mathcal{N}}({\mathcal{Y}})}\in\operatorname{Shv}_{\mathcal{N}}({\mathcal{Y}})\otimes\operatorname{Shv}_{\mathcal{N}}({\mathcal{Y}})^{\vee}

of (
5.3
), and the pairing

\operatorname{ev}^{l}_{\mathcal{Y}}:\operatorname{Shv}_{\mathcal{N}}({\mathcal{Y}})\otimes\operatorname{Shv}_{\mathcal{N}}({\mathcal{Y}})\to\operatorname{Vect}

corresponds to the pairing

\operatorname{u}_{\operatorname{Shv}_{\mathcal{N}}({\mathcal{Y}})}^{L}:\operatorname{Shv}_{\mathcal{N}}({\mathcal{Y}})\otimes\operatorname{Shv}_{\mathcal{N}}({\mathcal{Y}})^{\vee}\to\operatorname{Vect}

of (
5.1
).

Proof.

By Corollary
5.1.7
and Proposition
5.2.5
, we only have to show that the functor
\operatorname{ev}^{l}_{\mathcal{Y}}
is the left adjoint of

\operatorname{Vect}\overset{\operatorname{u}_{{\mathcal{Y}},{\mathcal{N}}}}{\longrightarrow}\operatorname{Shv}_{\mathcal{N}}({\mathcal{Y}})\otimes\operatorname{Shv}_{\mathcal{N}}({\mathcal{Y}}).

However, this is formal from Corollary
5.4.5
.

∎

5.6.
Relation to the miraculous functor

Let
{\mathcal{Y}}
be quasi-compact, and let
({\mathcal{Y}},{\mathcal{N}})
be constraccessible.

In this subsection
we will relate the Serre functor on
\operatorname{Shv}_{\mathcal{N}}({\mathcal{Y}})
to the miraculous functor.

5.6.1.

Consider the object

\operatorname{ps-u}_{{\mathcal{Y}},{\mathcal{N}}}\in\operatorname{Shv}_{\mathcal{N}}({\mathcal{Y}})\otimes\operatorname{Shv}_{\mathcal{N}}({\mathcal{Y}})\subset\operatorname{Shv}({\mathcal{Y}}\times{\mathcal{Y}}),

see Sect.
5.5.1
. According to Corollary
5.4.4
, we have

\operatorname{ps-u}_{{\mathcal{Y}},{\mathcal{N}}}\simeq({\mathsf{P}}_{{\mathcal{Y}},{\mathcal{N}}}\otimes{\mathsf{P}}_{{\mathcal{Y}},{\mathcal{N}}})(\operatorname{ps-u}_{\mathcal{Y}}).

5.6.2.

Let

\operatorname{Mir}_{\mathcal{Y}}:\operatorname{Shv}({\mathcal{Y}})\to\operatorname{Shv}({\mathcal{Y}})

be the
miraculous
functor, i.e., the functor defined by the kernel

\operatorname{ps-u}_{\mathcal{Y}}\in\operatorname{Shv}({\mathcal{Y}}\times{\mathcal{Y}}),

see Sect.
B.4.1
.

5.6.3.

From Sect.
5.6.1
we obtain:

Lemma 5.6.4
.

The endofunctor of
\operatorname{Shv}({\mathcal{Y}})
defined by
\operatorname{ps-u}_{{\mathcal{Y}},{\mathcal{N}}}
as a kernel is the composite

\operatorname{Shv}({\mathcal{Y}})\overset{{\mathsf{P}}_{{\mathcal{Y}},{\mathcal{N}}}}{\longrightarrow}\operatorname{Shv}({\mathcal{Y}})\overset{\operatorname{Mir}_{\mathcal{Y}}}{\longrightarrow}\operatorname{Shv}({\mathcal{Y}})\overset{{\mathsf{P}}_{{\mathcal{Y}},{\mathcal{N}}}}{\to}\operatorname{Shv}({\mathcal{Y}}).

5.6.5.

We give the following definition:

Definition 5.6.6
.

We will say that
{\mathcal{N}}\subset T^{*}({\mathcal{Y}})
is
miraculous-compatible
if the functor
\operatorname{Mir}_{\mathcal{Y}}

preserves the subcategory
\operatorname{Shv}_{\mathcal{N}}({\mathcal{Y}})\subset\operatorname{Shv}({\mathcal{Y}})
.

If
{\mathcal{N}}
is miraculous-compatible, we will denote by
\operatorname{Mir}_{{\mathcal{Y}},{\mathcal{N}}}
the resulting endofunctor of
\operatorname{Shv}_{\mathcal{N}}({\mathcal{Y}})
.

5.6.7.

From Lemma
5.6.4
, we obtain:

Corollary 5.6.8
.

Suppose that
{\mathcal{N}}
be miraculous-compatible. Then:

(a)
The endofunctor of
\operatorname{Shv}_{\mathcal{N}}({\mathcal{Y}})
defined by
\operatorname{ps-u}_{{\mathcal{Y}},{\mathcal{N}}}

identifies canonically with
\operatorname{Mir}_{{\mathcal{Y}},{\mathcal{N}}}
.

(b) We have canonical isomorphisms:

({\mathsf{P}}_{{\mathcal{Y}},{\mathcal{N}}}\otimes\operatorname{Id}_{{\mathcal{Y}}})(\operatorname{ps-u}_{\mathcal{Y}})\simeq\operatorname{ps-u}_{{\mathcal{Y}},{\mathcal{N}}}\simeq(\operatorname{Id}_{{\mathcal{Y}}}\otimes{\mathsf{P}}_{{\mathcal{Y}},{\mathcal{N}}})(\operatorname{ps-u}_{\mathcal{Y}}).

(c) We have canonical isomorphisms of endofunctors of
\operatorname{Shv}({\mathcal{Y}})

\operatorname{Mir}_{\mathcal{Y}}\circ{\mathsf{P}}_{{\mathcal{Y}},{\mathcal{N}}}\simeq{\mathsf{P}}_{{\mathcal{Y}},{\mathcal{N}}}\circ\operatorname{Mir}_{\mathcal{Y}}.

Proof.

Point (a) is immediate from Lemma
5.6.4
. For point (b), by symmetry, it suffices to
prove the first isomorphism. Since

({\mathsf{P}}_{{\mathcal{Y}},{\mathcal{N}}}\otimes\operatorname{Id}_{{\mathcal{Y}}})(\operatorname{ps-u}_{\mathcal{Y}})\in\operatorname{Shv}_{\mathcal{N}}({\mathcal{Y}})\otimes\operatorname{Shv}({\mathcal{Y}})

(by Lemma
5.4.3
), it suffices to show that

(\operatorname{ev}_{\mathcal{Y}}\otimes\operatorname{Id})({\mathcal{F}}\otimes({\mathsf{P}}_{{\mathcal{Y}},{\mathcal{N}}}\otimes\operatorname{Id}_{{\mathcal{Y}}})(\operatorname{ps-u}_{\mathcal{Y}}))\simeq(\operatorname{ev}_{\mathcal{Y}}\otimes\operatorname{Id})({\mathcal{F}}\otimes({\mathsf{P}}_{{\mathcal{Y}},{\mathcal{N}}}\otimes{\mathsf{P}}_{{\mathcal{Y}},{\mathcal{N}}})(\operatorname{ps-u}_{\mathcal{Y}})),\quad{\mathcal{F}}\in\operatorname{Shv}_{\mathcal{N}}({\mathcal{Y}}).

The left-hand side identifies with

(\operatorname{ev}_{\mathcal{Y}}\otimes\operatorname{Id})({\mathcal{F}}\otimes\operatorname{ps-u}_{\mathcal{Y}})\simeq\operatorname{Mir}_{\mathcal{Y}}({\mathcal{F}}),

while the right-hand side also identifies with
\operatorname{Mir}_{\mathcal{Y}}({\mathcal{F}})
, by point (a).

Point (c) is formal from point (b).

∎

5.6.9.

Finally, we claim:

Corollary 5.6.10
.

Suppose that
{\mathcal{N}}
is miraculous-compatible and that
({\mathcal{Y}},{\mathcal{N}})
is Serre. Then:

(a)
\operatorname{Mir}_{{\mathcal{Y}},{\mathcal{N}}}\simeq\operatorname{Ps-Id}_{\operatorname{Shv}_{\mathcal{N}}({\mathcal{Y}})}
;

(b)
\operatorname{Mir}_{{\mathcal{Y}},{\mathcal{N}}}\simeq(\operatorname{Se}_{\operatorname{Shv}_{\mathcal{N}}({\mathcal{Y}})})^{-1}
.

(c) The diagram

\begin{CD}\operatorname{Shv}_{\mathcal{N}}({\mathcal{Y}})\otimes\operatorname{Shv}_{\mathcal{N}}({\mathcal{Y}})@>{\operatorname{ev}^{l}_{\mathcal{Y}}}>{}>\operatorname{Vect}\\
@A{\operatorname{Mir}_{{\mathcal{Y}},{\mathcal{N}}}\otimes\operatorname{Id}}A{}A@A{}A{\operatorname{Id}}A\\
\operatorname{Shv}_{\mathcal{N}}({\mathcal{Y}})\otimes\operatorname{Shv}_{\mathcal{N}}({\mathcal{Y}})@>{\operatorname{ev}_{\mathcal{Y}}}>{}>\operatorname{Vect}\end{CD}

commutes.

Proof.

Point (a) follows by combining Corollary
5.6.8
(a) and Theorem
5.5.9
. Point (b)
follows from Corollary
5.2.7
. Point (c) follows from Sect.
5.2.8
.

∎

Corollary 5.6.11
.

Suppose that
{\mathcal{N}}
is miraculous-compatible and that
({\mathcal{Y}},{\mathcal{N}})
is Serre. Then
the endofunctor
\operatorname{Mir}_{{\mathcal{Y}},{\mathcal{N}}}
of
\operatorname{Shv}_{\mathcal{N}}({\mathcal{Y}})
is an equivalence.

Remark 5.6.12
.

Suppose that in the setting of Corollary
5.6.10
above, the stack
{\mathcal{Y}}
