is miraculous (see Sect.
B.7.1
for what this means). Recall the notation

{\mathbb{D}}^{\operatorname{Mir}}:(\operatorname{Shv}({\mathcal{Y}})^{c})^{\operatorname{op}}\to\operatorname{Shv}({\mathcal{Y}})^{c},

see Sect.
B.7.2
. Recall also the notation
{\mathbb{D}}^{\operatorname{new}}
, see Sect.
5.1.9
. We obtain:

{\mathbb{D}}^{\operatorname{Mir}}\simeq{\mathbb{D}}^{\operatorname{new}}.

5.7.
Relation to the miraculous functor, continued

We retain the assumptions of the previous subsection, and additionally require that

{\mathcal{N}}
is miraculous-compatible and that
({\mathcal{Y}},{\mathcal{N}})
is Serre.

5.7.1.

Note that Corollary
5.6.10
implies that we have a canonical isomorphism

(5.15)

\operatorname{ev}^{l}_{\mathcal{Y}}({\mathcal{F}}_{1},\operatorname{Mir}_{\mathcal{Y}}({\mathcal{F}}_{2}))\simeq\operatorname{ev}_{\mathcal{Y}}({\mathcal{F}}_{1},{\mathcal{F}}_{2}),\quad{\mathcal{F}}_{1},{\mathcal{F}}_{2}\in\operatorname{Shv}_{\mathcal{N}}({\mathcal{Y}}).

We claim:

Proposition 5.7.2
.

The isomorphism (
5.15
) identifies with the natural transformation
(
4.1
).

Proof.

Repeats the proof of Proposition
4.1.3
, with the difference that the vertical arrows in
the counterpart of diagram (
4.5
) are furnished by that natural transformation

{\mathsf{P}}_{{\mathcal{Y}},{\mathcal{N}}}\to\operatorname{Id},

given by the counit of the adjunction.

∎

5.7.3.

As in Theorem
4.2.2
, from Proposition
5.7.2
we obtain:

Corollary 5.7.4
.

Let
{\mathcal{F}}
be an object in
\operatorname{Shv}_{\mathcal{N}}({\mathcal{Y}})^{constr}
. Then the functor

{\mathsf{F}}:\operatorname{Shv}({\mathcal{Y}})\to\operatorname{Vect}

that it defines admits a right adjoint, as a functor defined by a kernel.

Further, as in Corollary
4.2.5
, we obtain:

Corollary 5.7.5
.

Let
{\mathcal{F}}
be an object in
\operatorname{Shv}_{\mathcal{N}}({\mathcal{Y}})
. Then the functor

{\mathsf{F}}:\operatorname{Shv}({\mathcal{Y}})\to\operatorname{Vect}

that it defines, is defined and codefined by a kernel.

As in Corollary
4.3.2
, we obtain:

Corollary 5.7.6
.

Let
{\mathcal{F}}
be an object in
\operatorname{Shv}_{\mathcal{N}}({\mathcal{Y}})
. Then for an algebraic stack
{\mathcal{Z}}
, the natural transformation

(p_{\mathcal{Z}})_{!}({\mathcal{F}}^{\prime}\overset{*}{\otimes}p_{\mathcal{Y}}^{*}(\operatorname{Mir}_{\mathcal{Y}}({\mathcal{F}})))\to(p_{\mathcal{Z}})_{\blacktriangle}({\mathcal{F}}^{\prime}\overset{!}{\otimes}p_{\mathcal{Y}}^{!}({\mathcal{F}})),\quad{\mathcal{F}}^{\prime}\in\operatorname{Shv}({\mathcal{Z}}\times{\mathcal{Y}})

is an isomorphism.

Finally, as in Corollary
4.3.5
, we obtain:

Corollary 5.7.7
.

Let
{\mathcal{Y}}
be miraculous, and let
{\mathcal{F}}
be an object in
\operatorname{Shv}_{\mathcal{N}}({\mathcal{Y}})
.
Then for an algebraic stack
{\mathcal{Z}}
, we have a canonical isomorphism
the natural transformation

(p_{\mathcal{Z}})_{!}((\operatorname{Id}_{\mathcal{Z}}\boxtimes\operatorname{Mir}_{\mathcal{Y}})({\mathcal{F}}^{\prime})\overset{*}{\otimes}p_{\mathcal{Y}}^{*}({\mathcal{F}}))\simeq(p_{\mathcal{Z}})_{\blacktriangle}({\mathcal{F}}^{\prime}\overset{!}{\otimes}p_{\mathcal{Y}}^{!}({\mathcal{F}})),\quad{\mathcal{F}}^{\prime}\in\operatorname{Shv}({\mathcal{Z}}\times{\mathcal{Y}}).

5.8.
The (non)-Serre property of
\operatorname{Bun}_{G}

In this subsection we will study the Serre property of the category
\operatorname{Shv}_{\operatorname{Nilp}}(\operatorname{Bun}_{G})
, and also of
\operatorname{Shv}_{\operatorname{Nilp}}({\mathcal{U}})
,
for universally Nilp-cotruncative quasi-compact open substacks
{\mathcal{U}}\subset\operatorname{Bun}_{G}
.

5.8.1.

Let
{\mathcal{U}}\subset\operatorname{Bun}_{G}
be a universally Nilp-cotruncative quasi-compact open substack. Note that
it follows from Proposition
2.3.3
that the object that was denoted
\operatorname{u}_{{\mathcal{U}},{\operatorname{Nilp}}}
in
Sect.
2.2.4
is the same as the object that appears in Sect.
5.3.3

(for
{\mathcal{Y}}={\mathcal{U}}
and
{\mathcal{N}}={\operatorname{Nilp}}
).

Furthermore, it follows from Proposition
2.2.5
that the endofunctor of
\operatorname{Shv}({\mathcal{U}})

denoted
{\mathsf{P}}_{{\mathcal{U}},{\operatorname{Nilp}}}
in Sect.
1.8.1
identifies,
as an endofunctor defined by a kernel
,
with the endofunctor that appears in Sect.
5.3.5
(for
{\mathcal{Y}}={\mathcal{U}}
and
{\mathcal{N}}={\operatorname{Nilp}}
).

Hence, the notations
\operatorname{u}_{{\mathcal{U}},{\operatorname{Nilp}}}
and
{\mathsf{P}}_{{\mathcal{U}},{\operatorname{Nilp}}}
are unambiguous.

5.8.2.

From now on, for the duration of this subsection, we will assume
[
AGKRRV
, Conjecture 14.1.8]
.
I.e., we will assume that
\operatorname{Shv}_{{\operatorname{Nilp}}}(\operatorname{Bun}_{G})
is generated by objects that are compact in

\operatorname{Shv}(\operatorname{Bun}_{G})
. By
[
AGKRRV
, Lemma F.8.10]
, this implies, that for every
{\mathcal{U}}
as above, the pair

({\mathcal{U}},{\operatorname{Nilp}})
is constraccessible.

Applying Corollary
2.4.9
, we obtain that the object
\operatorname{ps-u}_{{\mathcal{U}},{\operatorname{Nilp}}}
introduced in Sect.
3.6.1

identifies with each of the following objects

({\mathsf{P}}_{{\mathcal{U}},{\operatorname{Nilp}}}\otimes\operatorname{Id}_{\mathcal{U}})(\operatorname{ps-u}_{\mathcal{U}}),\,\,({\mathsf{P}}_{{\mathcal{U}},{\operatorname{Nilp}}}\otimes{\mathsf{P}}_{{\mathcal{U}},{\operatorname{Nilp}}})(\operatorname{ps-u}_{\mathcal{U}}),\,\,(\operatorname{Id}_{\mathcal{U}}\otimes{\mathsf{P}}_{{\mathcal{U}},{\operatorname{Nilp}}})(\operatorname{ps-u}_{\mathcal{U}}).

Combining with Sect.
5.6.1
, we obtain that
\operatorname{ps-u}_{{\mathcal{U}},{\operatorname{Nilp}}}

of Sect.
3.6.1
is the same as the object
that appears in Sect.
5.5.1
(for
{\mathcal{Y}}={\mathcal{U}}
and
{\mathcal{N}}={\operatorname{Nilp}}
).

Hence, the notation
\operatorname{ps-u}_{{\mathcal{U}},{\operatorname{Nilp}}}
is also unambiguous.

5.8.3.

We now claim:

Corollary 5.8.4
.

The pair
({\mathcal{U}},{\operatorname{Nilp}})
is Serre.

Proof.

By Corollary
3.6.3
, we know that

\operatorname{ev}^{l}_{{\mathcal{U}}}:\operatorname{Shv}_{{\operatorname{Nilp}}}({{\mathcal{U}}})\otimes\operatorname{Shv}_{{\operatorname{Nilp}}}({\mathcal{U}})\to\operatorname{Vect},\quad{\mathcal{F}}_{1},{\mathcal{F}}_{2}\mapsto\operatorname{C}^{\cdot}_{c}({{\mathcal{U}}},{\mathcal{F}}_{1}\overset{*}{\otimes}{\mathcal{F}}_{2})

and the object
\operatorname{ps-u}_{{\mathcal{U}},{\operatorname{Nilp}}}
define a duality datum.

In particular, we obtain a canonical isomorphism

(\operatorname{ev}_{\mathcal{U}}^{*}\otimes\operatorname{Id})\circ(\operatorname{Id}\otimes\operatorname{ps-u}_{{\mathcal{U}},{\operatorname{Nilp}}})\simeq\operatorname{Id}.

However, unwinding the definitions, one shows that the above isomorphism agrees
with the natural transformation in Definition
5.5.4
.

∎

5.8.5.

We now consider the non quasi-compact stack
\operatorname{Bun}_{G}
. From Corollary
2.4.7
we obtain:

Corollary 5.8.6
.

The object
\operatorname{ps-u}_{\operatorname{Bun}_{G},{\operatorname{Nilp}}}
of Sect.
3.1.2
identifies canonically with the
value of the right adjoint to

\operatorname{Shv}_{{\operatorname{Nilp}}}(\operatorname{Bun}_{G})\otimes\operatorname{Shv}_{{\operatorname{Nilp}}}(\operatorname{Bun}_{G})\hookrightarrow\operatorname{Shv}(\operatorname{Bun}_{G}\times\operatorname{Bun}_{G})

on
\operatorname{ps-u}_{\operatorname{Bun}_{G}}
.

Hence, as in Corollary
5.8.4
, from Theorem
3.2.2
, we obtain that
the pair
(\operatorname{Bun}_{G},{\operatorname{Nilp}})
is Serre.

5.8.7.

The assumption that
[
AGKRRV
, Conjecture 14.1.8]
holds implies, in particular, that
the category
\operatorname{Shv}_{\operatorname{Nilp}}(\operatorname{Bun}_{G})
is proper. Hence, we can consider the Serre functor

\operatorname{Se}_{\operatorname{Shv}_{\operatorname{Nilp}}(\operatorname{Bun}_{G})}:\operatorname{Shv}_{\operatorname{Nilp}}(\operatorname{Bun}_{G})\to\operatorname{Shv}_{\operatorname{Nilp}}(\operatorname{Bun}_{G}).

We claim:

Theorem 5.8.8
.

The functor
\operatorname{Se}_{\operatorname{Shv}_{\operatorname{Nilp}}(\operatorname{Bun}_{G})}
is canonically isomorphic to the composition

\operatorname{Shv}_{\operatorname{Nilp}}(\operatorname{Bun}_{G})\overset{\operatorname{Mir}^{-1}_{\operatorname{Bun}_{G}}}{\longrightarrow}\operatorname{Shv}_{\operatorname{Nilp}}(\operatorname{Bun}_{G})_{\operatorname{co}}\overset{\operatorname{Id}^{\operatorname{naive}}_{\operatorname{Bun}_{G}}}{\longrightarrow}\operatorname{Shv}_{\operatorname{Nilp}}(\operatorname{Bun}_{G}).

Proof.

Let
{\mathcal{U}}\overset{j}{\hookrightarrow}\operatorname{Bun}_{G}
be a universally Nilp-cotruncative quasi-compact open substack.
Unwinding the definitions, we obtain a canonical isomorphism

(5.16)

\operatorname{Se}_{\operatorname{Shv}_{\operatorname{Nilp}}(\operatorname{Bun}_{G})}\circ j_{!}\simeq j_{*}\circ\operatorname{Se}_{\operatorname{Shv}_{\operatorname{Nilp}}({\mathcal{U}})}.

Furthermore, for an inclusion
{\mathcal{U}}_{1}\overset{j_{1,2}}{\hookrightarrow}{\mathcal{U}}_{2}
, we have

(5.17)

\operatorname{Se}_{\operatorname{Shv}_{\operatorname{Nilp}}({\mathcal{U}}_{2})}\circ(j_{1,2})_{!}\simeq(j_{1,2})_{*}\circ\operatorname{Se}_{\operatorname{Shv}_{\operatorname{Nilp}}({\mathcal{U}}_{1})},

compatible with (
5.16
).

By (
2.22
), we also have a system of isomorphisms

\operatorname{Id}^{\operatorname{naive}}_{\operatorname{Bun}_{G}}\circ\operatorname{Mir}^{-1}_{\operatorname{Bun}_{G}}\circ j_{!}\simeq j_{*}\circ\operatorname{Mir}^{-1}_{{\mathcal{U}}},

compatible with

\operatorname{Mir}^{-1}_{{\mathcal{U}}_{2}}\circ(j_{1,2})_{!}\simeq(j_{1,2})_{*}\circ\operatorname{Mir}^{-1}_{{\mathcal{U}}_{1}}.

Hence, it suffices to show that the system of isomorphisms

\operatorname{Se}_{\operatorname{Shv}_{\operatorname{Nilp}}({\mathcal{U}})}\simeq\operatorname{Mir}^{-1}_{{\mathcal{U}},{\operatorname{Nilp}}}

of Corollary
5.6.10
(b) makes the system of diagrams

\begin{CD}\operatorname{Se}_{\operatorname{Shv}_{\operatorname{Nilp}}({\mathcal{U}}_{2})}\circ(j_{1,2})_{!}@>{\sim}>{}>(j_{1,2})_{*}\circ\operatorname{Se}_{\operatorname{Shv}_{\operatorname{Nilp}}({\mathcal{U}}_{1})}\\
@V{\sim}V{}V@V{\sim}V{}V\\
\operatorname{Mir}^{-1}_{{\mathcal{U}}_{2}}\circ(j_{1,2})_{!}@>{\sim}>{}>(j_{1,2})_{*}\circ\operatorname{Mir}^{-1}_{{\mathcal{U}}_{1}}.\end{CD}

However, this follows by unwinding the definitions.

∎

5.8.9.

It follows from Theorem
5.8.8
that the functor
\operatorname{Se}_{\operatorname{Shv}_{\operatorname{Nilp}}(\operatorname{Bun}_{G})}
sends compact objects
of
\operatorname{Shv}_{\operatorname{Nilp}}(\operatorname{Bun}_{G})
to objects that lie in the essential image of the
fully faithful
functor

(\operatorname{Shv}_{\operatorname{Nilp}}(\operatorname{Bun}_{G})_{\operatorname{co}})^{c}\hookrightarrow\operatorname{Shv}_{\operatorname{Nilp}}(\operatorname{Bun}_{G})_{\operatorname{co}}\overset{\operatorname{Id}^{\operatorname{naive}}_{\operatorname{Bun}_{G}}}{\longrightarrow}\operatorname{Shv}_{\operatorname{Nilp}}(\operatorname{Bun}_{G}).

Denote by

\operatorname{Se}_{\operatorname{Shv}_{\operatorname{Nilp}}(\operatorname{Bun}_{G}),\operatorname{co}}

the ind-extension of the resulting functor

\operatorname{Shv}_{\operatorname{Nilp}}(\operatorname{Bun}_{G})\to\operatorname{Shv}_{\operatorname{Nilp}}(\operatorname{Bun}_{G})_{\operatorname{co}}.

From Theorem
5.8.8
, we obtain:

Corollary 5.8.10
.

The functor
\operatorname{Se}_{\operatorname{Shv}_{\operatorname{Nilp}}(\operatorname{Bun}_{G}),\operatorname{co}}
identifies canonically with
(\operatorname{Mir}^{-1}_{\operatorname{Bun}_{G}})|_{\operatorname{Shv}_{\operatorname{Nilp}}(\operatorname{Bun}_{G})}
.

Appendix A
Sheaves on stacks

In this section we review the theory of
\operatorname{Shv}(-)
on stacks, especially the aspects that
have to do with Verdier duality, used in the bulk of the paper.

A.1.
The basics

In this subsection we name the main players in
\operatorname{Shv}(-)
on stacks.

A.1.1.

In this paper we work with a constructible sheaf theory, denoted
\operatorname{Shv}
, which we view as a functor

(\operatorname{Sch}^{\operatorname{aff}}_{\operatorname{ft}/k})^{\operatorname{op}}\to\operatorname{DGCat},

see
[
AGKRRV
, Sects. 1.1]
, where for
f:S_{1}\to S_{2}
, the functor

\operatorname{Shv}(S_{2})\to\operatorname{Shv}(S_{1})
is
f^{!}
.

For a given affine scheme
S
, the category
\operatorname{Shv}(S)
is defined as the ind-completion of the category

\operatorname{Shv}(S)^{\operatorname{constr}}
of constructible sheaves, so that
\operatorname{Shv}(S)^{c}
recovers
\operatorname{Shv}(S)^{\operatorname{constr}}
.

A.1.2.

We extend
\operatorname{Shv}(-)
from affine schemes to algebraic stacks by the procedure of right Kan extension.
Explicitly, for an algebraic stack
{\mathcal{Y}}
,

(A.1)

\operatorname{Shv}({\mathcal{Y}}):=\underset{S\to{\mathcal{Y}}}{\operatorname{lim}^{!}}\,\operatorname{Shv}(S),

where the index category is any of the following:

•

All affine schemes
S
of finite type over
{\mathcal{Y}}
;

•

Affine schemes
S
that map smoothly to
{\mathcal{Y}}
, and all maps
f:S_{1}\to S_{2}
;

•

Affine schemes
S
that map smoothly to
{\mathcal{Y}}
, and smooth maps
f:S_{1}\to S_{2}
.

In the formation of the limit, the transition functors
\operatorname{Shv}(S_{2})\to\operatorname{Shv}(S_{1})
are given by
f^{!}
.

A.1.3.

One can also rewrite
\operatorname{Shv}({\mathcal{Y}})
as

(A.2)

\underset{S\to{\mathcal{Y}}}{\operatorname{lim}^{*}}\,\operatorname{Shv}(S),

over the same choice of index categories, but with transition functors
\operatorname{Shv}(S_{2})\to\operatorname{Shv}(S_{1})
given by
f^{*}
.

The two limits are equivalent via the following procedure: use the third index category in both cases. Applying the cohomological
shift by
[2\dim(S/{\mathcal{Y}})]
on each
\operatorname{Shv}(S)
, we can isomorph the limit (
A.2
) to

(A.3)

\underset{S\to{\mathcal{Y}}}{\operatorname{lim}}\!^{*,\operatorname{shift}}\,\operatorname{Shv}(S),

where the transition functors are now
f^{*}[2\dim(S_{2}/S_{1})]
.

Now, the limit (
A.3
) is equivalent to (
A.1
) term-wise.

A.1.4.

For a stack
{\mathcal{Y}}
, we let
\operatorname{Shv}({\mathcal{Y}})^{\operatorname{constr}}
be the full subcategory of
\operatorname{Shv}({\mathcal{Y}})
equal to

\underset{S\to{\mathcal{Y}}}{\operatorname{lim}}\,\operatorname{Shv}(S)^{\operatorname{constr}}
(with respect to any of the above three index
categories and either
-^{!}
or
-^{*}
version).

For an algebraic stack
{\mathcal{Y}}
, we let

\underline{{\mathsf{e}}}_{\mathcal{Y}}\in\operatorname{Shv}({\mathcal{Y}})^{\operatorname{constr}}

denote the constant sheaf.

A.1.5.

Built into the definition of
\operatorname{Shv}(-)
are the functors

f^{!},f^{*}:\operatorname{Shv}({\mathcal{Y}}_{2})\to\operatorname{Shv}({\mathcal{Y}}_{1})

for a morphism
f:{\mathcal{Y}}_{2}\to{\mathcal{Y}}_{1}
between algebraic stacks.

In addition, the
(-_{!},-^{*})
(resp.,
(-_{*},-^{!})
base change isomorphisms for maps between
schemes give rise to functors

f_{!},f_{*}:\operatorname{Shv}({\mathcal{Y}}_{1})\to\operatorname{Shv}({\mathcal{Y}}_{2})

when
f
is schematic. It follows from the construction that
f_{!}
(resp.,
f_{*}
) is the left (resp., right)
adjoint of
f^{!}
(resp.,
f^{*}
).

We will explain how extend this definition for maps that are not necessarily schematic
in Sect.
A.1.7
-Sect.
A.1.8
below.

A.1.6.

As is explained in
[
AGKRRV
, Sect. F.1.1]
, for any algebraic stack
{\mathcal{Y}}
, the category

\operatorname{Shv}({\mathcal{Y}})
is compactly generated.
Namely, we can take as generators objects of the form
g_{!}({\mathcal{F}})
for

g:S\to{\mathcal{Y}},\quad{\mathcal{F}}\in\operatorname{Shv}(S)^{c},\quad S\in\operatorname{Sch}^{\operatorname{aff}}_{\operatorname{ft}/k}.

We have an inclusion

\operatorname{Shv}({\mathcal{Y}})^{c}\subset\operatorname{Shv}({\mathcal{Y}})^{\operatorname{constr}},

but this inclusion is in general
not
an equality. For example,
the object
\underline{{\mathsf{e}}}_{\mathcal{Y}}
is not compact for
{\mathcal{Y}}=B{\mathbb{G}}_{m}
.

It easy to see, however, that the subcategory
\operatorname{Shv}({\mathcal{Y}})^{c}
is preserved by the

\overset{*}{\otimes}
operation with objects in
\operatorname{Shv}({\mathcal{Y}})^{\operatorname{constr}}

(see (
A.5
) below).

A.1.7.

Let
f:{\mathcal{Y}}_{1}\to{\mathcal{Y}}_{2}
be a morphism between algebraic stacks. We define the functor

f_{!}:\operatorname{Shv}({\mathcal{Y}}_{1})\to\operatorname{Shv}({\mathcal{Y}}_{2})

as the left adjoint of
f^{!}
.

To show that
f_{!}
exists, it is enough to show that it is defined on the generators of

\operatorname{Shv}({\mathcal{Y}}_{1})
. Taking the generators described in Sect.
A.1.6
, we have

f_{!}(g_{!}({\mathcal{F}}))\simeq(f\circ g)_{!}({\mathcal{F}}),

where the right-hand side is well-defined because the morphism
f\circ g
is schematic.

The same argument shows that the
-_{!}
pushforward
satisfies base change against the
-^{*}
pullback.

A.1.8.

For
f
as above, we define the functor

f_{*}:\operatorname{Shv}({\mathcal{Y}}_{1})\to\operatorname{Shv}({\mathcal{Y}}_{2})

as the right adjoint of
f^{*}
.

This functor exists for general reasons
5
5

5

I.e., Lurie’s Adjoint Functor Theorem, which in particular
says that any colimit preserving functor between presentable DG categories admits a right adjoint.
,
however it may be discontinuous (because the functor
f^{*}
does not necessarily preserve compactness).

However,
-_{*}
pushforward does satisfy base change against
-^{!}
pullback. This follows
by passing to right adjoints from the
(-_{!},-^{*})
base change.

A.1.9.

For any algebraic stack
{\mathcal{Y}}
, Verdier duality defines a contravariant self-equivalence

(A.4)

{\mathbb{D}}^{\operatorname{Verdier}}:(\operatorname{Shv}({\mathcal{Y}})^{\operatorname{constr}})^{\operatorname{op}}\to\operatorname{Shv}({\mathcal{Y}})^{\operatorname{constr}}.

Its basic property is that for
{\mathcal{F}}\in\operatorname{Shv}({\mathcal{Y}})^{\operatorname{constr}}

(A.5)

{\mathop{\operatorname{{\mathcal{H}}\it om}}}_{\operatorname{Shv}({\mathcal{Y}})}({\mathcal{F}}\overset{*}{\otimes}{\mathcal{F}}_{1},{\mathcal{F}}_{2})\simeq{\mathop{\operatorname{{\mathcal{H}}\it om}}}_{\operatorname{Shv}({\mathcal{Y}})}({\mathcal{F}}_{1},{\mathbb{D}}^{\operatorname{Verdier}}({\mathcal{F}})\overset{!}{\otimes}{\mathcal{F}}_{2}),\quad{\mathcal{F}}_{1},{\mathcal{F}}_{2}\in\operatorname{Shv}({\mathcal{Y}}).

We will denote by
\omega_{\mathcal{Y}}\in\operatorname{Shv}({\mathcal{Y}})
the dualizing sheaf on
{\mathcal{Y}}
:

{\mathbb{D}}^{\operatorname{Verdier}}(\underline{{\mathsf{e}}}_{\mathcal{Y}}).

A.2.
Verdier-compatible stacks

In this subsection we will assume that algebraic stacks are quasi-compact.

We review the notion of what it means for an algebraic stack to be Verdier compatible.
On the one hand, this property confers some particularly favorable properties on to
\operatorname{Shv}({\mathcal{Y}})
.
On the other hand, it is quite ubiquitous.

A.2.1.

It is not clear whether for a general algebraic stack
{\mathcal{Y}}
, the Verdier involution (
A.4
)
always sends
\operatorname{Shv}({\mathcal{Y}})^{c}
to
\operatorname{Shv}({\mathcal{Y}})^{c}
.

If this is the case, following
[
AGKRRV
, Sect. F.2.6]
, we shall say that
{\mathcal{Y}}
is
Verdier compatible
.
This property always holds under the assumption on algebraic stacks imposed in Sect.
0.5.1
, see

[
AGKRRV
, Theorem F.2.8]
.

Thus, we will assume that all stacks involved are Verdier compatible.

A.2.2.

Since Verdier duality swaps
-_{*}
and
-_{!}
, we obtain that objects of the form
g_{*}({\mathcal{F}})
, where

g:S\to{\mathcal{Y}},\quad{\mathcal{F}}\in\operatorname{Shv}(S)^{c},\quad S\in\operatorname{Sch}^{\operatorname{aff}}_{\operatorname{ft}/k}

are compact in and generate
\operatorname{Shv}({\mathcal{F}})
.

This implies that for a morphism
f:{\mathcal{Y}}_{1}\to{\mathcal{Y}}_{2}
, the functor
f_{*}
sends

\operatorname{Shv}({\mathcal{Y}}_{1})^{c}
to
\operatorname{Shv}({\mathcal{Y}}_{2})^{c}
.

This also implies that the subcategory
\operatorname{Shv}({\mathcal{Y}})^{c}
is preserved by the

\overset{!}{\otimes}
operation with objects in
\operatorname{Shv}({\mathcal{Y}})^{\operatorname{constr}}
.

A.2.3.

For a map between stacks
f:{\mathcal{Y}}_{1}\to{\mathcal{Y}}_{2}
, the usual functor of
direct image
f_{*}:\operatorname{Shv}({\mathcal{Y}}_{1})\to\operatorname{Shv}({\mathcal{Y}}_{2})
is not in general colimit-preserving.
We define the
renormalized
functor of direct image, denoted

f_{\blacktriangle}:\operatorname{Shv}({\mathcal{Y}}_{1})\to\operatorname{Shv}({\mathcal{Y}}_{2}),

to be the unique colimit-preserving functor such that restricts to
f_{*}
on
\operatorname{Shv}({\mathcal{Y}}_{1})^{c}
.
We have a tautologically defined natural transformation

f_{\blacktriangle}\to f_{*},

which is an isomorphism if
f
is schematic.

If follows from Sect.
A.2.2
that the functor
f_{\blacktriangle}

preserves compactness and satisfies the projection formula.

This in turn implies that for a pair of composable
morphisms

{\mathcal{Y}}_{1}\overset{f_{1,2}}{\to}{\mathcal{Y}}_{2}\overset{f_{2,3}}{\to}{\mathcal{Y}}_{3},

the natural transformation

(f_{2,3})_{\blacktriangle}\circ(f_{1,2})_{\blacktriangle}\to(f_{2,3}\circ f_{1,2})_{\blacktriangle}

is an isomorphism.

A.2.4.

For
{\mathcal{Y}}_{1}={\mathcal{Y}}
and
{\mathcal{Y}}_{2}=\operatorname{pt}
, we obtain the renormalized functor
of sheaf cochains, denoted

\operatorname{C}^{\cdot}_{\blacktriangle}({\mathcal{Y}},-):\operatorname{Shv}({\mathcal{Y}})\to\operatorname{Vect}.

By Sect.
A.2.3
, for a map
f:{\mathcal{Y}}_{1}\to{\mathcal{Y}}_{2}
, we have

\operatorname{C}^{\cdot}_{\blacktriangle}({\mathcal{Y}}_{1},-)\simeq\operatorname{C}^{\cdot}_{\blacktriangle}({\mathcal{Y}}_{2},-)\circ f_{\blacktriangle}.

A.2.5.

For future reference, we claim:

Lemma A.2.6
.

The functor
f_{\blacktriangle}:\operatorname{Shv}({\mathcal{Y}}_{1})\to\operatorname{Shv}({\mathcal{Y}}_{2})
has a cohomological
amplitude bounded on the right.

Proof.

Covering
{\mathcal{Y}}_{2}
by a scheme (and using base change, see Sect.
A.3.1
below),
we can assume that
{\mathcal{Y}}_{2}
is a scheme
Y_{2}
.

By the assumption in Sect.
0.5.1
, we can assume that

{\mathcal{Y}}_{1}
is of the form
Y_{1}/H
, where
Y_{1}
is a (quasi-compact) scheme and
H

is an algebraic group. We can factor the map
f
as

Y_{1}/H\to Y_{2}/H=Y_{2}\times BH\to Y_{2}.

The arrow
Y_{1}/H\to Y_{2}/H
is schematic, so the
-_{\blacktriangle}
functor is the same as

-_{*}
, and so has a finite cohomological amplitude. Hence, it remains to consider the morphism
of the form

p_{Y}:Y\times BH\to Y.

Any object
{\mathcal{F}}\in\operatorname{Shv}(Y\times BH)
admits a finite canonical filtration with subquotients of the form

p_{Y}^{*}\circ q_{Y}^{*}({\mathcal{F}})[n],

where
q_{Y}
is the map
Y\to Y\times BH
. Hence, it remains to show
that

\operatorname{C}^{\cdot}_{\blacktriangle}(BH,\underline{{\mathsf{e}}}_{BH})\in\operatorname{Vect}

is bounded above.

However, this is a computation performed in
[
DrGa0
, Example 9.1.6]
.

∎

A.2.7.

in general, the functors
f_{*}
and
f_{\blacktriangle}
do not preserve constructibility. However,
the argument in Lemma
A.2.6
shows that both these functors send constructible
objects to objects with constructible perverse cohomologies.

Furthermore, we have the following assertion:

Proposition A.2.8
.

For a constructible object
{\mathcal{F}}\in\operatorname{Shv}({\mathcal{Y}}_{1})
, the following conditions are equivalent:

(i) The map
f_{\blacktriangle}({\mathcal{F}})\to f_{*}({\mathcal{F}})

is an isomorphism;

(ii) The object
f_{\blacktriangle}({\mathcal{F}})\in\operatorname{Shv}({\mathcal{Y}}_{2})
is cohomologically bounded on the left;

(ii) The object
f_{*}({\mathcal{F}})\in\operatorname{Shv}({\mathcal{Y}}_{2})
is cohomologically bounded on the right.

Proof.

Since both objects
f_{\blacktriangle}({\mathcal{F}})
and
f_{*}({\mathcal{F}})
have constructible cohomologies, it is enough to
show that for every
k
-point

\operatorname{pt}\overset{{\mathbf{i}}_{y_{2}}}{\longrightarrow}{\mathcal{Y}}_{2},

the following conditions are equivalent:

(i) The map
{\mathbf{i}}_{y_{2}}^{!}\circ f_{\blacktriangle}({\mathcal{F}})\to{\mathbf{i}}_{y_{2}}^{!}\circ f_{*}({\mathcal{F}})

is an isomorphism;

(ii) The object
{\mathbf{i}}_{y_{2}}^{!}\circ f_{\blacktriangle}({\mathcal{F}})\in\operatorname{Vect}
is cohomologically bounded on the left;

(ii) The object
{\mathbf{i}}_{y_{2}}^{!}\circ f_{*}({\mathcal{F}})\in\operatorname{Vect}
is cohomologically bounded on the right.

Note that both functors
f_{\blacktriangle}
and
f_{*}
satisfy base change against !-pullbacks:
this is obvious for
f_{*}
, e.g., by passing to left adjoints, and for
f_{\blacktriangle}

this is established in Sect.
A.3.1
below.

This reduces the proposition to the case when
{\mathcal{Y}}_{2}=\operatorname{pt}
. Write
{\mathcal{Y}}_{1}=:{\mathcal{Y}}=Z/H
, where
Z
is
a scheme, and let is factor the projection
{\mathcal{Y}}\to\operatorname{pt}
as

{\mathcal{Y}}\overset{g}{\to}\operatorname{pt}/H\to\operatorname{pt}.

The map
g
schematic, and hence
g_{\blacktriangle}\to g_{*}
is an isomorphism. This allows us to replace

{\mathcal{Y}}
by
\operatorname{pt}/H
. However, in the latter case, the required assertion was established in
[
DrGa0
, Proposition 10.4.7]
.

∎

A.3.
Base change maps

Let

\begin{CD}{\mathcal{Y}}^{\prime}_{1}@>{f^{\prime}}>{}>{\mathcal{Y}}^{\prime}_{2}\\
@V{g_{1}}V{}V@V{}V{g_{2}}V\\
{\mathcal{Y}}_{1}@>{f}>{}>{\mathcal{Y}}_{2}\end{CD}

be a Cartesian diagram of quasi-compact algebraic stacks.

In this subsection we will construct certain natural transformations that have to
do with applying various direct/inverse image functors along the arrows in the
above diagram.

A.3.1.

First, we claim that there is a canonical isomorphism

(A.6)

g_{2}^{!}\circ f_{\blacktriangle}\simeq f^{\prime}_{\blacktriangle}\circ g_{1}^{!}.

First, we consider the case when
g_{2}
is schematic (and hence so is
g_{1}
).

Note that for a schematic map
g:{\mathcal{Y}}^{\prime}\to{\mathcal{Y}}
the functor
g^{*}
preserves compactness. Indeed,
its right adjoint
g_{*}
is continuous. Hence,
g^{!}
also preserves compactness, by Verdier-compatibility.

Hence, (
A.6
) is obtained by ind-extending the restriction to compact objects
the usual base change isomorphism

g_{2}^{!}\circ f_{*}\simeq f^{\prime}_{*}\circ g_{1}^{!}.

A.3.2.

For a general
g_{2}
we proceed as follows.

It suffices to construct a compatible system of isomorphisms

(A.7)

h_{2}^{!}\circ g_{2}^{!}\circ f_{\blacktriangle}\simeq h_{2}^{!}\circ f^{\prime}_{\blacktriangle}\circ g_{1}^{!}

in the Cartesian diagrams

(A.8)

\begin{CD}{\mathcal{Y}}^{\prime\prime}_{1}@>{f^{\prime\prime}}>{}>{\mathcal{Y}}^{\prime\prime}_{2}\\
@V{h_{1}}V{}V@V{}V{h_{2}}V\\
{\mathcal{Y}}^{\prime}_{1}@>{f^{\prime}}>{}>{\mathcal{Y}}^{\prime}_{2}\\
@V{g_{1}}V{}V@V{}V{g_{2}}V\\
{\mathcal{Y}}_{1}@>{f}>{}>{\mathcal{Y}}_{2},\end{CD}

where
h_{2}:{\mathcal{Y}}^{\prime\prime}_{2}\to{\mathcal{Y}}^{\prime}_{2}
is a smooth map from an affine scheme.

We have

h_{2}^{!}\circ g_{2}^{!}\circ f_{\blacktriangle}\simeq(g_{2}\circ h_{2})^{!}\circ f_{\blacktriangle}\overset{g_{2}\circ h_{2}\text{ is schematic}}{\simeq}f^{\prime\prime}_{\blacktriangle}\circ(g_{1}\circ h_{1})^{!}\simeq f^{\prime\prime}_{\blacktriangle}\circ h_{1}^{!}\circ g_{1}^{!}\overset{h_{2}\text{ is schematic}}{\simeq}h_{2}^{!}\circ f^{\prime}_{\blacktriangle}\circ g_{1}^{!}.

A.3.3.

Next, we claim that there is natural transformation

(g_{2})_{!}\circ f^{\prime}_{\blacktriangle}\to f_{\blacktriangle}\circ(g_{1})_{!}.

Indeed, it is obtained by ind-extending the restriction to compact objects
the natural transformation

(g_{2})_{!}\circ f^{\prime}_{*}\to f_{*}\circ(g_{1})_{!}

arising by adjunction from the base change isomorphism
f^{\prime}_{*}\circ g_{1}^{!}\simeq g_{2}^{!}\circ f_{*}
.

A.3.4.

Finally, we claim that there is a natural transformation

(A.9)

g_{2}^{*}\circ f_{\blacktriangle}\to f^{\prime}_{\blacktriangle}\circ(g_{1})^{*}.

First, the if
g_{2}
is schematic (and hence so is
g_{1}
, and so the functors
g_{2}^{*}
and
g_{1}^{*}
preserve compactness),
the natural transformation (
A.9
) is obtained by ind-extending the restriction to compact objects
the natural transformation

g_{2}^{*}\circ f_{*}\to f^{\prime}_{*}\circ g_{1}^{*}

arising by adjunction from the isomorphism
f_{*}\circ(g_{1})_{*}\simeq(g_{2})_{*}\circ f^{\prime}_{*}
.

Note that (
A.9
) is an isomorphism if
g_{2}
is smooth.

A.3.5.

For a general
g_{2}
we proceed as follows:

It suffices to construct a compatible system of natural transformations

(A.10)

h_{2}^{*}\circ g_{2}^{*}\circ f_{\blacktriangle}\to h_{2}^{*}\circ f^{\prime}_{\blacktriangle}\circ g_{1}^{*}

for
h_{2}
as in Sect.
A.8
.

We rewrite the left-hand side in (
A.10
) as

(g_{2}\circ h_{2})^{*}\circ f_{\blacktriangle}
, and it admits a natural transformation to

f^{\prime\prime}_{\blacktriangle}\circ(g_{1}\circ h_{1})^{*}
, since the map
g_{2}\circ h_{2}
is schematic.

Finally, we rewrite
f^{\prime\prime}_{\blacktriangle}\circ(g_{1}\circ h_{1})^{*}
as

f^{\prime\prime}_{\blacktriangle}\circ h_{1}^{*}\circ g_{1}^{*}\overset{h_{2}\text{ is smooth}}{\simeq}h_{2}^{*}\circ f^{\prime}_{\blacktriangle}\circ g_{1}^{*},

which is the right-hand side in (
A.10
).

A.4.
Verdier self-duality

In this subsection we continue to assume that our substacks are quasi-compact
(and Verdier-compatible).

We will show how the Verdier (anti)-involution
\operatorname{Shv}({\mathcal{Y}})^{c}

gives rise to a self-duality of
\operatorname{Shv}({\mathcal{Y}})
as a DG category.

A.4.1.

By assumption, the functor (
A.4
) induces an equivalence

(A.11)

{\mathbb{D}}^{\operatorname{Verdier}}:(\operatorname{Shv}({\mathcal{Y}})^{c})^{\operatorname{op}}\to\operatorname{Shv}({\mathcal{Y}})^{c}.

Hence, we obtain an identification

(A.12)

\operatorname{Shv}({\mathcal{Y}})^{\vee}\simeq\operatorname{Shv}({\mathcal{Y}}),

see Sect.
0.5.3
.

A.4.2.

We claim that the counit of the duality (
A.12
) is given by the functor
\operatorname{ev}_{\mathcal{Y}}

(A.13)

\operatorname{Shv}({\mathcal{Y}})\otimes\operatorname{Shv}({\mathcal{Y}})\overset{\overset{!}{\otimes}}{\to}\operatorname{Shv}({\mathcal{Y}})\overset{\operatorname{C}^{\cdot}_{\blacktriangle}({\mathcal{Y}},-)}{\longrightarrow}\operatorname{Vect}.

Indeed, this follows from (
A.5
).

A.4.3.

The unit of the duality (
A.12
) is an object that we denote

\operatorname{u}_{\operatorname{Shv}({\mathcal{Y}})}\in\operatorname{Shv}({\mathcal{Y}})\otimes\operatorname{Shv}({\mathcal{Y}}).

This object should not be confused with the object

(A.14)

\operatorname{u}_{\mathcal{Y}}=(\Delta_{\mathcal{Y}})_{*}(\omega_{\mathcal{Y}})\in\operatorname{Shv}({\mathcal{Y}}\times{\mathcal{Y}}),

see Sect.
2.1.2
.

A.4.4.

It follows from the projection formula that for a map
f:{\mathcal{Y}}_{1}\to{\mathcal{Y}}_{2}
between algebraic stacks, we have

(f^{!})^{\vee}\simeq f_{\blacktriangle},

where we identify

\operatorname{Shv}({\mathcal{Y}}_{1})^{\vee}\simeq\operatorname{Shv}({\mathcal{Y}}_{1})\text{ and }\operatorname{Shv}({\mathcal{Y}}_{2})^{\vee}\simeq\operatorname{Shv}({\mathcal{Y}}_{2})

by means of (
A.12
).

A.5.
Specifying singular support

In this subsection we will see how the notions reviewed in the previous subsection interact with
the condition imposed by singular support.

A.5.1.

Let
{\mathcal{N}}
be a conical Zariski-closed subset of
T^{*}({\mathcal{Y}})
(see
[
AGKRRV
, Sects. F.6.1-F.6.2]
).
In this case, following
[
AGKRRV
, Sect. F.6.3]
,
we define a full subcategory

\operatorname{Shv}_{\mathcal{N}}({\mathcal{Y}})\subset\operatorname{Shv}({\mathcal{Y}}).

For
{\mathcal{N}}
being the zero-section
\{0\}
and
{\mathcal{Y}}
smooth, we will use the notation

\operatorname{QLisse}({\mathcal{Y}}):=\operatorname{Shv}_{\{0\}}({\mathcal{Y}}).

A.5.2.

Following
[
AGKRRV
, Sect. F.7]
, we shall say that the pair
({\mathcal{Y}},{\mathcal{N}})
is

constraccessible
if
\operatorname{Shv}_{\mathcal{N}}({\mathcal{Y}})
is generated by objects that are compact in
\operatorname{Shv}({\mathcal{Y}})
.

In general, it may happen that
\operatorname{Shv}_{\mathcal{N}}({\mathcal{Y}})
is compactly generated as an abstract DG category,
but its compact generators are
not
compact as objects of
\operatorname{Shv}({\mathcal{Y}})
. For example, this happens for
{\mathcal{Y}}={\mathbb{P}}^{1}
,

{\mathcal{N}}=\{0\}
, see
[
AGKRRV
, Sect. E.2.6]
.

Remark A.5.3
.

The above definition of constraccessibility is slightly more restrictive than the one given in

[
AGKRRV
, Definition F.7.5]
. However, the two definitions agree under our assumptions
on algebraic stacks (see Sect.
0.5.1
) by
[
AGKRRV
, Proposition F.7.9]
.

If
{\mathcal{Y}}
is non-quasi-compact, but is
{\mathcal{N}}
-truncatable (see Sect.
C.1.6
below),
then the two definitions agree by
[
AGKRRV
, Corollary F.8.11]
.

A.5.4.

Assume for a moment that
{\mathcal{Y}}
is quasi-compact.

We shall say that the pair
({\mathcal{Y}},{\mathcal{N}})
is
duality-adapted
if the restriction of (
A.13
) along

\operatorname{Shv}_{\mathcal{N}}({\mathcal{Y}})\otimes\operatorname{Shv}_{\mathcal{N}}({\mathcal{Y}})\to\operatorname{Shv}({\mathcal{Y}})\otimes\operatorname{Shv}({\mathcal{Y}})

defines the counit of a self-duality.

This is always the case when
({\mathcal{Y}},{\mathcal{N}})
is constraccessible: indeed,
the resulting contravariant self-equivalence

(\operatorname{Shv}_{\mathcal{N}}({\mathcal{Y}})^{c})^{\operatorname{op}}\to\operatorname{Shv}_{\mathcal{N}}({\mathcal{Y}})^{c}

is induced by restricting the Verdier duality functor
{\mathbb{D}}^{\operatorname{Verdier}}
to

\operatorname{Shv}_{\mathcal{N}}({\mathcal{Y}})^{c}\simeq\operatorname{Shv}_{\mathcal{N}}({\mathcal{Y}})\cap\operatorname{Shv}({\mathcal{Y}})^{c}\subset\operatorname{Shv}({\mathcal{Y}})^{\operatorname{constr}}.

However, the pair
({\mathcal{Y}},{\mathcal{N}})
may be duality-adapted
without being constraccessible
, see again

[
AGKRRV
, Sect. E.2.6 and Corollary E.4.7]
.

A.5.5.

According to
[
AGKRRV
, Corollary E.4.7]
, if
X
is a smooth algebraic curve, then the pair
(X,\{0\})
is
duality-adapted.

A.6.
Categorical Künneth formulas

Let
{\mathcal{Y}}_{1}
and
{\mathcal{Y}}_{2}
be a pair of algebraic stacks. External tensor product defines a functor

(A.15)

\operatorname{Shv}({\mathcal{Y}}_{1})\otimes\operatorname{Shv}({\mathcal{Y}}_{2})\overset{\boxtimes}{\to}\operatorname{Shv}({\mathcal{Y}}_{1}\times{\mathcal{Y}}_{2}),

which is easily seen to be fully faithful. However, (
A.15
) is rarely an equivalence.

Categorical Künneth formulas are assertions to the effect that (
A.15
) becomes
an isomorphism after adjusting both sides. One such assertion is formulated here as
Theorem
A.6.5
.

A.6.1.

Let
{\mathcal{N}}_{i}\subset T^{*}({\mathcal{Y}}_{i})
be conical Zariski-closed subsets. Then the functor (
A.15
)
gives rise to a functor

(A.16)

\operatorname{Shv}_{{\mathcal{N}}_{1}}({\mathcal{Y}}_{1})\otimes\operatorname{Shv}_{{\mathcal{N}}_{2}}({\mathcal{Y}}_{2})\to\operatorname{Shv}_{{\mathcal{N}}_{1}\times{\mathcal{N}}_{2}}({\mathcal{Y}}_{1}\times{\mathcal{Y}}_{2}).

If one of the categories
\operatorname{Shv}_{{\mathcal{N}}_{1}}({\mathcal{Y}}_{1})
or
\operatorname{Shv}_{{\mathcal{N}}_{2}}({\mathcal{Y}}_{2})
is dualizable, then
(
A.16
) is also fully faithful.

A.6.2.

Throughout the paper, we use the following notation. Let
{\mathcal{N}}_{1}\subset T^{*}({\mathcal{Y}}_{1})
be a closed conical subset.

Let

\operatorname{Shv}_{{\mathcal{N}}_{1}\times\frac{1}{2}\operatorname{-dim}}({\mathcal{Y}}_{1}\times{\mathcal{Y}}_{2})\subset\operatorname{Shv}({\mathcal{Y}}_{1}\times{\mathcal{Y}}_{2})

be the full subcategory consisting of objects
{\mathcal{F}}
with the property that for every
m
and every constructible sub-object

{\mathcal{F}}^{\prime}
of
H^{m}({\mathcal{F}}^{\prime})
, the singular support of
{\mathcal{F}}^{\prime}
is contained in a subset of the form

{\mathcal{N}}_{1}\times{\mathcal{N}}_{2}\subset T^{*}({\mathcal{Y}}_{1}\times{\mathcal{Y}}_{2}),

where
{\mathcal{N}}_{2}\subset T^{*}({\mathcal{Y}}_{2})
is
half-dimensional
.

A.6.3.

We have the obvious inclusion

\operatorname{Shv}_{{\mathcal{N}}_{1}\times\frac{1}{2}\operatorname{-dim}}({\mathcal{Y}}_{1}\times{\mathcal{Y}}_{2})\subset\operatorname{Shv}_{{\mathcal{N}}_{1}\times T^{*}({\mathcal{Y}}_{2})}({\mathcal{Y}}_{1}\times{\mathcal{Y}}_{2}).

If
{\mathcal{N}}_{1}
is Lagrangian
and

\operatorname{char}(k)=0
, then the above inclusion is an equality: this follows from the Lagrangian
property of the singular support. But if
\operatorname{char}(k)\neq 0
, this may be a proper inclusion.

Note that the image of the (fully faithful) functor

\operatorname{Shv}_{{\mathcal{N}}_{1}}({\mathcal{Y}}_{1})\otimes\operatorname{Shv}({\mathcal{Y}}_{2})\to\operatorname{Shv}({\mathcal{Y}}_{1}\times{\mathcal{Y}}_{2})

is contained in
\operatorname{Shv}_{{\mathcal{N}}_{1}\times\frac{1}{2}\operatorname{-dim}}({\mathcal{Y}}_{1}\times{\mathcal{Y}}_{2})
.

A.6.4.

Assume now that
{\mathcal{Y}}_{1}=Y_{1}
is a smooth and proper scheme. In this case, we have the following assertion
(see
[
AGKRRV
, Theorems E.9.5 and F.9.7]
):

Theorem A.6.5
.

(a)
Assume that
\operatorname{QLisse}(Y_{1})
is dualizable as a DG category. Then the functor

\operatorname{QLisse}(Y_{1})\otimes\operatorname{Shv}({\mathcal{Y}}_{2})\to\operatorname{Shv}_{\{0\}\times\frac{1}{2}\operatorname{-dim}}(Y_{1}\times{\mathcal{Y}}_{2})

is an equivalence.

(b)
Assume that the pair
(Y_{1},\{0\})
is duality-adapted. Then for a half-dimensional closed conical
subset
{\mathcal{N}}_{2}\subset T^{*}({\mathcal{Y}}_{2})
, the functor

\operatorname{QLisse}(Y_{1})\otimes\operatorname{Shv}_{{\mathcal{N}}_{2}}({\mathcal{Y}}_{2})\to\operatorname{Shv}_{\{0\}\times{\mathcal{N}}_{2}}(Y_{1}\times{\mathcal{Y}}_{2})

is an equivalence.

Appendix B
Functors defined by kernels and miraculous duality

Some of the key methods employed in the bulk of this paper have to do
with the formalism of
functors defined by kernels
. In this subsection
we review the relevant theory.

We should also mentioned that for the main results of this paper, all we need is
the contents of Sect.
B.1
. Other subsections of this section are needed for
the material in Sect.
4
.

B.1.
Functors defined by kernels

In this subsection we introduce what we mean by functors defined by kernels.

B.1.1.

Let
{\mathcal{Y}}_{1}
and
{\mathcal{Y}}_{2}
be quasi-compact algebraic stacks. The

category of functors
\operatorname{Shv}({\mathcal{Y}}_{1})\to\operatorname{Shv}({\mathcal{Y}}_{2})
defined by kernels

is by definition

\operatorname{Shv}({\mathcal{Y}}_{1}\times{\mathcal{Y}}_{2}).

An object
{\mathcal{Q}}\in\operatorname{Shv}({\mathcal{Y}}_{1}\times{\mathcal{Y}}_{2})
defines an actual functor

(B.1)

{\mathsf{Q}}:\operatorname{Shv}({\mathcal{Y}}_{1})\to\operatorname{Shv}({\mathcal{Y}}_{2}),\quad{\mathcal{F}}\mapsto(p_{2})_{\blacktriangle}(p_{1}^{!}({\mathcal{F}})\overset{!}{\otimes}{\mathcal{Q}}).

We shall say that a given functor
\operatorname{Shv}({\mathcal{Y}}_{1})\to\operatorname{Shv}({\mathcal{Y}}_{2})
is defined by a kernel if it comes
from an object in
\operatorname{Shv}({\mathcal{Y}}_{1}\times{\mathcal{Y}}_{2})
by the above procedure.

B.1.2.

For an object
{\mathcal{Q}}\in\operatorname{Shv}({\mathcal{Y}}_{1}\times{\mathcal{Y}}_{2})
we will denote by
{\mathcal{Q}}^{\sigma}
the object of

\operatorname{Shv}({\mathcal{Y}}_{2}\times{\mathcal{Y}}_{1})
obtained by swapping the two factors.

The corresponding functor
{\mathsf{Q}}^{\sigma}
is the dual functor of
{\mathsf{Q}}
with respect to the Verdier
self-duality of
\operatorname{Shv}({\mathcal{Y}}_{i})
:

\operatorname{ev}_{{\mathcal{Y}}_{1}}({\mathcal{F}}_{1}\otimes{\mathsf{Q}}^{\sigma}({\mathcal{F}}_{2}))\simeq\operatorname{ev}_{{\mathcal{Y}}_{2}}({\mathsf{Q}}({\mathcal{F}}_{1})\otimes{\mathcal{F}}_{2})

(see (
A.13
) for the notation
\operatorname{ev}_{\mathcal{Y}}
).

B.1.3.

More generally, for any algebraic stack
{\mathcal{Z}}
we have a well-defined
functor

(B.2)

\operatorname{Id}_{\mathcal{Z}}\boxtimes{\mathsf{Q}}:\operatorname{Shv}({\mathcal{Z}}\times{\mathcal{Y}}_{1})\to\operatorname{Shv}({\mathcal{Z}}\times{\mathcal{Y}}_{2}),\quad{\mathcal{F}}\mapsto(p_{{\mathcal{Z}},{\mathcal{Y}}_{2}})_{\blacktriangle}(p_{{\mathcal{Z}},{\mathcal{Y}}_{1}}^{!}({\mathcal{F}})\overset{!}{\otimes}p_{{\mathcal{Y}}_{1},{\mathcal{Y}}_{2}}^{!}({\mathcal{Q}})),

where
p_{{\mathcal{Z}},{\mathcal{Y}}_{1}}
,
p_{{\mathcal{Z}},{\mathcal{Y}}_{2}}
and
p_{{\mathcal{Y}}_{1},{\mathcal{Y}}_{2}}
are the maps

{\mathcal{Z}}\times{\mathcal{Y}}_{1}\times{\mathcal{Y}}_{2}\to{\mathcal{Y}}_{1}\times{\mathcal{Y}}_{2},\,\,{\mathcal{Z}}\times{\mathcal{Y}}_{1}\times{\mathcal{Y}}_{2}\to{\mathcal{Z}}\times{\mathcal{Y}}_{1}\text{ and }{\mathcal{Z}}\times{\mathcal{Y}}_{1}\times{\mathcal{Y}}_{2}\to{\mathcal{Z}}\times{\mathcal{Y}}_{2},

respectively.

Note that we have a commutative diagram

(B.3)

\begin{CD}\operatorname{Shv}({\mathcal{Z}})\otimes\operatorname{Shv}({\mathcal{Y}}_{1})@>{}>{}>\operatorname{Shv}({\mathcal{Z}}\times{\mathcal{Y}}_{1})\\
@V{\operatorname{Id}_{\operatorname{Shv}({\mathcal{Z}})}\otimes{\mathsf{Q}}}V{}V@V{}V{\operatorname{Id}_{\mathcal{Z}}\boxtimes{\mathsf{Q}}}V\\
\operatorname{Shv}({\mathcal{Z}})\otimes\operatorname{Shv}({\mathcal{Y}}_{2})@>{}>{}>\operatorname{Shv}({\mathcal{Z}}\times{\mathcal{Y}}_{2}).\end{CD}

B.1.4.

It follows from the description of compact generators in Sect.
A.2.2
that if
{\mathcal{Q}}
is compact, then
the functors
\operatorname{Id}_{\mathcal{Z}}\boxtimes{\mathsf{Q}}
send compact objects to compact objects.

B.1.5.

Using Sect.
A.3.1
, we obtain that
the functors
\operatorname{Id}_{\mathcal{Z}}\boxtimes{\mathsf{Q}}
commute with the following operations:

•

For a map
f:{\mathcal{Z}}^{\prime}\to{\mathcal{Z}}
, the diagram

\begin{CD}\operatorname{Shv}({\mathcal{Z}}^{\prime}\times{\mathcal{Y}}_{1})@>{\operatorname{Id}_{{\mathcal{Z}}^{\prime}}\boxtimes{\mathsf{Q}}}>{}>\operatorname{Shv}({\mathcal{Z}}^{\prime}\times{\mathcal{Y}}_{2})\\
@V{(f\times\operatorname{id})_{\blacktriangle}}V{}V@V{}V{(f\times\operatorname{id})_{\blacktriangle}}V\\
\operatorname{Shv}({\mathcal{Z}}\times{\mathcal{Y}}_{1})@>{\operatorname{Id}_{\mathcal{Z}}\boxtimes{\mathsf{Q}}}>{}>\operatorname{Shv}({\mathcal{Z}}\times{\mathcal{Y}}_{2})\end{CD}

commutes.

•

For a map
f:{\mathcal{Z}}^{\prime}\to{\mathcal{Z}}
, the diagram

\begin{CD}\operatorname{Shv}({\mathcal{Z}}^{\prime}\times{\mathcal{Y}}_{1})@>{\operatorname{Id}_{{\mathcal{Z}}^{\prime}}\boxtimes{\mathsf{Q}}}>{}>\operatorname{Shv}({\mathcal{Z}}^{\prime}\times{\mathcal{Y}}_{2})\\
@A{(f\times\operatorname{id})^{!}}A{}A@A{}A{(f\times\operatorname{id})^{!}}A\\
\operatorname{Shv}({\mathcal{Z}}\times{\mathcal{Y}}_{1})@>{\operatorname{Id}_{\mathcal{Z}}\boxtimes{\mathsf{Q}}}>{}>\operatorname{Shv}({\mathcal{Z}}\times{\mathcal{Y}}_{2})\end{CD}

commutes.

•

For
{\mathcal{F}}\in\operatorname{Shv}({\mathcal{Z}})
, the diagram

\begin{CD}\operatorname{Shv}({\mathcal{Z}}\times{\mathcal{Z}}^{\prime}\times{\mathcal{Y}}_{1})@>{\operatorname{Id}_{{\mathcal{Z}}\times{\mathcal{Z}}^{\prime}}\boxtimes{\mathsf{Q}}}>{}>\operatorname{Shv}({\mathcal{Z}}\times{\mathcal{Z}}^{\prime}\times{\mathcal{Y}}_{2})\\
@A{{\mathcal{F}}\boxtimes-}A{}A@A{}A{{\mathcal{F}}\boxtimes-}A\\
\operatorname{Shv}({\mathcal{Z}}^{\prime}\times{\mathcal{Y}}_{1})@>{\operatorname{Id}_{{\mathcal{Z}}^{\prime}}\boxtimes{\mathsf{Q}}}>{}>\operatorname{Shv}({\mathcal{Z}}\times{\mathcal{Y}}_{2})\end{CD}

commutes.

Vice versa, a system of functors
\operatorname{Id}_{\mathcal{Z}}\boxtimes{\mathsf{Q}}
as in (
B.2
) that satisfies the above three properties,
satisfying an appropriate system of compatibilities for diagrams of morphisms between the
{\mathcal{Z}}
’s,
comes from a uniquely defined object
{\mathcal{Q}}\in\operatorname{Shv}({\mathcal{Y}}_{1}\times{\mathcal{Y}}_{2})
. Namely,
{\mathcal{Q}}
is recovered as

(\operatorname{Id}_{{\mathcal{Y}}_{1}}\boxtimes{\mathsf{Q}})(\operatorname{u}_{{\mathcal{Y}}_{1}}),

where
\operatorname{u}_{{\mathcal{Y}}_{1}}
is as in (
A.14
).

B.1.6.

Let
{\mathcal{Q}}\in\operatorname{Shv}({\mathcal{Y}}_{1}\times{\mathcal{Y}}_{2})
and
{\mathcal{Q}}^{\prime}\in\operatorname{Shv}({\mathcal{Y}}^{\prime}_{1}\times{\mathcal{Y}}^{\prime}_{2})
be a pair of objects.
We can consider

({\mathcal{Q}}\boxtimes{\mathcal{Q}}^{\prime})^{\sigma_{2,3}}\in\operatorname{Shv}(({\mathcal{Y}}_{1}\times{\mathcal{Y}}^{\prime}_{1})\times({\mathcal{Y}}_{2}\times{\mathcal{Y}}^{\prime}_{2})).

We will denote the corresponding functor
\operatorname{Shv}({\mathcal{Y}}_{1}\times{\mathcal{Y}}^{\prime}_{1})\to\operatorname{Shv}({\mathcal{Y}}_{2}\times{\mathcal{Y}}^{\prime}_{2})
by
{\mathsf{Q}}\boxtimes{\mathsf{Q}}^{\prime}
.
We have

(B.4)

({\mathsf{Q}}\boxtimes\operatorname{Id}_{{\mathcal{Y}}^{\prime}_{2}})\circ(\operatorname{Id}_{{\mathcal{Y}}_{1}}\boxtimes{\mathsf{Q}}^{\prime})\simeq{\mathsf{Q}}\boxtimes{\mathsf{Q}}^{\prime}\simeq(\operatorname{Id}_{{\mathcal{Y}}_{2}}\boxtimes{\mathsf{Q}}^{\prime})\circ({\mathsf{Q}}\boxtimes\operatorname{Id}_{{\mathcal{Y}}^{\prime}_{1}}).

B.1.7.

For
{\mathcal{Q}}_{1,2}\in\operatorname{Shv}({\mathcal{Y}}_{1}\times{\mathcal{Y}}_{2})
and
{\mathcal{Q}}_{2,3}\in\operatorname{Shv}({\mathcal{Y}}_{2}\times{\mathcal{Y}}_{3})
we have a naturally defined composition

{\mathcal{Q}}_{1,3}:={\mathcal{Q}}_{2,3}\star{\mathcal{Q}}_{1,2}:=(p_{1,3})_{\blacktriangle}(p_{2,3}^{!}({\mathcal{Q}}_{2,3})\overset{!}{\otimes}p_{1,2}^{!}({\mathcal{Q}}_{1,2}))\in\operatorname{Shv}({\mathcal{Y}}_{1}\times{\mathcal{Y}}_{3}).

From Sect.
A.3.1
, we obtain that for any
{\mathcal{Z}}
, we have

\operatorname{Id}_{\mathcal{Z}}\boxtimes{\mathsf{Q}}_{1,3}\simeq(\operatorname{Id}_{\mathcal{Z}}\boxtimes{\mathsf{Q}}_{2,3})\circ(\operatorname{Id}_{\mathcal{Z}}\boxtimes{\mathsf{Q}}_{1,2}).

Note that we have

(B.5)

(\operatorname{Id}_{{\mathcal{Y}}_{1}}\boxtimes{\mathsf{Q}}_{2,3})({\mathcal{Q}}_{1,2})\simeq{\mathcal{Q}}_{1,3}\simeq({\mathsf{Q}}_{1,2}\boxtimes\operatorname{Id}_{{\mathcal{Y}}_{3}})({\mathcal{Q}}_{2,3}).

B.1.8.

We can form an
(\infty,2)
-category whose objects are algebraic stacks, and whose categories of 1-morphisms
are
\operatorname{Shv}({\mathcal{Y}}_{1}\times{\mathcal{Y}}_{2})
, with composition functors defined as above. (For the purposes of this paper,
we only need the
homotopy category
of this
(\infty,2)
-category, i.e., we can replace the
spaces

of 2-morphisms by their sets of connected components.)

For a given
{\mathcal{Y}}
, the unit 1-morphism in the above 2-category is given by the object

(B.6)

\operatorname{u}_{\mathcal{Y}}=(\Delta_{\mathcal{Y}})_{*}(\omega_{\mathcal{Y}})\in\operatorname{Shv}({\mathcal{Y}}\times{\mathcal{Y}})

of (
A.14
).

B.2.
A discontinuous version

B.2.1.

Note that in addition to the functor (
B.1
), one can consider the functor

(B.7)

{\mathsf{Q}}_{\operatorname{disc}}:\operatorname{Shv}({\mathcal{Y}}_{1})\to\operatorname{Shv}({\mathcal{Y}}_{2}),\quad{\mathcal{F}}\mapsto(p_{2})_{*}(p_{1}^{!}({\mathcal{F}})\overset{!}{\otimes}{\mathcal{Q}}).

We have a tautologically defined map

(B.8)

{\mathsf{Q}}\to{\mathsf{Q}}_{\operatorname{disc}}.

It follows from the description of compact generators of
\operatorname{Shv}(-)
in Sect.
A.2.2
and
the projection formula that
the natural transformation (
B.8
) is an isomorphism when evaluated
on compact objects.

In particular, both sides of (
B.8
) send compact objects to constructible ones.

Similarly, (
B.8
) is an isomorphism if
{\mathcal{Q}}
is compact as an object of
\operatorname{Shv}({\mathcal{Y}}_{1}\times{\mathcal{Y}}_{2})
.

If
{\mathcal{Q}}
is just constructible, in general, neither side in (
B.8
) preserves constructibility. However, the functor
{\mathsf{Q}}

has a cohomological amplitude bounded on the right, and the functor
{\mathsf{Q}}_{\operatorname{disc}}

has a cohomological amplitude bounded on the left, and both functors send constructible objects to
objects with constructible perverse cohomologies, see Sect.
A.2.7
.

B.2.2.

We can similarly consider the (a priori, discontinuous) functors

\operatorname{Id}_{\mathcal{Z}}\boxtimes{\mathsf{Q}}_{\operatorname{disc}}:\operatorname{Shv}({\mathcal{Z}}\times{\mathcal{Y}}_{1})\to\operatorname{Shv}({\mathcal{Z}}\times{\mathcal{Y}}_{2}),

namely

(\operatorname{Id}_{\mathcal{Z}}\boxtimes{\mathsf{Q}}_{\operatorname{disc}})({\mathcal{F}}):=(p_{{\mathcal{Z}},{\mathcal{Y}}_{2}})_{*}(p_{{\mathcal{Z}},{\mathcal{Y}}_{1}}^{!}({\mathcal{F}})\overset{!}{\otimes}p_{{\mathcal{Y}}_{1},{\mathcal{Y}}_{2}}^{!}({\mathcal{Q}})).

The functors
\operatorname{Id}_{\mathcal{Z}}\boxtimes{\mathsf{Q}}_{\operatorname{disc}}
make the diagrams similar to ones in Sect.
B.1.5
commute, where
in the first diagram we use
-_{*}
instead of
-_{\blacktriangle}
.

We have the natural transformations

(B.9)

\operatorname{Id}_{\mathcal{Z}}\boxtimes{\mathsf{Q}}\to\operatorname{Id}_{\mathcal{Z}}\boxtimes{\mathsf{Q}}_{\operatorname{disc}}.

B.2.3.

We shall say that
{\mathsf{Q}}
is
safe
(as a functor defined by a kernel) if the natural transformations
(
B.9
) are isomorphisms.

By definition,
{\mathsf{Q}}
is safe if and only if the functors
\operatorname{Id}_{\mathcal{Z}}\boxtimes{\mathsf{Q}}_{\operatorname{disc}}
are
actually continuous.

For example, if
{\mathcal{Q}}
is compact as an object of
\operatorname{Shv}({\mathcal{Y}}_{1}\times{\mathcal{Y}}_{2})
, then
{\mathsf{Q}}
is safe.

In addition,
{\mathsf{Q}}
is automatically safe if
{\mathcal{Y}}_{1}
is a scheme.

B.2.4.

It follows from Proposition
A.2.8
that if
{\mathcal{Q}}
and
{\mathcal{F}}
are both constructible,
the following conditions are equivalent:

(i) The natural transformation (
B.9
) becomes an isomorphism when evaluated on
{\mathcal{F}}
;

(ii) The object
(\operatorname{Id}_{\mathcal{Z}}\boxtimes{\mathsf{Q}})({\mathcal{F}})
is cohomologically bounded on the left;

(iii) The object
(\operatorname{Id}_{\mathcal{Z}}\boxtimes{\mathsf{Q}}_{\operatorname{disc}})({\mathcal{F}})
is cohomologically bounded on the right.

In particular, we obtain that if
{\mathcal{Q}}
is constructible and
{\mathsf{Q}}
safe, then the functors in (
B.9
) preserve constructibility
and are of finite cohomological dimension.

B.2.5.

Given two objects
{\mathcal{Q}}_{1,2}\in\operatorname{Shv}({\mathcal{Y}}_{1}\times{\mathcal{Y}}_{2})
and
{\mathcal{Q}}_{2,3}\in\operatorname{Shv}({\mathcal{Y}}_{2}\times{\mathcal{Y}}_{3})
we can form

{\mathcal{Q}}_{1,3}:={\mathcal{Q}}_{2,3}\star_{\operatorname{disc}}{\mathcal{Q}}_{1,2}:=(p_{1,3})_{*}(p_{2,3}^{!}({\mathcal{Q}}_{2,3})\overset{!}{\otimes}p_{1,2}^{!}({\mathcal{Q}}_{1,2}))]\in\operatorname{Shv}({\mathcal{Y}}_{1}\times{\mathcal{Y}}_{3}).

We have

\operatorname{Id}_{\mathcal{Z}}\boxtimes({\mathsf{Q}}_{1,3})_{\operatorname{disc}}\simeq(\operatorname{Id}_{\mathcal{Z}}\boxtimes({\mathsf{Q}}_{2,3})_{\operatorname{disc}})\circ(\operatorname{Id}_{\mathcal{Z}}\boxtimes({\mathsf{Q}}_{1,2})_{\operatorname{disc}}).

The natural transformation (
B.9
) gives rise to a map

{\mathcal{Q}}_{2,3}\star{\mathcal{Q}}_{1,2}\to{\mathcal{Q}}_{2,3}\star_{\operatorname{disc}}{\mathcal{Q}}_{1,2},

which is an isomorphism if either
{\mathcal{Q}}_{2,3}
or
{\mathcal{Q}}_{1,2}^{\sigma}
is safe.

B.3.
Functors
co
-defined by kernels

In this subsection we explore the Verdier-dual notion: that of functors
codefined
by kernels.

A special family of interest is formed by functors that are both
defined and codefined
by kernels.

B.3.1.

Starting from an object
{\mathcal{P}}\in\operatorname{Shv}({\mathcal{Y}}_{1}\times{\mathcal{Y}}_{2})
we can produce another family of functors, denoted

(B.10)

\operatorname{Id}_{\mathcal{Z}}\boxtimes{\mathsf{P}}^{l}:\operatorname{Shv}({\mathcal{Z}}\times{\mathcal{Y}}_{1})\to\operatorname{Shv}({\mathcal{Z}}\times{\mathcal{Y}}_{2}),\quad{\mathcal{F}}\mapsto(p_{{\mathcal{Z}},{\mathcal{Y}}_{2}})_{!}(p_{{\mathcal{Z}},{\mathcal{Y}}_{1}}^{*}({\mathcal{F}})\overset{*}{\otimes}p_{{\mathcal{Y}}_{1},{\mathcal{Y}}_{2}}^{*}({\mathcal{P}})).

We will refer to this family as functors
codefined
by
{\mathcal{P}}
. They satisfy compatibility
conditions parallel to ones in Sect.
B.1.5
with
-^{!}
replaced by
-^{*}
and
-_{\blacktriangle}

replaced by
-_{!}
.

B.3.2.

Let
{\mathcal{P}}_{1,2}\in\operatorname{Shv}({\mathcal{Y}}_{1}\times{\mathcal{Y}}_{2})
and
{\mathcal{P}}_{2,3}\in\operatorname{Shv}({\mathcal{Y}}_{2}\times{\mathcal{Y}}_{3})
be a pair of objects.
Define

{\mathcal{P}}_{1,3}:={\mathcal{P}}_{2,3}\overset{l}{\star}{\mathcal{P}}_{1,2}:=(p_{1,3})_{!}(p_{2,3}^{*}({\mathcal{Q}}_{2,3})\overset{*}{\otimes}p_{1,2}^{*}({\mathcal{Q}}_{1,2}))\in\operatorname{Shv}({\mathcal{Y}}_{1}\times{\mathcal{Y}}_{3}).

We have

\operatorname{Id}_{\mathcal{Z}}\boxtimes{\mathsf{P}}^{l}_{1,3}\simeq(\operatorname{Id}_{\mathcal{Z}}\boxtimes{\mathsf{P}}^{l}_{2,3})\circ(\operatorname{Id}_{\mathcal{Z}}\boxtimes{\mathsf{P}}^{l}_{1,2}).

Furthermore,

(B.11)

(\operatorname{Id}_{{\mathcal{Y}}_{1}}\boxtimes{\mathsf{P}}^{l}_{2,3})({\mathcal{P}}_{1,2})\simeq{\mathcal{P}}_{1,3}\simeq({\mathsf{P}}^{l}_{1,2}\boxtimes\operatorname{Id}_{{\mathcal{Y}}_{3}})({\mathcal{P}}_{2,3}).

B.3.3.

Let
{\mathcal{Q}}\in\operatorname{Shv}({\mathcal{Y}}_{1}\times{\mathcal{Y}}_{2})
be constructible, and set
{\mathcal{P}}:={\mathbb{D}}^{\operatorname{Verdier}}({\mathcal{Q}})
. Let
{\mathcal{F}}\in\operatorname{Shv}({\mathcal{Z}}\times{\mathcal{Y}}_{1})^{\operatorname{constr}}

be an object satisfying the equivalent conditions of Sect.
B.2.4
.

We obtain that in this case

(\operatorname{Id}_{\mathcal{Z}}\boxtimes{\mathsf{P}}^{l})({\mathbb{D}}^{\operatorname{Verdier}}({\mathcal{F}}))\simeq{\mathbb{D}}^{\operatorname{Verdier}}\left((\operatorname{Id}_{\mathcal{Z}}\boxtimes{\mathsf{Q}}_{\operatorname{disc}})({\mathcal{F}})\right)\simeq{\mathbb{D}}^{\operatorname{Verdier}}\left((\operatorname{Id}_{\mathcal{Z}}\boxtimes{\mathsf{Q}})({\mathcal{F}})\right).

B.3.4.

Let us be given two objects

{\mathcal{Q}}\in\operatorname{Shv}({\mathcal{Y}}_{1}\times{\mathcal{Y}}_{2})\text{ and }\widetilde{\mathcal{P}}\in\operatorname{Shv}(\widetilde{\mathcal{Y}}_{1}\times\widetilde{\mathcal{Y}}_{2}).

From Sects.
A.3.3
-
A.3.4
, we obtain a natural transformation

(B.12)

(\operatorname{Id}_{{\mathcal{Y}}_{2}}\boxtimes\widetilde{\mathsf{P}}^{l})\circ({\mathsf{Q}}\boxtimes\operatorname{Id}_{\widetilde{\mathcal{Y}}_{1}})\to({\mathsf{Q}}\boxtimes\operatorname{Id}_{\widetilde{\mathcal{Y}}_{2}})\circ(\operatorname{Id}_{{\mathcal{Y}}_{1}}\boxtimes\widetilde{\mathsf{P}}^{l}),\quad\operatorname{Shv}({\mathcal{Y}}_{1}\times\widetilde{\mathcal{Y}}_{1})\rightrightarrows\operatorname{Shv}({\mathcal{Y}}_{2}\times\widetilde{\mathcal{Y}}_{2}),

i.e., a 2-morphism in the diagram

(B.13)

\textstyle{\operatorname{Shv}({\mathcal{Y}}_{1}\times\widetilde{\mathcal{Y}}_{1})}
\textstyle{\operatorname{Shv}({\mathcal{Y}}_{2}\times\widetilde{\mathcal{Y}}_{1})}
\textstyle{\operatorname{Shv}({\mathcal{Y}}_{1}\times\widetilde{\mathcal{Y}}_{2})}
\textstyle{\operatorname{Shv}({\mathcal{Y}}_{2}\times\widetilde{\mathcal{Y}}_{2})}
\scriptstyle{{\mathsf{Q}}\boxtimes\operatorname{Id}_{\widetilde{\mathcal{Y}}_{1}}}
\scriptstyle{{\mathsf{Q}}\boxtimes\operatorname{Id}_{\widetilde{\mathcal{Y}}_{2}}}
\scriptstyle{\operatorname{Id}_{{\mathcal{Y}}_{1}}\boxtimes\widetilde{\mathsf{P}}^{l}}
\scriptstyle{\operatorname{Id}_{{\mathcal{Y}}_{2}}\boxtimes\widetilde{\mathsf{P}}^{l}}

B.3.5.

Similarly (but more tautologically), we obtain a natural transformation

(B.14)

(\operatorname{Id}_{{\mathcal{Y}}_{2}}\boxtimes\widetilde{\mathsf{P}}^{l})\circ({\mathsf{Q}}_{\operatorname{disc}}\boxtimes\operatorname{Id}_{\widetilde{\mathcal{Y}}_{1}})\to({\mathsf{Q}}_{\operatorname{disc}}\boxtimes\operatorname{Id}_{\widetilde{\mathcal{Y}}_{2}})\circ(\operatorname{Id}_{{\mathcal{Y}}_{1}}\boxtimes\widetilde{\mathsf{P}}^{l}).

B.3.6.

In particular, given a functor
{\mathsf{Q}}
defined by a kernel, the diagrams in Sect.
B.1.5

with
-^{!}
replaced by
-^{*}
and
-_{\blacktriangle}
replaced by
-_{!}
,
commute up to natural transformations
.

We shall say that
{\mathsf{Q}}
is
defined and codefined by kernels
if these natural transformations
are isomorphisms. In particular, a functor defined and codefined by a kernel gives rise to a functor
codefined by a kernel, i.e., it corresponds to an object
{\mathcal{P}}\in\operatorname{Shv}({\mathcal{Y}}_{1}\times{\mathcal{Y}}_{2})
, so that

(B.15)

\operatorname{Id}_{\mathcal{Z}}\boxtimes{\mathsf{Q}}\simeq\operatorname{Id}_{\mathcal{Z}}\boxtimes{\mathsf{P}}^{l}

for all
{\mathcal{Z}}
, in a way compatible with
!-
and
*-
pullbacks and
!-
and
\blacktriangle-

pushforwards.

Note that in this case, the natural transformations (
B.12
) are the
isomorphisms

(\operatorname{Id}_{{\mathcal{Y}}_{2}}\boxtimes\widetilde{\mathsf{P}}^{l})\circ({\mathsf{P}}^{l}\boxtimes\operatorname{Id}_{\widetilde{\mathcal{Y}}_{1}})\simeq{\mathsf{P}}^{l}\boxtimes\widetilde{\mathsf{P}}^{l}\simeq({\mathsf{P}}^{l}\boxtimes\operatorname{Id}_{\widetilde{\mathcal{Y}}_{2}})\circ(\operatorname{Id}_{{\mathcal{Y}}_{1}}\boxtimes\widetilde{\mathsf{P}}^{l}).

In what follows we will give an explicit description of
{\mathcal{P}}
in terms of
{\mathcal{Q}}
, see Proposition
B.4.4
.

B.3.7.

Similarly, given a functor
{\mathsf{P}}^{l}
codefined by a kernel
{\mathcal{P}}\in\operatorname{Shv}({\mathcal{Y}}_{1}\times{\mathcal{Y}}_{2})
, it may have the property that
it is
defined and codefined by kernels
, and thus corresponds to an object
{\mathcal{Q}}\in\operatorname{Shv}({\mathcal{Y}}_{1}\times{\mathcal{Y}}_{2})
.

B.4.
The miraculous functor

In this subsection we recall the construction of the
miraculous
endofunctor
of
\operatorname{Shv}({\mathcal{Y}})
, defined for any algebraic stack.

Apart from its other applications in the bulk of the paper, the miraculous functor allows
us to relate functors defined and codefined by kernels.

B.4.1.

Let
{\mathcal{Y}}
be a quasi-compact algebraic stack. The miraculous functor on
{\mathcal{Y}}
, denoted
\operatorname{Mir}_{\mathcal{Y}}
,
is the functor defined by the kernel

(B.16)

\operatorname{ps-u}_{\mathcal{Y}}:=(\Delta_{\mathcal{Y}})_{!}({\mathsf{e}}_{\mathcal{Y}}).

B.4.2.

For
{\mathcal{Q}}\in\operatorname{Shv}({\mathcal{Y}}_{1}\times{\mathcal{Y}}_{2})
, set

{\mathcal{P}}\simeq(\operatorname{Mir}_{{\mathcal{Y}}_{1}}\boxtimes\operatorname{Id}_{{\mathcal{Y}}_{2}})({\mathcal{Q}})\in\operatorname{Shv}({\mathcal{Y}}_{1}\times{\mathcal{Y}}_{2}).

We claim that there is a canonically defined natural transformation

(B.17)

\operatorname{Id}_{\mathcal{Z}}\boxtimes{\mathsf{P}}^{l}\to\operatorname{Id}_{\mathcal{Z}}\boxtimes{\mathsf{Q}}.

Indeed, the value of (
B.17
) on a given
{\mathcal{F}}\in\operatorname{Shv}({\mathcal{Z}}\times{\mathcal{Y}}_{1})
is the value on

\operatorname{ps-u}_{{\mathcal{Y}}_{1}}
of
the natural transformation (
B.13
) in the diagram

\textstyle{\operatorname{Shv}({\mathcal{Y}}_{1}\times{\mathcal{Y}}_{1})}
\textstyle{\operatorname{Shv}({\mathcal{Y}}_{1}\times{\mathcal{Y}}_{2})}
\textstyle{\operatorname{Shv}({\mathcal{Z}}\times{\mathcal{Y}}_{1})}
\textstyle{\operatorname{Shv}({\mathcal{Z}}\times{\mathcal{Y}}_{2})}
\scriptstyle{\operatorname{Id}_{{\mathcal{Y}}_{1}}\boxtimes{\mathsf{Q}}}
\scriptstyle{\operatorname{Id}_{{\mathcal{Z}}}\boxtimes{\mathsf{Q}}}
\scriptstyle{({\mathsf{F}}^{\sigma})^{l}\boxtimes\operatorname{Id}_{{\mathcal{Y}}_{1}}}
\scriptstyle{({\mathsf{F}}^{\sigma})^{l}\boxtimes\operatorname{Id}_{{\mathcal{Y}}_{2}}}

where
({\mathsf{F}}^{\sigma})^{l}
is the functor codefined by
{\mathcal{F}}^{\sigma}\in\operatorname{Shv}({\mathcal{Y}}_{1}\times{\mathcal{Z}})
.

B.4.3.

The following is immediate from the above diagram:

Proposition B.4.4
.

(a) A functor defined by
{\mathcal{Q}}
is defined and codefined by a kernel if and only if
the natural transformations (
B.17
) are isomorphisms (for all
{\mathcal{Z}}
).

(b) In the situation of point (a), the co-defining object is given by

{\mathcal{P}}\simeq(\operatorname{Mir}_{{\mathcal{Y}}_{1}}\boxtimes\operatorname{Id}_{{\mathcal{Y}}_{2}})({\mathcal{Q}}).

B.4.5.

Note that in the particular case of

{\mathcal{Y}}_{1}={\mathcal{Y}},\quad{\mathcal{Y}}_{2}={\mathcal{Z}}=\operatorname{pt},\quad{\mathcal{Q}}={\mathcal{F}}\in\operatorname{Shv}({\mathcal{Y}}),

the natural transformation (
B.17
) gives rise to a map

(B.18)

\operatorname{ev}_{\mathcal{Y}}^{l}({\mathcal{F}}^{\prime},\operatorname{Mir}_{\mathcal{Y}}({\mathcal{F}}))\to\operatorname{ev}_{\mathcal{Y}}({\mathcal{F}}^{\prime},{\mathcal{F}}),\quad{\mathcal{F}}^{\prime}\in\operatorname{Shv}({\mathcal{Y}}),

where we recall that
\operatorname{ev}^{l}_{\mathcal{Y}}(-,-)
and
\operatorname{ev}_{\mathcal{Y}}(-,-)
denote the pairings

\operatorname{C}^{\cdot}_{c}({\mathcal{Y}},-\overset{*}{\otimes}-)\text{ and }\operatorname{C}^{\cdot}_{\blacktriangle}({\mathcal{Y}},-\overset{!}{\otimes}-),

respectively.

Let us write down the map (
B.18
) explicitly. Namely, it is obtained by applying the natural
transformation

((\operatorname{C}^{\cdot}_{c}({\mathcal{Y}},-)\circ\Delta_{\mathcal{Y}}^{*})\boxtimes\operatorname{Id}_{\operatorname{pt}})\circ(\operatorname{Id}_{{\mathcal{Y}}\times{\mathcal{Y}}}\boxtimes(\operatorname{C}^{\cdot}_{\blacktriangle}({\mathcal{Y}},-)\circ\Delta_{\mathcal{Y}}^{!}))\to(\operatorname{Id}_{\operatorname{pt}}\boxtimes(\operatorname{C}^{\cdot}_{\blacktriangle}({\mathcal{Y}},-)\circ\Delta_{\mathcal{Y}}^{!}))\circ((\operatorname{C}^{\cdot}_{c}({\mathcal{Y}},-)\circ\Delta_{\mathcal{Y}}^{*})\boxtimes\operatorname{Id}_{{\mathcal{Y}}\times{\mathcal{Y}}})

of (
B.12
) to the object

{\mathcal{F}}\boxtimes\operatorname{ps-u}_{\mathcal{Y}}\boxtimes{\mathcal{F}}^{\prime}\in\operatorname{Shv}({\mathcal{Y}}\times{\mathcal{Y}}\times{\mathcal{Y}}\times{\mathcal{Y}}).

B.4.6.

Note also that in the situation of Sects.
B.1.7
and
B.3.2
, for

{\mathcal{Q}}_{1,2}\in\operatorname{Shv}({\mathcal{Y}}_{1}\times{\mathcal{Y}}_{2})\text{ and }{\mathcal{Q}}_{2,3}\in\operatorname{Shv}({\mathcal{Y}}_{2}\times{\mathcal{Y}}_{3})

we have a map

(B.19)

{\mathcal{Q}}_{2,3}\overset{l}{\star}((\operatorname{Id}_{{\mathcal{Y}}_{1}}\boxtimes\operatorname{Mir}_{{\mathcal{Y}}_{2}})({\mathcal{Q}}_{1,2}))\to{\mathcal{Q}}_{2,3}\star{\mathcal{Q}}_{1,2}.

It follows from Proposition
B.4.4
that if
{\mathsf{Q}}_{1,2}
is defined and codefined by a kernel,
the map (
B.19
) is an isomorphism.

B.4.7.

Consider the endofunctor
codefined
by
\operatorname{u}_{\mathcal{Y}}
; denote it by
\operatorname{Id}^{l}_{\mathcal{Y}}
.

Let
{\mathcal{P}}
be an object of
\operatorname{Shv}({\mathcal{Y}}_{1}\times{\mathcal{Y}}_{2})
and set

{\mathcal{Q}}:=(\operatorname{Id}^{l}_{{\mathcal{Y}}_{1}}\boxtimes\operatorname{Id}_{{\mathcal{Y}}_{2}})({\mathcal{P}}).

As in Sect.
B.4.2
we produce a natural transformation

(B.20)

\operatorname{Id}_{\mathcal{Z}}\boxtimes{\mathsf{P}}^{l}\to\operatorname{Id}_{\mathcal{Z}}\boxtimes{\mathsf{Q}}.

As in Proposition
B.4.4
we have:

Proposition B.4.8
.

(a) A functor codefined by
{\mathcal{P}}
is defined and codefined by a kernel if and only if
the natural transformations (
B.20
) are isomorphisms (for all
{\mathcal{Z}}
).

(b) In the situation of point (a), the defining object is given by

{\mathcal{Q}}\simeq(\operatorname{Id}^{l}\boxtimes\operatorname{Id}_{{\mathcal{Y}}_{2}})({\mathcal{P}}).

B.4.9.

The natural transformations (
B.17
) and (
B.20
) are compatible as follows.
Start with an object
{\mathcal{Q}}\in\operatorname{Shv}({\mathcal{Y}}_{1}\times{\mathcal{Y}}_{2})
, and set

{\mathcal{P}}:=(\operatorname{Mir}_{{\mathcal{Y}}_{1}}\boxtimes\operatorname{Id}_{{\mathcal{Y}}_{2}})({\mathcal{Q}})\text{ and }{\mathcal{Q}}^{\prime}:=(\operatorname{Id}^{l}_{{\mathcal{Y}}_{1}}\boxtimes\operatorname{Id}_{{\mathcal{Y}}_{2}})({\mathcal{P}}).

Using adjunction (see Sect.
B.6.1
below) and the natural transformation (
B.9
), we obtain a map

(B.21)

{\mathcal{Q}}^{\prime}\to{\mathcal{Q}}.

It is straightforward to check that the composition

\operatorname{Id}_{\mathcal{Z}}\boxtimes{\mathsf{P}}^{l}\overset{\text{\eqref{e:left-to-right trans bis}}}{\longrightarrow}\operatorname{Id}_{\mathcal{Z}}\boxtimes{\mathsf{Q}}^{\prime}\overset{\text{\eqref{e:back to Q}}}{\longrightarrow}\operatorname{Id}_{\mathcal{Z}}\boxtimes{\mathsf{Q}}

identifies with the map (
B.17
).

In particular, if
{\mathsf{Q}}
is defined and codefined by a kernel, all of the above maps are isomorphisms,
including the map (
B.21
).

B.4.10.

As in Sect.
B.4.6
, for

{\mathcal{P}}_{1,2}\in\operatorname{Shv}({\mathcal{Y}}_{1}\times{\mathcal{Y}}_{2})\text{ and }{\mathcal{P}}_{2,3}\in\operatorname{Shv}({\mathcal{Y}}_{2}\times{\mathcal{Y}}_{3}),

we have a map

(B.22)

{\mathcal{P}}_{2,3}\overset{l}{\star}{\mathcal{P}}_{1,2}\to{\mathcal{P}}_{2,3}\star((\operatorname{Id}_{{\mathcal{Y}}_{1}}\boxtimes\operatorname{Id}^{l}_{{\mathcal{Y}}_{2}})({\mathcal{P}}_{1,2})).

If
{\mathsf{P}}^{l}_{1,2}
is defined and codefined by a kernel,
the map (
B.22
) is an isomorphism.

B.5.
Adjunctions for functors defined by kernels

In this subsection we focus on the phenomenon of adjunction
of functors defined by kernels.

B.5.1.

Consider the notion of adjunction of 1-morphisms in the 2-category
introduced in Sect.
B.1.8
.

Explicitly, given a pair of objects

{\mathcal{Q}}_{1,2}\in\operatorname{Shv}({\mathcal{Y}}_{1}\times{\mathcal{Y}}_{2})\text{ and }{\mathcal{Q}}_{2,1}\in\operatorname{Shv}({\mathcal{Y}}_{2}\times{\mathcal{Y}}_{1}),

a datum that makes
({\mathcal{Q}}_{1,2},{\mathcal{Q}}_{2,1})
into an adjoint pair is a pair of morphisms

\operatorname{u}_{{\mathcal{Y}}_{1}}\to{\mathcal{Q}}_{2,1}\star{\mathcal{Q}}_{1,2}\text{ and }{\mathcal{Q}}_{1,2}\star{\mathcal{Q}}_{2,1}\to\operatorname{u}_{{\mathcal{Y}}_{2}}

that satisfy the usual axioms.

B.5.2.

Note that in this case, for any
{\mathcal{Z}}
, the corresponding functors

(B.23)

(\operatorname{Id}_{\mathcal{Z}}\boxtimes{\mathsf{Q}}_{1,2},\operatorname{Id}_{\mathcal{Z}}\boxtimes{\mathsf{Q}}_{2,1})

form an adjoint pair.

Vice versa, the datum of adjunction
({\mathcal{Q}}_{1,2},{\mathcal{Q}}_{2,1})
is equivalent to the datum of adjunction
of the functors (
B.23
) compatible with the isomorphisms from Sect.
B.1.5
.

Remark B.5.3
.

In what follows we will sometimes abuse the terminology as follows: for a functor

{\mathsf{Q}}:\operatorname{Shv}({\mathcal{Y}}_{1})\to\operatorname{Shv}({\mathcal{Y}}_{2})
defined by a kernel we will say that it
admits an adjoint
as a functor defined by the kernel
if the corresponding object
{\mathcal{Q}}\in\operatorname{Shv}({\mathcal{Y}}_{1}\times{\mathcal{Y}}_{2})

has this property.

B.5.4.

Note that if
({\mathcal{Q}}_{1,2},{\mathcal{Q}}_{2,1})

is an adjoint pair, then so is

({\mathcal{Q}}^{\sigma}_{2,1},{\mathcal{Q}}^{\sigma}_{1,2}).

B.5.5.

We claim:

Proposition B.5.6
.

Suppose that
{\mathcal{Q}}\in\operatorname{Shv}({\mathcal{Y}}_{1}\times{\mathcal{Y}}_{2})
admits a right adjoint, to be denoted
{\mathcal{Q}}^{R}\in\operatorname{Shv}({\mathcal{Y}}_{2}\times{\mathcal{Y}}_{1})
.
Then both
{\mathcal{Q}}
and
{\mathcal{Q}}^{R}
are constructible.

Proof.

First, observe that the statement for
{\mathcal{Q}}
implies that for
{\mathcal{Q}}^{R}
by Sect.
B.5.4
.

Choose a smooth covering by an affine scheme
f:S_{1}\to{\mathcal{Y}}_{1}
. In order to show that
{\mathcal{Q}}
is constructible, it suffices to show
that
(f\times\operatorname{id})^{!}({\mathcal{Q}})
is compact.

Since the diagonal morphism

\Gamma_{f}:S_{1}\to S_{1}\times{\mathcal{Y}}_{1}

is schematic, the object

(\Gamma_{f})_{*}(\omega_{S_{1}})\in\operatorname{Shv}(S_{1}\times{\mathcal{Y}}_{1})

is compact. Note that

(f\times\operatorname{id})^{!}({\mathcal{Q}})\simeq(\operatorname{Id}_{S_{1}}\boxtimes{\mathsf{Q}})((\Gamma_{f})_{*}(\omega_{S_{1}})).

Now, since the functor
\operatorname{Id}_{S_{1}}\boxtimes{\mathsf{Q}}
admits a continuous right adjoint, we obtain that

(\operatorname{Id}_{S_{1}}\boxtimes{\mathsf{Q}})((\Gamma_{f})_{*}(\omega_{S_{1}}))\in\operatorname{Shv}(S_{1}\times{\mathcal{Y}}_{2})

is compact, as required.

∎

B.6.
Adjunctions and Verdier duality

In this subsection we relate the phenomenon of adjunction of functors to
Verdier duality of kernels that define them.

B.6.1.

Let
{\mathcal{Q}}\in\operatorname{Shv}({\mathcal{Y}}_{1}\times{\mathcal{Y}}_{2})
be constructible; in particular
{\mathbb{D}}^{\operatorname{Verdier}}({\mathcal{Q}})

is well-defined. Denote

{\mathcal{P}}:={\mathbb{D}}^{\operatorname{Verdier}}({\mathcal{Q}})^{\sigma}\in\operatorname{Shv}({\mathcal{Y}}_{2}\times{\mathcal{Y}}_{1}).

Note that for an individual
{\mathcal{Z}}
, the functor

\operatorname{Id}_{\mathcal{Z}}\boxtimes{\mathsf{P}}^{l}:\operatorname{Shv}({\mathcal{Z}}\times{\mathcal{Y}}_{2})\to\operatorname{Shv}({\mathcal{Z}}\times{\mathcal{Y}}_{1})

is the left adjoint of the functor

\operatorname{Id}_{\mathcal{Z}}\boxtimes{\mathsf{Q}}_{\operatorname{disc}}:\operatorname{Shv}({\mathcal{Z}}\times{\mathcal{Y}}_{1})\to\operatorname{Shv}({\mathcal{Z}}\times{\mathcal{Y}}_{2}),

see Sect.
B.2.2
for the notation.

B.6.2.

We claim:

Theorem B.6.3
.

Suppose that
{\mathsf{Q}}
admits a right adjoint
{\mathsf{Q}}^{R}
as a functor defined by a kernel with the
corresponding object of
\operatorname{Shv}({\mathcal{Y}}_{2}\times{\mathcal{Y}}_{1})
denoted by
{\mathcal{Q}}^{R}
. Denote

{\mathcal{P}}:={\mathbb{D}}^{\operatorname{Verdier}}({\mathcal{Q}}^{R})^{\sigma}.

Then:

(a) The functor
{\mathsf{Q}}
is defined and codefined by a kernel, with codefining object
{\mathcal{P}}
.

(b) The functor
{\mathsf{Q}}^{R}
is safe.
6
6

6

See Sect.
B.2.3
for what this means.

Let us emphasize that point (a) of the theorem says that

\operatorname{Id}_{\mathcal{Z}}\boxtimes{\mathsf{Q}}\simeq\operatorname{Id}_{\mathcal{Z}}\boxtimes{\mathsf{P}}^{l}

for
{\mathsf{P}}
codefined by the object
{\mathcal{P}}
above.

Proof.

It suffices to prove point (a): indeed, point (b) would follow from Sects.
B.5.2
and
B.6.1
,
since the functors
\operatorname{Id}_{\mathcal{Z}}\boxtimes{\mathsf{Q}}^{R}
and

\operatorname{Id}_{\mathcal{Z}}\boxtimes{\mathsf{Q}}^{R}_{\operatorname{disc}}
are both right adjoint to
\operatorname{Id}_{\mathcal{Z}}\boxtimes{\mathsf{Q}}\simeq\operatorname{Id}_{\mathcal{Z}}\boxtimes{\mathsf{P}}^{l}
.

Let
f:\widetilde{\mathcal{Y}}_{2}\to{\mathcal{Y}}_{2}
be a smooth cover by an affine scheme. In order to prove point (a), it suffices to construct isomorphisms

(B.24)

(\operatorname{id}\times f)^{*}\circ(\operatorname{Id}_{\mathcal{Z}}\boxtimes{\mathsf{Q}})\simeq(\operatorname{id}\times f)^{*}\circ(\operatorname{Id}_{\mathcal{Z}}\boxtimes{\mathsf{P}}^{l})

as functors

\operatorname{Shv}({\mathcal{Z}}\times{\mathcal{Y}}_{1})\to\operatorname{Shv}({\mathcal{Z}}\times\widetilde{\mathcal{Y}}_{2}).

Note that since
f
is smooth, the functor
f^{*}
is both defined and codefined by a kernel so that

(\operatorname{id}\times f)^{*}\simeq(\operatorname{Id}_{\mathcal{Z}}\boxtimes f^{*}).

Denote
\widetilde{\mathcal{Q}}=(\operatorname{id}\times f)^{*}({\mathcal{Q}})
,
so that

\operatorname{Id}_{\mathcal{Z}}\boxtimes\widetilde{\mathsf{Q}}\simeq(\operatorname{Id}_{\mathcal{Z}}\boxtimes f^{*})\circ(\operatorname{Id}_{\mathcal{Z}}\boxtimes{\mathsf{Q}}).

Since the
f^{*}
admits a right adjoint as a functor defined by a kernel (namely,
f_{*})
, so does the functor

\widetilde{\mathsf{Q}}
. Note that the corresponding functor
\widetilde{\mathsf{P}}^{l}
is also given by
f^{*}\circ{\mathsf{P}}^{l}
, as a functor

codefined
by a kernel.

Hence, the sought-for isomorphism (
B.24
) becomes

\operatorname{Id}_{\mathcal{Z}}\boxtimes\widetilde{\mathsf{Q}}\simeq\operatorname{Id}_{\mathcal{Z}}\boxtimes\widetilde{\mathsf{P}}^{l}.

In other words, we have reduced the verification of point (a) to the case when
{\mathcal{Y}}_{2}
is an affine scheme,
which we will from now on assume.

By Sect.
B.6.1
, the functors
(\operatorname{Id}_{\mathcal{Z}}\boxtimes{\mathsf{P}}^{l},\operatorname{Id}_{\mathcal{Z}}\boxtimes{\mathsf{Q}}^{R}_{\operatorname{disc}})

form an adjoint pair. Note, however, that since
{\mathcal{Y}}_{2}
is an affine scheme, the functor

\operatorname{Id}_{\mathcal{Z}}\boxtimes{\mathsf{P}}^{l}
preserves compactness (this is true for any object codefined by a constructible
kernel with target a scheme, see Sect.
A.1.6
). Hence, its right adjoint is continuous. This implies
that
\operatorname{Id}_{\mathcal{Z}}\boxtimes{\mathsf{Q}}^{R}_{\operatorname{disc}}
is continuous, so
{\mathsf{Q}}^{R}
is safe.
(Note that this proves point (b) in the case when
{\mathcal{Y}}_{2}
is an affine scheme.)

In particular, we obtain that
(\operatorname{Id}_{\mathcal{Z}}\boxtimes{\mathsf{P}}^{l},\operatorname{Id}_{\mathcal{Z}}\boxtimes{\mathsf{Q}}^{R})

form an adjoint pair. However, by Sect.
B.5.2
, the functors
(\operatorname{Id}_{\mathcal{Z}}\boxtimes{\mathsf{Q}},\operatorname{Id}_{\mathcal{Z}}\boxtimes{\mathsf{Q}}^{R})

also form an adjoint pair. This implies

\operatorname{Id}_{\mathcal{Z}}\boxtimes{\mathsf{Q}}\simeq\operatorname{Id}_{\mathcal{Z}}\boxtimes{\mathsf{P}}^{l},

i.e., the isomorphism of point (a).

∎

B.6.4.

We also have the following partial converse of Theorem
B.6.3
:

Proposition B.6.5
.

Let
{\mathcal{Q}}
be constructible, and suppose that the functor
{\mathsf{Q}}
is defined and
codefined by a kernel with codefining object
{\mathcal{P}}
, i.e.,

\operatorname{Id}_{\mathcal{Z}}\boxtimes{\mathsf{Q}}\simeq\operatorname{Id}_{\mathcal{Z}}\boxtimes{\mathsf{P}}^{l}
. Suppose also that the functors

\operatorname{Id}_{\mathcal{Z}}\boxtimes{\mathsf{Q}}
preserve compactness. Then:

(a) The object
{\mathcal{P}}
is constructible.

(b) The functor
{\mathsf{Q}}
admits a right adjoint as a functor defined by a kernel with the
corresponding object of
\operatorname{Shv}({\mathcal{Y}}_{2}\times{\mathcal{Y}}_{1})
being
{\mathcal{Q}}^{R}:={\mathbb{D}}^{\operatorname{Verdier}}({\mathcal{P}})^{\sigma}
.

(c) The functor
{\mathsf{Q}}^{R}
is safe.

Proof.

The fact that
{\mathcal{P}}
is constructible follows as in Proposition
B.5.6
.

By Sect.
B.6.1
, the functor
\operatorname{Id}_{\mathcal{Z}}\boxtimes{\mathsf{Q}}^{R}_{\operatorname{disc}}
is the right adjoint
of
\operatorname{Id}_{\mathcal{Z}}\boxtimes{\mathsf{P}}^{l}
, and since the latter preserves compactness, we obtain that

\operatorname{Id}_{\mathcal{Z}}\boxtimes{\mathsf{Q}}^{R}_{\operatorname{disc}}
is continuous. Hence,
{\mathsf{Q}}^{R}
is safe; this proves point (c).

Thus, the
natural transformation
\operatorname{Id}_{\mathcal{Z}}\boxtimes{\mathsf{Q}}^{R}\to\operatorname{Id}_{\mathcal{Z}}\boxtimes{\mathsf{Q}}^{R}_{\operatorname{disc}}

is an isomorphism, we obtain that
\operatorname{Id}_{\mathcal{Z}}\boxtimes{\mathsf{Q}}^{R}
is the right adjoint of

\operatorname{Id}_{\mathcal{Z}}\boxtimes{\mathsf{Q}}
. This proves point (b).

∎

Remark B.6.6
.

Note that the assertion of Proposition
B.6.5
would be false without the assumption that
the functors
\operatorname{Id}_{\mathcal{Z}}\boxtimes{\mathsf{Q}}
preserve compactness.

For example, take
{\mathcal{Y}}_{1}=\operatorname{pt}
,
{\mathcal{Y}}_{2}=B{\mathbb{G}}_{m}
and let
{\mathcal{Q}}
be the constant sheaf.

B.6.7.

Combining Theorem
B.6.3
and Proposition
B.4.4
, we obtain:

Corollary B.6.8
.

Assume that a functor
{\mathsf{Q}}
defined by the kernel
{\mathcal{Q}}\in\operatorname{Shv}({\mathcal{Y}}_{1}\times{\mathcal{Y}}_{2})
admits a right adjoint
as a functor defined by a kernel. Denote the corresponding
object by
{\mathcal{Q}}^{R}\in\operatorname{Shv}({\mathcal{Y}}_{2}\times{\mathcal{Y}}_{1})
. Then

{\mathbb{D}}^{\operatorname{Verdier}}({\mathcal{Q}}^{R})\simeq\left((\operatorname{Mir}_{{\mathcal{Y}}_{1}}\boxtimes\operatorname{Id}_{{\mathcal{Y}}_{2}})({\mathcal{Q}})\right)^{\sigma}

as objects of
\operatorname{Shv}({\mathcal{Y}}_{1}\times{\mathcal{Y}}_{2})
. In particular, the object

{\mathcal{P}}:=(\operatorname{Mir}_{{\mathcal{Y}}_{1}}\boxtimes\operatorname{Id}_{{\mathcal{Y}}_{2}})({\mathcal{Q}})

is constructible.

The next assertion reproduces
[
Ga2
, Theorem 6.3.2]
:

Corollary B.6.9
.

In the situation of Corollary
B.6.8
, we have

{\mathbb{D}}^{\operatorname{Verdier}}({\mathcal{Q}})\simeq(\operatorname{Mir}_{{\mathcal{Y}}_{1}}\boxtimes\operatorname{Id}_{{\mathcal{Y}}_{2}})(({\mathcal{Q}}^{R})^{\sigma}).

Proof.

Follows by combining Sect.
B.5.4
and Corollary
B.6.8
.

∎

Remark B.6.10
.

At one point on the main body of the paper (namely, Theorem
4.5.4
),
we use an extension of Theorem
B.6.3
to the case when the stack
{\mathcal{Y}}_{1}
is not
necessarily quasi-compact, but is truncatable (see Sect.
C.1.4
for what this means).

In this case,
{\mathcal{Q}}
is an object of the category
\operatorname{Shv}({\mathcal{Y}}_{1}\times{\mathcal{Y}}_{2})_{\operatorname{co}}

(see Sect.
C.2.2
for the notation), such that for any cotruncative
quasi-compact open substack
{\mathcal{U}}_{1}\overset{j}{\hookrightarrow}{\mathcal{Y}}_{1}
, the object

(j^{?}\boxtimes\operatorname{id})({\mathcal{Q}})\in\operatorname{Shv}({\mathcal{U}}_{1}\times{\mathcal{Y}}_{2})

is constructible.

The right adjoint
{\mathcal{Q}}^{R}
is characterized by the property that

(j\times\operatorname{id})^{*}({\mathcal{Q}}_{R})
is the right adjoint of
(j^{?}\boxtimes\operatorname{id})({\mathcal{Q}})
.
The codefining object

{\mathcal{P}}\in\operatorname{Shv}({\mathcal{Y}}_{1}\times{\mathcal{Y}}_{2})

is related to
{\mathcal{Q}}^{R}
by the same formula as in Theorem
B.6.3
.

The statement of Corollary
B.6.8
remains unchanged, where we now understand

\operatorname{Mir}_{{\mathcal{Y}}_{1}}\boxtimes\operatorname{Id}_{{\mathcal{Y}}_{2}}
as a functor

\operatorname{Shv}({\mathcal{Y}}_{1}\times{\mathcal{Y}}_{2})_{\operatorname{co}}\to\operatorname{Shv}({\mathcal{Y}}_{1}\times{\mathcal{Y}}_{2}).

Remark B.6.11
.

Let us continue to be in the setting of Remark
B.6.10
. Let

{\mathcal{Q}}
be a constructible object of
\operatorname{Shv}({\mathcal{Y}}_{1}\times{\mathcal{Y}}_{2})
, and let
us view
{\mathsf{Q}}
is a functor
\operatorname{Shv}({\mathcal{Y}}_{1})_{\operatorname{co}}\to\operatorname{Shv}({\mathcal{Y}}_{2})
, defined by a kernel
(see Sect.
C.4
).

Suppose that
{\mathcal{Q}}
admits a right adjoint
7
7

7

In the sense of 2-category of Sect.
B.1.8
.
, which is now an object

{\mathcal{Q}}^{R}\in\operatorname{Shv}({\mathcal{Y}}_{2}\times{\mathcal{Y}}_{1})_{\operatorname{co}}.

We have

(j^{?}\boxtimes\operatorname{id})({\mathcal{Q}}^{R})\simeq\left((j\times\operatorname{id})^{*}({\mathcal{Q}})\right)^{R},

for
{\mathcal{U}}_{1}\overset{j}{\hookrightarrow}{\mathcal{Y}}_{1}
as in Remark
B.6.10
.

In particular, Corollary
B.6.9
is applicable. Explicitly, we have

{\mathcal{Q}}^{R}\simeq(\operatorname{Id}^{l}_{{\mathcal{Y}}_{1}}\boxtimes\operatorname{Id}_{{\mathcal{Y}}_{2}})({\mathbb{D}}^{\operatorname{Verdier}}({\mathcal{Q}}))^{\sigma},

where we view
\operatorname{Id}^{l}_{{\mathcal{Y}}_{1}}
as a functor

\operatorname{Shv}({\mathcal{Y}}_{1})\to\operatorname{Shv}({\mathcal{Y}}_{1})_{\operatorname{co}}
, codefined by a kernel, see Sect.
C.4.9
.

Note, however, that in this case, there is no sense in which we can talk about
{\mathsf{Q}}
being
codefined by a kernel.

B.7.
Miraculous stacks

In this subsection we review what it means for a quasi-compact algebraic stack to
be miraculous.

B.7.1.

Let
{\mathcal{Y}}
be a quasi-compact stack. We shall say that
{\mathcal{Y}}
is
miraculous

if the functor
\operatorname{Mir}_{\mathcal{Y}}
is
invertible
, as a functor defined by a kernel.

Using Sect.
B.1.5
, this is equivalent to the requirement that

\operatorname{Id}_{\mathcal{Z}}\boxtimes\operatorname{Mir}_{\mathcal{Y}}:\operatorname{Shv}({\mathcal{Z}}\times{\mathcal{Y}})\to\operatorname{Shv}({\mathcal{Z}}\times{\mathcal{Y}})

is an equivalence for every
{\mathcal{Z}}
.

B.7.2.

Assume that
{\mathcal{Y}}
is miraculous. In particular,
\operatorname{Mir}_{\mathcal{Y}}
, viewed as a plain endofunctor
of
\operatorname{Shv}({\mathcal{Y}})
is a self-equivalence.

In this case, we can define a
new
self-duality on
\operatorname{Shv}({\mathcal{Y}})
with counit given by
