\operatorname{Shv}({\mathcal{Y}})\otimes\operatorname{Shv}({\mathcal{Y}})\overset{\operatorname{Mir}_{\mathcal{Y}}^{-1}\otimes\operatorname{Id}}{\longrightarrow}\operatorname{Shv}({\mathcal{Y}})\otimes\operatorname{Shv}({\mathcal{Y}})\overset{\operatorname{ev}_{\mathcal{Y}}}{\longrightarrow}\operatorname{Vect};

we denote it by
\operatorname{ev}^{\operatorname{Mir}}_{\mathcal{Y}}
.

We will refer to it as the
miraculous self-duality
of
\operatorname{Shv}({\mathcal{Y}})
. The corresponding
contravariant self-equivalence, denoted

{\mathbb{D}}^{\operatorname{Mir}}:(\operatorname{Shv}({\mathcal{Y}})^{c})^{\operatorname{op}}\to\operatorname{Shv}({\mathcal{Y}})^{c},

is given by

{\mathbb{D}}^{\operatorname{Mir}}\simeq{\mathbb{D}}^{\operatorname{Verdier}}\circ\operatorname{Mir}_{\mathcal{Y}}^{-1}.

B.7.3.

Since the object
\operatorname{ps-u}_{\mathcal{Y}}
is swap-equivariant, we have

(\operatorname{Mir}_{\mathcal{Y}})^{\vee}\simeq\operatorname{Mir}_{\mathcal{Y}},

and hence

(\operatorname{Mir}_{\mathcal{Y}}^{-1})^{\vee}\simeq\operatorname{Mir}_{\mathcal{Y}}^{-1}.

From here we obtain that the pairing
\operatorname{ev}^{\operatorname{Mir}}_{\mathcal{Y}}
is swap-equivariant. Hence, the
functor
{\mathbb{D}}^{\operatorname{Mir}}
is involutive.

B.7.4.

Let
{\mathcal{Y}}
be miraculous. In particular, we obtain that the functor
\operatorname{Mir}_{\mathcal{Y}}

admits a left adjoint as a functor defined by a kernel.

Applying Theorem
B.6.3
(b) we obtain that the functor
\operatorname{Mir}_{\mathcal{Y}}
is safe. In particular,
it preserves constructibility.

Furthermore, from Theorem
B.6.3
(a) we obtain that the inverse of
\operatorname{Mir}_{\mathcal{Y}}
, viewed
as a functor codefined by a kernel is given by
\operatorname{Id}^{l}_{\mathcal{Y}}
, see Sect.
B.4.7
for the notation.

B.8.
A criterion for admitting an adjoint

B.8.1.

Let
{\mathcal{Q}}\in\operatorname{Shv}({\mathcal{Y}}_{1}\times{\mathcal{Y}}_{2})
be a constructible object. Denote

(B.25)

^{\prime}\!{\mathcal{Q}}^{R}:=(\operatorname{Id}_{{\mathcal{Y}}_{2}}\boxtimes\operatorname{Id}^{l}_{{\mathcal{Y}}_{1}})({\mathbb{D}}^{\operatorname{Verdier}}({\mathcal{Q}})^{\sigma}).

Remark B.8.2
.

Assume for a moment that
{\mathcal{Q}}
admits a right adjoint
8
8

8

In the sense of 2-category of Sect.
B.1.8
.
,
so that

{\mathbb{D}}^{\operatorname{Verdier}}({\mathcal{Q}}^{R})\simeq\left((\operatorname{Mir}_{{\mathcal{Y}}_{1}}\boxtimes\operatorname{Id}_{{\mathcal{Y}}_{2}})({\mathcal{Q}})\right)^{\sigma},

by Corollary
B.6.8
.

Since
{\mathbb{D}}^{\operatorname{Verdier}}({\mathcal{Q}}^{R})
is constructible, it follows from Sect.
B.3.3
that in this case we have

{}^{\prime}\!{\mathcal{Q}}^{R}\simeq{\mathcal{Q}}^{R}.

B.8.3.

Assume that
{}^{\prime}\!{\mathsf{Q}}^{R}
is safe.

Note that this assumption holds if
{\mathcal{Q}}
admits a right adjoint.
Indeed, this follows from Theorem
B.6.3
and Remark

B.8.2
above.

Note also that the safety condition is automatic whenever
{}^{\prime}\!{\mathcal{Q}}^{R}
is compact or
{\mathcal{Y}}_{2}
is a scheme, see Sect.
B.2.3
.

B.8.4.

We claim that there exists a canonically defined map

(B.26)

\operatorname{u}_{{\mathcal{Y}}_{1}}\to{}^{\prime}\!{\mathcal{Q}}^{R}\star{\mathcal{Q}}.

We start with the map

(B.27)

\operatorname{ps-u}_{{\mathcal{Y}}_{1}}\to{\mathbb{D}}^{\operatorname{Verdier}}({\mathcal{Q}})^{\sigma}\star_{\operatorname{disc}}{\mathcal{Q}},

arising by adjunction, see Sect.
B.2.5
for the notation
\star_{\operatorname{disc}}
.

We apply to both sides the functor

\operatorname{Id}_{{\mathcal{Y}}_{1}}\boxtimes\operatorname{Id}^{l}_{{\mathcal{Y}}_{1}},

and we obtain a map

(B.28)

\operatorname{u}_{{\mathcal{Y}}_{1}}\to(\operatorname{Id}_{{\mathcal{Y}}_{1}}\boxtimes\operatorname{Id}^{l}_{{\mathcal{Y}}_{1}})({\mathbb{D}}^{\operatorname{Verdier}}({\mathcal{Q}})^{\sigma}\star_{\operatorname{disc}}{\mathcal{Q}}),

and we follow it by the map

(\operatorname{Id}_{{\mathcal{Y}}_{1}}\boxtimes\operatorname{Id}^{l}_{{\mathcal{Y}}_{1}})({\mathbb{D}}^{\operatorname{Verdier}}({\mathcal{Q}})^{\sigma}\star_{\operatorname{disc}}{\mathcal{Q}})\overset{\text{\eqref{e:left to right disc}}}{\longrightarrow}((\operatorname{Id}_{{\mathcal{Y}}_{2}}\boxtimes\operatorname{Id}^{l}_{{\mathcal{Y}}_{1}})({\mathbb{D}}^{\operatorname{Verdier}}({\mathcal{Q}})^{\sigma}))\star_{\operatorname{disc}}{\mathcal{Q}}={}^{\prime}\!{\mathcal{Q}}^{R}\star_{\operatorname{disc}}{\mathcal{Q}}.

Finally, using the assumption that
{}^{\prime}\!{\mathcal{Q}}^{R}
is safe, we obtain that the map

{}^{\prime}\!{\mathcal{Q}}^{R}\star{\mathcal{Q}}\to^{\prime}\!{\mathcal{Q}}^{R}\star_{\operatorname{disc}}{\mathcal{Q}}

is an isomorphism.

B.8.5.

Note also that by adjunction, we have a canonically defined map

(B.29)

{\mathcal{Q}}\overset{l}{\star}{\mathbb{D}}^{\operatorname{Verdier}}({\mathcal{Q}})^{\sigma}\to\operatorname{u}_{{\mathcal{Y}}_{2}}.

B.8.6.

Recall now that we have a canonically defined map

(B.30)

{\mathcal{Q}}\overset{l}{\star}{\mathbb{D}}^{\operatorname{Verdier}}({\mathcal{Q}})^{\sigma}\to{\mathcal{Q}}\star{}^{\prime}\!{\mathcal{Q}}^{R},

see (
B.22
).

B.8.7.

We claim:

Theorem B.8.8
.

For a constructible object
{\mathcal{Q}}\in\operatorname{Shv}({\mathcal{Y}}_{1}\times{\mathcal{Y}}_{2})
,
the following conditions are equivalent
9
9

9

In the statements below we refer to adjunctions in the 2-category of Sect.
B.1.8
.
:

(i) The object
{\mathcal{Q}}
admits a right adjoint;

(ii) The map (
B.26
) is the unit of an adjunction;

(iii) The map (
B.30
) is an isomorphism;

(iv) The map (
B.30
) is an isomorphism, and the map
(
B.29
) precomposed with the inverse of the isomorphism (
B.30
)
is the counit of an adjunction;

(v) The map (
B.30
) is an isomorphism, and the map
(
B.29
) precomposed with the inverse of the isomorphism (
B.30
)
is the counit of an adjunction, with the unit being (
B.26
).

Remark B.8.9
.

At one place in the main body of the paper (namely, Corollary
4.5.6
), we use the extension of Theorem
B.8.8

to the case when
{\mathcal{Y}}_{1}
is not necessarily quasi-compact, but truncatable.

Here
{\mathcal{Q}}
is an object of
\operatorname{Shv}({\mathcal{Y}}_{1}\times{\mathcal{Y}}_{2})
, and we view
{\mathsf{Q}}

as a functor
\operatorname{Shv}({\mathcal{Y}}_{1})_{\operatorname{co}}\to\operatorname{Shv}({\mathcal{Y}}_{2})
, defined by a kernel (see Sect.
C.4
).

We view
\operatorname{Id}^{l}_{{\mathcal{Y}}_{1}}
as a functor
\operatorname{Shv}({\mathcal{Y}}_{1})\to\operatorname{Shv}({\mathcal{Y}}_{1})_{\operatorname{co}}
,
codefined by a kernel, so
{}^{\prime}\!{\mathcal{Q}}^{R}
is an object of

\operatorname{Shv}({\mathcal{Y}}_{1}\times{\mathcal{Y}}_{2})_{\operatorname{co}}
, see Sect.
C.4.9
.

The proof of Theorem
B.8.8
extends to this case in a straightforward way
(instead of Theorem
B.6.3
we use its variant described in
Remark
B.6.11
).

B.9.
Proof of Theorem
B.8.8

B.9.1.

We have the tautological implications (v)
\Rightarrow
(ii)
\Rightarrow
(i) and (v)
\Rightarrow
(iv)
\Rightarrow
(iii).

B.9.2.

Assume that
{\mathcal{Q}}
admits a right adjoint. Then the map (
B.30
) is an isomorphism
by Theorem
B.6.3
and Sect.
B.4.10
(i.e., we have (i)
\Rightarrow
(iii)).

Furthermore, by Remark
B.8.2
, in this case
{}^{\prime}\!{\mathcal{Q}}^{R}\simeq{\mathcal{Q}}^{R}
.
Unwinding the identification of Theorem
B.6.3
(a),
we obtain that the unit of the
({\mathcal{Q}},{\mathcal{Q}}^{R})
-adjunction is given by (
B.26
) and the counit is given by
the map (
B.29
) precomposed with the isomorphism (
B.30
).

Thus, we obtain that (i)
\Rightarrow
(v).

B.9.3.

It remains to show that (iii)
\Rightarrow
(i) or equivalently (iii)
\Rightarrow
(v).
As a first step, we will reduce the assertion to the case when

{\mathcal{Y}}_{2}
is a scheme.

Choose a smooth cover
f:\widetilde{\mathcal{Y}}_{2}\to{\mathcal{Y}}_{2}
, where
\widetilde{\mathcal{Y}}_{2}
is a scheme. Denote

\widetilde{\mathcal{Q}}:=(\operatorname{id}\times f)^{*}({\mathcal{Q}})\in\operatorname{Shv}({\mathcal{Y}}_{1}\times\widetilde{\mathcal{Y}}_{2}).

Note that we have a canonical identification

{}^{\prime}\!\widetilde{\mathsf{Q}}^{R}\simeq{}^{\prime}\!{\mathsf{Q}}^{R}\circ f_{*},

as functors defined by kernels.

Furthermore, it is easy to see that condition (iii) for
{\mathcal{Q}}
implies the corresponding condition for
\widetilde{\mathcal{Q}}
.

Assume that the implication (iii)
\Rightarrow
(v) holds for
\widetilde{\mathcal{Q}}
. Let us show that this implies
condition (i) for the original
{\mathcal{Q}}
.

By assumption, the map

{\mathop{\operatorname{{\mathcal{H}}\it om}}}_{\operatorname{Shv}({\mathcal{Z}}\times\widetilde{\mathcal{Y}}_{2})}((\operatorname{Id}_{\mathcal{Z}}\boxtimes\widetilde{\mathsf{Q}})({\mathcal{F}}),\widetilde{\mathcal{F}}^{\prime})\to{\mathop{\operatorname{{\mathcal{H}}\it om}}}_{\operatorname{Shv}({\mathcal{Z}}\times{\mathcal{Y}}_{1})}({\mathcal{F}},(\operatorname{Id}_{\mathcal{Z}}\boxtimes{}^{\prime}\!\widetilde{\mathsf{Q}}^{R})(\widetilde{\mathcal{F}}^{\prime})),\quad\widetilde{\mathcal{F}}^{\prime}\in\operatorname{Shv}({\mathcal{Z}}\times\widetilde{\mathcal{Y}}_{2}),

induced by (
B.26
), is an isomorphism.

We have a commutative diagram

\begin{CD}{\mathop{\operatorname{{\mathcal{H}}\it om}}}_{\operatorname{Shv}({\mathcal{Z}}\times\widetilde{\mathcal{Y}}_{2})}((\operatorname{Id}_{\mathcal{Z}}\boxtimes\widetilde{\mathsf{Q}})({\mathcal{F}}),\widetilde{\mathcal{F}}^{\prime})@>{}>{}>{\mathop{\operatorname{{\mathcal{H}}\it om}}}_{\operatorname{Shv}({\mathcal{Z}}\times{\mathcal{Y}}_{1})}({\mathcal{F}},(\operatorname{Id}_{\mathcal{Z}}\boxtimes{}^{\prime}\!\widetilde{\mathsf{Q}}^{R})(\widetilde{\mathcal{F}}^{\prime}))\\
@V{\sim}V{}V@V{}V{\sim}V\\
{\mathop{\operatorname{{\mathcal{H}}\it om}}}_{\operatorname{Shv}({\mathcal{Z}}\times{\mathcal{Y}}_{2})}\left((\operatorname{Id}_{\mathcal{Z}}\boxtimes{\mathsf{Q}})({\mathcal{F}}),(\operatorname{id}\times f)_{*}(\widetilde{\mathcal{F}}^{\prime})\right)@>{}>{}>{\mathop{\operatorname{{\mathcal{H}}\it om}}}_{\operatorname{Shv}({\mathcal{Z}}\times{\mathcal{Y}}_{1})}\left({\mathcal{F}},(\operatorname{Id}_{\mathcal{Z}}\boxtimes{}^{\prime}\!{\mathsf{Q}}^{R})((\operatorname{id}\times f)_{*}(\widetilde{\mathcal{F}}^{\prime}))\right).\end{CD}

Hence, we obtain that the map

(B.31)

{\mathop{\operatorname{{\mathcal{H}}\it om}}}_{\operatorname{Shv}({\mathcal{Z}}\times{\mathcal{Y}}_{2})}((\operatorname{Id}_{\mathcal{Z}}\boxtimes{\mathsf{Q}})({\mathcal{F}}),{\mathcal{F}}^{\prime})\to{\mathop{\operatorname{{\mathcal{H}}\it om}}}_{\operatorname{Shv}({\mathcal{Z}}\times{\mathcal{Y}}_{1})}({\mathcal{F}},(\operatorname{Id}_{\mathcal{Z}}\boxtimes{}^{\prime}\!{\mathsf{Q}}^{R})({\mathcal{F}}^{\prime})),

induced by (
B.26
), is an isomorphism, whenever
{\mathcal{F}}^{\prime}\in\operatorname{Shv}({\mathcal{Z}}\times{\mathcal{Y}}_{2})
lies in the essential
image of the functor

(\operatorname{id}\times f)_{*}:\operatorname{Shv}({\mathcal{Z}}\times\widetilde{\mathcal{Y}}_{2})\to\operatorname{Shv}({\mathcal{Z}}\times{\mathcal{Y}}_{2}).

We claim that this implies that (
B.31
) is an isomorphism for all
{\mathcal{F}}^{\prime}\in\operatorname{Shv}({\mathcal{Z}}\times{\mathcal{Y}}_{2})
,
which would mean that (i) holds.

Indeed, by the assumption in Sect.
B.8.3
, the functor

\operatorname{Id}_{\mathcal{Z}}\boxtimes{}^{\prime}\!{\mathsf{Q}}^{R}
maps isomorphically to
\operatorname{Id}_{\mathcal{Z}}\boxtimes{}^{\prime}\!{\mathsf{Q}}^{R}_{\operatorname{disc}}
,
and the latter functor commutes with
limits
. Now, every object in
{\mathcal{F}}^{\prime}\in\operatorname{Shv}({\mathcal{Z}}\times{\mathcal{Y}}_{2})
can
be written as a totalization of a cosimplicial object, whose terms belong to the essential
image of
(\operatorname{id}\times f)_{*}
.

B.9.4.

Thus, we can assume that
{\mathcal{Y}}_{2}
is a scheme. (Note that in this case, the assumption in
Sect.
B.8.3
holds automatically.)

We will show that (iii) implies (v). Namely, we will show that if (iii) holds, then the morphisms
(
B.26
) and (
B.29
) satisfy the adjunction axioms.

We will show that the composition

(B.32)

{\mathcal{Q}}\simeq{\mathcal{Q}}\star\operatorname{u}_{{\mathcal{Y}}_{1}}\overset{\text{\eqref{e:ker adj unit}}}{\longrightarrow}{\mathcal{Q}}\star({}^{\prime}\!{\mathcal{Q}}^{R}\star{\mathcal{Q}})\simeq({\mathcal{Q}}\star{}^{\prime}\!{\mathcal{Q}}^{R})\star{\mathcal{Q}}\overset{\text{\eqref{e:compare counits}}}{\simeq}({\mathcal{Q}}\overset{l}{\star}{\mathbb{D}}^{\operatorname{Verdier}}({\mathcal{Q}})^{\sigma})\star{\mathcal{Q}}\overset{\text{\eqref{e:ker adj counit prel}}}{\longrightarrow}\operatorname{u}_{{\mathcal{Y}}_{2}}\star{\mathcal{Q}}\simeq{\mathcal{Q}}

is the identity map. The other adjunction axiom is checked similarly.

B.9.5.

Since
{\mathcal{Y}}_{2}
is a scheme, the map

{\mathbb{D}}^{\operatorname{Verdier}}({\mathcal{Q}})^{\sigma}\star{\mathcal{Q}}\to{\mathbb{D}}^{\operatorname{Verdier}}({\mathcal{Q}})^{\sigma}\star_{\operatorname{disc}}{\mathcal{Q}}

is an isomorphism.

Hence, we can view the map (
B.27
) as a map

(B.33)

\operatorname{ps-u}_{{\mathcal{Y}}_{1}}\to{\mathbb{D}}^{\operatorname{Verdier}}({\mathcal{Q}})^{\sigma}\star{\mathcal{Q}}.

Similarly, we can view the map (
B.28
) as a map

(B.34)

\operatorname{u}_{{\mathcal{Y}}_{1}}\to(\operatorname{Id}_{{\mathcal{Y}}_{1}}\boxtimes\operatorname{Id}^{l}_{{\mathcal{Y}}_{1}})({\mathbb{D}}^{\operatorname{Verdier}}({\mathcal{Q}})^{\sigma}\star{\mathcal{Q}}),

B.9.6.

For any
{\mathcal{Q}}\in\operatorname{Shv}({\mathcal{Y}}_{1}\times{\mathcal{Y}}_{2})^{\operatorname{constr}}
, we have a commutative diagram

\begin{CD}{\mathcal{Q}}@>{\operatorname{id}}>{}>{\mathcal{Q}}\\
@V{\sim}V{}V@V{}V{\sim}V\\
{\mathcal{Q}}\overset{l}{\star}\operatorname{ps-u}_{{\mathcal{Y}}_{1}}@>{\text{\eqref{e:right vs left conv}}}>{}>{\mathcal{Q}}\star\operatorname{u}_{{\mathcal{Y}}_{1}}\\
@V{\text{\eqref{e:ker adj unit prel}}}V{}V@V{}V{\text{\eqref{e:ker adj unit prel bis}}}V\\
{\mathcal{Q}}\overset{l}{\star}({\mathbb{D}}^{\operatorname{Verdier}}({\mathcal{Q}})^{\sigma}\star{\mathcal{Q}})@>{\text{\eqref{e:right vs left conv}}}>{}>{\mathcal{Q}}\star((\operatorname{Id}_{{\mathcal{Y}}_{1}}\boxtimes\operatorname{Id}^{l}_{{\mathcal{Y}}_{1}})({\mathbb{D}}^{\operatorname{Verdier}}({\mathcal{Q}})^{\sigma}\star{\mathcal{Q}}))\\
@V{\text{\eqref{e:left to right}}}V{}V@V{}V{\text{\eqref{e:left to right}}}V\\
({\mathcal{Q}}\overset{l}{\star}{\mathbb{D}}^{\operatorname{Verdier}}({\mathcal{Q}})^{\sigma})\star{\mathcal{Q}}&&{\mathcal{Q}}\star({}^{\prime}\!{\mathcal{Q}}^{R}\star{\mathcal{Q}})\\
@V{\operatorname{id}}V{}V@V{}V{\sim}V\\
({\mathcal{Q}}\overset{l}{\star}{\mathbb{D}}^{\operatorname{Verdier}}({\mathcal{Q}})^{\sigma})\star{\mathcal{Q}}@>{\text{\eqref{e:right vs left conv}}=\text{\eqref{e:compare counits}}}>{}>({\mathcal{Q}}\star{}^{\prime}\!{\mathcal{Q}}^{R})\star{\mathcal{Q}}\\
@V{\text{\eqref{e:ker adj counit prel}}}V{}V\\
\operatorname{u}_{{\mathcal{Y}}_{2}}\star{\mathcal{Q}}\\
@V{\sim}V{}V\\
{\mathcal{Q}}.\end{CD}

If (iii) holds, then the bottom horizontal arrow in the above diagram is an isomorphism. In this
case, by construction,
the composition (
B.32
) identifies with the composite left vertical
arrow in this diagram.

However, it is a straightforward verification that this composite
map is the identity.

B.10.
An example: the ULA property

In this subsection we will recast the property of a sheaf to be ULA (with respect to a given map)
in terms of adjunction of functors given by kernels.

B.10.1.

Let
f:{\mathcal{Y}}\to S
be a map between algebraic stacks, where
S
is a separated scheme.
Let
{\mathcal{F}}
be an object of
\operatorname{Shv}({\mathcal{Y}})
.

Definition B.10.2
.

We shall say that
{\mathcal{F}}
is ULA with respect to
f
if the functor

{\mathsf{F}}:\operatorname{Shv}(S)\to\operatorname{Shv}({\mathcal{Y}}),\quad{\mathcal{G}}\mapsto{\mathcal{F}}\overset{!}{\otimes}f^{!}({\mathcal{G}}),

viewed as a functor defined by the kernel

(\operatorname{Graph}_{f})_{*}({\mathcal{F}})\in\operatorname{Shv}(S\times{\mathcal{Y}}),

is defined and codefined by a kernel.

Remark B.10.3
.

Usually, one defines the notion of ULA for objects that are constructible. The above definition is
justified by the following fact (see
[
Ga3
]
):

An object
{\mathcal{F}}\in\operatorname{Shv}({\mathcal{Y}})
is ULA with respect to
f:{\mathcal{Y}}\to S
if and only if for every
k
,
every constructible sub-object
{\mathcal{F}}^{\prime}\subset H^{k}({\mathcal{F}})
has this property.

For constructible objects, the above notion of ULA coincides with the classical one,
see
[
BG
, Appendix B]
.

Remark B.10.4
.

Assume for a moment that
{\mathcal{F}}
is compact. From Proposition
B.6.5
, we obtain that
{\mathcal{F}}
is ULA
if and only if the above functor
{\mathsf{F}}
admits a right adjoint, as a functor defined by a kernel.

Remark B.10.5
.

We claim that in order to check that a given object
{\mathcal{F}}\in\operatorname{Shv}({\mathcal{Y}})^{\operatorname{constr}}
is ULA with respect to
f
, it is enough to
check that one particular morphism taking place in
\operatorname{Shv}({\mathcal{Y}}\underset{S}{\times}{\mathcal{Y}})
is an isomorphism
(cf.
[
HS
, Proposition 3.3]
).

This map in question is

{}^{\prime}\!\Delta_{S}^{*}({\mathcal{F}}\boxtimes{\mathbb{D}}^{\operatorname{Verdier}}({\mathcal{F}}))\to{}^{\prime}\!\Delta_{S}^{!}({\mathcal{F}}\boxtimes(f^{*}(\underline{{\mathsf{e}}}_{S})\overset{*}{\otimes}{\mathbb{D}}^{\operatorname{Verdier}}({\mathcal{F}}))),

where
{}^{\prime}\!\Delta_{S}
is the map

{\mathcal{Y}}\underset{S}{\times}{\mathcal{Y}}\to{\mathcal{Y}}\times{\mathcal{Y}}.

Indeed, since the ULA condition is local on
{\mathcal{Y}}
, we can assume that
{\mathcal{Y}}
is a scheme, and hence

{\mathcal{F}}
is compact. Then our assertion follows from Remark
B.10.4
and
the equivalence (i)
\Leftrightarrow
(iii) in Theorem
B.8.8
.

B.10.6.

We will now prove a statement that the property of being ULA is preserved by certain
functors.

Let
S
be as in Sect.
B.10.1
, and let
f_{i}:{\mathcal{Y}}_{i}\to S
,
i=1,2
be a pair of algebraic
stacks over
S
. Let
{\mathcal{Q}}
be an object of
\operatorname{Shv}({\mathcal{Y}}_{1}\times{\mathcal{Y}}_{2})
, such that the corresponding
functor
{\mathsf{Q}}
is defined and codefined by a kernel. Assume
that
{\mathcal{Q}}
is supported on

{\mathcal{Y}}_{1}\underset{S}{\times}{\mathcal{Y}}_{2}\subset{\mathcal{Y}}_{1}\times{\mathcal{Y}}_{2}.

We claim:

Corollary B.10.7
.

Under the above circumstances, the functor
{\mathsf{Q}}:\operatorname{Shv}({\mathcal{Y}}_{1})\to\operatorname{Shv}({\mathcal{Y}}_{2})

maps objects that are ULA with respect to
f_{1}
to objects that are ULA with respect to
f_{2}
.

Proof.

Let
{\mathcal{F}}
be an object of
\operatorname{Shv}({\mathcal{Y}}_{1})
that is ULA over
S
. Consider the object

(\operatorname{Graph}_{f_{1}})_{*}({\mathcal{F}})\in\operatorname{Shv}(S\times{\mathcal{Y}}_{1})

and the corresponding functor
{\mathsf{F}}:\operatorname{Shv}(S)\to\operatorname{Shv}({\mathcal{Y}}_{1})
.

By assumption, the functor
{\mathsf{F}}
is defined and codefined by a kernel. By assumption, the same is true
for the composition
{\mathsf{Q}}\circ{\mathsf{F}}
.

The functor
{\mathsf{Q}}\circ{\mathsf{F}}
is defined by the kernel

(\operatorname{Id}_{S}\boxtimes{\mathsf{Q}})((\operatorname{Graph}_{f_{1}})_{*}({\mathcal{F}}))\in\operatorname{Shv}(S\times{\mathcal{Y}}_{2}).

Now, the fact that
{\mathcal{Q}}
is supported on
{\mathcal{Y}}_{1}\underset{S}{\times}{\mathcal{Y}}_{2}
implies that

(\operatorname{Id}_{S}\boxtimes{\mathsf{Q}})((\operatorname{Graph}_{f_{1}})_{*}({\mathcal{F}}))\simeq(\operatorname{Graph}_{f_{2}})_{*}({\mathsf{Q}}({\mathcal{F}})).

Hence,
{\mathsf{Q}}({\mathcal{F}})
is ULA with respect to

f_{2}
, as desired.

∎

B.10.8.

An example of the situation described in Sect.
B.10.6
is when

{\mathcal{Y}}_{i}\simeq S\times{\mathcal{Y}}^{\prime}_{i},

and
{\mathcal{Q}}
comes from an object
{\mathcal{Q}}^{\prime}\in\operatorname{Shv}({\mathcal{Y}}^{\prime}_{1}\times{\mathcal{Y}}^{\prime}_{2})
such that the
corresponding functor
{\mathsf{Q}}^{\prime}
is defined and codefined by a kernel.

B.10.9.

In the main body of the paper, we use the following particular case of Corollary
B.10.7
,
in the setting of Sect.
B.10.8
.

Let
{\mathcal{Z}}
be an algebraic stack, and let
{\mathcal{N}}
be a conical Zariski-closed subset
of
T^{*}({\mathcal{Z}})
. Let
{\mathcal{Y}}
be another algebraic stack, and let
{\mathcal{F}}\in\operatorname{Shv}({\mathcal{Y}})

be an object such that the corresponding functor

{\mathsf{F}}:\operatorname{Shv}({\mathcal{Y}})\to\operatorname{Vect}

is defined and codefined by a kernel.

We claim:

Corollary B.10.10
.

Under the above circumstances, the functor

\operatorname{Id}_{\mathcal{Z}}\boxtimes{\mathsf{F}}:\operatorname{Shv}({\mathcal{Z}}\times{\mathcal{Y}})\to\operatorname{Shv}({\mathcal{Z}})

sends the subcategory

\operatorname{Shv}_{{\mathcal{N}}\times T^{*}({\mathcal{Y}})}({\mathcal{Z}}\times{\mathcal{Y}})\subset\operatorname{Shv}({\mathcal{Z}}\times{\mathcal{Y}})

to

\operatorname{Shv}_{\mathcal{N}}({\mathcal{Z}})\subset\operatorname{Shv}({\mathcal{Z}}).

Proof.

By the definition of singular support in
[
Bei
]
10
10

10

This definition is applicable for objects of
\operatorname{Shv}(-)
rather
than
\operatorname{Shv}(-)^{\operatorname{constr}}
using Remark
B.10.3
.

, and up to base changing
{\mathcal{Z}}
to a scheme, we have to show that for a map of schemes

f:S\to S^{\prime}
, if an object
{\mathcal{G}}\in\operatorname{Shv}_{{\mathcal{N}}\times T^{*}(S)}(S\times{\mathcal{Y}})^{\operatorname{constr}}

is ULA with respect to the composition

S\times{\mathcal{Y}}\to S\overset{f}{\to}S^{\prime},

then
(\operatorname{Id}_{S}\boxtimes{\mathsf{F}})({\mathcal{G}})\in\operatorname{Shv}(S)
is ULA with respect to
f
.

However, this follows from Corollary
B.10.7
.

∎

Appendix C
Sheaves on non quasi-compact algebraic stacks

The theory reviewed in Sects.
A
-
B
mostly pertains to quasi-compact algebraic stacks.
In this section we explore the adjustments once needs to make in order to extend the theory to the non
quasi-compact case.

C.1.
Cotruncative substacks

In this subsection, we review, mostly following
[
DrGa1
]
, the notion of
cotruncativeness

of an open embedding, as well associated notions in the presence of a singular support condition.

C.1.1.

Let

j:{\mathcal{U}}_{1}\hookrightarrow{\mathcal{U}}_{2}

be an open embedding of (quasi-compact) algebraic stacks.

We shall say that
{\mathcal{U}}_{1}
is a
cotruncative
substack of
{\mathcal{U}}_{2}
if the functor
j_{*}
,
viewed as a functor defined by a kernel
,
admits a right adjoint, to be denoted
j^{?}
. Let
{\mathcal{Q}}\in\operatorname{Shv}({\mathcal{U}}_{2}\times{\mathcal{U}}_{1})
be the kernel of this right adjoint.

It follows from Sect.
B.5.4
that the object
{\mathcal{Q}}^{\sigma}\in\operatorname{Shv}({\mathcal{U}}_{1}\times{\mathcal{U}}_{2})

defines the
left
adjoint to
j^{*}
, as a functor defined by a kernel. In particular, the system of functors

(\operatorname{id}\times j)_{!}:\operatorname{Shv}({\mathcal{Z}}\times{\mathcal{U}}_{1})\to\operatorname{Shv}({\mathcal{Z}}\times{\mathcal{U}}_{2})

satisfies the compatibilities of Sect.
B.1.5
.

As in
[
DrGa1
, Lemma 3.7.1]
, one shows:

Lemma C.1.2
.

If
{\mathcal{U}}^{\prime}_{1}\subset{\mathcal{U}}_{2}
and
{\mathcal{U}}^{\prime\prime}_{1}\subset{\mathcal{U}}_{2}

are cotruncative, then so is their union.

C.1.3.

Let

j:{\mathcal{U}}\hookrightarrow{\mathcal{Y}}

be an open embedding of not necessarily quasi-compact algebraic stacks. We say that
{\mathcal{U}}
is
a
cotruncative
substack of
{\mathcal{Y}}
if for every quasi-compact open
{\mathcal{U}}^{\prime}\subset{\mathcal{Y}}
,
the intersection
{\mathcal{U}}\cap{\mathcal{U}}^{\prime}
is cotruncative as an open substack of
{\mathcal{U}}^{\prime}
.

C.1.4.

We shall say that a (not necessarily quasi-compact) algebraic stack
{\mathcal{Y}}
is
truncatable

if it can be written as a union of quasi-compact cotruncative open substacks.

If
{\mathcal{Y}}
is truncatable, by Lemma
C.1.2
the poset of its quasi-compact cotruncative
open substacks is filtered and cofinal in the poset of all quasi-compact open substacks.

C.1.5.

Let
j:{\mathcal{U}}\hookrightarrow{\mathcal{Y}}
be as above. Let
{\mathcal{N}}
be a conical Zariski closed subset
of
T^{*}({\mathcal{Y}})
. By a slight abuse of notation we will denote by the same symbol
{\mathcal{N}}

its restriction to
{\mathcal{U}}
.

We shall say that
{\mathcal{U}}
is
{\mathcal{N}}
-
cotruncative
if it is cotruncative
and
the extension
functor
j_{!}
(equivalently,
j_{*}
) sends

\operatorname{Shv}_{{\mathcal{N}}}({\mathcal{U}})\to\operatorname{Shv}_{{\mathcal{N}}}({\mathcal{Y}}).

We shall say that
{\mathcal{U}}
is
universally

{\mathcal{N}}
-
cotruncative
if it is cotruncative
and

for any algebraic stack
{\mathcal{Z}}
and a closed conical subset
{\mathcal{N}}_{\mathcal{Z}}\subset T^{*}({\mathcal{Z}})
, the extension
functor
(\operatorname{id}\times j)_{!}
(equivalently,
(\operatorname{id}\times j)_{*}
)
sends

\operatorname{Shv}_{{\mathcal{N}}_{\mathcal{Z}}\times{\mathcal{N}}}({\mathcal{Z}}\times{\mathcal{U}})\to\operatorname{Shv}_{{\mathcal{N}}_{\mathcal{Z}}\times{\mathcal{N}}}({\mathcal{Z}}\times{\mathcal{Y}}).

C.1.6.

We shall say that
{\mathcal{Y}}
is
{\mathcal{N}}
-truncatable (resp., universally
{\mathcal{N}}
-truncatable)
if it can be written as a
filtered
union of its
{\mathcal{N}}
-cotruncative (resp., universally
{\mathcal{N}}
-cotruncative)
quasi-compact open substacks.

C.2.
The non-quasi-compact case: the “co”-category

If
{\mathcal{Y}}
is a non quasi-compact algebraic stack, Verdier duality is no longer a self-duality on
\operatorname{Shv}({\mathcal{Y}})
,
but rather a duality between
\operatorname{Shv}({\mathcal{Y}})
and another category, denoted
\operatorname{Shv}({\mathcal{Y}})_{\operatorname{co}}
.

In this subsection we introduce the category
\operatorname{Shv}({\mathcal{Y}})_{\operatorname{co}}
and study its basic properties.

C.2.1.

Let
{\mathcal{Y}}
be a (not necessarily quasi-compact) algebraic stack. By definition,
\operatorname{Shv}({\mathcal{Y}})
is

\underset{{\mathcal{U}}}{\operatorname{lim}}^{*}\,\operatorname{Shv}({\mathcal{U}}),

where the index category is the poset of quasi-compact open substacks
{\mathcal{U}}\subset{\mathcal{Y}}
,
and for
{\mathcal{U}}_{1}\overset{j}{\hookrightarrow}{\mathcal{U}}_{2}
the transition functor

\operatorname{Shv}({\mathcal{U}}_{2})\to\operatorname{Shv}({\mathcal{U}}_{1})
is
j^{*}
.

By
[
DrGa1
, Proposition 1.7.5]
, we can rewrite the above limit as a colimit

\underset{{\mathcal{U}}}{\operatorname{colim}_{!}}\,\operatorname{Shv}({\mathcal{U}}),

where the transition functors are

\operatorname{Shv}({\mathcal{U}}_{1})\to\operatorname{Shv}({\mathcal{U}}_{2})
are
j_{!}
.

C.2.2.

We define the category
\operatorname{Shv}({\mathcal{Y}})_{\operatorname{co}}
as

\underset{{\mathcal{U}}}{\operatorname{colim}_{*}}\,\operatorname{Shv}({\mathcal{U}}),

where the transition functors are

\operatorname{Shv}({\mathcal{U}}_{1})\to\operatorname{Shv}({\mathcal{U}}_{2})
are
j_{*}
.

For a given quasi-compact

U\overset{j}{\hookrightarrow}{\mathcal{Y}},

we will denote by
j_{*,\operatorname{co}}
the tautologically defined functor

\operatorname{Shv}({\mathcal{U}})\to\operatorname{Shv}({\mathcal{Y}})_{\operatorname{co}}.

Note that when
{\mathcal{Y}}
is truncatable, we can replace the poset of all
{\mathcal{U}}
by the cotruncative
ones. In this case, we can write
\operatorname{Shv}({\mathcal{Y}})_{\operatorname{co}}
also as

\underset{{\mathcal{U}}}{\operatorname{lim}^{?}}\,\operatorname{Shv}({\mathcal{U}}),

where the transition functors are

\operatorname{Shv}({\mathcal{U}}_{2})\to\operatorname{Shv}({\mathcal{U}}_{1})
are
j^{?}
.

C.2.3.

We have a tautologically defined functor

\operatorname{Id}^{\operatorname{naive}}_{\mathcal{Y}}:\operatorname{Shv}({\mathcal{Y}})_{\operatorname{co}}\to\operatorname{Shv}({\mathcal{Y}}).

It corresponds to the compatible family of functors

({\mathcal{U}}\overset{j}{\hookrightarrow}{\mathcal{Y}})\rightsquigarrow(j_{*}:\operatorname{Shv}({\mathcal{U}})\to\operatorname{Shv}({\mathcal{Y}})).

For a quasi-compact
U\overset{j}{\hookrightarrow}{\mathcal{Y}}
we have

\operatorname{Id}^{\operatorname{naive}}_{\mathcal{Y}}\circ j_{*,\operatorname{co}}\simeq j_{*}.

In addition, we have a naturally defined monoidal action of
\operatorname{Shv}({\mathcal{Y}})
on
\operatorname{Shv}({\mathcal{Y}})_{\operatorname{co}}

given by
\overset{!}{\otimes}
.

C.2.4.

Let
{\mathcal{N}}
be a closed conical subset in
T^{*}({\mathcal{Y}})
, and assume that
{\mathcal{Y}}
is
{\mathcal{N}}
-truncatable.

In this case we define the category

\operatorname{Shv}_{\mathcal{N}}({\mathcal{Y}})_{\operatorname{co}}

as

\underset{{\mathcal{U}}}{\operatorname{colim}_{*}}\,\operatorname{Shv}_{\mathcal{N}}({\mathcal{U}}),

where the colimit is taken over the poset of
{\mathcal{N}}
-cotruncative quasi-compact open substacks of

\operatorname{Bun}_{G}
.

The fact that the above poset is filtered implies that the tautologically defined functor

\operatorname{Shv}_{\mathcal{N}}({\mathcal{Y}})_{\operatorname{co}}\to\operatorname{Shv}({\mathcal{Y}})_{\operatorname{co}}

is fully faithful.

Furthermore, the functor
\operatorname{Id}^{\operatorname{naive}}_{\mathcal{Y}}
sends

\operatorname{Shv}_{\mathcal{N}}({\mathcal{Y}})_{\operatorname{co}}\to\operatorname{Shv}_{\mathcal{N}}({\mathcal{Y}}).

C.3.
Verdier duality in the non-quasi-compact case

In this subsection we establish a duality between
\operatorname{Shv}({\mathcal{Y}})
and
\operatorname{Shv}({\mathcal{Y}})_{\operatorname{co}}
.

C.3.1.

Recall the following paradigm:

Let

i\mapsto{\mathbf{C}}_{i},\quad i\in I

be a diagram of DG categories. Denote

{\mathbf{C}}:=\underset{i\in I}{\operatorname{colim}}\,{\mathbf{C}}_{i}.

Suppose that for each arrow
i_{1}\to i_{2}
, the transition functor

\phi_{i_{1},i_{2}}:{\mathbf{C}}_{i_{1}}\to{\mathbf{C}}_{i_{2}}

admits a continuous right adjoint. Suppose also that each
{\mathbf{C}}_{i}
is dualizable.

We can form a new family

(C.1)

i\mapsto{\mathbf{C}}^{\vee}_{i},\quad i\in I,

where the transition functor
{\mathbf{C}}^{\vee}_{i_{1}}\to{\mathbf{C}}^{\vee}_{i_{2}}

is
(\phi_{i_{1},i_{2}}^{R})^{\vee}
.

Denote

\widetilde{\mathbf{C}}:=\underset{i\in I}{\operatorname{colim}}\,{\mathbf{C}}^{\vee}_{i}.

Supposed for simplicity that the index category
I
is sifted, and that the transition functors

\phi_{i_{1},i_{2}}
are fully faithful.

Then we have a naturally defined pairing

(C.2)

{\mathbf{C}}\otimes\widetilde{\mathbf{C}}\to\operatorname{Vect}.

Namely, using the siftedness assumption on
I
, we write

{\mathbf{C}}\otimes\widetilde{\mathbf{C}}\simeq\underset{i\in I}{\operatorname{colim}}\,{\mathbf{C}}_{i}\otimes{\mathbf{C}}^{\vee}_{i},

and the sought-for pairing is given by the tautological pairings
{\mathbf{C}}_{i}\otimes{\mathbf{C}}^{\vee}_{i}\to\operatorname{Vect}
.

The following is a particular case of
[
DrGa1
, Proposition 1.8.3]
:

Proposition C.3.2
.

The functor (
C.2
) identifies
\widetilde{\mathbf{C}}
with the dual of
{\mathbf{C}}
.

C.3.3.

We apply the paradigm of Sect.
C.3.1
as follows. Let
{\mathcal{Y}}
be a (not necessarily quasi-compact) algebraic stack.
Consider the index set consisting of its quasi-compact open substacks, and consider the functor

{\mathcal{U}}\rightsquigarrow\operatorname{Shv}({\mathcal{U}}),\,\,({\mathcal{U}}_{1}\overset{j_{1,2}}{\hookrightarrow}{\mathcal{U}}_{2})\rightsquigarrow(j_{1,2})_{!}.

By Sect.
C.2.1
,

\underset{{\mathcal{U}}}{\operatorname{colim}}\,\operatorname{Shv}({\mathcal{U}})=:\underset{{\mathcal{U}}}{\operatorname{colim}}{}_{!}\,\operatorname{Shv}({\mathcal{U}})\simeq\operatorname{Shv}({\mathcal{Y}}).

Recall (see Sect.
A.4.1
) that for a quasi-compact stack the category of sheaves is naturally
self-dual via Verdier duality.

The corresponding dual family (
C.1
) identifies with

{\mathcal{U}}\rightsquigarrow\operatorname{Shv}({\mathcal{U}}),\,\,({\mathcal{U}}_{1}\overset{j_{1,2}}{\hookrightarrow}{\mathcal{U}}_{2})\rightsquigarrow(j_{1,2})_{*},

and its colimit

\underset{{\mathcal{U}}}{\operatorname{colim}}{}_{*}\,\operatorname{Shv}({\mathcal{U}})

is by definition
\operatorname{Shv}({\mathcal{Y}})_{\operatorname{co}}
.

Applying Proposition
C.3.2
, we obtain a canonical identification

(C.3)

\operatorname{Shv}({\mathcal{Y}})^{\vee}\simeq\operatorname{Shv}({\mathcal{Y}})_{\operatorname{co}}.

C.3.4.

Unwinding the definitions, we obtain that the counit of the duality (
C.3
) is given by the functor
\operatorname{ev}_{\mathcal{Y}}
, i.e.,

\operatorname{Shv}({\mathcal{Y}})\otimes\operatorname{Shv}({\mathcal{Y}})_{\operatorname{co}}\overset{\overset{!}{\otimes}}{\to}\operatorname{Shv}({\mathcal{Y}})_{\operatorname{co}}\overset{\operatorname{C}^{\cdot}_{\blacktriangle}({\mathcal{Y}},-)}{\longrightarrow}\operatorname{Vect},

where

\operatorname{C}^{\cdot}_{\blacktriangle}({\mathcal{Y}},-):\operatorname{Shv}({\mathcal{Y}})_{\operatorname{co}}\to\operatorname{Vect}

is the functor given by the compatible collection of functors

\operatorname{C}^{\cdot}_{\blacktriangle}({\mathcal{U}},-):\operatorname{Shv}({\mathcal{U}})\to\operatorname{Vect},\quad{\mathcal{U}}\text{ is a quasi-compact open in }{\mathcal{Y}}.

The corresponding contravariant self-equivalence

{\mathbb{D}}^{\operatorname{Verdier}}:(\operatorname{Shv}({\mathcal{Y}})^{c})^{\operatorname{op}}\to(\operatorname{Shv}({\mathcal{Y}})_{\operatorname{co}})^{c}

sends

j_{!}({\mathcal{F}}_{\mathcal{U}})\mapsto j_{*,\operatorname{co}}({\mathbb{D}}^{\operatorname{Verdier}}({\mathcal{F}}_{\mathcal{U}})),\quad{\mathcal{F}}_{\mathcal{U}}\in\operatorname{Shv}({\mathcal{U}})^{c}

for a quasi-compact open
{\mathcal{U}}\overset{j}{\hookrightarrow}{\mathcal{Y}}
.

C.3.5.

Let now
{\mathcal{N}}\subset T^{*}({\mathcal{Y}})
be a conical Zariski-closed subset. Assume that
{\mathcal{Y}}
is (universally)
{\operatorname{Nilp}}
-truncatable.

Let us suppose now that for every (universally)
{\mathcal{N}}
-cotruncative quasi-compact open substack
{\mathcal{U}}\subset{\mathcal{Y}}
,
the pair
({\mathcal{U}},{\mathcal{N}})
is duality-adapted.

It then follows from Proposition
C.3.2
that the restriction of
\operatorname{ev}_{\mathcal{Y}}
along

\operatorname{Shv}_{\mathcal{N}}({\mathcal{Y}})\otimes\operatorname{Shv}_{\mathcal{N}}({\mathcal{Y}})_{\operatorname{co}}\to\operatorname{Shv}({\mathcal{Y}})\otimes\operatorname{Shv}({\mathcal{Y}})_{\operatorname{co}}

defines an identification

\operatorname{Shv}_{\mathcal{N}}({\mathcal{Y}})^{\vee}\simeq\operatorname{Shv}_{\mathcal{N}}({\mathcal{Y}})_{\operatorname{co}}.

C.4.
Functors defined by kernels in the non-quasi-compact case

In this subsection we explain modifications to the formalism of functors defined by kernels, required in order
to consider the non quasi-compact case.

C.4.1.

We first consider
the formalism of functors
codefined
be kernels. This theory requires no modifications
from the quasi-compact case. Namely, an object

{\mathcal{P}}\in\operatorname{Shv}({\mathcal{Y}}_{1}\times{\mathcal{Y}}_{2}),

defines a family of functors

\operatorname{Id}_{\mathcal{Z}}\boxtimes{\mathsf{P}}^{l}:\operatorname{Shv}({\mathcal{Z}}\times{\mathcal{Y}}_{1})\to\operatorname{Shv}({\mathcal{Z}}\times{\mathcal{Y}}_{2}),

by the same formula

(\operatorname{Id}_{\mathcal{Z}}\boxtimes{\mathsf{P}}^{l})({\mathcal{F}})=(p_{2})_{!}(p_{1}^{*}({\mathcal{F}})\overset{*}{\otimes}{\mathcal{P}}).

The observation in Sect.
B.1.5
applies with
-_{\blacktriangle}
and
-^{!}
replaced by

-_{!}
and
-^{*}
, respectively.

C.4.2.

We now consider functors
defined
by kernels.

Let
{\mathcal{Y}}
and
{\mathcal{Z}}
be a pair of (not necessarily quasi-compact) algebraic stacks.
In this case, in addition to

\operatorname{Shv}({\mathcal{Z}}\times{\mathcal{Y}})\text{ and }\operatorname{Shv}({\mathcal{Z}}\times{\mathcal{Y}})_{\operatorname{co}},

one can define the mixed categories

\operatorname{Shv}({\mathcal{Z}}\times{\mathcal{Y}})_{\operatorname{co}_{\mathcal{Z}}}\text{ and }\operatorname{Shv}({\mathcal{Z}}\times{\mathcal{Y}})_{\operatorname{co}_{\mathcal{Y}}}.

Namely,

\operatorname{Shv}({\mathcal{Z}}\times{\mathcal{Y}})_{\operatorname{co}_{\mathcal{Z}}}:=\underset{{\mathcal{U}}_{\mathcal{Y}}\subset{\mathcal{Y}}}{\operatorname{lim}^{*}}\,\operatorname{Shv}({\mathcal{Z}}\times{\mathcal{U}}_{\mathcal{Y}})_{\operatorname{co}}\simeq\underset{{\mathcal{U}}_{\mathcal{Y}}\subset{\mathcal{Y}}}{\operatorname{lim}^{*}}\,\underset{{\mathcal{U}}_{\mathcal{Z}}\subset{\mathcal{Z}}}{\operatorname{colim}_{*}}\,\operatorname{Shv}({\mathcal{U}}_{\mathcal{Z}}\times{\mathcal{U}}_{\mathcal{Y}})

and

\operatorname{Shv}({\mathcal{Z}}\times{\mathcal{Y}})_{\operatorname{co}_{\mathcal{Y}}}:=\underset{{\mathcal{U}}_{\mathcal{Z}}\subset{\mathcal{Z}}}{\operatorname{lim}^{*}}\,\operatorname{Shv}({\mathcal{U}}_{\mathcal{Z}}\times{\mathcal{Y}})_{\operatorname{co}}\simeq\underset{{\mathcal{U}}_{\mathcal{Z}}\subset{\mathcal{Z}}}{\operatorname{lim}^{*}}\,\underset{{\mathcal{U}}_{\mathcal{Y}}\subset{\mathcal{Y}}}{\operatorname{colim}_{*}}\,\operatorname{Shv}({\mathcal{U}}_{\mathcal{Z}}\times{\mathcal{U}}_{\mathcal{Y}}),

where
{\mathcal{U}}_{\mathcal{Y}}
and
{\mathcal{U}}_{\mathcal{Z}}
are quasi-compact open substacks of
{\mathcal{Y}}
and
{\mathcal{Z}}
, respectively.

We have naturally defined functors

\operatorname{Id}_{\mathcal{Z}}\boxtimes\operatorname{Id}^{\operatorname{naive}}_{\mathcal{Y}}:\operatorname{Shv}({\mathcal{Z}}\times{\mathcal{Y}})_{\operatorname{co}}\to\operatorname{Shv}({\mathcal{Z}}\times{\mathcal{Y}})_{\operatorname{co}_{\mathcal{Z}}}

and

\operatorname{Id}^{\operatorname{naive}}_{\mathcal{Z}}\boxtimes\operatorname{Id}_{\mathcal{Y}}:\operatorname{Shv}({\mathcal{Z}}\times{\mathcal{Y}})_{\operatorname{co}_{\mathcal{Z}}}\to\operatorname{Shv}({\mathcal{Z}}\times{\mathcal{Y}}),

whose composition is the functor

\operatorname{Id}^{\operatorname{naive}}_{{\mathcal{Z}}\times{\mathcal{Y}}}:\operatorname{Shv}({\mathcal{Z}}\times{\mathcal{Y}})_{\operatorname{co}}\to\operatorname{Shv}({\mathcal{Z}}\times{\mathcal{Y}}).

C.4.3.

Assume now that
{\mathcal{Y}}
is truncatable. Then we claim that we can rewrite
\operatorname{Shv}({\mathcal{Z}}\times{\mathcal{Y}})_{\operatorname{co}_{\mathcal{Z}}}

also we as

\underset{{\mathcal{U}}_{\mathcal{Z}}\subset{\mathcal{Z}}}{\operatorname{colim}_{*}}\,\underset{{\mathcal{U}}_{\mathcal{Y}}\subset{\mathcal{Y}}}{\operatorname{lim}^{*}}\,\operatorname{Shv}({\mathcal{U}}_{\mathcal{Z}}\times{\mathcal{U}}_{\mathcal{Y}})\simeq\underset{{\mathcal{U}}_{\mathcal{Z}}\subset{\mathcal{Z}}}{\operatorname{colim}_{*}}\,\operatorname{Shv}({\mathcal{U}}_{\mathcal{Z}}\times{\mathcal{Y}}).

Indeed, we note that for a cotruncative

{\mathcal{U}}_{1,{\mathcal{Y}}}\overset{j_{\mathcal{Y}}}{\hookrightarrow}{\mathcal{U}}_{2,{\mathcal{Y}}}

and any
{\mathcal{U}}_{1,{\mathcal{Z}}}\overset{j_{\mathcal{Z}}}{\hookrightarrow}{\mathcal{U}}_{2,{\mathcal{Z}}}
, the diagram

\begin{CD}\operatorname{Shv}({\mathcal{U}}_{1,{\mathcal{U}}}\times{\mathcal{U}}_{1,{\mathcal{Z}}})@>{(j_{\mathcal{Y}}\times\operatorname{id})_{!}}>{}>\operatorname{Shv}({\mathcal{U}}_{2,{\mathcal{U}}}\times{\mathcal{U}}_{1,{\mathcal{Z}}})\\
@V{(\operatorname{id}\times j_{\mathcal{Z}})_{*}}V{}V@V{}V{(\operatorname{id}\times j_{\mathcal{Z}})_{*}}V\\
\operatorname{Shv}({\mathcal{U}}_{1,{\mathcal{U}}}\times{\mathcal{U}}_{2,{\mathcal{Z}}})@>{(j_{\mathcal{Y}}\times\operatorname{id})_{!}}>{}>\operatorname{Shv}({\mathcal{U}}_{2,{\mathcal{U}}}\times{\mathcal{U}}_{2,{\mathcal{Z}}})\end{CD}

commutes.

Hence, we can rewrite

\underset{{\mathcal{U}}_{\mathcal{Y}}\subset{\mathcal{Y}}}{\operatorname{lim}^{*}}\,\underset{{\mathcal{U}}_{\mathcal{Z}}\subset{\mathcal{Z}}}{\operatorname{colim}_{*}}\,\operatorname{Shv}({\mathcal{U}}_{\mathcal{Z}}\times{\mathcal{U}}_{\mathcal{Y}})\simeq\underset{{\mathcal{U}}_{\mathcal{Y}}\subset{\mathcal{Y}}}{\operatorname{colim}_{!}}\,\underset{{\mathcal{U}}_{\mathcal{Z}}\subset{\mathcal{Z}}}{\operatorname{colim}_{*}}\,\operatorname{Shv}({\mathcal{U}}_{\mathcal{Z}}\times{\mathcal{U}}_{\mathcal{Y}})\simeq\\
\simeq\underset{{\mathcal{U}}_{\mathcal{Z}}\subset{\mathcal{Z}}}{\operatorname{colim}_{*}}\,\underset{{\mathcal{U}}_{\mathcal{Y}}\subset{\mathcal{Y}}}{\operatorname{colim}_{!}}\,\operatorname{Shv}({\mathcal{U}}_{\mathcal{Z}}\times{\mathcal{U}}_{\mathcal{Y}})\simeq\underset{{\mathcal{U}}_{\mathcal{Z}}\subset{\mathcal{Z}}}{\operatorname{colim}_{*}}\,\underset{{\mathcal{U}}_{\mathcal{Y}}\subset{\mathcal{Y}}}{\operatorname{lim}^{*}}\,\operatorname{Shv}({\mathcal{U}}_{\mathcal{Z}}\times{\mathcal{U}}_{\mathcal{Y}}).

C.4.4.

The material in Sect.
B.1
goes through with the following modifications:

Let
{\mathcal{Y}}_{1}
and
{\mathcal{Y}}_{2}
be a pair of truncatable stacks, and let
{\mathcal{Z}}
be another algebraic stack.

•

An object
{\mathcal{Q}}\in\operatorname{Shv}({\mathcal{Y}}_{1}\times{\mathcal{Y}}_{2})
gives rise to functors

\operatorname{Id}_{\mathcal{Z}}\boxtimes{\mathsf{Q}}:\operatorname{Shv}({\mathcal{Z}}\times{\mathcal{Y}}_{1})_{\operatorname{co}}\to\operatorname{Shv}({\mathcal{Z}}\times{\mathcal{Y}}_{2})_{\operatorname{co}_{\mathcal{Z}}}\text{ and }\operatorname{Id}_{\mathcal{Z}}\boxtimes{\mathsf{Q}}:\operatorname{Shv}({\mathcal{Z}}\times{\mathcal{Y}}_{1})_{\operatorname{co}_{{\mathcal{Y}}_{1}}}\to\operatorname{Shv}({\mathcal{Z}}\times{\mathcal{Y}}_{2}).

•

An object
{\mathcal{Q}}\in\operatorname{Shv}({\mathcal{Y}}_{1}\times{\mathcal{Y}}_{2})_{\operatorname{co}}
gives rise to functors

\operatorname{Id}_{\mathcal{Z}}\boxtimes{\mathsf{Q}}:\operatorname{Shv}({\mathcal{Z}}\times{\mathcal{Y}}_{1})\to\operatorname{Shv}({\mathcal{Z}}\times{\mathcal{Y}}_{2})_{\operatorname{co}_{{\mathcal{Y}}_{2}}}\text{ and }\operatorname{Id}_{\mathcal{Z}}\boxtimes{\mathsf{Q}}:\operatorname{Shv}({\mathcal{Z}}\times{\mathcal{Y}}_{1})_{\operatorname{co}_{\mathcal{Z}}}\to\operatorname{Shv}({\mathcal{Z}}\times{\mathcal{Y}}_{2})_{\operatorname{co}}.

•

An object
{\mathcal{Q}}\in\operatorname{Shv}({\mathcal{Y}}_{1}\times{\mathcal{Y}}_{2})_{\operatorname{co}_{{\mathcal{Y}}_{1}}}
gives rise to functors

\operatorname{Id}_{\mathcal{Z}}\boxtimes{\mathsf{Q}}:\operatorname{Shv}({\mathcal{Z}}\times{\mathcal{Y}}_{1})\to\operatorname{Shv}({\mathcal{Z}}\times{\mathcal{Y}}_{2})\text{ and }\operatorname{Id}_{\mathcal{Z}}\boxtimes{\mathsf{Q}}:\operatorname{Shv}({\mathcal{Z}}\times{\mathcal{Y}}_{1})_{\operatorname{co}_{\mathcal{Z}}}\to\operatorname{Shv}({\mathcal{Z}}\times{\mathcal{Y}}_{2})_{\operatorname{co}_{\mathcal{Z}}}.

•

An object
{\mathcal{Q}}\in\operatorname{Shv}({\mathcal{Y}}_{1}\times{\mathcal{Y}}_{2})_{\operatorname{co}_{{\mathcal{Y}}_{2}}}
gives rise to functors

\operatorname{Id}\otimes{\mathsf{Q}}:\operatorname{Shv}({\mathcal{Z}}\times{\mathcal{Y}}_{1})_{\operatorname{co}_{{\mathcal{Y}}_{1}}}\to\operatorname{Shv}({\mathcal{Z}}\times{\mathcal{Y}}_{2})_{\operatorname{co}_{{\mathcal{Y}}_{2}}}\text{ and }\operatorname{Id}\otimes{\mathsf{Q}}:\operatorname{Shv}({\mathcal{Z}}\times{\mathcal{Y}}_{1})_{\operatorname{co}}\to\operatorname{Shv}({\mathcal{Z}}\times{\mathcal{Y}}_{2})_{\operatorname{co}}.

C.4.5.

When
{\mathcal{Z}}={\mathcal{Y}}
, we will use the notations

\operatorname{Shv}({\mathcal{Y}}\times{\mathcal{Y}})_{\operatorname{co}_{1}}\text{ and }\operatorname{Shv}({\mathcal{Y}}\times{\mathcal{Y}})_{\operatorname{co}_{2}}

for the corresponding mixed categories.

Let
{\mathcal{F}}
be an object of
\operatorname{Shv}({\mathcal{Y}})
. We have
the usual diagonal object

(\Delta_{\mathcal{Y}})_{*}({\mathcal{F}})\in\operatorname{Shv}({\mathcal{Y}}\times{\mathcal{Y}}).

In addition, we can consider the objects

(\Delta_{\mathcal{Y}})_{*}({\mathcal{F}})_{\operatorname{co}_{1}}\in\operatorname{Shv}({\mathcal{Y}}\times{\mathcal{Y}})_{\operatorname{co}_{1}}\text{ and }(\Delta_{\mathcal{Y}})_{*}({\mathcal{F}})_{\operatorname{co}_{2}}\in\operatorname{Shv}({\mathcal{Y}}\times{\mathcal{Y}})_{\operatorname{co}_{2}},

defined as follows:

The restriction of
(\Delta_{\mathcal{Y}})_{*}({\mathcal{F}})_{\operatorname{co}_{1}}
to
{\mathcal{Y}}\times{\mathcal{U}}
for a quasi-compact open

{\mathcal{U}}\overset{j}{\hookrightarrow}{\mathcal{Y}}
is

(j\times\operatorname{id})_{*,\operatorname{co}}\circ(\Delta_{\mathcal{U}})_{*}\circ j^{*}({\mathcal{F}})\in\operatorname{Shv}({\mathcal{Y}}\times{\mathcal{U}})_{\operatorname{co}},

and similarly for
(\Delta_{\mathcal{Y}})_{*}({\mathcal{F}})_{\operatorname{co}_{2}}
.

By Sect.
C.4.4
, the object
(\Delta_{\mathcal{Y}})_{*}({\mathcal{F}})_{\operatorname{co}_{1}}
defines a functor

{\mathsf{F}}:\operatorname{Shv}({\mathcal{Y}})\to\operatorname{Shv}({\mathcal{Y}}),

and the object
(\Delta_{\mathcal{Y}})_{*}({\mathcal{F}})_{\operatorname{co}_{2}}
defines a functor

{\mathsf{F}}:\operatorname{Shv}({\mathcal{Y}})_{\operatorname{co}}\to\operatorname{Shv}({\mathcal{Y}})_{\operatorname{co}}.

In both contexts, the functor
{\mathsf{F}}
is given by
{\mathcal{F}}\overset{!}{\otimes}-
.

Let now
{\mathcal{F}}
be an object of
\operatorname{Shv}({\mathcal{Y}})_{\operatorname{co}}
. Then
(\Delta_{\mathcal{Y}})_{*}({\mathcal{F}})
is naturally
an object of
\operatorname{Shv}({\mathcal{Y}}\times{\mathcal{Y}})_{\operatorname{co}}
. The resulting functor

{\mathsf{F}}:\operatorname{Shv}({\mathcal{Y}})\to\operatorname{Shv}({\mathcal{Y}})_{\operatorname{co}}

is given by
-\overset{!}{\otimes}{\mathcal{F}}
.

C.4.6.

When
{\mathcal{F}}=\omega_{\mathcal{Y}}\in\operatorname{Shv}({\mathcal{Y}})
, we will use the notation

\operatorname{u}_{\mathcal{Y}}^{\operatorname{naive}}:=(\Delta_{\mathcal{Y}})_{*}(\omega_{\mathcal{Y}}),\,\,\operatorname{u}_{{\mathcal{Y}},\operatorname{co}_{1}}:=(\Delta_{\mathcal{Y}})_{*}(\omega_{\mathcal{Y}})_{\operatorname{co}_{1}},\,\,\operatorname{u}_{{\mathcal{Y}},\operatorname{co}_{2}}:=(\Delta_{\mathcal{Y}})_{*}(\omega_{\mathcal{Y}})_{\operatorname{co}_{2}}.

In terms of Sect.
C.4.4
, the object
\operatorname{u}_{\mathcal{Y}}^{\operatorname{naive}}
defines the functors

\operatorname{Id}_{\mathcal{Z}}\boxtimes\operatorname{Id}^{\operatorname{naive}}_{\mathcal{Y}}:\operatorname{Shv}({\mathcal{Z}}\times{\mathcal{Y}})_{\operatorname{co}}\to\operatorname{Shv}({\mathcal{Z}}\times{\mathcal{Y}})_{\operatorname{co}_{\mathcal{Z}}}

and

\operatorname{Id}_{\mathcal{Z}}\boxtimes\operatorname{Id}^{\operatorname{naive}}_{\mathcal{Y}}:\operatorname{Shv}({\mathcal{Z}}\times{\mathcal{Y}})_{\operatorname{co}_{\mathcal{Y}}}\to\operatorname{Shv}({\mathcal{Z}}\times{\mathcal{Y}}).

The object
\operatorname{u}_{{\mathcal{Y}},\operatorname{co}_{1}}
defines
the identity
functors

\operatorname{Shv}({\mathcal{Z}}\times{\mathcal{Y}})\to\operatorname{Shv}({\mathcal{Z}}\times{\mathcal{Y}})\text{ and }\operatorname{Shv}({\mathcal{Z}}\times{\mathcal{Y}})_{\operatorname{co}_{\mathcal{Z}}}\to\operatorname{Shv}({\mathcal{Z}}\times{\mathcal{Y}})_{\operatorname{co}_{\mathcal{Z}}}

and the
object
\operatorname{u}_{{\mathcal{Y}},\operatorname{co}_{2}}
defines
the identity
functors

\operatorname{Shv}({\mathcal{Z}}\times{\mathcal{Y}})_{\operatorname{co}}\to\operatorname{Shv}({\mathcal{Z}}\times{\mathcal{Y}})_{\operatorname{co}}\text{ and }\operatorname{Shv}({\mathcal{Z}}\times{\mathcal{Y}})_{\operatorname{co}_{\mathcal{Y}}}\to\operatorname{Shv}({\mathcal{Z}}\times{\mathcal{Y}})_{\operatorname{co}_{\mathcal{Y}}}.

C.4.7.

We now consider the object

\operatorname{ps-u}_{\mathcal{Y}}:=(\Delta_{\mathcal{Y}})_{!}({\mathsf{e}}_{\mathcal{Y}})\in\operatorname{Shv}({\mathcal{Y}}\times{\mathcal{Y}}).

By Sect.
C.4.4
, it defines a functor

\operatorname{Shv}({\mathcal{Y}})_{\operatorname{co}}\to\operatorname{Shv}({\mathcal{Y}}).

This is the
miraculous functor
, to be denoted
\operatorname{Mir}_{\mathcal{Y}}
.

C.4.8.

Let
{\mathcal{Y}}^{\prime}\overset{j}{\hookrightarrow}{\mathcal{Y}}
be a cotruncative open embedding.
It follows formally that we have

(C.4)

j_{!}\circ\operatorname{Mir}_{{\mathcal{Y}}^{\prime}}\simeq\operatorname{Mir}_{\mathcal{Y}}\circ j_{*,\operatorname{co}},

as functors given by kernels.

C.4.9.

We will now consider one more variant of the construction of functors attached to
sheaves on the product of two stacks.

Let
{\mathcal{Y}}_{1}
and
{\mathcal{Y}}_{2}
be truncatable, and let
{\mathcal{P}}
be an object of
\operatorname{Shv}({\mathcal{Y}}_{1}\times{\mathcal{Y}}_{2})_{\operatorname{co}_{{\mathcal{Y}}_{2}}}
.
Then for any
{\mathcal{Z}}
, we have a well-defined functor

\operatorname{Id}_{\mathcal{Z}}\boxtimes{\mathsf{P}}^{l}:\operatorname{Shv}({\mathcal{Z}}\times{\mathcal{Y}}_{1})\to\operatorname{Shv}({\mathcal{Z}}\times{\mathcal{Y}}_{2})_{\operatorname{co}_{{\mathcal{Y}}_{2}}}.

C.5.
Miraculous stacks–the non quasi compact case

In this subsection we define what it means for a not necessarily quasi-compact algebraic stack to
be miraculous.

C.5.1.

Let now
{\mathcal{Y}}
by a not necessarily quasi-compact truncatable stack.

We shall say that
{\mathcal{Y}}
is
miraculous

if
\operatorname{Mir}_{\mathcal{Y}}
is
invertible, as a functor defined by a kernel
. I.e., if there exists an object

{\mathcal{Q}}\in\operatorname{Shv}({\mathcal{Y}}\times{\mathcal{Y}})_{\operatorname{co}}

such that

{\mathcal{Q}}\star\operatorname{ps-u}_{\mathcal{Y}}\simeq\operatorname{u}_{{\mathcal{U}},\operatorname{co}_{2}}

as objects of
\operatorname{Shv}({\mathcal{Y}}\times{\mathcal{Y}})_{\operatorname{co}_{2}}

and

\operatorname{ps-u}_{\mathcal{Y}}\star{\mathcal{Q}}\simeq\operatorname{u}_{{\mathcal{U}},\operatorname{co}_{1}}

as objects of
\operatorname{Shv}({\mathcal{Y}}\times{\mathcal{Y}})_{\operatorname{co}_{1}}
.

C.5.2.

It is easy to see that
{\mathcal{Y}}
is miraculous if and only if every
cotruncative
quasi-compact open substack
{\mathcal{U}}\subset{\mathcal{Y}}

is miraculous:

Indeed, the “if” direction follows from (
C.4
). For the “only if” direction, if
{\mathcal{Y}}
is miraculous and
{\mathcal{U}}\subset{\mathcal{Y}}

is cotruncative, formula (
C.4
) implies that
\operatorname{Id}_{\mathcal{Z}}\boxtimes\operatorname{Mir}_{\mathcal{U}}
is fully faithful. The essential surjectivity follows from the formula
obtained from (
C.4
) by duality:

\operatorname{Mir}_{\mathcal{U}}\circ j^{?}\simeq j^{*}\circ\operatorname{Mir}_{\mathcal{Y}},

where
j^{?}
is the right adjoint of
j_{*,\operatorname{co}}
.

C.5.3.

Still equivalently,
{\mathcal{Y}}
is miraculous if and only if the functor

(\operatorname{Id}_{\mathcal{Z}}\boxtimes\operatorname{Mir}_{\mathcal{Y}}):\operatorname{Shv}({\mathcal{Z}}\times{\mathcal{Y}})_{\operatorname{co}}\to\operatorname{Shv}({\mathcal{Z}}\times{\mathcal{Y}})

is an equivalence for every quasi-compact
{\mathcal{Z}}
.

If
{\mathcal{Y}}
is miraculous, then the functor

(\operatorname{Id}_{\mathcal{Z}}\boxtimes\operatorname{Mir}_{\mathcal{Y}}):\operatorname{Shv}({\mathcal{Z}}\times{\mathcal{Y}})_{\operatorname{co}_{\mathcal{Y}}}\to\operatorname{Shv}({\mathcal{Z}}\times{\mathcal{Y}})

and

(\operatorname{Id}_{\mathcal{Z}}\boxtimes\operatorname{Mir}_{\mathcal{Y}}):\operatorname{Shv}({\mathcal{Z}}\times{\mathcal{Y}})_{\operatorname{co}}\to\operatorname{Shv}({\mathcal{Z}}\times{\mathcal{Y}})_{\operatorname{co}_{\mathcal{Z}}}

are equivalences for an arbitrary
{\mathcal{Z}}
.

C.5.4.

Let
{\mathcal{Y}}
be miraculous. In particular,
\operatorname{Mir}_{\mathcal{Y}}
, viewed just as a functor

\operatorname{Shv}({\mathcal{Y}})_{\operatorname{co}}\to\operatorname{Shv}({\mathcal{Y}}),

is an equivalence.

In this case we define the
miraculous self-duality
of
\operatorname{Shv}({\mathcal{Y}})
to have as counit the functor

\operatorname{Shv}({\mathcal{Y}})\otimes\operatorname{Shv}({\mathcal{Y}})\overset{\operatorname{Mir}_{\mathcal{Y}}^{-1}\otimes\operatorname{Id}}{\longrightarrow}\operatorname{Shv}({\mathcal{Y}})_{\operatorname{co}}\otimes\operatorname{Shv}({\mathcal{Y}})\overset{\operatorname{ev}_{\mathcal{Y}}}{\longrightarrow}\operatorname{Vect},

which we denote
\operatorname{ev}^{\operatorname{Mir}}_{\mathcal{Y}}
.

The corresponding contravariant self-equivalence, denoted

{\mathbb{D}}^{\operatorname{Mir}}:(\operatorname{Shv}({\mathcal{Y}})^{c})^{\operatorname{op}}\to\operatorname{Shv}({\mathcal{Y}})^{c},

is given by

{\mathbb{D}}^{\operatorname{Mir}}\simeq{\mathbb{D}}^{\operatorname{Verdier}}\circ\operatorname{Mir}_{\mathcal{Y}}^{-1}.

Thus, explicitly, for a cotruncative quasi-compact
{\mathcal{U}}\overset{j}{\hookrightarrow}\operatorname{Bun}_{G}
and
{\mathcal{F}}_{\mathcal{U}}\in\operatorname{Shv}({\mathcal{U}})^{c}
,
using (
C.4
), we obtain

{\mathbb{D}}^{\operatorname{Mir}}(j_{!}({\mathcal{F}}_{\mathcal{U}}))\simeq j_{!}\left({\mathbb{D}}^{\operatorname{Verdier}}\circ\operatorname{Mir}_{\mathcal{U}}^{-1}({\mathcal{F}}_{\mathcal{U}})\right).

As in Sect.
B.7.3
, one shows that the pairing
\operatorname{ev}^{\operatorname{Mir}}_{\mathcal{Y}}
is swap-equivariant, and hence the
functor
{\mathbb{D}}^{\operatorname{Mir}}
is involutive.

Remark C.5.5
.

Note that unlike the quasi-compact case, Verdier duality is
not
a self-duality of

\operatorname{Shv}({\mathcal{Y}})
, but rather a duality between
\operatorname{Shv}({\mathcal{Y}})
and
\operatorname{Shv}({\mathcal{Y}})_{\operatorname{co}}
.
So, a priori, miraculous duality is our only option for a self-duality of
\operatorname{Shv}({\mathcal{Y}})
.
